[[sep-vplan-area-1]]
==== 1. Crypto and entropy

[[sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test]]
===== 1.1 `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves that one entropy source feeds the Key Manager and AES at once: both draw beats inside one window, each AES beat equals the next word on its own feed, the entropy stack matches its golden at every stage, and the AES first block equals its golden.

*Test Coverage*: One entropy source with Key Manager and AES consuming together.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image; park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC; leave AES released.
. Bring up entropy with strict golden checking and per-consumer scoring; wait for generated bits; start draining the queue; boot the Key Manager.
. Fork: one key-generate on the Key Manager, concurrently with reseed-and-encrypt cycles on AES.
. Confirm both consumers' beat counts advanced; compare the AES first-block ciphertext against golden.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONCUR
|Both consumers' beat counts advance within the forked window. This is an in-window advance, not same-cycle arbiter contention: the check would hold equally if one sink drained completely before the other began.
|CHK-AESKAT
|The AES first-block ciphertext equals the independent golden.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES beat equals the next word on its own feed, in order; both consumers' words are members of the golden generated-bits set. This row is the per-sink entropy checker; the scoreboard reports it as `CHK5_aes` / `CHK5_km` / `CHK5_axis1`.
|CHK-NONVAC
|A non-zero number of AXI transactions was observed, so the run cannot pass having done nothing. This is a transaction floor, not a comparison count — the cited line reports zero value-verified reads, because the value comparisons for this test live in the entropy scoreboard rather than the AXI scoreboard.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The shared entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|CHK4
|Generated bits match the golden. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this `glen=32` budget.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: CHK-ROUTING is reported by the entropy scoreboard as `CHK5_aes` / `CHK5_km` / `CHK5_axis1`, and CHK-NONVAC is the AXI scoreboard's transaction floor; neither carries its own `CHK-` record.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test]]
===== 1.2 `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`target = "lsu_stub_all_live"`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`)

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves the entropy stack is alive end to end on the open-source build and that its output reaches a real consumer.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy stack seed-out to a real consumer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Release the Key Manager with a small entropy-consuming ROM image.
. Configure the source, generator and distribution blocks in order and enable them.
. Start the golden scoreboard; drain the source queue through the front door.
. Wait for generated bits, for the Key Manager to take a word, and for a non-zero word to appear in its memory. The stored word is checked for presence, not value. The waits for generated bits and for the Key Manager handshake start before the Key Manager release, because its firmware reads one word only.
. Check the generator and distribution error and alert status.
. Send one instantiate (0x901, flag0 false) on the CSRNG software port, so that CSRNG takes a second seed from the source.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|Generator and distribution error and alert status are clear. The four status registers read are the generator's `0x10915054` / `0x10915050` and the distribution block's `0x1091583c` / `0x10915838`, each required to be zero.
|CHK1
|Decorrelated output matches the golden model.
|CHK2
|Compressed output, observed at the queue front door, matches golden.
|CHK3
|The seed matches golden.
|CHK-SEED-SW
|A CSRNG software-port instantiate (0x901, flag0 false) reaches `SW_CMD_STS.CMD_ACK`, CHK3 records one more seed handshake, and each 32-bit word of that seed equals a conditioner word of the golden that no earlier seed took. A command that is not sent, not acknowledged or that takes no seed fails, and so does a seed word that is not such a conditioner word. Golden: `CMD_RDY` before the write and `CMD_ACK` after it, `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/csrng/regs/gen/adoc/csrng.adoc:188-195`; flag0 false with clen 0 uses only the entropy source seed and requests it on the entropy source interface, `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/csrng/doc/theory_of_operation.md:19`, `:224`; the seed comes only from the conditioner, `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:148`. The position of those words in the conditioner stream is not graded: no text states which conditioner words the seed packer takes once an earlier seed is packed. The seed bits and the next packed seed of the golden are logged as `OBS-SEED-SW-BITS`. The `CMD_STS` code is logged as `OBS-SEED-SW-STS` and not graded: no text states the code of a successful software command (the open question of `OBS-CSRNG-STS`).
|CHK4
|Generated bits match golden. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this `glen=32` budget.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word and it matches golden.
|CHK-E2E-SCOREBOARD
|The strict CHK1..CHK5 scoreboard report ends with no mismatch. This is the leaf's own evidence line; CHK-ALERTS-ZERO comes from a shared base helper.
|CHK5_pool
|Mux endpoint [2] (entropy-pool adapter): each post-adapter pool beat equals the next AXIS2 word, and `pool_edn_fips_o=1` on every completed beat, so the adapter forwarded the DRBG FIPS bit. Does not drain `0x1095_0000`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: owner window 747, shared by both entries. The leaf opens the window after bring-up of the bench and before the entropy bring-up, so the strict scoreboard grades from the first seed. The sampler holds each event of the window as pending and samples it only on the rise of the pass label `esrc_e2e_pass`, which the leaf writes after every check of the run passed, the strict scoreboard report included. A FAIL closes the window first, so no pending event of a failing run samples. Bins: `cp_biw_scored[compress_match]` (a FIFO_RDATA read at level 1 or more that equals a conditioner word; graded by CHK2), `cp_seed_handshake[full_seed_acked_match]` (a CSRNG seed handshake of 384 bits that equals 12 packed words; CHK3), `cp_genbits_fips[fips_1]` (each generated-bits beat; CHK4; the illegal bin `fips_0` samples at once) and `cp_esrc_sink[drbg_seed_only]` (sampled on the label rise when at least two seed handshakes of the window had every 32-bit word equal to a held conditioner output word and none had a word that is not held: the EDN instantiate of the bring-up and the software instantiate of CHK-SEED-SW; no word order is required, matching CHK-SEED-SW; CHK3 grades the first seed bit-exact). No bin depends on the seed.

*Variant*: `sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test` runs this module with `+esrc_fifo_closed` and `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE=0`; see <<sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test>>.

[[esrc-noise-exception]]
*What `+esrc_noise_force` is, and what it costs*: a ring oscillator is a
free-running combinational loop — `entropy_ring_oscillator` is a NAND-plus-buffer
delay chain closed on itself — and no event-driven simulator produces physical
randomness from it. Injection is therefore the only way to exercise the stack,
and it is applied at the decorrelator noise input, upstream of every stage this
plan checks. This is the same node and the same default mode the entropy IP's own
testbench uses.

*Not claimed*: the injection point sits downstream of the entire noise source —
the ring oscillator, the metastable sampling flop, and the sample-clock-to-core-clock
synchroniser — and the oscillator tune/detune path drives that same stage. All of
that is real logic, and no test in this plan exercises it: no testlist entry runs
this stack with the injection off. Entropy quality, oscillator tuning and health
tests over real noise belong to the entropy IP's own testbench. Every stage after
the injection point is real logic and is golden-checked.

'''''

[[sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test]]
===== 1.3 `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Walks the nine-cell product of {ECB, CBC, CTR} x {128, 192, 256} in one invocation, with key, IV and plaintext randomised per cell.

*Test Coverage*: Nine AES cells: {ECB, CBC, CTR} × {128, 192, 256}.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and park non-AES EDN clients.
. Walk all nine {ECB, CBC, CTR} × {128, 192, 256} cells against `sep_aes_golden`.
. Score CHK1..CHK4 bit-exact and CHK5_aes routing.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the ciphertext equals the golden, the round trip returns the plaintext, and no alert fired. For ECB and CBC the round trip programs a real DECRYPT operation; for CTR it re-runs ENCRYPT, which is a keystream-determinism check rather than a decrypt.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every mode x key-size cell ran in one invocation and produced a *distinct* ciphertext, so the stimulus stream is live rather than repeating one configuration. Note the key, IV and plaintext are re-drawn per cell, so this does not by itself prove the cells programmed different modes — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard: the decorrelator, compress, seed and genbits stages each match the chained golden bit-exactly. OTBN, KMAC and HMAC are parked, so AES is the only crypto EDN client.
|CHK5_aes
|Each post-adapter AES beat equals the next AXIS1 word — the single-sink routing golden. The Key Manager is unused on this path. The minimum-evidence floor is raised to 32 scored beats, so the per-beat claim cannot be discharged by a single beat.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test]]
===== 1.4 `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Walks 17 cells: keyed HMAC over every legal key length for each of SHA-256/384/512, plus plain SHA for each variant.

*Test Coverage*: Seventeen HMAC/SHA cells across SHA-256/384/512 and legal key lengths.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`.
. Walk the 17 HMAC/SHA cells against `sep_hmac_golden`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONV
|The engine honours the *specified* software-key register convention. `hmac.hjson` (the `KEY` multireg) defines `key[1023:0] = {KEY0, ... , KEY31}`, so `KEY_0` is the most-significant word and no word reversal is needed; `hmac.sv` implements it (`secret_key_d` from `reg2hw.key[31-i]`). The convention is pinned from the specification, not discovered by asking the engine which one it agrees with, and not transcribed from the RTL it checks — see <<audit>>. On mismatch the failure reports whether the reversed order would have matched, which separates a key word-order regression from a general digest fault.
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the digest equals the golden, the done interrupt sets and clears on write-one-to-clear, and the error status is clean.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every legal cell ran, each producing a distinct digest. The matrix is derived from the generated digest-size and key-length banks minus `HMAC_ILLEGAL_KEYED`, which the sequence computes from the register specification's block-size rule (`hmac.adoc`: the key length cannot exceed the block size, 512-bit for SHA-2 256) rather than from the engine's own bound. It is the single source of truth for legality, so the sequence and this walk cannot drift apart. As on the AES sweep, the key and message are re-drawn per cell, so distinctness alone does not prove the cells programmed different variants — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|SKIP-ILLEGAL-KEYED
|Each digest-size / key-length pair the engine rejects as `invalid_config` is named in the log with its reason, rather than being dropped silently from the matrix. A declaration, not a checker: no negative cell is driven, so nothing here provokes `ERR_CODE`.
|CHK-ERR
|`ERR_CODE` and the error interrupt bit read zero across the walk. This is a passive observation: nothing in this entry induces an error, so there is no positive control that the register can ever be non-zero on this path.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test]]
===== 1.5 `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Thirteen cells across SHA3-224/256/384/512, SHAKE-128/256, cSHAKE-128/256 and KMAC-128/256 spanning all five key lengths.

*Test Coverage*: Thirteen KMAC/SHA3/SHAKE/cSHAKE cells across the legal key lengths. One run with an undefined `KEY_LEN` code and a 512-bit software key follows the walk and is logged only (`OBS-KMAC-KEYLEN-UNDEF`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`.
. Walk the thirteen SHA3/SHAKE/cSHAKE/KMAC cells against `sep_kmac_golden`.
. Undefined key-length run, after the walk and with no check on it. Draw `KEY_LEN` from the codes 5 to 7 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/doc/registers.md:693-704`: "Other values are reserved."), 1 to 8 message words and a 512-bit software key, after every draw of the walk. Run one KMAC-256 hash with that `KEY_LEN`. Poll `STATUS.sha3_squeeze` and `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` (a bounded wait of 80000 `clk_i` cycles), read `ERR_CODE` and the digest, then clear the engine.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the digest equals the golden, and the error status is clean.
|CHK-DONE-RW1C
|The done interrupt is observed *set*, written to clear, and re-read as zero — a real three-step contract rather than a single sample.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every cell ran, each producing a distinct digest. The walk covers the complete legal SHA3 strength set (224/256/384/512) and all five KMAC key lengths. As on the AES sweep, the key and message are re-drawn per cell, so distinctness alone does not prove the cells programmed different modes — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|CHK-ERR
|`ERR_CODE` and the error interrupt bit read zero across the walk. Passive observation only: nothing in this entry induces an error, so there is no positive control that the register can ever be non-zero on this path.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard: the decorrelator, compress, seed and genbits stages each match the chained golden bit-exactly. OTBN, AES and HMAC are parked, so KMAC is the only crypto EDN client. Evidence: `CHK-ENTROPY-CHAIN PASS: seed=<n> CHK1_decor=match<k>/mismatch0 CHK2_compress=match<k>/mismatch0 CHK3_seed=match<k>/mismatch0 CHK4_genbits=match<k>/mismatch0`.
|CHK5_kmac
|Each post-adapter KMAC beat equals the next AXIS1 word — the single-sink routing golden. The Key Manager is unused on this path. The minimum-evidence floor is 3 scored beats. KMAC draws entropy once per operation, not per block, so the floor sits below the beats one walk delivers. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-ROUTING PASS: seed=<n> dut_items=<k> match=<k> mismatch=0 min=3`.
|OBS-KMAC-KEYLEN-UNDEF
|Not a graded check. No text states the result of an undefined `KEY_LEN` code. The leaf logs whether the run reaches squeeze, the `kmac_err` bit, `ERR_CODE`, and whether the digest equals the golden of the 512-bit key. Evidence: `OBS-KMAC-KEYLEN-UNDEF key_len=<k> squeeze=<s> kmac_err=<e> err_code=0x<c> digest_eq_512=<d> digest0=<w>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Shared-code limit*: the SP800-185 encoders (`right_encode`, `encode_string`) are
shared between the stimulus and the golden, so an encoder error would program the
DUT and build the expectation identically. This is mitigated rather than
eliminated: the golden's import-time KATs exercise those same encoders against
NIST samples.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test]]
===== 1.6 `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Two crypto sinks draw EDN entropy concurrently and both complete correctly: each AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact and the KMAC digest matches its own, while both are shown to have taken real post-adapter EDN beats inside the contention window. An AES-alone baseline in which KMAC takes zero beats is the control that keeps the both-beats check from passing vacuously.

*Test Coverage*: AES and KMAC draw EDN together, plus an AES-alone control.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and `+esrc_noise_force`. Resolve the AES plaintext and the KMAC message from the seed and log them.
. Start the entropy stack in strict mode, with the AES and KMAC sinks scored against the golden. Wait for CSRNG genbits and start the FIFO drain.
. Reseed the AES masking PRNG once and configure AES-256-ECB.
. Load the AES key and encrypt one block with KMAC idle. Require the ciphertext to match the golden and the KMAC sink to have zero beats (CHK-NONVAC). Require at least one AES beat (CHK-SINK-BIND).
. Record both sink beat counts. Start two concurrent tasks: AES reloads the key and encrypts two blocks, and KMAC runs two keyed KMAC-256 operations. Require every AES block and every KMAC digest to match its golden (CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE).
. Require each sink's beat count to rise across the fork (CHK-BOTH-BEATS). Require the AES and KMAC beat time spans in the fork to overlap (CHK-OVERLAP).
. Require clean AES and KMAC status, stop the FIFO drain, and require zero CSRNG and EDN alerts.
. Call the strict `report()`. It grades CHK1..CHK4 and the per-sink routing against the AXIS1 grant stream (CHK-ROUTING, CHK-MEMBERSHIP).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|An AES-alone baseline reproduces its known-answer ciphertext while the KMAC sink takes *zero* beats — a real control showing the both-beats check below is not always true.
|CHK-SINK-BIND
|The same AES-alone baseline gives the AES sink at least one AES-named beat, so the per-sink beat attribution the later checkers use is bound to the AES client and not to the adapter order.
|CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE
|Under contention both clients complete correctly: every AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact, and the KMAC digest matches its own golden.
|CHK-BOTH-BEATS
|Both crypto sinks took real post-adapter EDN beats during the contention window.
|CHK-OVERLAP
|The two sinks' beat windows overlap in time, so one DRBG time-multiplexed two live clients. This is interval-span containment, *not* interleaving: it is satisfied when one client's entire burst falls in a gap between two of the other's beats. Same-cycle dual `edn_req` and alternating grants are <<sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test>>, not this entry.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES post-adapter beat and each KMAC post-adapter beat equals the AXIS1 word the adapter granted that cycle. Unscored clients (OTBN wipe) still consume AXIS1 words; those grants are popped so AES/KMAC stay aligned.
|CHK-MEMBERSHIP
|Every word either sink received is a member of the CHK4 genbits golden set (AXIS1 is chained to genbits in `report()`).
|CHK1..CHK4
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at each stage, in strict mode, with `report()` called. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this budget. CHK1's golden initial state is seeded from the live RTL shift register after reset, so CHK1 verifies the *recurrence* rather than the reset phase, and CHK2..CHK4 take the previous verified stage's DUT output as their input.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test]]
===== 1.7 `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves that the EDN fan-out `u_axis_edn_crypto_s3c_scan` (`drbg_axis_edn_adapter`), which `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` describes as a small round-robin native-protocol fan-out to AES, KMAC, OTBN RND and OTBN URND, grants and routes entropy to all four clients under full load, with the entropy pool scored as a fifth endpoint.

*Test Coverage*: EDN fan-out under its full client load. AES and OTBN URND are graded for sharing (both are granted); the grant order under simultaneous request is not stated and is logged only; OTBN RND and KMAC are graded for routing and consume; the entropy pool is scored as a concurrent fifth endpoint.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Hold AES PRNG reseed and OTBN URND `edn_req` together before EDN is enabled.
. Bring up the entropy stack (noise force, seed, then enable EDN).
. Sample `crypto_edn_ack_o` for a fixed grant budget, then let the checkers decide. The collection loop stops at 16 grants or at the end of the budget.
. Then load a four-read RND program into OTBN IMEM and issue EXECUTE without polling STATUS, so the blocked `crypto_edn_req[2]` is observable. RND cannot join the dual-request window above: it only requests while a program is blocked on the RND CSR, and OTBN cannot execute until its post-reset secure wipe has consumed URND, which already needs EDN enabled.
. Wait for IDLE, read `ERR_BITS`, and read the four RND words the program stored to DMEM.
. Then run a keyed KMAC-256 cell, the adapter's fourth client, and compare its digest against the Keccak golden.
. Collect the set of client bits granted anywhere in the run from the monitor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DUAL-REQ
|At least one cycle both selected clients have `edn_req` high. Without this, CHK-NO-STARVE does not see the two clients compete.
|CHK-NO-STARVE
|Each requesting client receives at least one `edn_ack` in the window.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES post-adapter beat and each OTBN URND post-adapter beat equals the AXIS1 word the adapter granted that cycle.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard on the same bring-up that feeds the dual-request window: FIFO drain after the seed, then `report()`.
|CHK-RND-REQ
|`crypto_edn_req[2]` (OTBN RND) is high while the loaded program is blocked on the RND CSR. Without this request the adapter never grants RND and CHK5_otbn_rnd cannot fail, so this is the control for it.
|CHK-RND-CONSUME
|The RND program retires with `ERR_BITS` == 0 in every bit except `rnd_rep_chk_fail` (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/doc/theory_of_operation.md:137`: `ERR_BITS` is set to 0 for a successful execution; `otbn.hjson`, `ERR_BITS`). Two equal EDN words set that bit by chance, so it is logged and not graded. A DUT whose RND reads end in an error, or that never completes the program, fails here. This is the consume proof at the OTBN end; CHK5_otbn_rnd is the bit-exact routing proof at the adapter, and its floor of one match per 32-bit beat of every RND read fails a DUT that does not fetch a new EDN word for each read. No text states the values of the four words the program stores to DMEM or that they differ, so they are logged only. Evidence: `CHK-RND-CONSUME PASS: 4 RND CSR reads retired with ERR_BITS=0x<e> (0 without rnd_rep_chk_fail)` and `INFO-RND-WORDS rnd_rep_chk_fail=<0|1> distinct=<k> words=[..]`.
|CHK-KMAC-CLIENT
|The fourth adapter client completes a keyed KMAC-256 whose digest equals the Keccak golden. A client that took EDN beats but produced the wrong digest is not a working client, so the digest rather than the beat count is what is graded here. The value matrix itself is walked by `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`; this cell is fixed.
|CHK-FOUR-CLIENT
|Every one of the adapter's four clients was granted at some point in the run. The grant sampler of the dual-request window watches only AES and OTBN URND, so this is taken from the per-client grant bits accumulated across the whole run.
|CHK5_aes / CHK5_kmac / CHK5_otbn_rnd / CHK5_otbn_urnd / CHK5_pool
|All four crypto sinks match the AXIS1 grant-order stream and the entropy pool matches the AXIS2 stream, with zero mismatches on all five. RND carries the only stimulus-derived floor: one match per 32-bit beat of every RND CSR read the program issues. The AES, OTBN URND, KMAC and entropy-pool floors are non-vacuity floors -- above zero, below the traffic any seed produces -- so they fail a sink that took nothing and do not bound one that started and then starved; the zero-mismatch requirement plus the grant-stream checkers are what is claimed for those four. Sharing is claimed for AES and OTBN URND only: RND and KMAC are granted outside that window, so this entry claims their routing and their consume, not their arbitration.
|OBS-EDN-GRANT-SEQ
|Not a graded check. The length of the alternating prefix of the post-adapter grants, the first 16 post-adapter grants and the first 12 dual-request grants, logged as lists. `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc` names round-robin arbitration, but the arbiter fills a one-entry FIFO per client (`sep-trng-distribution.svg`), so the order of `edn_ack` at the client ports, where this leaf samples, is not the arbitration order, and no text states it. Evidence: `OBS-EDN-GRANT-SEQ alt_pairs=<k> grants=[..] dual_grants=[..]`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Standing rule*: any test that moves data across the DRBG runs CHK1..CHK4
(strict scoreboard, FIFO drain, `report()`) and enables CHK5 on every live sink.
CHK5 mode is the strongest the stimulus allows: golden routing when the adapter
grant order is observed from `edn_ack` (one or more crypto sinks); membership
when the pull order is firmware-driven; observe when even membership is not
scored (`rom_main` KM). This test uses golden routing.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test]]
===== 1.8 `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force +km_rom_hex=km_rom_entropy.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Phase 2 DRBG iconic feature: a CSRNG Generate command completes at the commanded length and `gen_last` asserts. The trailing CTR_DRBG Update is not observed: this vehicle completes exactly one Generate, so no golden-compared block lands after that Update.

*Test Coverage*: CSRNG Generate at the commanded length and `gen_last`. The trailing Update is out of scope for this vehicle.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Release the Key Manager with the small entropy-consuming ROM image.
. Program the boot Generate and the auto Generate to `glen=4` from the same config object that feeds the golden.
. Bring up the entropy stack, drain the source queue through the front door, and wait for generated bits and a Key Manager consume.
. Value-compare the word the KM firmware stored to KM SRAM against the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint.
. Require at least one completed Generate of exactly four blocks, then report the strict CHK1..CHK5 scoreboard.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SEGMENTATION
|At least one Generate command completed, and every completed command carried exactly the commanded `glen=4` blocks — so `gen_last` was observed and a segment of any other length would fail. *The trailing CTR_DRBG Update is not observed*: it fires after the last block of a command, and this vehicle completes exactly one command, so no golden-compared block lands on its far side. EDN issues no second Generate on this vehicle, so the poll window is not what bounds the observation.
|CHK1..CHK4
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at each stage, in strict mode, with `report()` called. Bit-exactness *across* a trailing Update boundary is not claimed — see CHK-SEGMENTATION for why this vehicle cannot reach one.
|CHK5_km
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word on the post-mux AXIS endpoint and it matches golden bit-exactly — the same consumer close as the end-to-end smoke. It says nothing about Key Manager *memory*; `CHK5_km_sram` is the row that covers the store.
|CHK5_km_sram
|The word the Key Manager firmware stored to KM SRAM equals the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint. `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so a wrong-word store, a dropped store, or a store to the wrong offset fails here — where a non-zero-only liveness poll would pass. The stored value is a genbits word and so differs per run; only the invariant is claimed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: CHK5_km_sram is logged as `CHK-KM-SRAM`.

*Scope limit*: `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores
exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so `CHK5_km_sram` compares that one
delivered word against KM SRAM word 0; it does not claim a sequence of stores. A
second AUTO Generate after the Update is not claimed — the Key Manager ROM reads
one word and parks, which is enough for the legality check to execute.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test]]
===== 1.9 `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the per-IP software reset domains are isolated, using live crypto results rather than a poked status bit: each engine computes a golden-checked value, then one engine is reset and the other's held result must survive bit-exact. A software reset of OTBN, AES, or KMAC cancels that engine's EDN endpoints from the first cycle its paths report isolated, ahead of its gated reset, and leaves the other endpoints alone. The HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge domains also grade drain-before-reset. ABR grades the JTAG `IC_RESET` override on this leaf; its live-result isolation is on <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>.

*Test Coverage*: Per-IP software reset; neighbour crypto result survives bit-exact; crypto EDN endpoints cancelled ahead of an OTBN, AES, or KMAC software reset, with the held word never acknowledged afterwards; a KMAC software reset keeps the KMAC EDN response at 0 while URND completes its own beat; HMAC, KMAC and ABR drain-before-reset; ABR JTAG override.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Compute a golden-checked result on two engines.
. Reset one engine; the other's held result must survive bit-exact. Both directions. The AES pulse is graded on the first `CTRL_SHADOWED` read after it, which must equal the register-map reset, against a pre-pulse read that shows the programmed value. The KMAC and OTBN own pulses are graded the same way on `STATUS` and `LOAD_CHECKSUM`. The `STATE` of KMAC after its own pulse reads 0 in both shares. The `DIGEST` of HMAC after its own pulse and the `DATA_OUT` of AES after its pulse are logged and not graded. Each helper pulse logs `CTRL-RESET-PULSE` once the engine's gated reset fell and released.
. Hold HMAC in software reset and prove the same `DIGEST_0` read and a `CFG` write complete SLVERR, while the sibling AES port stays OKAY.
. Hold the KMAC reset and prove the gated reset waits for both its host and Key Manager paths to isolate, and that a read arriving inside the host-path drain window completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED`. Accepted-traffic drain is not claimed for KMAC; see *Drain-before-reset* below.
. Hold the Adams Bridge reset and prove the gated reset waits for both the full-AXI host path and the shared Key Manager path to isolate, that in-flight host reads resolve, that a read arriving inside the drain window completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED`, and that the JTAG override holds the domain while `SW_RESET_N` still shows it released.
. Before EDN is enabled, hold an OTBN URND request and an AES request. Resetting the requester drops that request with `edn_ack` low. A KMAC reset in the same window leaves the AES request high. With OTBN then held in reset, enabling EDN still grants AES, and releasing OTBN lets URND be granted.
. Once URND is being granted, re-arm that stream and step a KMAC software reset across one measured acknowledge period. On each gated-reset edge the KMAC endpoint's `edn_ack` is 0; its `edn_bus` is logged. URND may acknowledge on an edge; the word it presents is the word it had staged. A quiet edge does not drop URND `edn_req` unless the previous cycle already acknowledged, and does not replace a requested word with a different word. A URND word that reads 0 on an edge with `edn_ack` low is the last word acknowledged to URND before the edge, and URND is acknowledged again after it.
. Software-reset KMAC, AES and OTBN (after starting the RND program) while that engine's EDN request is live, until two resets of each find it live at the cancel; reset OTBN six times at a different offset from a URND acknowledge each time. From the first cycle all of the engine's paths report isolated until its gated reset releases, no endpoint of the engine is acknowledged; the endpoint's `edn_bus` is logged. After the six URND trials, walk OTBN resets from a fresh URND burst over every phase of one URND acknowledge period until a cancel start finds a URND word committed and not yet acknowledged. Then pulse OTBN so URND requests, and reset OTBN while the URND request is live, until two resets find it live at the cancel.
. Step a KMAC software reset across the phase at which URND holds a pending staged word (not the last word acknowledged to URND), and require that word to survive the edge and be the word URND is acknowledged with.
. Record every crypto EDN acknowledge through these steps. No word committed and not yet acknowledged on the cycle before a cancel start is acknowledged to any client afterwards; at least one such word is required. Each word an endpoint held at a reset request is logged with any later acknowledge of it. After the last check of the step, write the pass label (see Functional Coverage).
. TRNG-only reset leg. Re-establish the HMAC digest and the AES block. Write 1 to `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` and read it as 1 (`CTRL-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-SET`). Hold the TRNG reset (bit 5 of `SW_RESET_N`) and wait for the gated reset to assert; require the five accelerator bits released and the held digest and block unchanged (`CHK-TRNG-NEIGHBORS`). Inside the hold, read the first CSR word of ESRC, CSRNG and EDN; each read completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED`, and the gated reset is still asserted after the three reads (`CHK-TRNG-ISO-RESP`). Release the reset, wait for the gated reset to release and read `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` (`CHK-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-RESET`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both held results are golden-matched and survive a *second* independent read, so the survives/cleared checks below are observations rather than restatements of one sample.
|CHK-SELF-RESET
|The first AES `CTRL_SHADOWED` read after the AES reset pulse returns every field to its register-map reset, read under the field mask: `OP` 0x1, `MODE` 0x3F, `KEY_LEN` 1, `SIDELOAD` 0, `PRNG_RESEED_RATE` 1, `MANUAL_OPERATION` 0. The read before the pulse returns the programmed ECB-256 encrypt value, which differs from that reset, so a pulse that did not reach the AES domain fails. Golden: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson:627` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`), `:650`, `:675`, `:730`, `:762`, `:770` and `:804` (the reset values of the fields), and `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:97` (each accelerator has its own software reset); the compare is `word_reset(AES_CTRL_SHADOWED)` under the field mask. The `DATA_OUT` after the pulse is a secure-wipe pattern with no fixed value and is logged (`OBS-AES-DATA-OUT`); the reserved bits of `CTRL_SHADOWED` are logged (`OBS-AES-CTRL-RESERVED`).
|CHK-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES
|The HMAC digest is bit-exact intact across the AES reset and across the KMAC reset.
|CHK-REVERSE
|The reverse direction: the AES output is bit-exact intact across the HMAC reset. The `DIGEST` register of HMAC has no stated reset value (an external register with no reset, `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/hmac.rdl:242`), so the digest after the HMAC pulse is logged and not graded (`OBS-HMAC-DIGEST`).
|CHK-KMAC-SELF
|KMAC `SW_RESET_N` restores `STATUS` to its register-map reset on the fields with a stated reset value: `sha3_idle` 1, `fifo_empty` 1 and the two alert bits 0 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/data/kmac.hjson:707`, `:737`, `:745`, `:759`). `sha3_absorb`, `sha3_squeeze`, `fifo_depth` and `fifo_full` have no stated reset value and are logged (`OBS-KMAC-STATUS-UNSTATED`); the reserved bits are logged (`OBS-KMAC-STATUS-RESERVED`). The read before the pulse differs from that reset under the RDL mask (`sha3_squeeze` reads 1), so a pulse that did not land fails. The HMAC digest across the KMAC reset is graded by `CHK-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES`.
|CHK-KMAC-STATE
|After KMAC's own pulse, with `sha3_idle` = 1 graded by `CHK-KMAC-SELF`, the raw words 0 to 7 of both `STATE` shares read 0 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/doc/theory_of_operation.md`, "Keccak State Access": "While in an idle state or in the sponge absorbing stage, the value is zero."). Each share is read on its own, so two equal non-zero shares fail. The digest held before the pulse is non-zero, so a pulse that kept `STATE` fails. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-STATE PASS: STATE after KMAC's own SW_RESET_N pulse: share0 and share1 words 0..7 all 0 while idle (held [0]=0x<d>)`.
|CTRL-RESET-PULSE
|Control for `CHK-REVERSE`, `CHK-KMAC-NEIGHBOR`, `CHK-OTBN` and the AES and OTBN own pulses: each helper pulse waits for the engine's gated-reset probe to fall after the request and to rise after the release, and then logs `CTRL-RESET-PULSE <probe> fell=1 released=1`. A probe that does not move within the bound fails the leaf.
|CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER
|The Adams Bridge reset does not assert until both paths its domain depends on
report isolated: the full-AXI SEP host path and the shared Key Manager path. The
host isolate bit is 0 when the request is issued. The Key Manager path may
already be isolated (the request is the OR of this engine and the held Key
Manager isolate). The two are sampled every cycle from the reset request, so the
order is observed at the assert edge rather than read off a settled host path. A
design that never isolates the host path never releases the sequencer and fails
here.
|CHK-ABR-HOST-DRAIN
|Host reads issued in the same cycle as the ABR reset request resolve OKAY or
SLVERR, never DECERR and never a hang. The test records the event identities
still unretired at the cycle the isolate request rises, requires that set to be
non-empty, and requires at least one event from that exact set to drain OKAY.
Thus an earlier completed read cannot satisfy the drain verdict, and the leg
grades traffic the isolate had to drain rather than beats that had already
completed. The `CHK-HOST-DRAIN` terms apply to the ABR reads: SLVERR with
`0x1501A7ED` for a read accepted on `s_axi` after the request, `0x1501A7ED` in
every SLVERR, and no OKAY after a SLVERR of the same ID. The first clock with
ARVALID high and the AR handshake of each read at the slave port of the ABR host
isolate stage, with the isolate request at each, are logged only
(`OBS-ABR-HOST-DRAIN-STAGE`).
|CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|The `CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL` contract on the ABR full-AXI isolate: the arrival AR
is accepted on `s_axi` while the isolate request is up and the gated reset is
still released, and the read completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED`.
|CHK-ABR-JTAG-OVERRIDE
|The JTAG `IC_RESET` override holds the ABR domain while `SW_RESET_N`, read back
inside the window, still reports it released -- so the hold is attributable to
JTAG rather than to software -- and the domain returns when the override
releases. Both mux directions are graded.
|CHK-KMAC-NEIGHBOR
|HMAC `SW_RESET_N` leaves KMAC STATE bit-exact intact.
|CHK-OTBN
|OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` (rst_ni CSR) survives an AES reset and clears to its register-map reset on OTBN's own pulse; the checksum held before the pulse is a mark that differs from the reset (a positive probe that the pulse is what clears it, and an AES pulse that leaks into OTBN fails the first compare). The register-map reset is the generated reset of `LOAD_CHECKSUM` (`OTBN.reset32("LOAD_CHECKSUM")`). DMEM holds the staged mark across the AES neighbour pulse (cross-domain leak). OTBN's own reset runs a secure wipe, so DMEM retention across that pulse is not claimed.
|CHK-ISOLATE-PRE
|HMAC `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY with the live golden digest before the isolate request, so the later SLVERR is at a live mapped address.
|CHK-ISOLATE-LANDED
|`SW_RESET_N` readback shows the HMAC bit held, so a refusal cannot be a missed write.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SLVERR
|During the window a read of the same `DIGEST_0` completes SLVERR, not OKAY, DECERR, or hang, and its data is `0x1501A7ED` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-141`: a new transaction on an isolated path completes with SLVERR and read data `0x1501A7ED` in each 32-bit word). A beat with any other data, the live digest included, fails.
|CHK-ISOLATE-WR
|During the window a write to HMAC `CFG` completes SLVERR. The write target is the RW `CFG` register, not the RO digest.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SIBLING
|AES `DATA_OUT_0` answers OKAY with the held ciphertext in the same HMAC window (port-scoped, not a global bus kill).
|CHK-DRAIN-ORDER
|The HMAC gated reset does not assert until both AXI-Lite paths that domain depends on — the SEP host path and the Key Manager path — report isolated. The host isolate bit is 0 when the request is issued. The Key Manager path may already be isolated: that request is the OR of this engine and the Key Manager isolate, and the Key Manager stays at its reset default on this leaf. Read from the two named TB probe bits, sampled every cycle so the assert edge cannot be missed.
|CHK-HOST-DRAIN
|Host reads issued in the same cycle as the reset request resolve OKAY or SLVERR — never DECERR and never a hang. The event identities still unretired when the isolate request rises are captured, and at least one event from that exact set must resolve OKAY, so a read completed before the drain window cannot satisfy the verdict. Once isolation is requested, new transactions complete SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-142`). The drain reads share one AXI ID, so the n-th AR handshake with that ID on `s_axi` is the n-th read; a read accepted there with the request up completes SLVERR with `0x1501A7ED`, and a missing `s_axi` handshake fails. Every SLVERR drain read carries `0x1501A7ED`, and no read completes OKAY after an earlier read with the same ID completed SLVERR. A read accepted on `s_axi` before the request passes `u_axi_demux` and the bus conversion before the isolate stage (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:121-124`); no specification states where the stage accepts a read, so its response is not graded beyond the terms above. The test samples the AR handshake (`iso_slv_ar_hs_probe_o`) and the AR valid (`iso_slv_ar_valid_probe_o`) on the slave port of the HMAC host isolate stage with the isolate request probe on every clock, and logs them for each read (`OBS-HOST-DRAIN-STAGE`; the n-th stage handshake is the n-th drain read, because no other traffic targets the stage and the arrival read queues behind the drain reads). The log records the `s_axi` handshake time, the first clock ARVALID is high at the stage, the stage handshake time, the request at each, and the response and the data of every read.
|CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|A read *accepted inside an observed drain window* also resolves. The window is the cycles in which the HMAC host isolate request probe is high and the gated reset is still released. The arrival read is issued on the first such cycle, with an empty AR queue and its own AXI ID, and its `s_axi` AR handshake must fall inside the window. The log records the handshake time, the probe values at it, and the reset edge. The read is a new transaction on a path whose isolation is requested, so it completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-141`). Any other response or data fails, and so does a hang.
|CHK-ISOLATE-REOPEN
|After release the same `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY again. This is also what shows the drain did not leave the path wedged.
|OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-REOPEN
|The `DIGEST_0` data after release, with the held digest, logged and not graded: the `DIGEST` register has no stated reset value (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/hmac.rdl:242`). No golden.
|CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER
|The KMAC gated reset does not assert until both the host and Key Manager AXI-Lite paths report isolated, sampled in the same cycle as the observed assert edge. The check runs on the drain leg and again on each graded KMAC `CHK-EDN-LEAD` trial, with the same compare at the reset edge. Golden: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:97-98` (the reset controller drains and isolates both paths before the reset). The host isolate bit and the gated reset are 0 / released when the request is issued. The Key Manager path may already be isolated (same OR as HMAC; Key Manager held). A domain that asserted with no host-path drain window fails rather than scoring a steady state.
|CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|The `CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL` contract on the KMAC host path: a read issued on the first cycle the KMAC host isolate request is seen has its AR accepted on `s_axi` while the request is up and the gated reset is still released, and it completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED`. Fails explicitly if no window was ever observed or the handshake falls after the reset edge.
|CHK-TRNG-NEIGHBORS
|Parking the shared TRNG reset holds the TRNG `SW_RESET_N` bit and leaves the five accelerator bits released (OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, ABR). HMAC DIGEST and AES DATA_OUT stay unchanged in that window. KMAC STATE, OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` and the ABR datapath are not sampled here.
|CHK-EDN-PEND
|Before EDN is enabled, OTBN URND `edn_req` is high and `edn_ack` is low. `CHK-EDN-PEND-AES` is the same contract for the AES client, armed by a PRNG reseed that cannot yet be granted.
|CHK-EDN-FLUSH
|Resetting OTBN drops URND `edn_req` and URND `edn_ack` stays low. `CHK-EDN-FLUSH-AES` is the same contract for AES. `CHK-EDN-FLUSH-KMAC` resets KMAC while AES is still requesting: AES `edn_req` stays high and AES `edn_ack` stays low, so resetting one endpoint does not cancel or acknowledge a sibling.
|CHK-EDN-SIBLING
|With OTBN held in reset after that flush, and EDN enabled, AES receives `edn_ack`. URND `edn_req` and `edn_ack` stay low for the whole wait, so the cancelled request is not acknowledged once entropy is flowing.
|CHK-EDN-REARM
|Releasing OTBN lets URND assert `edn_req` again and receive `edn_ack`.
|CHK-EDN-EDGE
|After that grant stream is up, OTBN is re-armed and a KMAC software reset is stepped one cycle at a time across one measured URND acknowledge period. On each falling edge of the KMAC gated reset, KMAC `edn_ack` is 0. The KMAC `edn_bus` and `edn_fips` on the edge have no stated value and are logged (`OBS-EDN-CANCEL-BUS`). The walk includes at least one edge where URND `edn_ack` is high, and the bus on that edge is URND's own staged word. An edge with URND `edn_ack` low does not drop URND `edn_req` unless the previous cycle already acknowledged, and does not replace a requested word with a different non-zero word. A URND word (`edn_req` high, `edn_ack` low, non-zero bus on the cycle before the edge) that reads 0 on the edge with `edn_ack` low is graded by the acknowledge record. EDN acknowledges the word at the head of the endpoint FIFO and leaves it on `edn_bus`, and the next `edn_req` pops it (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/edn/doc/theory_of_operation.md:152-160`: "the next request will change the prior data by popping the data FIFO"), so a 0 on the edge is the pop of a delivered word only when the last URND acknowledge before the edge carried that word. A different word there, or no URND acknowledge before the edge, means the KMAC reset cleared a word that URND never received, and the check fails (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:161-165`: other clients are unaffected). With the KMAC reset still asserted, the next URND acknowledge must then come within 20000 cycles with a non-zero bus, or the check fails. Each such edge logs the cycles from that last acknowledge to the edge, and from the edge to the next acknowledge. The PASS line counts these words. The software-reset write is an AXI transaction and outlasts one KMAC seed, so an outstanding KMAC request on the edge is graded only when one occurs. An edge with no KMAC `edn_req` outstanding on the cycle before it is logged as `INFO-EDN-EDGE-KMAC-IDLE` and does not grade the KMAC cancel; the PASS line counts the edges with a live and an idle KMAC request. `CHK-EDN-LEAD` grades the KMAC cancel on a live request, ahead of the reset. The armed staged-word hold is `CHK-EDN-HOLD`.
|CHK-EDN-LEAD
|A software reset of KMAC, AES or OTBN cancels that engine's crypto EDN endpoints from the first cycle all of its paths report isolated (the host and Key Manager bits of `crypto_isolated_probe_o`) until its reset releases. KMAC, AES, OTBN URND and OTBN RND are each reset twice with the endpoint's request live: `edn_req` high on the last released cycle before the gated reset falls, and no `edn_ack` on that cycle or the one before. Control (`CTRL-EDN-LEAD-DUE`), per engine after its live trials: the same arm runs once more with no reset, and the endpoint must be acknowledged no later than the latest cycle, counted from the cycle the request is seen, on which the gated reset fell in a live trial of that engine; otherwise the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING CHK-EDN-LEAD` and fails. The control line logs the acknowledge cycle, the bound, the cancel window of each live trial (cancel start to fall, counted the same way) and `due_in_window`, the number of those windows that hold the acknowledge cycle; the engine is then pulsed back to reset. Evidence: `CTRL-EDN-LEAD-DUE client=<name> ack_after_req=<k> bound=<b> windows=<list> due_in_window=<n>`. The gated reset drops the engine's `edn_req` (`CHK-EDN-FLUSH`), so an acknowledge a missing cancel lets through is due on the released cycles of the window. KMAC is armed by a `CFG` commit with EDN entropy, AES by a PRNG reseed and OTBN RND by the RND program; each reseed spans several EDN words. OTBN URND is armed by an OTBN reset pulse: a cold OTBN raises URND `edn_req` and holds it until an acknowledge. Each attempt writes the reset on the AXI master itself 0 to 12 cycles after the request is seen, and up to 26 attempts per engine run until two find the request live; fewer than two fails. No endpoint of the engine (OTBN RND and URND together) is acknowledged on any cycle of that window: the released cycles after the paths report isolated, the cycle the gated reset falls, and every cycle it is asserted. The trials are the KMAC, AES, OTBN URND and OTBN RND live-request attempts, six OTBN phase trials that request the reset on a cycle where URND holds a word with `edn_ack` low, at least 0 to 5 cycles after its last acknowledge, and the OTBN trials of the committed-word walk (see `CHK-EDN-DISCARD`). The PASS line counts the URND-live resets of the phase trials and the walk. The `edn_bus` of a cancelled endpoint has no stated value; it is logged per trial (`OBS-EDN-CANCEL-BUS`) and not graded. Golden: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:161-165` (an OTBN, AES or KMAC software reset cancels that engine's endpoints in the shared crypto EDN adapter, from the cycle its paths report isolated until its reset releases).
|CHK-EDN-DISCARD
|Every crypto EDN acknowledge through the EDN steps is recorded as client and word. No word an endpoint held committed and not yet acknowledged on the cycle before its cancel started is acknowledged to any client afterwards. A committed word is one on the endpoint's `edn_bus` with `edn_ack` low that no client was acknowledged with before. URND shows such a word on one cycle of each acknowledge period. After the six OTBN URND trials, a walk of OTBN trials starts a fresh URND burst each time, waits for its first acknowledge, waits 0 to 12 cycles (one step per trial, above the measured acknowledge period) and writes the OTBN reset on the AXI master itself, so one pass puts a cancel start on every phase of the period. It stops at the first trial that finds a committed word. Fails if no cancel start found one, so the discard is graded on a word the endpoint would otherwise deliver. A word an endpoint held at the reset request is logged only (`OBS-EDN-HELD-AT-REQUEST`): EDN keeps serving the engine while it drains, and no text states whether such a word is committed. Golden: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:164-165`.
|CHK-EDN-HOLD
|A software reset of KMAC must not rewrite a sibling's in-flight EDN data. Armed on an edge where URND held `edn_req` with a pending word on `edn_bus` and no acknowledge yet, and that same word is still present on the edge. A word equal to the last URND acknowledge before the edge is a delivered word that EDN leaves on the bus until the next request pops it (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/edn/doc/theory_of_operation.md:152-160`), so it is not pending and is not the arm. An edge that zeroes a staged word with `edn_ack` low is not the arm: after the KMAC reset is released, the last URND acknowledge before the edge must carry that word, and the next URND acknowledge must come within 20000 cycles with a non-zero bus, or the attempt fails (the rule of `CHK-EDN-EDGE`). An edge that presents a different word fails. It fails if no attempt arms, so it cannot pass on an idle bus. Golden: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:161-165` (a software reset cancels only that engine's endpoints; other clients are unaffected).
|CHK-EDN-HOLD-KEEP
|The word URND had staged when the unrelated engine was reset must be the word URND is acknowledged with, within a bounded recovery window. The acknowledge may be the reset-edge cycle itself.
|CHK-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-RESET
|`FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` set to 1 before the TRNG-only reset reads its RDL reset value after the release. The shared reset is a full-IP reset in which "all entropy-source CSRs and `FIPS_LOCK` return to reset values" (`hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:224-227`). A DUT that keeps the lock through the reset reads 1 and fails. Anchor: `FIPS_LOCK` readback after the release of the gated reset. Control (`CTRL-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-SET`): `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` reads 1 after the write and before the reset; a read of 0 logs `CTRL-MISSING CHK-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-RESET` and fails the leaf. Evidence: `CTRL-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-SET: FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1 before the TRNG reset`; `CHK-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-RESET PASS: FIPS_LOCK.LOCK held 0x1 before the TRNG-only reset, read 0x<l> == reset 0x<r> after the release`.
|CHK-TRNG-ISO-RESP
|The TRNG domain isolates the ESRC, CSRNG and EDN host paths, and a new transaction on an isolated path completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-141`, `:155`). Inside the TRNG hold the leaf reads the first CSR word of each IP; each read must complete SLVERR with `0x1501A7ED` and not time out. A DUT that answers OKAY, DECERR, other data or no response fails. Anchor: the TRNG gated-reset probe reads 0 after the three reads, so the reads were inside the hold. Evidence: `CHK-TRNG-ISO-RESP PASS: ip=esrc addr=0x<a> resp=2 rdata=0x1501a7ed timed_out=0; ip=csrng ...; ip=edn ...; trng_gated_rst_n_low_after=1; each read SLVERR with 0x1501a7ed`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Logged and not graded*: `OBS-AES-DATA-OUT` (the `DATA_OUT` after the AES pulse), `OBS-AES-CTRL-RESERVED` (the reserved bits of `CTRL_SHADOWED`), `OBS-HMAC-DIGEST` (the digest after the HMAC pulse), `OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-REOPEN` (see its row), `OBS-KMAC-STATUS-UNSTATED` (`sha3_absorb`, `sha3_squeeze`, `fifo_depth` and `fifo_full` of the KMAC `STATUS` after the pulse), `OBS-KMAC-STATUS-RESERVED` (the reserved bits of the KMAC `STATUS`) `OBS-EDN-CANCEL-BUS` (the `edn_bus` and `edn_fips` of a cancelled EDN endpoint) and `OBS-EDN-HELD-AT-REQUEST` (each word an endpoint held at a reset request, with any later acknowledge of it). No stated value exists for any of them, and no coverage bin depends on them.

*Functional Coverage*: owner window 741. Windows: one per graded `CHK-EDN-LEAD` trial (the KMAC, AES, OTBN URND and OTBN RND live-request attempts and the committed-word walk), opened on the clock edge before the reset request and closed 2 clocks after the release is seen; one around the KMAC drain-leg reset and the first `STATUS` read after it; one around the AES pulse and the first `CTRL_SHADOWED` read; one around the OTBN pulse, the idle wait (status reads only) and the first `LOAD_CHECKSUM` read. The HMAC pulses, `CHK-EDN-EDGE`, `CHK-EDN-HOLD`, the OTBN pulses that arm URND and the six OTBN URND phase trials are outside every window, so they feed no cell with an unchecked value. Bins: `cp_self_reset[aes]` (the first `CTRL_SHADOWED` read after the AES fall, under the field mask; `CHK-SELF-RESET`), `cp_self_reset[kmac]` (the first `STATUS` read after the KMAC fall, under the mask of the fields with a stated reset value, when the last OKAY `STATUS` read before the fall differed from that reset; `CHK-KMAC-SELF`) and `cp_self_reset[otbn]` (`CHK-OTBN`) each sample the compare of their check. `cp_self_reset[hmac]` is an `ignore_bins` cell: the HMAC `DIGEST` has no stated reset value. `cp_iso_order_per_domain[kmac_fall]` samples on each KMAC software fall in a window, graded by `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER` (the drain leg and each graded KMAC lead trial). `cp_sw_rst_edn_cancel` {aes, kmac, otbn_rnd, otbn_urnd} is held at each reset release of a graded lead trial that found that client's request live (the `CHK-EDN-LEAD` rule) and sampled on the rise of the pass label `iprst_edn_pass`, which the leaf writes after `CHK-EDN-DISCARD` passed (`CHK-EDN-LEAD` grades the lead and `CHK-EDN-DISCARD` the discard); the label is held for 2 clocks and then cleared. Its illegal cell `held_word_acked_to_other` is armed at a release inside a window and watches for 65536 clocks, also after the window closes. No bin depends on the seed: the vectors are fixed.

*Evidence*: `CHK-HMAC-DRAIN-OPEN`, `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-OPEN` and `CHK-ABR-DRAIN-OPEN` record the opening clause of CHK-DRAIN-ORDER, CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER and CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER: when the reset request is issued, the host isolate bit is 0 and the gated reset is still released.

*Not claimed*: the JTAG `IC_RESET` override of OTBN, AES or KMAC. It bypasses the endpoint cancel along with the isolation sequencing, so the EDN checks above take their resets from `SW_RESET_N` only. A word committed to an endpoint in the same cycle its cancel rises is not targeted at SoC level; `CHK-EDN-DISCARD` grades a word committed before that cycle. The adapter waives the arbiter request checks only on the first cycle of an endpoint flush; `AxisEdnCancelledEndpointIdle_A` and `AxisEdnReqStableUnlessEndpointCancelled_A` are the per-endpoint cancel and request-stability checks, enabled for all four crypto endpoints. Every assertion-based contract here needs VCS -- `prim_assert.sv` withholds `OCAH_OT_INC_ASSERT` when `VERILATOR` is defined, so the arbiter and adapter assertions do not exist in the default Verilator build.

*Scope limit*: live-result isolation walks AES, HMAC, KMAC and OTBN. Drain-before-reset and the JTAG override walk Adams Bridge on this leaf; ABR live-result isolation is on <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>. The Key Manager bit stays at its reset default (a different mechanism) and is not claimed.

*Drain-before-reset*: claimed for the HMAC domain by `CHK-DRAIN-ORDER`,
`CHK-HOST-DRAIN` and `CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`, for the Adams Bridge domain by
`CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER`, `CHK-ABR-HOST-DRAIN` and `CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`,
and for the KMAC domain by `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER` and `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`.
The KMAC leg does not claim accepted-traffic drain: its drain reads go out with
the request only to give the isolate traffic, and their outcome is not graded. Adams Bridge does carry that property, on a full-AXI isolate. HMAC
drain runs against a live golden-checked result on a path this entry isolates.
ABR drain counts the host reads still outstanding when the isolate request
rises, so it grades traffic the isolate has to retire. The shared TRNG domain grades drain-before-reset in
<<sep_trng_reset_recovery_test>>.

*Not claimed*: drain-before-reset for the AES and OTBN domains — the
mechanism is shared, but a per-domain demonstration is not made here. Key
Manager isolate termination is not claimed here: `CHK-DRAIN-ORDER` reads the
`km_hmac` isolate bit, which is already isolated while the Key Manager is held
at its reset default, so it grades neither the KM path reaching isolation nor
what a KM transaction receives. That response is graded for the five key paths (AES, KMAC, OTBN, HMAC, Adams Bridge) in
<<sep_km_isolate_termination_test>>, which runs a KM CPU image so the
transaction comes from the master that owns the path. SMC CPU-MMIO isolate is
out of this subsystem.

'''''

[[sep_trng_reset_recovery_test]]
===== 1.10 `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves the one `SW_RESET_N` bit that resets ESRC, CSRNG and EDN
together behaves as a single coordinated domain: the reset waits for all three
AXI ports to isolate, its downstream clear changes only after two `clk_i`
synchronizer edges, the entropy pool scrubs whatever it held, the external source
path stays outside the domain, and recovery admits only fresh entropy.

*Test Coverage*: Shared TRNG `SW_RESET_N` drain-before-reset and ordered recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the internal chain up and fill the fabric entropy pool through
  ESRC → CSRNG → EDN, recording the pool level and that `FIPS_LOCK` is set.
. Write a source-select value that is not the RDL reset and confirm the write
  took. That non-reset value is the pre-reset baseline the later check must
  still see.
. Hold every entropy consumer (KM, OTBN, AES, KMAC), then request the TRNG reset
  while concurrent reads are in flight on all three internal CSR ports. The ESRC
  read goes out before the reset write; the seed draws for each of the CSRNG and
  EDN reads an issue slot, before the reset write or 0 to 15 `clk_i` edges after
  the write starts, so on some seeds a read meets the isolation request and is
  refused. Open the graded window right before the three concurrent reads; it
  stays open to the end of the recovery check.
. Through the reset, observe the isolate probe, the fate of the in-flight
  accesses, the two-stage clear synchronizer, and the pool's packer depth.
. With the domain in reset, read the three internal ports and the
  source-select register; then exercise the JTAG reset pair to hold and
  release the same coordinated reset.
. Reinitialize ESRC, CSRNG and EDN with consumers still held. Watch the seed
  valid output of ESRC from the clear of the JTAG override until the generators
  are enabled: it stays low. Require a fresh seed after the enable and fresh pool
  progress, and only then restore the consumer resets.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRNG-FILL
|The initial DRBG produces genbits and `FIPS_LOCK` reads set, so the pool level the later checks compare against is real entropy rather than a reset default.
|CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL
|While the reset is requested the isolate probe reads all three ports isolated. This is the drain-before-reset evidence: the reset does not assert until ESRC, CSRNG and EDN have all reported isolated.
|CHK-TRNG-DRAIN
|Every in-flight pre-reset access completes either OKAY or SLVERR — never DECERR and never a hang — and at least one completes OKAY, so accepted traffic provably drained rather than being dropped. Every refused read carries the isolated read data `0x1501A7ED`. An OKAY read of ESRC `COMPONENT_ID` returns the RDL reset of that read-only register (`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl` `COMPONENT_ID`, `0x01000001`), so an isolate that ends an accepted read with OKAY without forwarding it fails. The OKAY data of the CSRNG and EDN `INTR_STATE` reads is live interrupt state that no text fixes at this point, so it is logged only. Each read is graded on the answer it gets: the seed-drawn issue slot of the CSRNG and EDN reads decides whether a read is accepted or refused, and no compare depends on which. Golden: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:127-130` (new transactions on an isolated path complete with SLVERR and read data `0x1501A7ED`; each isolate unit drains its accepted transactions). The line logs the issue slots, the read count, the codes, the data, and the OKAY and SLVERR counts.
|CHK-TRNG-CLEAR-SYNC
|Inside `u_sep_trng` a 2-flop synchronizer samples the inverted TRNG reset and drives `trng_reset_active` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:110-111`). The leaf samples one picosecond after each rising `clk_i` edge. Edge 0 is the edge after which the gated reset first reads low; the clear still reads 0 there. The first flop takes the asserted reset on edge 1 and the second flop on edge 2, so the clear reads 1 first after edge 2: a clear after edge 0 fails as asynchronous, after edge 1 as a 1-flop path, and after edge 3 or later as a third flop. The release side in CHK-TRNG-JTAG counts from the release edge the same way.
|CHK-TRNG-PACKER
|The entropy pool's packer depth is zero, so a half-packed word does not survive the reset.
|CHK-TRNG-SLVERR
|All three internal CSR ports answer SLVERR while isolated, and each answer reads the data `0x1501A7ED`, so a port that answers SLVERR with other data fails. Golden: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:127-129`; the constant is `ISO_DATA` (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/tests/system/sep_trng_reset_recovery_test.py:101`).
|CHK-TRNG-EXTERNAL
|After the packer is parked the test writes a source-select value that is not the RDL reset of `sel[2:0]` (`0x7` in `sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl`), confirms the write took, then asserts the same value after the internal reset. A match on the reset value would be vacuous. The `0x1091_7000` passthrough is an adopter aperture; intra-window decode is not graded.
|CHK-TRNG-JTAG
|The JTAG override holds the coordinated reset asserted while software releases it. The override bit reaches the per-IP override mux through a 2-flop synchronizer, and the mux output passes only the scan bypass mux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:53-57`). The leaf clears the override between two rising `clk_i` edges (at a falling edge), so the first flop takes the clear on the first rising edge after it and the second flop on the second: the gated reset is still low a picosecond after the clear and after the first edge, and is released after the second edge, not later. A third flop or a missing flop fails. The downstream clear remains asserted through the first `clk_i` edge after that release and releases after the second.
|CHK-TRNG-CONSUMERS-HELD
|The `SW_RESET_N` readback shows the four consumers still held and the TRNG bit released, so the recovery ordering is observed rather than assumed.
|CHK-TRNG-CSR-RESET
|After the reset `FIPS_LOCK` is clear, the CSRNG and EDN interrupt enables return to their register-map reset values (`CSRNG.reset("INTR_ENABLE")` and `EDN.reset("INTR_ENABLE")` on the implemented masks) from the full masks written before the reset, and reserved ESRC `CTRL[0]` reads zero before a write of 1 and still reads zero after — RAZ and WI, not write-ignore alone. The interrupt enables are driven off reset first, so this is a 1->0 traversal rather than a read of two registers that were never written.
|CHK-TRNG-STALE
|The pool level read just before the reset, after the half-word park, is greater than 0, so the reset has queued words to scrub; a level of 0 there fails the leaf before the reset. With the pool level at 0 after the reset, a pop answers SLVERR ("Reading an empty pool returns `SLVERR` rather than `OKAY`", `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc:31-33`), so no pre-reset word is left to pop. No text states the read data of the refused pop, so the data is logged only (`OBS-TRNG-STALE-RDATA rdata=0x<d> resp=<r>`). A DECERR here would mean the aperture had fallen into the reset domain, which is the opposite of what this proves, so the response is pinned rather than tested as any non-OKAY.
|CHK-TRNG-NO-EARLY-SEED
|ESRC does not present a seed (`drbg_seed_valid_o` high, an unknown value counts as high) between the clear of the JTAG override and the enable of the generators, that is, before the reinitialization: "after release the internal chain produces no data until ESRC, CSRNG and EDN are reinitialized" (`hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:198-199`). A DUT that keeps or restarts a seed through the reset fails on the zero count of seed-valid clocks, which the leaf grades before the generator enable. Positive probe in the PASS line: the watch window is non-empty, and a seed follows the enable (`seed_after_enable`). Graded twice in `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/tests/system/sep_trng_reset_recovery_test.py`: the zero half before the generator enable, the full line after the seed. Evidence: `CHK-TRNG-NO-EARLY-SEED PASS seed_valid_high_clocks=0 watched_clocks=<w> seed_after_enable=1`.
|CHK-TRNG-FRESH
|ESRC produces a fresh seed, `FIPS_LOCK` sets again, and a pool read succeeds, all before the consumer resets are restored to their saved value.
|CHK-TRNG-RECOVERY
|Closing summary line over the thirteen rows above: the pool held words at the fill and just before the reset, emptied and refilled (the line logs all three levels), the stale read was rejected, fresh data was delivered, and the consumer resets were restored to their saved value. It carries no contract of its own.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: owner window 746. The window opens right before the three drain reads and closes after `CHK-TRNG-FRESH`; a FAIL closes it first, and an assertion in the body closes it when the body ends. Every access inside the window is graded: the drain reads (`CHK-TRNG-DRAIN`), the packer probe (`CHK-TRNG-PACKER`), the isolated reads (`CHK-TRNG-SLVERR`), the select read (`CHK-TRNG-EXTERNAL`), the JTAG pair (`CHK-TRNG-JTAG`), the `SW_RESET_N` read (`CHK-TRNG-CONSUMERS-HELD`), the `FIPS_LOCK`, `INTR_ENABLE` and `CTRL` accesses (`CHK-TRNG-CSR-RESET`), the status polls and the empty pop (`CHK-TRNG-STALE`), and the reinitialization, the seed and the pool pop (`CHK-TRNG-NO-EARLY-SEED`, `CHK-TRNG-FRESH`). Bins: `cp_iso_order_per_domain[trng_fall]` is held at the TRNG reset fall with all three acknowledges set (`CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL`, the same compare) and sampled on the rise of `trng_drain_pass`, so a run that fails `CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL`, `CHK-TRNG-CLEAR-SYNC` or `CHK-TRNG-DRAIN` fills no cell; `cp_trng_drain[drained_okay, drained_slverr]` are held at the R beat of each drain read that is outstanding when the isolation sets, and sampled on the rise of the pass label `trng_drain_pass`, which the leaf writes right after `CHK-TRNG-DRAIN` passes and clears when the window closes; `cp_trng_reinit[fresh_seed]` samples only a seed rise that follows an OKAY write of `RING_OSC_ENABLE` with ENABLE not 0 since the TRNG fall, so a seed before the reinitialization never samples; `cp_trng_reinit[fips_lock_restored]` (`CHK-TRNG-CSR-RESET`, `CHK-TRNG-FRESH`) and `[pool_pop_okay]` (`CHK-TRNG-STALE`, `CHK-TRNG-FRESH`) sample the check reads. `cp_trng_drain[drained_slverr]` is seed-dependent: it needs a drain read that the LSU port accepted and the isolation then refused, and the seed draws the issue slot of the CSRNG and EDN reads around the reset write. A TRNG read whose AR follows the isolation set stops the count of drain answers, since its answer cannot be paired with a drain read.

*Why the pool is checked from outside the domain*: the entropy pool's AXI
responder is not in the TRNG reset domain, which is what makes the scrub
observable at all — the port stays live while its contents are invalidated.

*The RTL clear and isolate assertions are VCS-only*: the six
`ClearDropsRequest_A` / `ClearScrubs*` / `ClearResetsStall_A` /
`NoEntropyAcceptedDuringClear_A` / `ClearScrubsPendingRead_A` assertions in
`sep_entropy_fifo.sv`, the two `AxisEdnNo*DuringClear_A` in
`drbg_axis_edn_adapter.sv`, and `ResetOnlyWhenIsolated_A` /
`IsolateHeldThroughReset_A` in `sep_isolate_rst_seq.sv` all expand to nothing
under Verilator, because `prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` both gate on
`ifndef VERILATOR`. Verilator is the acceptance backend, so those ten assertions
give no evidence there. CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL and CHK-TRNG-PACKER grade the
drain-order and packer-scrub contracts from Python probes on Verilator.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test]]
===== 1.11 `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the ML-DSA-87 key-generation path through the ABR register aperture: reset status, an ACVP keyGen known-answer public key, completion status, the PIC error and notification legs, and `ZEROIZE`. A second keyGen on the ACVP seed with one bit flipped is logged, not graded (`OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-SENSITIVITY`). Randomization is RANDCFG — the masking entropy words, and the seed word and bit flipped in the logged keyGen, come from the run seed (`SepAbrKeygenCfg`).

*Test Coverage*: ML-DSA-87 keyGen known-answer, PIC legs, and `ZEROIZE`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read the ML-DSA `NAME` and `VERSION` words and log them as `OBS-ABR-MLDSA-ID`; no check grades them.
. Read `MLDSA_STATUS` and require READY, not VALID and not ERROR (CHK-RESET-STATUS).
. Enable the notifications and require PIC `[34]` and `[35]` low. Wait for READY and write the ACVP seed and the seeded entropy words over the bus with the window closed. Start the PIC vector watch. Open owner window 753. Raise the error interrupt with `error_intr_trig` and require one rise of `[34]` within 64 clocks (CHK-PIC-ERROR-RISE) with no other vector bit moving in that clock (CHK-SRC35-EXCL). Clear the field by `W1C` and require `[34]` low within 64 clocks (CHK-PIC-ERROR-W1C); `error_internal_sts` reads 1 after the trigger and 0 after the clear (CHK-PIC-ERROR-STS).
. Issue KEYGEN in the same window; `[35]` rises once after the command write (CHK-PIC-NOTIF-RISE) with no other vector bit moving outside the stated same-clock bits (CHK-SRC36-EXCL). Wait for VALID with ERROR=0 (CHK-STATUS-VALID). Read the 648-word public key and compare it to the vector (CHK-NIST-PK).
. Require `[34]` low and `[35]` high (CHK-PIC-NOTIF). Clear `notif_cmd_done_sts` by `W1C` and require `[35]` low within 64 clocks (CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C); the field reads 1 before and 0 after the clear (CHK-PIC-NOTIF-STS). After each of CHK-PIC-ERROR-RISE, CHK-PIC-ERROR-W1C, CHK-PIC-NOTIF-RISE and CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C passes, set the bit of its `cp_abr_pic` bin in `abr_pic_pass`. Grade CHK-SRC35-EXCL and CHK-SRC36-EXCL over every rise since the window opened, and after each passes set the bit of its source in `exo_pass`. Close the window. A FAIL closes it first.
. Require VALID=1, write `ZEROIZE`, wait for VALID to drop, and require every public-key word to read zero (CHK-ZEROIZE).
. Wait for READY, write the ACVP seed with the seeded bit flipped and the same entropy words, and run KEYGEN again with the window closed. Wait for VALID with ERROR=0 and log the public key (`OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-SENSITIVITY`: the flipped word and bit, word 0, the count of words equal to the vector and of non-zero words). Not graded: `MLDSA_SEED` is write-only and `ZEROIZE` clears it (`integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl:77-79`, `:133-135`), so the seed cannot be read back, and the tree holds no ACVP keyGen case for the flipped seed. A key that differs from the vector would also come from a keyGen on the cleared seed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET-STATUS
|Reset STATUS is READY, not VALID, not ERROR.
|CHK-NIST-PK
|648-word public key matches the vendored ACVP keyGen vector.
|CHK-STATUS-VALID
|Completion STATUS is VALID with ERROR=0.
|CHK-PIC-ERROR-RISE
|After the `error_intr_trig` write, aggregate `[34]` (source 35, `interrupts.adoc:194-196`, bit = source - 1 at `:22-23`) rises once within 64 clocks (`abr_reg.rdl:569-573` trigger pulse sets the status; `:518` level interrupt). A stuck-low probe fails this row; the later `[34]=0` at completion is then a live observation.
|CHK-PIC-ERROR-W1C
|`[34]` is high before the `W1C` of `error_internal_intr_r` and falls within 64 clocks after it (`abr_reg.rdl:518`, write one to clear).
|CHK-PIC-ERROR-STS
|`error_internal_sts` reads 1 after the trigger and 0 after the `W1C`. The field masks come from `abr_reg.rdl`.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF-RISE
|`[35]` (source 36, `interrupts.adoc:198-200`) rises once after the KEYGEN command write (`abr_reg.rdl:543` command-done status, `:534` level).
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF
|Aggregate `[35]` high and `[34]` low at completion.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C
|`[35]` is high before the `W1C` of `notif_internal_intr_r` and falls within 64 clocks after it.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF-STS
|`notif_cmd_done_sts` reads 1 after keyGen and 0 after the `W1C`. The field mask comes from `abr_reg.rdl`.
|CHK-SRC35-EXCL / CHK-SRC36-EXCL
|At the rise of `[34]` (source 35) and of `[35]` (source 36), every other vector bit equals its value one clock before (`interrupts.adoc:22-23`, bit i is source i + 1; `:33-34`, slots 1 to 43 are individually assigned). Not compared: the six unstated idle bits and the same-clock bits of the ABR stimulus (source 33 beside both, and the other ABR source). A run with no rise fails. Catches an OR-network smear or alias.
|CHK-ZEROIZE
