|STATUS reads VALID=1 just before the `ZEROIZE` write, `ZEROIZE` then drops VALID, and every word of the pubkey window then reads zero. `MLDSA_STATUS.READY` is low through the `ABR_ZEROIZE` walk (`abr_ready = (abr_prog_cntr == ABR_RESET)`), so a READY-high wait would hang rather than pass vacuously. Scope: the pubkey read port is gated on `mldsa_valid_reg`, so a zero window proves the key is not readable, *not* that the RAM was wiped. The memory walk is not claimed; proving it needs a probe on the pubkey RAM or on `zeroize_mem_done`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The aperture is a `sep_crypto` demux rule at `AbrRegMapBaseAddr` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`), reachable from the CPU-LSU master the way the OTBN tests reach their block. It is *not* behind an `axi_lite_isolate` unit: unlike the ESRC, CSRNG and EDN legs, the external TRNG passthrough in `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_axi_interconnect.sv` carries none. Accesses are 32-bit beats (`AxSIZE=2`), one register each. Other access sizes and strobes are graded by <<sep_abr_access_size_test>>. The Key-Manager-side key CSR is the generated PeakRDL `abr_wrapper_key_reg`. The ABR register block behind this aperture is real RTL. This leaf writes the seed window over the bus. The key-vault seed path is not claimed here; <<sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test>> applies the same ACVP vector over it. The ML-DSA `NAME` and `VERSION` words are information only: `abr_reg.rdl` declares them `sw = r` with no reset and no SEP document gives their values, so this leaf does not grade them. The bus-sweep leaves use them as read targets against a reference captured from the DUT (`CHK-ABR-ID-REF`).

*Anti-vacuity*: the public key is a cryptographic function of the seed words
written over the bus, so an engine that drops a seed write fails CHK-NIST-PK.
The flipped-seed keyGen is logged only (`OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-SENSITIVITY`) and
carries no anti-vacuity claim.
`[34]` is driven high through `error_intr_trig` and cleared before the KAT, so a
stuck-low aggregator cannot satisfy both `CHK-PIC-ERROR-RISE` and `CHK-PIC-NOTIF`.

*Functional coverage*: `sep_abr_completion_cg.cp_abr_pic` and
`sep_pic_source_map_cg.cp_src_exclusive_other[s35, s36]`, owner window 753. The
window holds only the trigger, command and `W1C` writes and the edges they grade;
the seed and entropy writes run before it. Each `cp_abr_pic` edge is held and
samples only after the check of that edge passed: the leaf sets the bit of the
bin in the `abr_pic_pass` label after CHK-PIC-ERROR-RISE (one rise of `[34]`),
CHK-PIC-ERROR-W1C, CHK-PIC-NOTIF-RISE (one rise of `[35]`) and
CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C, so a second rise or a failing check leaves the bin at 0. The
`cp_src_exclusive_other` rise of each source is held and samples only after its
check passed: after CHK-SRC35-EXCL passes the leaf sets the bit of source 35 in
the `exo_pass` label, and after CHK-SRC36-EXCL the bit of source 36, inside the
window. Each label value is held three clocks, so each release samples before
the next step and before the end of the run.

*Not claimed*: the key-vault seed path — a green keyGen does not close it, and
this leaf writes the seed window over the bus. It is owned by
<<sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test>>. ML-KEM-1024 is equally not closed
by a green keyGen, and is owned by <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>; sign and verify by
<<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test]]
===== 1.12 `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the two ML-DSA-87 consume commands the keyGen leaf does not
reach. `MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` encodes SIGNING (0x2) and VERIFYING (0x3) beside KEYGEN
(0x1), and this leaf owns both, together with the signature and verify-result
windows they drive. Randomization is RANDCFG — the masking entropy words come from the run
seed (`SepAbrKeygenCfg`); the vectors are fixed.

*Test Coverage*: ML-DSA-87 sigGen known-answer, sigVer accept and reject, and
`ZEROIZE` between commands.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read the ML-DSA `NAME` words and log them.
. Before the first command, enable the notifications and require PIC `[34]` low. Raise the error interrupt with `error_intr_trig` and require `[34]` high with `error_internal_sts` set. Clear `error_internal_sts` by W1C and require `[34]` low (CHK-PIC-ERROR).
. Wait for READY. Write the private key, mu to `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU`, and the seeded entropy words over the bus. Issue SIGNING with `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` set, and wait for VALID with ERROR=0.
. Read the 1157-word `MLDSA_SIGNATURE` window and compare it word-for-word to the vector. Require `[34]` low (CHK-SIGN).
. Write `ZEROIZE`, wait for VALID to drop and READY to rise, and require every signature word to read zero (CHK-ZEROIZE).
. For the accepting case, then the rejecting case: wait for READY, write the public key, mu and the signature, and issue VERIFYING with `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` set. Wait for VALID with ERROR=0 and compare `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` to the signature's c~ (CHK-VERIFY-ACCEPT, CHK-VERIFY-REJECT). Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for VALID to drop and READY to rise.
. Require the two `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` values to differ (CHK-VERIFY-REJECT).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PIC-ERROR
|PIC `[34]` reads 0 before the trigger, 1 after `error_intr_trig` with the `error_internal_sts` field set, and 0 again after the W1C with that field clear. The field mask comes from `abr_reg.rdl`. An X or Z on the bit is a failure, not a low. This is the positive control for the PIC `[34]`-low clause in CHK-SIGN.
|CHK-SIGN
|The 1157-word `MLDSA_SIGNATURE` window matches the vendored ACVP sigGen vector
word-for-word, with STATUS VALID and ERROR=0, and PIC `[34]` low.
|CHK-VERIFY-ACCEPT
|The ACVP case that must verify completes VALID and `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` (the RDL array) equals the first part of the submitted signature, i.e. c~. That equality is the RDL pass condition.
|CHK-VERIFY-REJECT
|The ACVP case that must not verify completes VALID and `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` is not equal to that signature's c~. An engine that copies the submitted c~ into the result register -- unconditional success -- fails this checker.
|CHK-ZEROIZE
|The `ZEROIZE` after SIGN drops VALID, and every word of the signature window
that CHK-SIGN just read live then reads zero, so the open read and the closed
read are on the same signature. Same scope limit as the keyGen leaf: the read
port is gated on the valid register, so a zero window proves the signature is
not readable, *not* that the RAM was wiped. The `ZEROIZE` after each
verify command must drop VALID and raise READY; its window is not read.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The aperture is the same `sep_crypto` demux rule at `AbrRegMapBaseAddr` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`) the keyGen leaf uses, with the same 32-bit beats and no `axi_lite_isolate` in front of it. Both vectors are ACVP external-mu groups, so mu is written to `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` rather than a raw message being hashed on the way in. Signing is the deterministic variant, so the signature is a fixed function of the private key and mu and is compared word-for-word. A randomised signature could only be checked by verifying it, which is the weaker claim. `MLDSA_STATUS.VALID` is sticky and READY does not re-assert until a `ZEROIZE`, so every command is separated by one. The private key and public key are written over the bus. The key-vault seed path is not claimed. The ML-DSA `NAME` words are information only, not graded: `abr_reg.rdl` gives them no reset and no SEP document gives their values. A dead aperture fails `CHK-SIGN`, whose window is compared against the published vector.

*Anti-vacuity*: the rejecting sigVer case is the load-bearing half. An engine
that copies each submitted signature's c~ into `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` (success
unconditionally, in the RDL's terms) passes the accepting compare and fails
`CHK-VERIFY-REJECT`. On the signing side, deterministic external-mu signing
makes the signature a fixed function of inputs written over the bus, so a
cached, constant or non-running engine cannot match 1157 words.

*Not claimed*: `KEYGEN_SIGN` (0x4), the key-vault seed path, and
`CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` consume after a live ABR capture. ML-KEM is a
separate vehicle, <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_abr_pipelined_read_test]]
===== 1.13 `sep_abr_pipelined_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_pipelined_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: ABR is the only crypto block reached over AHB rather than AXI, through `axi_lite_to_ahb` in `sep_crypto_abr_wrapper.sv`. A bridge that streams pipelined reads can present the later transfers of a stream with a truncated address, and every such read still answers OKAY. A master on the SoC-facing inbound port may legitimately hold several single-beat reads outstanding with distinct `ARID`s -- AXI places no restriction on that (IHI 0022 A5.3), and no SEP document places one on this aperture -- and each must be answered with its own register's value. The KAT leaves read one word at a time and wait, so they carry no streaming evidence.

*Test Coverage*: The ABR register aperture, read from the SMN inbound master at 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 reads in flight.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Open inbound filter entry 0 for the ABR page. The filter denies by default; the rule is programmed from the CPU-LSU side, as every inbound test reaches these CSRs.
. Read `MLDSA_VERSION1` once on its own through the sequencer, establishing the value the aperture holds and that it is readable at all.
. For each depth in 1, 2, 3, 4, 8: issue that many single-beat reads of THAT SAME address with distinct `ARID`s, presenting every `AR` before consuming any `R`, then collect each response. A bus watch records every `AR` and `R` handshake on the `m_axi` pins during the step.
. Require every response at every depth to equal the control value and to carry an OKAY response.
. Require, at every depth, that the `m_axi` pins accept all of that depth's `AR`s before the first `R` handshake, and that the recorded read count in flight reaches the depth.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-CONTROL
|The control read of `MLDSA_VERSION1` answers OKAY with a known (no X/Z, enforced by the bus monitor) and nonzero value. `abr_reg.rdl` gives the register no reset and no SEP document gives its value, so the control read alone is the comparand for the whole sweep; a control that answered zero, or errored, would let every pipelined read compare equal to it and this leaf would be green over a dead aperture. The address comes from the RDL, never a literal.
|CHK-ABR-PIPELINED-READ
|Every read, at every depth, returns the value the register returns when read alone, with an OKAY response. `MLDSA_VERSION1` is read-only with unconditional combinational readback, so it holds one value and any other response is wrong. An error response is recorded separately from silently wrong data. A timeout counts as a failure, not a skip.
|CHK-ABR-PIPELINE-OVERLAP
|At every depth N, the `AR` and `R` handshakes recorded on the `m_axi` pins show exactly N `AR`s accepted, all N before the first `R`, and N reads outstanding at once. A path that serialised the reads would still return the right values and pass CHK-ABR-PIPELINED-READ; this row fails it, so the depth sweep is a measured overlap and not only the order in which the test presented the reads.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Testlist*: `crypto.toml`; `all` and `cpu_stub` in `all.toml`.

*Why the inbound master*: `m_axi` is the SoC-facing port
(`smn_inbound_axi_req_i` of `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv`, which reaches the fabric on
`.ext_axi_req_i`) and a real master. `s_axi` presents what the VeeR LSU
presents, and the LSU serialises MMIO loads, so a pipelined read on that port is
stimulus the real design cannot carry.

*Why the comparison cannot be explained away*: every read targets one address with one expected value, so reordering, id-to-event mis-association or per-id state reuse in the VIP could only redistribute correct values among the responses -- none of them can turn the register's value into zero. Single-beat only (`ARLEN = 0`): the crypto demux routes any `AxLEN != 0` to its error slave (`aw_is_burst` / `ar_is_burst` in `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_axi_interconnect.sv`), which `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` states to software, so no burst is involved or possible.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test]]
===== 1.14 `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the ML-KEM-1024 commands. ML-KEM is a second register block
inside the Adams Bridge aperture, with its own `MLKEM_CTRL` and `MLKEM_STATUS`
beside the ML-DSA pair, and this leaf owns it: the encapsulation key,
decapsulation key, ciphertext and shared-key windows are driven and read here.
Not randomized -- every input is a published vector.

*Test Coverage*: ML-KEM-1024 keyGen, encapsulation, and decapsulation known
answers, decapsulation walked as both the valid and the implicit-rejection case,
`ZEROIZE` between commands, and the Adams Bridge per-IP `SW_RESET_N` domain
graded on a live encaps result.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read the ML-KEM `NAME` and `VERSION` words and log them as `OBS-ABR-MLKEM-ID`; no check grades them.
. For every command below, wait for `MLKEM_STATUS.READY`, write `MLKEM_CTRL`, then wait for VALID with ERROR=0.
. Write the ACVP seeds d and z over the bus and issue keyGen. Read the 392-word encapsulation key and the 792-word decapsulation key and compare both to the vector (CHK-KEM-KEYGEN). Write `ZEROIZE`.
. Write the encapsulation key and the message randomness m over the bus and issue encapsulation. Read the ciphertext and the shared key and compare both to the vector (CHK-KEM-ENCAPS).
. Write `ZEROIZE`. Require VALID to drop and the shared-key window to read zero (CHK-KEM-ZEROIZE).
. For the ACVP case that decapsulates normally, then the case whose ciphertext was modified: write the decapsulation key and the ciphertext, issue decapsulation, read the shared key and compare it to the vector (CHK-KEM-DECAPS, CHK-KEM-DECAPS-REJECT). Write `ZEROIZE` after each case.
. Require the two decapsulation shared keys to differ (CHK-KEM-DECAPS-REJECT).
. Run a final encapsulation. Require a vector-matched shared key and `MLKEM_STATUS.VALID` set.
. Pulse the HMAC bit of `SW_RESET_N` and wait for the HMAC gated reset to assert. After release, require VALID still set and the shared key still bit-exact (CHK-ABR-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES).
. Assert `SW_RESET_N.abr_sw_rst_n` and require a read of `MLKEM_STATUS` to answer SLVERR (CHK-ABR-ISOLATE).
. Release the ABR reset. Wait for READY and require VALID clear (CHK-ABR-SELF-RESET).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KEM-KEYGEN
|For the ACVP seeds, the 392-word encapsulation key and the 792-word
decapsulation key both match the published vector word-for-word.
|CHK-KEM-ENCAPS
|For that encapsulation key and the published message randomness, the 392-word
ciphertext and the shared key both match the vector. Because the randomness is
supplied over the bus this is an absolute value, not a self-consistency check.
|CHK-KEM-DECAPS
|The valid ACVP decapsulation case recovers the published shared key.
|CHK-KEM-DECAPS-REJECT
|The modified-ciphertext case returns the published *implicit-rejection* key --
a different value, still exact. An engine that reported an error, returned
zeros, skipped implicit rejection, or reused the previous shared key fails here
while passing the accepting case.
|CHK-ABR-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES
|A live encaps result -- `STATUS.VALID` set and the shared key bit-exact against
the ACVP vector -- is intact after a sibling HMAC `SW_RESET_N` pulse. The
pre-pulse state is asserted first, so a run in which no result was live fails
rather than passing on an empty compare.
|CHK-ABR-ISOLATE
|The `MLKEM_STATUS` address that just returned OKAY with a live result returns
SLVERR while the ABR `SW_RESET_N` bit is held. This is the full-AXI host isolate;
every other accelerator host path is AXI-Lite.
|CHK-ABR-SELF-RESET
|The live result does not survive ABR's own reset: after release the engine
returns READY with `VALID` clear. `VALID` is hardware-driven, so a reset that
never reached the datapath leaves it set. The shared-key window is read-gated on
`VALID`, so its post-reset zeros are *not* offered as evidence of a wipe.
|CHK-KEM-ZEROIZE
|`ZEROIZE` between commands drops `VALID` and the shared-key window then reads
zero. Same scope limit as the ML-DSA leaves: the read port is gated on the valid
register, so a zero window proves the key is not readable, *not* that the
RAM was wiped.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The aperture is the same `sep_crypto` demux rule at `AbrRegMapBaseAddr` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`) the ML-DSA leaves use. The ML-KEM windows sit higher in the same 64 KB region; offsets resolve from the vendor `abr_reg.rdl` by symbol. `MLKEM_CTRL` is writable only while `MLKEM_STATUS.READY` is set. A write to a busy engine is dropped with no error, and the previous `VALID` would otherwise be read as this command's completion. keyGen takes the two seeds d and z over the bus and produces both keys. Encapsulation takes the message randomness m over the bus as well as the encapsulation key, which is what makes the ciphertext a fixed function of published inputs rather than a value only a round trip could check. Under FIPS-203 implicit rejection the modified ciphertext produces a different shared key rather than an error, and ACVP publishes that key. `MLKEM_STATUS.VALID` is sticky, so every command is separated by a `ZEROIZE`. The ML-KEM `NAME` and `VERSION` words are information only, not graded: `abr_reg.rdl` gives them no reset and no SEP document gives their values. A dead decode fails every window compare against the published vectors.

*Anti-vacuity*: the ML-KEM read ports return zero before a command completes, so
a compare against zero would pass on an engine that never ran -- every expected
value here is non-zero, and the vector loader refuses an all-zero expectation at
import. The rejecting decapsulation case is the load-bearing one: implicit
rejection has no error to observe, so the only thing that can fail is the value.

*Not claimed*: `KEYGEN+DECAPS` (0x4), the key-vault seed and message read lanes,
the shared-key write lane to the key vault, and ML-DSA.

'''''

[[sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test]]
===== 1.15 `sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, both TB masters

*Randomization*: none. The depths and orders are the cells, and the expected
words are the identity reference captured by `CHK-ABR-ID-REF`.

*Objective*: Proves that reads held outstanding together on the Adams Bridge
aperture each return the value of the address they name. The ABR host path ends
in a bridge that performs one AHB transfer at a time behind a fabric that holds
several accesses outstanding. A bridge that streams reads and drops
`HADDR[2:0]` on the streamed transfers answers every read OKAY and returns the
word at the wrong address from the third read of a pipeline on, so a lone read
cannot show it.

*Test Coverage*: pipelines of depth 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 on `m_axi` and on `s_axi`;
four address orders over the ML-DSA identity words; one order on a single ARID;
both masters pipelining at once.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read each identity word alone on `s_axi` and keep the values as the reference for every read below (CHK-ABR-ID-REF).
. On `s_axi`, program one read window over the whole ABR aperture (`0x1094_0000`..`0x1094_FFFF`) before `m_axi` reads.
. Read `MLDSA_VERSION1` alone on each master as the control (CHK-ABR-PIPE-CTRL).
. In each leg below, issue all of the leg's reads in the same cycle by driving the VIP master sequence directly. Record every AR and R handshake on the TB pins (CHK-ABR-PIPE-RID, CHK-ABR-PIPE-OVERLAP).
. On each master, issue pipelines of depth 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 of `MLDSA_VERSION1` (CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-M-AXI, CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-S-AXI).
. On each master, issue the mixed address orders over the identity words (CHK-ABR-PIPE-MIXED).
. On each master, issue four reads on one ARID (CHK-ABR-PIPE-SAME-ID).
. Issue eight interleaved reads on `m_axi` and eight on `s_axi` at the same time (CHK-ABR-PIPE-DUAL).
. Run every leg before the verdict, so that one run lists every failing leg with the RID, address and data of each read.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-ID-REF
|Before any other ABR read, each of the eight identity words (`MLDSA_NAME`, `MLDSA_VERSION`, `MLKEM_NAME`, `MLKEM_VERSION`) is read twice alone on `s_axi` at AxSIZE=2. Each read answers OKAY, the two reads agree, the value is nonzero, and each word differs from the other word of its 8-byte granule, so a lane or half swap is detectable. The bus monitor fails an OKAY beat with X/Z in the lanes read. `abr_reg.rdl` declares these words `sw = r` with no reset and no SEP document gives their values, so this capture, not a DV constant, is the reference for every later identity read. The values themselves are not claimed.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-CTRL
|A lone read of `MLDSA_VERSION1` returns its captured reference, OKAY, on each master. The pipelined legs assume a working single read, so a failure here is a path fault rather than a pipelining one.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-M-AXI
|On `m_axi`, every read of each depth-1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 pipeline of `MLDSA_VERSION1` returns its captured reference, OKAY, both through the VIP result and as the lane of the R beat carrying its RID.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-S-AXI
|The same on `s_axi`.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-MIXED
|On each master, pipelines over `NAME0`, `NAME1`, `VERSION0` and `VERSION1` in ascending, descending and even-words-first order, and an eight-read interleaving with repeats, each return the word they name. `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` requires the two words of each granule to differ, so a response for the wrong half of a granule is a mismatch.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-SAME-ID
|Four reads on one ARID return their own words in request order, so a path that reorders same-ID reads hands a request its neighbour's word.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-DUAL
|`m_axi` and `s_axi` each pipelining eight reads at the same time get their own words, with both masters outstanding in the same cycles.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-RID
|On every leg the multiset of RIDs on the R beats equals the ARIDs issued and accepted. The VIP's own ID capture samples the first response after issue, which under concurrency may belong to another request, so the grade comes from the pin record.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-OVERLAP
|On every distinct-ID leg all reads were accepted before the first response, and on the single-ARID legs at least three. Without the overlap the data compare proves nothing about pipelining.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The inbound filter denies by default, so `s_axi` must open a read window before `m_axi` can reach the aperture. The SEP AXI sequencer awaits each item to completion, so nothing overlaps through it; the pipelined legs therefore drive the VIP master sequence directly.

*Anti-vacuity*: the overlap is measured, not assumed, and graded alongside the
data. `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` requires the reference words to be nonzero and to differ
within each granule, so a zero or the granule neighbour's word is a mismatch
rather than a coincidence.

*Not claimed*: writes, which <<sep_abr_access_size_test>> and
<<sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test>> own, and the AHB transfer sequence itself.

'''''

[[sep_abr_access_size_test]]
===== 1.16 `sep_abr_access_size_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_access_size_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the data values, two dead word offsets past the
register map, one in-extent hole word and the two counter values of
CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD32 come from the run seed (`SepAbrAccessSizeCfg`). Every size, offset and strobe pattern, and every
directed dead word and hole word, is walked on every seed.

*Objective*: Proves the ABR access-size rules in
`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`. Every ABR register is 32 bits wide: an
8-byte-aligned 64-bit access is two 32-bit accesses, and one
at `addr[2]=1` reaches only the upper register; a write whose strobe covers
only one of the two words writes that word alone; a word whose strobe is partial
is not written and the access answers SLVERR, while the other word of a 64-bit
write is still written when its strobe is full; a narrow read reads the whole
register and returns only the bytes it names, with the other byte lanes zero.
`abr_top` ties the register block's byte enables high and the AHB slave
zero-extends a narrow write, so a partial write that got through would clear the
rest of the register. Inside the register map, an access to a word that no
register or memory window owns returns no bus error: the vendored register
block has no address check (`adams_bridge.adoc:107-108`). The response codes and
the read data of such a hole word are the "Hole in Extent" cell of the ABR row
of the generated SEP memory map (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc:66`,
"OKAY, 0x0 / OKAY"), which the default hole response of the SEP register
description generates (`hw/sys/sep/regs/include/sep_memory_map.rdl`, `ocah_hole_resp`); a
difference between it and the specification is a specification question and not
a change of the check.

*Test Coverage*: 32-bit, 64-bit, AxSIZE=3 at `addr[2]=1`, byte and halfword
reads; 32-bit, 64-bit full-strobe, `WSTRB=0x0F`, `WSTRB=0xF0` and partial-word
writes, including 64-bit beats that cover one word whole and the other in part;
32-bit reads, word writes and partial-word writes at unpopulated offsets past
the register map; 32-bit reads and writes at two in-extent hole words.
Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` `x_abr_access` (15 bins), owner
window 742. The window opens right before the first graded read and closes
after `CHK-ABR-INMAP-HOLE`, or at the first failing check. Each check that
passes writes the `x_abr_access` cells it graded to the `crypto_bus_pass` label,
holds it two clocks and writes 0, so a cell never samples before its own check
passes.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. `s_axi`, one access at a time through the SEP AXI sequencer, so the SEP
scoreboard also fails any response the test did not mark as refused.
. Reads are graded on the eight ML-DSA and ML-KEM identity words, against the
reference `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` captures from 32-bit reads alone at the start.
Neither `abr_reg.rdl` nor any SEP document gives their values.
. Writes use the `global_intr_en_r` / `error_intr_en_r` pair (`+0x8100` /
`+0x8104`), the one 8-byte granule with a read-write register in each half, and
the 32-bit `error_internal_intr_count_r` counter (`+0x8200`), whose upper
neighbour owns no register and reads zero. CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD32 also writes the
`notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r` counter (`+0x8280`).
. Every refused write targets a register primed with a set bit in every byte it
holds and writes the complement, so a write that landed on any byte moves the
readback.
. The AR, R, AW and B handshakes on the `s_axi` pins are recorded, so narrow reads and
AxSIZE=3 reads at `addr[2]=1` are graded on the whole 64-bit R beat as well as
through the VIP result.
. Past the register map, the five read-write registers are primed with values
that have a set bit in every byte, then each dead word is read, word-written and
partial-written. The map end is 4 bytes past the highest register
`abr_reg.rdl` places, cross-checked against the ABR extent the SEP memory map
states. Each expected response is the one the SEP memory map states for that
word (`env/sep_decode_resp.py`). The dead words are the first two past the end,
the last word of the window, the `+0x4000` mirror of every graded register that
lands past the end, and two seeded words.
. Inside the register map, at the upper neighbour of the counter (`+0x8204`) and at one seeded word that no generated register address and no memory window of the ABR block owns, read at AxSIZE=2, write all ones, read back, write zero, read back, and read every graded register against its primed or captured value. The all-ones write shows that the word stores nothing, and the zero write clears bits of a primed register that the word aliases onto.
. Every register written is restored to its entry value.
. Around every access of the leaf, the entry reads and the restore included, read the bench AHB counters into `abr_top` (`abr_ahb_xfer_count_o`, `abr_ahb_wait_count_o`, `abr_ahb_err_count_o`, `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`) before the request and after the response. The error count must not move (CHK-ABR-AHB-HRESP); a full-word access with an OKAY response at a word that a generated ABR register address or memory window owns must raise the transfer count (CTRL-ABR-AHB-TAP); the transfer and wait-clock deltas of each access are logged (OBS-ABR-AHB-RESP).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-ID-REF
|As <<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test>>.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD32
|Each identity word reads its captured reference at AxSIZE=2. The two event counters `error_internal_intr_count_r` and `notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r` (`sw = rw`, `integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl:624-654`) are written at AxSIZE=2 with two distinct seeded non-zero values, and each 32-bit read returns the value written to its own register; a read of the wrong register, or a read path that returns a constant, fails. Only this check releases the `rd32` cell of `x_abr_access`.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD64
|A 64-bit read at each identity pair returns `{high word, low word}`.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD64-UNALIGNED
|An AxSIZE=3 read starting at the upper word of a granule returns that word, and the lower four byte lanes of its R beat read zero.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD-NARROW
|Every byte and aligned halfword of the ML-DSA NAME and VERSION granules reads back the addressed bytes of the captured reference, and every other byte lane of its R beat reads zero.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR32
|32-bit writes land on each write target, walked to all-ones and back to zero on the enables and through seeded values on the counter.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-FULL
|A full-strobe 64-bit write lands both words of the pair, and all 32 bits of the low word on the counter while the unpopulated upper word still reads zero.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-LO
|AxSIZE=3 with `WSTRB=0x0F` writes the low word alone, OKAY, the upper register unchanged.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-HI
|AxSIZE=3 at `addr[2]=1` with `WSTRB=0xF0` writes the upper word alone, OKAY.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR32-HI
|AxSIZE=2 at `addr[2]=1` (`WSTRB=0xF0`) sets and clears the upper register, OKAY, the lower unchanged.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR-PARTIAL
|Each of the eight partial-word strobes (single bytes, both halfwords, both three-byte runs) on the low word, the upper word and the counter answers SLVERR and leaves the register unchanged.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-MIXED
|A 64-bit beat with `WSTRB=0x7F` writes the low word, leaves the upper one, and answers SLVERR; `WSTRB=0xFC` does the reverse.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD-NARROW-RW
|Byte and halfword reads of the counter return the addressed bytes of its seeded value, and every other byte lane of the R beat reads zero.
|CHK-ABR-UNPOP-RD
|A 32-bit read at every dead word past the register map answers the response code and read data the SEP memory map's Past Extent column states, while every read-write register holds a nonzero value, so an aliased read returns data. Where the map states OKAY, every byte lane of the R beat also reads zero. `adams_bridge.adoc` (aperture-decode paragraph): the crypto demux refuses every access past the register map.
|CHK-ABR-UNPOP-WR
|An all-ones word write at every dead word answers the map's write code, the word reads back as the map states, and all five read-write registers and eight identity words read their primed or captured values afterwards.
|CHK-ABR-UNPOP-PARTIAL
|A byte, a halfword and a three-byte write at the first dead word past the map and at the last word of the window are each refused with the map's write code and leave every graded register unchanged. Where the map answers a word write OKAY, the write reaches ABR and the expected refusal is SLVERR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, "ABR access size").
|CHK-ABR-INMAP-HOLE
|At each of the two in-extent hole words the read answers the response code, the read data (0) and the whole R beat that the "Hole in Extent" cell of the SEP memory map states, both writes answer the write code of that cell, the readback returns the same code and data, exactly two AW handshakes at the word appear on the `s_axi` pins, and no graded register moves. A DUT that answers an error inside the extent, returns non-zero data or non-zero byte lanes, stores the written value, or aliases the word onto a graded register fails. The response class is `hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc:107-108`; the codes and the data are `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc:66`, accepted as the golden by decision (see Objective). The leaf logs, per word, the response, the data and the R beat of the read, both write responses, the readback, the AW count and the moved registers.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RESTORE
|Every register written reads back its entry value.
|CHK-ABR-AHB-HRESP
|"ABR never drives `hresp` to error, so the AHB error response its slave interface implements is unreachable" (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc:99-100`). Over every access of the leaf (reads, full-word, partial-strobe and 64-bit writes, past-extent and in-extent hole accesses) the bench count of clocks with HRESP high on the AHB-lite bus into `abr_top` (`abr_ahb_err_count_o`) does not change. A DUT that answers any access with an AHB error response, the SLVERR of a partial strobe included, fails. Anchor: the monitor-only counter on the wrapper AHB nets. Control: CTRL-ABR-AHB-TAP. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-AHB-HRESP PASS: <n> accesses, the hresp error count did not move`; failure (raises): `CHK-ABR-AHB-HRESP FAIL: addr=0x<a> <rd or wr> bytes=<b> size=<s> hresp_err_clocks=<k> transfers=<t>, expected 0 (ABR never drives hresp to error)`.
|CTRL-ABR-AHB-TAP
|Reach control of CHK-ABR-AHB-HRESP. Each full-word access (4 or 8 bytes, word-aligned) answered OKAY at a word that a generated ABR register address or memory window owns raises the transfer count (`abr_ahb_xfer_count_o`) by at least 1, so the counters watch the live bus; at least one such access runs (every seed walks them). A missing control raises and fails the leaf. Evidence: `CTRL-ABR-AHB-TAP seen: <n> full-word accesses each raised the transfer count`; failure: `CTRL-MISSING ABR-AHB-TAP: addr=0x<a> <op> bytes=<b> size=<s> xfer_delta=0, expected >= 1 for a full-word access`.
|OBS-ABR-AHB-RESP
|Not a graded check. No text states the AHB wait states of an ABR access (`adams_bridge.adoc:51-56` states only one transfer at a time). The leaf logs, per access, the AXI response, the transfer count and the clocks with HREADYOUT low that the access added. Evidence: `OBS-ABR-AHB-RESP addr=0x<a> <rd or wr> bytes=<b> size=<s> resp=<r> xfer_delta=<t> wait_clocks=<w>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: each write target is driven through values it does not hold
before its compare, and the counter carries a full 32-bit seeded value, so a
dropped write, a write on the wrong lane or a zero-extended partial write each
change a readback.

*Not claimed*: concurrency, which <<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test>> and
<<sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test>> own, and `m_axi`. The order of the two 32-bit
accesses of a 64-bit access (`adams_bridge.adoc`: low address first): the test
records only the host `s_axi` pins, and every outcome it grades is the same in
either order. Unpopulated offsets between
populated objects below `0xC018` other than the two hole words are not walked; the no-alias compare covers
the graded registers only, not every populated ABR register.

'''''

[[sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test]]
===== 1.17 `sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, both TB masters

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the op mix, addresses, data, per-master
second-half depth (3 to 6 per master; the first half of each stream runs at depth 1), AWIDs and the RREADY / BREADY throttle patterns come from the
run seed (`SepAbrConcurrentRwCfg`); `reseed = 3`.

*Objective*: Proves the statement in `adams_bridge.adoc` that any number of
reads and writes may be outstanding on the aperture from any mix of masters,
and that each returns the value of the address it names.

*Test Coverage*: 32-bit and 64-bit reads of identity and read-write registers,
and 32-bit writes and refused partial-word writes, from `s_axi` and `m_axi` at
the same time with both response channels throttled; 64-bit writes from `s_axi`
alone, which owns the one read-write register pair in an 8-byte granule.
Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` `x_abr_concurrent` (6 bins),
owner window 750. The depth-2 cells fill in the lockstep first half of each stream (both masters
at depth 1), the depth 3-to-6 cells in the second half; which cells a seed
fills also depends on the op mix and timing, so they close over merged
regressions. The window opens
right before the two streams start and closes after the pass label or at the
first failing check.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. `s_axi` opens a read-write inbound-filter window over the ABR aperture for
`m_axi`, then sets each read-write register to a seeded start value.
. Each read-write register is written by one master only, and that master
issues all its writes on one AWID, so its writes to the register land in issue
order. `s_axi` owns the interrupt-enable pair and `error_internal_intr_count_r`;
`m_axi` owns `notif_intr_en_r` and `notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r`.
. `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` first captures every identity word from the DUT, and the
seeded streams are built after it. Identity reads name only words whose
captured value no other identity word read holds, so a response swapped
between two identity reads is a mismatch.
. Both masters run their seeded streams at once while seeded pause patterns
hold RREADY and BREADY low: the first half of each stream with one access
outstanding per master, then, after both halves drain, the second half with
the drawn number (3 to 6) outstanding.
. The FCOV owner window opens before the streams start and closes after `CHK-ABR-CRW-STRESS`. The leaf writes the pass label of the six `x_abr_concurrent` cells (`crypto_bus_pass`) only after `CHK-ABR-CRW-WRITE-RESP`, `CHK-ABR-CRW-READ` and `CHK-ABR-CRW-STRESS` pass, holds it two clocks and writes 0, so a response that a failing check graded never samples.
. After both drain, every register is read from both masters, then restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-ID-REF
|As <<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test>>.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-WRITE-RESP
|Every full-word write answers OKAY and every partial-word write SLVERR.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-READ
|Every read answers OKAY. An identity word returns its captured reference exactly. A read-write word returns a value inside its owner's write window: the last write whose response came back before the read was issued, or any later write issued before the read returned. At least one read-write word had a single legal value and at least one cross-master read observed a value the other master wrote.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-STRESS
|The pins show one AR per read and one AW and B per write, each master reaching its drawn second-half depth, at least 3, in accesses outstanding at once (`adams_bridge.adoc`: any number of reads and writes may be outstanding; a DUT that holds one access per master fails), cycles with both masters outstanding, and RREADY and BREADY each held low while a response was valid.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-FINAL
|After both streams drain, each read-write register reads its owner's last written value from both masters.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-RESTORE
|Every register reads back its entry value.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the window is computed from each access's issue and completion
times rather than widened to every value ever written, and the strict final
check does not depend on timing at all. The stress floors are measured on the
pins, so a run whose streams serialized fails rather than passing on sequential
traffic.

*Not claimed*: the order in which the bridge chooses between a waiting read and
a waiting write, and two masters writing the same register.

'''''

[[sep_abr_absent_decerr_test]]
===== 1.18 `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_abr_absent.parhex`, target `lsu_stub_abr_absent`. Verilator only (`tools = ["verilator"]` in `testlists/crypto.toml`): VCS coverage runs elaborate the single `default` target, which keeps Adams Bridge.

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Objective*: The Adams Bridge build option. `doc/integrator/src/defines.adoc` (Adams Bridge defines) says that without `SEP_ABR_EN`, `sep_crypto` terminates the ABR AXI aperture and the Key Manager's ABR key bus with DECERR subordinates. The `lsu_stub_abr_absent` target in `sep_sim_cfg.toml` is the `lsu_stub_all_live` model with the `+define+SEP_ABR_EN` line dropped from its generated bender filelist; every other target keeps the define.

*Test Coverage*: host read and write of the ABR aperture; KM CPU store and load of the ABR wrapper key aperture.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring-up. Require the ABR reset released and the host ABR path not isolated, so a DECERR is not the isolation answer.
. Read an HMAC register over the same crypto interconnect (control), then read `MLDSA_NAME` and write `MLDSA_CTRL`.
. Release the KM and wait for the KM ABR path to open. The image `km_rom_abr_absent` W1Cs the `IRQ_STATUS` AXI error bits before each access and reports which bits the access set: a store to the HMAC wrapper key aperture (control), then a store and a load at the ABR wrapper key aperture.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |What it proves
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-OPEN
|ABR reset released; the host ABR path is not isolated during the host legs and the KM ABR path is not isolated before the KM legs.
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-CONTROL
|An HMAC `STATUS` read answers OKAY on the crypto interconnect.
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-HOST-RD
|A read of `MLDSA_NAME` answers DECERR.
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-HOST-WR
|A write of `MLDSA_CTRL` answers DECERR.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-PRECOND
|Every KM leg started with both AXI error bits clear.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-CONTROL
|The KM store to the HMAC wrapper key aperture sets neither error bit.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-WR
|The KM store to the ABR wrapper key aperture sets AXI_DECERR and not AXI_SLVERR.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-RD
|The KM load from the same address sets AXI_DECERR and not AXI_SLVERR.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the same leaf run on the `lsu_stub_all_live` model (ABR present) fails `CHK-ABR-ABSENT-HOST-RD` with OKAY, so the host checkers discriminate the two builds. The HMAC controls on both paths answer clean in the same run.

*Not claimed*: the ABR memory tie-off and the held-low ABR interrupts of the ABR-absent build. The image also loads the first KMCSR address past the generated block; the answer is logged and not graded (see Known Limitations, "KMCSR extent").

'''''

[[sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test]]
===== 1.19 `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 1 seed per regression, because the seed selects only the extra pop count

*Source Module*: `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — extra accepted pops come from the run seed (`SepEntropyPoolCfg`). The alias set and all three unique-dead offsets are walked every seed, so the decode coverage is seed-independent.

*Objective*: The fabric entropy pool answers only its three live registers. Occupancy, the live `pool_low` flag and fill-stall status reach software and the interrupt aggregator, a pop drains one word, and an empty pop is refused. Writes to each live offset and a representative unmapped offset return SLVERR without changing occupancy. Every mapped high-bit mirror and unique-dead offset walked by the test is refused with zero read data. A 32-bit read of a register's upper word is refused with zero read data and does not pop or disturb the FIFO.

*Test Coverage*: Three live pool registers; pop, empty-refuse, unmapped refuse, writes to STATUS, IRQ_CAUSE, DATA and one unmapped offset, plus the shared write refusal under all three AW/W arrival orders; 32-bit reads of the upper word of each register.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. With the pool empty, require level 0, log `status[6]`, `[36]` and `0x08` (`OBS-POOL-LOW-EMPTY`), and require `[36]` equal to `status[6]` (CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH). Log the `IRQ_CAUSE` value (`OBS-POOL-CAUSE-EMPTY`): no document states an `IRQ_CAUSE` value (`sep_entropy_pool.rdl` gives its fields with no description, and `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc:34` states only that the read is non-destructive), so each `OBS-POOL-CAUSE-*` line logs it and no check depends on it. Open the graded window after CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH, before the pool beat capture. Arm the level-step tracker (CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP) when the capture starts: from here on every pool aperture access, of every class, feeds it.
. Start the pool beat capture and the shared entropy bring-up, with the pool sink in observe mode. Wait for genbits and start the FIFO drain.
. Wait until the live `pool_low` flag reads 0. Require `fifo_level` greater than 0, `pool_low` 0 and `[36]` 0 at the same read (CHK-POOL-FILL, CHK-POOL-LOW-LOW, CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE). Log the `IRQ_CAUSE` value (`OBS-POOL-CAUSE-IDLE`).
. Disable ESRC. Read every high-bit mirror of the three live registers and all three unique-dead offsets, and require SLVERR with RDATA=0 (CHK-UNMAPPED).
. Disable EDN and pop until the pool is not full with a request outstanding. Write STATUS, IRQ_CAUSE, DATA and one unmapped offset with AW and W in the same cycle; require SLVERR and unchanged occupancy (CHK-WRITE-SLVERR).
. Pause the FIFO drain. Drive the shared write refusal two more ways at STATUS: AW leading and W leading, by arming an `AxiTimingProfile` on the master (CHK-WRITE-ORDER). Restart the drain.
. Poll `STATUS` every 64 clocks until `fill_stall` reads 1, up to the DV ceiling `STALL_POLL_CAP` (65536 clocks), and log the time to the flag (`OBS-POOL-STALL-TIME`). Require `fill_stall` and `[37]` high (CHK-STALL-ASSERT) and log the `IRQ_CAUSE` value (`OBS-POOL-CAUSE-STALL`). Hold the stall for `STALL_THRESH` x 3 (12288) more clocks and require `[37]` still high (CHK-STALL-DURATION); log the `IRQ_CAUSE` value again (`OBS-POOL-CAUSE-HELD`). With EDN disabled, so that no word is added, read `IRQ_CAUSE` twice and then `STATUS` once more: the level equals the level that the stall phase armed, and the tracker grades exactly one new pair of class `cause` (CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP).
. Re-enable EDN and ESRC and wait for `[37]` low (CHK-STALL-CLEAR).
. Disable EDN and ESRC and make the pool not full again. Pop once, issue the three 32-bit upper-word reads, and pop again (CHK-HALF-READ).
. Pop the seeded count of extra words. Require OKAY, level minus one, and for each pop the next packed word of the captured pool EDN beats after the CHK-HALF-READ pops, in the packing order that CHK-HALF-READ found (CHK-POP-DATA).
. Drain to empty, compare `[36]` to `status[6]` on each status read (CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE) and grade each drain pop as in CHK-POP-DATA. Log `status[6]` and `[36]` at empty (`OBS-POOL-LOW-REHIGH`). Pop once more and require SLVERR (CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR). Require that every access class (pop, status, cause, write, upper_data) has at least one graded pair (CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP). Close the graded window and stop the tracker.
. Stop the drain, require the CSRNG and EDN alerts zero (CHK-ALERTS-ZERO), and call `report()` (CHK5_pool).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP
|Between two 64-bit `STATUS` reads the `fifo_level` falls by exactly the number of OKAY 64-bit `DATA` reads between them, and by 0 for every other access: a `STATUS` or `IRQ_CAUSE` read, a write to any offset, and a 32-bit read of the upper word of any register. A pair of reads is graded only if no pool EDN beat (a request with its acknowledge) occurs from the issue of the first `STATUS` read to the end of the second, so a fill cannot change the level; a pair with such a beat is counted and skipped. The class of a pair is the last pop, `IRQ_CAUSE` read, write or 32-bit `DATA` upper-word read between the two reads, and `status` when none occurs. The compare is an exact integer compare. In the stall phase one added `STATUS` read follows the `IRQ_CAUSE` reads and must give exactly one new graded pair of class `cause` whose level equals the level that the phase armed. At the end, each of the five classes (pop, status, cause, write, upper_data) must have at least one graded pair. A `STATUS` or `IRQ_CAUSE` read that pops, a refused write or a refused upper-word `DATA` read that pops, a pop that removes 0 or 2 words, or a level that moves with no EDN beat fails. Golden: `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc:31` (a `DATA` read pops one word), `:34` (`STATUS` and `IRQ_CAUSE` are non-destructive), `:38-39` (a write changes no state), `:42-43` (a refused upper-word `DATA` read does not pop). Anchor: `STATUS` readback and the captured pool EDN beat list. Every class runs on every seed with EDN disabled, so the pairs are graded on every seed. The PASS line logs the graded and skipped pairs and the distinct (before, after) steps of each class. Graded at `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/tests/system/sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test.py:148-154` (per pair), `:677-687` (stall-phase pair) and `:770-776` (class floor).
|CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH
|At empty (level 0) the aggregate `[36]` equals the `pool_low` flag of `STATUS` (`status[6]`). The low watermark is not stated (`sep_entropy_pool.rdl` `STATUS.pool_low`: "below the configured low watermark"), so the flag value at empty is logged only (`OBS-POOL-LOW-EMPTY`).
|CHK-POOL-FILL
|After the fill, one `STATUS` read shows `fifo_level` greater than 0 with `pool_low` 0, and `[36]` reads 0 after it. The line claims that `pool_low` clears only when `pool_low` and `[36]` both read 1 at the empty read of CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH (`high_at_empty=1`); when they read 0 there, the line claims no clear, because the low watermark is not stated (`sep_entropy_pool.rdl` `STATUS.pool_low`: "below the configured low watermark") and a flag that reads 0 at level 0 is not a fault. `pool_err` reads 0 on every fill poll. `interrupts.adoc` source 39 says the pointer-integrity check is not built in the current configuration, so that source is tied to zero; the zero is not a fault-free claim and no pool integrity fault is claimed.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-LOW
|Aggregate `[36]` low after fill.
|CHK-STALL-DURATION
|Holding the same stall `STALL_THRESH` x 3 (12288, a DV-chosen hold) more clocks after the flag rose leaves `[37]` asserted. The fill request stays outstanding, so a counter that wrapped or a flag that self-cleared would drop the fault while the outage continued.
|CHK-WRITE-ORDER
|A write whose AW leads W, and one whose W leads AW, each answer a single BRESP=SLVERR and leave `fifo_level` unchanged. AW and W carry no ordering requirement between them (AMBA IHI 0022 A3.3). The backend presents both in the same cycle, so the split-arrival arms of that handshake need the master armed with an `AxiTimingProfile`; the profile is only a request, so each arm is credited from the order the `s_axi` monitor observed (first AWVALID and WVALID cycle), and the background FIFO drain on the same master is paused while the profile is armed. A slave that assumed same-cycle arrival would wedge or answer twice.
|CHK-WRITE-SLVERR
|Writes to STATUS, IRQ_CAUSE, DATA and one unmapped offset return SLVERR and do not change `fifo_level` while the pool is not full (a full FIFO cannot show a refused fill).
|CHK-UNMAPPED
|Every high-bit mirror of a live register, plus all three unique-dead offsets, returns SLVERR with RDATA=0. All three are walked every seed: `0x18` is the unused `[4:3]=11` code and catches a decode class the other two do not, so a seeded pick of one could miss it.
|CHK-STALL-ASSERT
|With the pool not full and the fill request outstanding, `STATUS.fill_stall` reads 1 within the DV poll ceiling (65536 clocks) and `[37]` is high. The stall threshold is not stated (`sep_entropy_pool.rdl` `STATUS.fill_stall`: "the configured stall threshold"), so the time to the flag is logged only (`OBS-POOL-STALL-TIME`); a DUT threshold above the ceiling fails.
|CHK-STALL-CLEAR
|`[37]` low after EDN and ESRC re-enable.
|CHK-HALF-READ
|`sep_entropy_pool.rdl` declares every register `accesswidth = 64`. A 32-bit read (AxSIZE=2) at `+0x4`, `+0xC` and `+0x14` answers SLVERR with RDATA=0 (X/Z-checked in the error-RDATA window) and leaves `fifo_level` unchanged. The 64-bit pop before the three reads and the pop after them return two consecutive words of the pool's EDN beat stream, which the test records from the `pool_edn_*` observation ports; the popped word must match exactly one adjacent beat pair, and the next pop must be the pair after it in the same packing order. A half read that popped, or that disturbed the head of the FIFO, fails the level or the word compare.
|CHK-POP-DATA
|Each accepted pop after CHK-HALF-READ, the drain pops included, answers OKAY, lowers the level by one and returns the next packed word of the captured pool EDN beats, in the packing order that CHK-HALF-READ found: two beats per 64-bit word in arrival order (`hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc:31`, a `DATA` read pops one word; the FIFO order follows the fill order). A wrong, repeated or skipped word fails the exact 64-bit compare.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE
|On the fill read and on each drain-leg status read down to empty, the `[36]` aggregate agrees with `status[6]`. Other status reads (poll, refusal and stall legs) are not compared to `[36]`. Occupancy is not compared against a FIFO watermark. The value of `status[6]` at empty is logged only (`OBS-POOL-LOW-REHIGH`).
|CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR
|Pop at `0x10` on empty returns SLVERR, after an accepted pop.
|CHK5_pool
|Native EDN beats reached the pool adapter (observe-mode floor). Bit-exact per-beat routing stays with `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, not this row.
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|After the aperture walk, CSRNG `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS` and EDN `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS` each read zero. The shared base-test step `check_entropy_alerts_zero` (`cocotb/tests/sep_base_test.py`) reads and grades them.
|OBS-POOL-LOW-LEVEL
|Not a graded check. No text states the low watermark of `pool_low`. The leaf records the `fifo_level` and `pool_low` of every `[36]` comparison read and logs the `fifo_level` at which `pool_low` first reads 1 in the drain, the `fifo_level` of the fill read at which it reads 0, the last fill poll level that still read 1, and the (`fifo_level`:`pool_low`) pairs of the drain. Evidence: `OBS-POOL-LOW-LEVEL drain_first_low_level=<l or None> fill_clear_level=<n> fill_last_low_level=<l or None> drain_trace=<level:low,...>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: owner window 745, `sep_entropy_delivery_cg` bins `cp_pool_access` (9 bins), `cp_pool_status_read` (`level_kept`, `cause_kept`) and `cp_pool_pic` (5 bins). The window opens after CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH and closes after CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR and the class floor of CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP; the body of the leaf between them is graded inside the window, and any FAIL raises and closes it. The housekeeping after the close (stop of the capture and the drain, `CHK-ALERTS-ZERO`, `CHK5_pool`) is outside. Each bin samples the value that its check compares, so no pass label is used: `w_live_slverr_kept` and `w_unmapped_slverr_kept` (a SLVERR write with the level unchanged at the next `STATUS` read; `CHK-WRITE-SLVERR`, `CHK-WRITE-ORDER`, `CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP` class `write`), `pop_okay_level_dec` (`CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP` class `pop`, `CHK-HALF-READ`, `CHK-POP-DATA`), `empty_pop_slverr` (`CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR`), `upper_status_slverr0` and `upper_cause_slverr0` (`CHK-HALF-READ`), `upper_data_slverr0_no_pop` (`CHK-HALF-READ` and class `upper_data`), `unmapped_rd_slverr0` and `past_extent_rd_slverr0` (`CHK-UNMAPPED`), `level_kept` (class `status`), `cause_kept` (the stall-phase `cause` pair), `low_fall` (`CHK-POOL-LOW-LOW`, `CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE`), `low_rise` and `low_rehigh` (`CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE`: the edge samples only with `pool_low` 1, the flag that check compares), `stall_rise` (`CHK-STALL-ASSERT`) and `stall_held` (`CHK-STALL-DURATION`). No bin depends on the seed.

*Scope*: The block is a 64 KB crossbar target at `entropy_fifo.main` (`0x1095_0000`). It fills from the native EDN endpoint after the shared entropy bring-up. Every seed walks the high-bit mirrors of the three live registers, plus all three unique-dead offsets, so a truncated decode cannot hide behind a seeded pick. Fill waits on the live `pool_low` flag, not on an occupancy watermark. `STALL_POLL_CAP` is the DV ceiling of the fill-stall poll; the stall threshold is not stated.

*Anti-vacuity*: fill is observed as `pool_low` 0 and `fifo_level` greater than 0 before any refusal is
scored; a clear of `pool_low` is claimed only after it read 1 at empty. An accepted pop happens before the empty-pop SLVERR. `[36]` is seen
low after the fill with `status[6]` low. `[37]` is the first fill-stall after the
fill path is starved with the pool not full. Every popped word after CHK-HALF-READ
is compared with the captured beat stream.

*RTL fact that arms `[37]`*: ESRC CTRL `MODULE_ENABLE=0` does not drop AUTO-mode
EDN acks while CSRNG still has a seed, and after that disable the pool runs to
full (level 32). `fill_stall` needs an outstanding fill request with no
acknowledge for the stall threshold, and a full pool holds the request low. The stall wait therefore also writes
EDN_CTRL `EDN_ENABLE=False` (legal mubi4, other fields unchanged) and pops
leftover words until the pool has room and acks have stopped.

*Not claimed*: full PIC delivery (claim / mask / ISR). Drain-under-fill and
recovery stress are not exercised here. Stall duration is graded by
`CHK-STALL-DURATION`; what remains unclaimed is a stall that outlasts a
recovery attempt.

'''''

[[sep_otbn_compute_rand_test]]
===== 1.20 `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden (strict CHK1..CHK4 plus `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode); the OTBN ISA description files `base-insns.yml`, `bignum-insns.yml` and `csr.yml` under `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/data/` as the golden source of the capability programs

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the operands of the ADD, XOR and AND cells and the values of the five capability programs (loop count and step, branch operands, call markers, addc and addm operands and modulus) come from the run seed. Every program runs on every seed.

*Objective*: Loads a tiny RV32I program into OTBN IMEM, stages two seeded DMEM operands, issues EXECUTE, and checks `ERR_BITS==0` plus the DMEM result against an independent Python golden, while the strict entropy scoreboard runs against OTBN's live URND draw. Five capability programs run the same way and each grades its result words against the ISA: a `loopi` loop, taken and not-taken `beq` and `bne`, a `jal x1` call with its `jalr x0, x1, 0` return, `bn.add` then `bn.addc` with the carry set, and `bn.addm` with a + b > MOD (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc:16-21`; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/data/base-insns.yml` and `bignum-insns.yml`).

*Test Coverage*: OTBN ADD/XOR/AND vs an independent golden, with URND observed, and the five capability programs (loop, branch, call and return, bn.addc carry, bn.addm reduction). Functional coverage: `sep_otbn_op_cg` `cp_capability`, owner window 738. Each program runs inside the window. The leaf writes the label `otbn_cap_kind` after the check of that program passes, holds it two clocks and writes 0, so a program whose check failed never samples. The first FAIL closes the window for the rest of the run.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up `no_cpu`, park AES/HMAC/KMAC, bring up entropy for OTBN URND wipe.
. Walk ADD, XOR, AND against the independent golden.
. For each capability program, stage IMEM and DMEM with the window closed, each result word pre-seeded with the complement of its golden. Open the window, wait two clocks, issue EXECUTE, poll `STATUS` to IDLE in a bounded wait, read `ERR_BITS` and the result words, and grade `CHK-OTBN-CAP`. On a PASS write the label, wait two clocks, write 0 and wait two clocks. Close the window.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ERRBITS
|Every compute cell retires with `ERR_BITS==0`.
|CHK-DMEM
|Every compute cell's DMEM result equals the Python golden for that op and those operands. The result word is pre-seeded with the golden's exact complement, so a program that never stores its result fails here rather than passing on a seed whose golden happens to be zero.
|CHK1..CHK4 / CHK5_otbn_urnd
|This entry also runs the strict entropy scoreboard against the chained golden, with `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode, because OTBN draws real URND entropy while it computes.
|CHK-OTBN-CAP
|Each of the five programs ends with `STATUS` = IDLE in a bounded poll, `ERR_BITS` = 0 (the whole word), and every result word equal to the ISA golden. The goldens: `loop`, `loopi n, 2` with a two-instruction body adds the step to one register and 1 to another, so they read n x step and n (`base-insns.yml`, `loopi`); `branch`, `beq` is taken only for equal operands and `bne` only for unequal ones, four branches (each taken and not taken), and the marker word is the sum of the markers of the instructions that are not skipped (`base-insns.yml`, `beq`, `bne`); `call_return`, `jal x1, f` pushes the return address and `jalr x0, x1, 0` pops it, so the word set in `f` and the word set by the instruction after the call both appear (`base-insns.yml`, `jal`, `jalr`); `bn_addc_carry`, `w4 = x + y mod 2^256`, `FG0` after `bn.add` reads `{Z, L, M, C}` and `w5 = u + v + C` (`bignum-insns.yml`, `bn.add`, `bn.addc`; `csr.yml`, `FG0`); `bn_addm_reduce`, with `MOD` loaded through `csrrw` of the modulus CSRs, `w = a + b - MOD` for a, b < MOD and a + b > MOD (`bignum-insns.yml`, `bn.addm`). A DUT with a wrong loop count or body size, an inverted or ignored branch, a call that is skipped or returns to the wrong place, a carry-less ALU, a carry that does not feed `bn.addc`, no reduction or a wrong `MOD` load fails. Each result word starts at the complement of its golden. Anchor: `STATUS`, `ERR_BITS` and DMEM readback. The operands are drawn so that the carry and the a + b > MOD condition hold by construction. Evidence: one `CHK-OTBN-CAP PASS seed=<n> kind=<k> label=<i> status=0x00 polls=<p> err_bits=0x00000000 dmem_base=<o> words=<w> got=<hex> exp=<hex> ...` line per program, with the drawn values of the program.
|CTRL-OTBN-CAP
|For `bn_addc_carry` the carry that the DUT stored (`FG0.C`) is 1 and the model carry is 1. For `bn_addm_reduce` the sum a + b is greater than `MOD`. A program whose stimulus never exercises the carry or the reduction logs `CTRL-MISSING OTBN-CAP` and fails the leaf.
|INFO-RANDCFG
|The leaf logs the walked cells and capability kinds. The line reports the stimulus the seed drew, cannot fail on the DUT, and is not a checker.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: one `CHK-ERRBITS` line and one `CHK-DMEM` line per ADD, XOR and AND cell (`cocotb/tests/otbn/sep_otbn_compute_rand_test.py`), and one `CHK-OTBN-CAP` line per capability program. A cell that fails either check fails under its own checker name.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test]]
===== 1.21 `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — all five `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` encodings and both
low-mode falls walk every seed; the illegal selector comes from the run seed.

*Objective*: Health-test watermark arming: each supported selector arms to `0x0000` high or `0xFFFF` low on `MODULE_ENABLE` 0->1, a selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value, and an illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI`. Scope: the ESRC → DRBG → EDN datapath row owns CHK1–CHK4 and stays where it is.

*Test Coverage*: Health-test watermark arming and the matching status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Every supported selector arms on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1.
. Changing the selector does not re-arm. An unsupported value selects `REPCNT_HI`; its readback is logged only (`OBS-HT-UNSUP-SEL`).
. A low watermark falls once `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE` is allowed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ARM-MEN
|Each supported selector arms to `0x0000` (high) or `0xFFFF` (low) on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1. The entry arm of the opposite polarity, forced before each leg, is graded the same way, so the leg's later compare can fail. A DUT that does not arm, or arms the wrong polarity for a mode, fails. Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:811` (a `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` rising edge arms high modes to zero and low modes to `0xFFFF`) and `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:132`.
|CHK-NO-REARM
|A selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value. Before the watermark read, the selector reads back as written (`HT_WATERMARK_NUM` is `sw = rw`), and the entry value differs from the arm value of the written selector. A write that never reached the DUT, or a write that re-arms or moves the watermark, fails. Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:800` ("Changing the selection preserves the current watermark") and `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:130`; the readback probe is `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:802`.
|CHK-UNSUPPORTED
|After a write of an illegal selector (5 to 15) the next MODULE_ENABLE 0→1 arms `0x0000`, the `REPCNT_HI` arm value, and the selector write alone does not move the watermark (`CHK-NO-REARM` on the unsupported value). The entry arms `0xFFFF` (`APT_LO`), so a write that never reached the DUT arms `0xFFFF` and fails. Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:805` ("Unsupported values select REPCNT_HI") and `:811`.
|OBS-HT-UNSUP-SEL
|The `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` readback after the write of the illegal selector. Logged and not graded: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:805` states which source an unsupported value selects, not the stored or read value. No golden.
|CHK-LOW-FALL
|The low mode first arms `0xFFFF`. After that, enabling health tests drops `HT_WATERMARK` below
`0xFFFF` within 80000 clocks; the bound fails. Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:811` (arm value `0xFFFF`) and `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:790-795` (`APT_LO` and `MARKOV_LO` track the lowest count). Both `APT_LO` and `MARKOV_LO` take this path on every seed: they drive
separate event counters in `entropy_source.sv`, so choosing one per seed would
leave the other permanently unproven.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The number of selectors whose DUT readback matched its documented arm value
equals the size of `enum WATERMARK_TEST`, read from `entropy_source.rdl` at
import. The bound comes from the RDL rather than from the walk generator, so a
skipped mode fails here. The same invocation covers the seed-selected illegal
selector and both low-mode falls.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: `x_ht_arm` (5 cells), `cp_ht_unsup` and `cp_ht_no_rearm`, in the owner window 744. The window opens after the health tests are held off, before the first selector write. It covers the five arm legs, `CHK-RANDCFG`, the unsupported leg and each low-mode fall up to its `CHK-LOW-FALL` grade. It closes on the first FAIL, after each fall grade (the hold-off between and after the falls runs outside it), and at the end; it reopens before the second fall. Each bin samples on the `HT_WATERMARK` read that its check compares: the leaf reads a supported selector back before every MODULE_ENABLE pulse, so the sample condition equals the compare of the check (`CHK-ARM-MEN` and `CHK-LOW-FALL` for `x_ht_arm`; `CHK-UNSUPPORTED` and `CHK-NO-REARM` for `cp_ht_unsup`; `CHK-NO-REARM` for `cp_ht_no_rearm`). `cp_ht_unsup` takes the unsupported write, no selector read, and the arm read; a rise after an unsupported write is not an `x_ht_arm` cell. The `CHK-NO-REARM` probe is graded before the read that `cp_ht_no_rearm` samples. No bin depends on the seed: the unsupported value (5 to 15) is the only draw, and every value fills `cp_ht_unsup`.

*Not claimed*: the shared TRNG reset path. It clears the selector and the
watermark together; no vehicle asserts that pair.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_fips_lock_test]]
===== 1.22 `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every certified-configuration class every seed; pre-lock value and rejected poke come from the run seed, and each field's poke is selected to differ from that field's pre-lock value. The seed also picks which observe tap is held at 1 and which at 0 across the lock, and the `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL` values before and under the lock. One more draw, taken after all the others so they keep their values: `h_mid`, one `MIN_ENTROPY_H` sweep point in [0x10, 0x80) (1.0 to under 8.0 bits per sample), added to the sweep on every seed. The sweep holds the anchors 0x00, the register reset (0x0C) and 0xFF, filled to eight points with seeded values, plus `h_mid`.

*Objective*: The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` clears it (the TRNG reset also clears it, `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:227`; `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` grades that path). The lock also freezes the debug-pin mux (`DEBUG_CTRL`) and both observe-tap enables (`BIW_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE`, `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE`), so a locked part cannot capture or route pre-conditioning entropy. Each conditioned word goes to the DRBG and into the main FIFO in parallel, so `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE` gates the software copy of the DRBG seed words: the walk clears it before the lock, as a certified configuration does, and the lock must hold it at 0. The advisory RCT/APT cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded against an SP 800-90B oracle. Scope: health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Test Coverage*: FIPS lock and the matching entropy-source status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Sweep `MIN_ENTROPY_H` over the Q4.4 points (eight or nine) and read `RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS` at each point, with the graded window closed (CHK-REC-THRESH, pass `window-closed`). The H = 0 point is logged and not graded (OBS-REC-THRESH-H0).
. Open the graded window and run the same sweep again (CHK-REC-THRESH, pass `window-open`). The sweep runs before the pre-lock walk, so the `MIN_ENTROPY_H` write of the walk sets the value that CHK-POST-LOCK later compares.
. Walk every field `entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel`, one target per register, except `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. Write each target's seeded pre-lock value and require the field to move off reset (CHK-PRE-LOCK).
. Write `CTRL.RSVD0` and require the write to be accepted (OKAY), `CTRL` to hold the pre-lock fields of its target, `RSVD0` to read 0 and `CTRL` to be unchanged (CHK-CTRL-RSVD).
. Write each observe-tap enable to the opposite of its seeded held value, then to the held value, with the pre-lock `LANE_SEL`. Require an OKAY response of each write and read back after it; `LANE_SEL` is graded on every pre-lock read (CHK-OBS-PRE-LOCK).
. Write `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`, require an OKAY response and read 1 (CHK-LOCK-SET). Write 0, require an OKAY response and read 1 (CHK-LOCK-W1S).
. Poke each target, require a B response of any code (logged as OBS-POST-LOCK-RESP), and require the pre-lock value to hold (CHK-POST-LOCK).
. Write each tap enable to the opposite of its held value, with the post-lock `LANE_SEL`. Require a B response of any code (logged as OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED-RESP) and the enable to hold (CHK-OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED), and `LANE_SEL` to move (CHK-OBS-LANE-SEL).
. Write `CTRL.RSVD0` again and require a B response of any code (logged as OBS-CTRL-RSVD-LOCKED-RESP), the `CTRL` fields held, `RSVD0` at 0 and `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1` (CHK-CTRL-RSVD).
. Read `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` as 1 just before the pulse, pulse `rst_ni` and require `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=0` (CHK-RST-NI).
. Read the released target at its reset, then poke it and read the poke (CHK-POST-UNLOCK). Read the tap that held 0, set it with an OKAY response and read 1, then clear it (CHK-OBS-POST-UNLOCK). Close the graded window.
. Grade the walk against the RDL lock inventory (CHK-RANDCFG).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PRE-LOCK
|A pre-lock write moves the field off reset.
|CHK-POST-LOCK
|The same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`. The poke must complete with a B response, so a write that never reached the DUT fails; no text states the code of a write the lock refuses, so the code is logged (OBS-POST-LOCK-RESP) and not graded. A locked field that takes the poke fails. Golden: the fields that `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel` ("FIPS_LOCK.LOCK prevents software changes", for example `MIN_ENTROPY_H` at `:951-953`); the held value is the target's pre-lock value, which differs from the poke by construction.
|CHK-LOCK-SET
|`FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` reads 1 after the arming write, and the write completes with an OKAY response. Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:752` (`LOCK` is RW1S, "Write one to set the configuration lock").
|CHK-LOCK-W1S
|Write-0 leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`, and the write completes with an OKAY response. Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:752` (RW1S, only hardware reset clears it).
|CHK-CTRL-RSVD
|Reserved `CTRL.RSVD0` reads 0, and writing it before the lock is accepted and leaves `CTRL` unchanged; the `CTRL` fields hold the pre-lock value of their target, where the other `CTRL` fields are demonstrably writable so an unchanged register isolates `RSVD0`. Repeated after the lock, the same write completes with a B response of any code (OBS-CTRL-RSVD-LOCKED-RESP; no text states the code of a write the lock refuses), the `CTRL` fields hold, `RSVD0` reads 0 and the write leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`. Golden: the `CTRL.RSVD0` field of `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl` ("reads zero and ignores writes") and `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:752`. The reserved bits of `FIPS_LOCK` (31:1, no stated reset, `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:751`) are logged and not graded (OBS-FIPS-LOCK-RSVD).
|CHK-RST-NI
|`rst_ni` clears `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` to 0, after a read of `LOCK=1` just before the pulse (a positive probe that the lock was set). Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:752` ("Only hardware reset clears it").
|CHK-POST-UNLOCK
|After `rst_ni` the released target reads its register reset, then takes the
poke that the lock rejected. The target is chosen so its poke differs from the
reset, otherwise the reset value alone would satisfy the readback.
|CHK-OBS-PRE-LOCK
|Before the lock, `BIW_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE` and `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE`
each take both values on readback, so a bit stuck at either value fails here.
Each write completes with an OKAY response.
|CHK-OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED
|Under the lock each tap enable rejects a write of the opposite value. One tap
is held at 1 and rejects a clear, the other is held at 0 and rejects a set,
picked by the seed, so each seed grades one direction per tap. The write must
complete with a B response (`B=` in the line), so a write that never reached the
DUT fails. The lock ignores the write
(`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl` `swwel`) and no text states its B
code, so the code is logged (OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED-RESP) and not graded.
|CHK-OBS-LANE-SEL
|The same `NOISE_OBS_CTRL` write under the lock moves `LANE_SEL`, which is not
locked. The lane moving while `RAW_ENABLE` holds shows the write reached the
register and the lock is per field.
|CHK-OBS-POST-UNLOCK
|After `rst_ni` the tap that held 0 reads its RDL reset value (`RAW_ENABLE` reset 0, taken from the generated register export, `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:988` and `:1035`) and takes the set the lock rejected, with an OKAY response.
|OBS-POST-LOCK-RESP, OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED-RESP, OBS-CTRL-RSVD-LOCKED-RESP
|The B response code of each write the lock refuses: the target pokes of CHK-POST-LOCK, the tap writes of CHK-OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED and the post-lock `CTRL.RSVD0` write. No golden: the `swwel` text of `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl` ("SW writes ignored while locked") states no response code. Evidence: `OBS-POST-LOCK-RESP: <target> B=<code>`, `OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED-RESP: <reg> B=<code>`, `OBS-CTRL-RSVD-LOCKED-RESP: CTRL B=<code>`.
|OBS-FIPS-LOCK-RSVD
|The reserved bits 31:1 of `FIPS_LOCK`, logged at each `FIPS_LOCK` read of CHK-LOCK-SET, CHK-LOCK-W1S, the post-lock CHK-CTRL-RSVD and CHK-RST-NI. No golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:751` states no reset value. Evidence: `OBS-FIPS-LOCK-RSVD: <when> rsvd=0x<r>`.
|CHK-REC-THRESH
|`RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS.RCT_LIMIT` tracks `MIN_ENTROPY_H` across the sweep of Q4.4 points with H > 0, compared against `C = 1 + ceil(20/H)`, the formula the register text gives (`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:966`; SP 800-90B 4.4.1), re-derived in the test. The oracle is the formula, not `entropy_source_rec_thresh_lut.sv`, which the checker never reads. `APT_LIMIT` is compared at every H > 0 point against the SP 800-90B 4.4.2 cutoff: the smallest C with Pr[X >= C] <= alpha for X ~ Binomial(1024, 2^-H).
