The register text derives both cutoffs per SP 800-90B (`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:956-957`) and fixes the 1024-sample window (`:971`); alpha = 2^-20 is the alpha of SP 800-90B (January 2018) 4.4, which states "alpha = 2^-20; this value will be used in all the calculations in the rest of this section" (4.4.1 and 4.4.2), and the alpha of the RCT formula (`:966`); H = h / 16 exactly (Q4.4, `:944`), so no rounding enters. The test sums the binomial tail in log space (`apt_limit_golden` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/seq_lib/sep_esrc_fips_lock_seq.py`) and never reads the design table. The sweep runs twice, with the graded window closed (`window-closed`) and open (`window-open`); each pass logs its own PASS line.
|OBS-REC-THRESH-H0
|The `RCT_LIMIT` and `APT_LIMIT` read at `MIN_ENTROPY_H=0x00`. Logged and not graded: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:966` and `:971` state no value at H = 0. No golden.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The set of (register, field) pairs that the targets poked under the lock cover, taken from each target's bus address and compare mask, equals every field `entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel`, less the declared exclusions (`CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` and the two observe-tap enables). A walk that drops a target, or a target that points at the wrong register or masks out a field, fails and names the missing or extra pairs. The two observe-tap enables are
graded by the `CHK-OBS-*` checkers. Every register that locks two
fields moves both off reset in the pre-lock write, so a field inside the compare
mask is never left unwritten.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: owner window 743, group `sep_crypto_reg_lock_cg`. The window opens after the closed-window sweep, before the in-window sweep, and stays open through `CHK-OBS-POST-UNLOCK`. The first FAIL closes it. Bins: `cp_esrc_locked_reg` (14 cells; the first OKAY read of a class after a write under the lock with a B response of any code, equal to the last pre-lock read and different from the poke; graded by `CHK-POST-LOCK`); `cp_fips_lock_bit` `set_by_w1` (`CHK-LOCK-SET`), `write0_kept` (`CHK-LOCK-W1S`), `rsvd_write_kept` (`CHK-CTRL-RSVD`, post-lock; the `CTRL` write takes a B response of any code) and `cleared_by_rst_ni` (`CHK-RST-NI`); `cp_min_entropy_h` (3 cells; a `RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS` read with H != 0 whose RCT limit equals the formula; `CHK-REC-THRESH`). The window-closed sweep proves the stimulus before the in-window repeat samples, so `cp_min_entropy_h` samples only on stimulus whose check already passed. No bin depends on the seed: `h_below_1` fills from the reset anchor, `h_1_to_7_9375` from `h_mid` and `h_8_up` from 0xFF, on every seed. No pass label is needed: the sample condition of each other bin equals the compare of its check.

*Scope*: This leaf is one of two vehicles for the ESRC lock-and-alert contract; the other is <<sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test>>. `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` is write-1-only. The observe-tap enables and the debug-pin mux are locked; `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL` is not. The walk sequence declares its coverage and reconciles it against the `swwel` inventory in `entropy_source.rdl` at import, so a lock added or dropped there fails the test instead of leaving the walk quietly short.

*Not claimed*: `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. It is locked too, but clearing it stops the
block for the rest of the walk, so its lock belongs to the reset-recovery
vehicle. The observe FIFOs' data path under the lock: `BIW_OBS_STATUS`,
`NOISE_OBS_STATUS` and the `*_OBS_RDATA` ports are not read, so a locked-off
tap is graded at the control bit only. The main-FIFO data path with
`FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE` locked off is graded by <<sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test]]
===== 1.23 `sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force +km_rom_hex=km_rom_entropy.parhex +esrc_fifo_closed`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, selected by `+esrc_fifo_closed`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE -- directed, with the same deterministic noise as `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`.

*Objective*: `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` gives `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE` one job: it gates the main-FIFO write. The words offered to the FIFO also seed the DRBG, and that seed stream does not depend on ENABLE. With ENABLE=0 from the ESRC configuration on, the DRBG must still seed and generate bit-exact against the golden, no word may enter the FIFO, and under `FIPS_LOCK` software must not be able to set ENABLE again.

*Test Coverage*: DRBG seeding with the `FIFO_RDATA` read path closed, and the lock on that read path.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Same bring-up as `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, with `SepEntropyCfg.fifo_enable=0`. The ESRC configuration writes `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE=0` before the generators start and before `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK`.
. Wait for a seed, set the lock, enable EDN, release the Key Manager, and wait for genbits and the Key Manager consume, as in the smoke.
. Over that phase, read `FIFO_STATUS` and pop `FIFO_RDATA` twice per sample. Keep sampling until a full seed of words (12) crosses the seed port after the lock and the DRBG packs a new seed.
. Under the lock, read `FIFO_CTRL`, write `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE=1` and `FIFO_CTRL.ENTROPY_CHURN_ENABLE=1` in one write, and read `FIFO_CTRL` again. The write must complete with one B response, of any code; the leaf logs the code. Send the CSRNG software instantiate of CHK-SEED-SW: CSRNG takes the seed that the DRBG holds queued, so the DRBG can queue a new one. Wait for 12 more seed-port words and a newly queued seed, then read `FIFO_STATUS`.
. After every check passed, write the pass label (see Functional Coverage).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK1
|Decorrelated output matches the golden model.
|CHK2
|Compressed output matches golden, scored at the FIFO input tap (`entropy_stream_data_o`, the DRBG seed port). The FIFO stays empty, so the front-door drain of the smoke has nothing to read.
|CHK3
|The seed that CSRNG accepts matches golden. Each word in it crossed the seed port with ENABLE=0.
|CHK4
|Generated bits match golden.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word and it matches golden.
|CHK5_pool
|As in `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`.
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|As in `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`.
|CHK-SEED-SW
|As in `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, sent after the locked `FIFO_CTRL` write.
|CHK-FIFO-CLOSED
|`INTR_STATUS.FIFO_UNDERFLOW` and `FIFO_OVERFLOW` read 0 before the first pop. Each `FIFO_STATUS` sample reads LEVEL=0 and WPTR at its reset, so no word went into the FIFO after the TRNG reset. The pops set `FIFO_UNDERFLOW`, which shows they reached the FIFO, and a W1C clears it. The PASS line gives the seed-port words, the seeds the DRBG packed and the seeds CSRNG accepted in the window. A window with fewer than 12 words or no packed seed fails, because an idle window cannot show a closed read path.
|CHK-FIFO-ENABLE-LOCKED
|Under `FIPS_LOCK` a write of ENABLE=1 and ENTROPY_CHURN_ENABLE=1 completes with one B response, and both fields read the stored closed configuration (ENABLE=0 and the stored ENTROPY_CHURN_ENABLE) before and after the write. After 12 more seed-port words and a seed that the DRBG queued after the write, LEVEL is 0 and WPTR is at its reset. A write that is never issued or never answered fails on the B response; a lock that does not hold either field, a FIFO that fills after the write, or no seed queued after it, fails. Golden: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:269` and `:275` ("FIPS_LOCK.LOCK prevents software changes" for `ENABLE` and `ENTROPY_CHURN_ENABLE`); the stored value is the closed configuration written before the lock (`_check_fifo_enable_locked` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/tests/system/sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test.py`, which also holds the B probe); LEVEL 0 and WPTR at reset while seed words cross: `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:150`.
|OBS-FIFO-ENABLE-LOCKED-RESP
|The B response code of the locked FIFO_CTRL write and the reserved bits of `FIFO_CTRL` after it. Logged and not graded: no specification states the response of a write to a locked field. No golden.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: the owner window and the pass label `esrc_e2e_pass` of `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, with the bins of that section. In this entry the label also releases `cp_fifo_flags[underflow_set]` (graded by CHK-FIFO-CLOSED, W1C part included), `cp_fifo_closed_seed[seed_packed_level0]` (each `FIFO_STATUS` read with LEVEL 0 after a locked seed rise; CHK-FIFO-CLOSED) and `cp_fifo_closed_seed[enable_write_locked]`. The last bin samples the `FIFO_CTRL` write of ENABLE=1 under the lock on its B handshake with any code, with ENABLE still 0, then a seed that the DRBG queues after the CHK-SEED-SW instantiate took the held one, and then the `FIFO_STATUS` probe read; CHK-FIFO-ENABLE-LOCKED grades it. The label rises only after every check of the run passed, so a failing run samples none of these bins.

*What a broken design does here*: if ENABLE also gated the seed port, no seed would reach CSRNG. CHK3 would have no seed, and the bounded seed wait fails the leaf.

*Not claimed*: the drain step of the certified sequence (clear ENABLE, read `FIFO_RDATA` until LEVEL is 0, then lock) with words already in the FIFO. This leaf clears ENABLE before a word reaches the FIFO, so that drain has nothing to read. The data an empty-FIFO read returns: the RDL calls it undefined, so it is not graded.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test]]
===== 1.24 `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed persistent-failure trip.

*Objective*: A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`,
and the alert bookkeeping the RDL defines is read at that trip. An opening
`INTR_TEST` sweep, before the trip, drives every interrupt source the RDL defines.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy-source alerts reach status and the aggregator.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` and confirm that the forced noise is routed. Require ALERT, PERSISTENT_FAILURE and PIC source 16 at 0 (CHK-NO-ALERT).
. From the clean pre-trip state, pulse each `INTR_TEST` source on its own (CHK-INTR-TEST).
. Enable only PERSISTENT_FAILURE, arm a short window, and hold the forced noise stuck until it trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD` (CHK-ALERT, CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE, CHK-PIC-16, CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO).
. Read the alert counters and the health-test latch at the trip (CHK-ANY-FAIL-WINDOWS, CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB, CHK-HT-STATUS, CHK-GEN-HEALTH).
. Leave AlertHang, then W1C the ALERT and PERSISTENT_FAILURE bits (CHK-W1C).
. Set `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE=0`, then W1C every latched health-test bit (CHK-HT-STATUS).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NO-ALERT
|ALERT, PERSISTENT_FAILURE, and PIC source 16 are 0 before the trip.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|Each `INTR_TEST` source `ENTROPY_SOURCE.fields("INTR_TEST")` names sets only
its own `INTR_STATUS` bit, raises PIC source 16, and clears on W1C. Swept
before the trip: `INTR_STATUS` bits are re-driven from their live cause, so a
source whose real condition is latched cannot clear. The expected count is
that field list, not a literal.
|CHK-ALERT
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ALERT` asserts after the threshold.
|CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE
|`INTR_STATUS.PERSISTENT_FAILURE` asserts. Note it is the OR of the AlertHang
trip, a main-FSM error and a counter-integrity error; CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO is what
makes this one attributable.
|CHK-PIC-16
|`sep_internal_interrupts[15]` (PIC source 16) asserts. Only PERSISTENT_FAILURE
is enabled for the trip, so this adds the aggregate slot wiring on top of
CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE rather than an independent cause.
|CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR` stays 0 across the trip, so PERSISTENT_FAILURE is
attributable to the alert threshold.
|CHK-ANY-FAIL-WINDOWS
|`ALERT_SUMMARY_FAIL_COUNTS.ANY_FAIL_COUNT` sits inside `[ALERT_THRESHOLD,
ALERT_THRESHOLD + 8]` at the trip and stays at or below the summed per-lane fail
pulses. `ALERT_THRESHOLD` is 3: at 1 the alert comparator already implies the
lower bound, and no denomination is distinguishable at a count of one. The upper
bound against the per-lane pulse total is what rejects a counter denominated in
events rather than in failing *windows*.
|CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB
|`REPCNT_TOTAL_FAILS` is non-zero, attributing the trip to the repetition test
under stuck noise with `REPETITION_LIMIT` = 5, and each lane's 32-bit total
covers its 4-bit `ALERT_FAIL_COUNTS` counter. The two take the same fail pulse
but carry different widths and different clears, so the compare is a
DUT-to-DUT contract. The reverse does not hold and is not claimed:
`entropy_source.rdl` scopes `ALERT_FAIL_COUNTS` to the current alert sequence,
which an accepted passing window ends, while each total counts since the last
`CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` rising edge.
|CHK-HT-STATUS
|`HEALTH_TEST_STATUS.HEALTH_STATUS` bit 0 (repetition) is set while the alert is
asserted: every lane is held at 0, and the trip is attributed to the repetition
test (CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB). `entropy_source.rdl` allocates repetition [0], APT [3], Markov high [4]
and Markov low [5] and states no value for [1:2] and [6:7], which are logged only
(`OBS-HT-RSVD`). After `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE=0` the latch still holds the trip
bits, and a write of one to every latched bit reads back 0.
|CHK-GEN-HEALTH
|All twelve `GENERATOR_n_HEALTH_STATUS` registers are read at the trip, and each
has bit 0 (repetition) set: every lane is held at 0, and `entropy_source.rdl`
gives each generator byte the `HEALTH_TEST_STATUS` allocation. A generator that
latches the wrong bit or none fails. The reserved bits are logged only (`OBS-HT-RSVD`).
|CHK-W1C
|After leaving AlertHang, W1C clears both bits and the PIC line.
|OBS-HT-RSVD
|Not a graded check. No text states the value of bits [1:2] and [6:7] of `HEALTH_TEST_STATUS` or of the twelve `GENERATOR_n_HEALTH_STATUS` registers. The leaf logs those bits as read at the trip. Evidence: `OBS-HT-RSVD graded=0 ht_rsvd=0x<v> gen_rsvd=['0x<v>', ...]`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: This leaf is the second vehicle for the ESRC lock-and-alert contract, with <<sep_esrc_fips_lock_test>>. Health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Not claimed*: `es_cntr_err` -> `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR`. This is the health-test
counter-redundancy countermeasure: `prim_count` holds each count twice, up and
down, and reports an error only when `cnt_q[0] + cnt_q[1]` stops equalling the
counter maximum. No stimulus can break that invariant — both copies take the same
enable and the same step — so the path is reachable by a physical fault and by
nothing else. Proving it therefore requires fault injection, which no vehicle
in `all` performs — see <<sep-vplan-scope>>.
CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO proves the bit is 0 on the alert path, which is what makes
PERSISTENT_FAILURE attributable to the threshold trip; it does not claim the
error path works.

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy]]
==== 1. Crypto and entropy, Phase 3

Phase 3 tests extend the open Phase 1 and Phase 2 tests of the crypto and
entropy area with drawn stimulus, error injection and checks that those tests
do not grade. Each entry names the open test that it extends and states what
that test does not grade. A contract that an open test can carry as a leg of
its own run is an extension leg of that open test and not a separate leaf (see
the entries for `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and
`sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`). An extension leg adds only the
cells that the open test does not grade. The entries carry the contract, the scenario, the checks and the
logged proof.

===== Common rules

Every entry follows these rules. An entry states only what differs.

* *Seed rule.* Every random value is a pure function of the run seed, from the
seeded SHA-256 counter-mode generator of the open environment.
`--stage sim --seed N` replays the leaf. The generator is not a CSPRNG.
* *Drawn cells.* Regression seeds are random per leaf, and a leaf does not know
its run index. No entry takes a cell from a seed index, and no testlist entry
pins a cell: each leaf has one testlist entry. An entry runs a cell in every
leaf only when the cell is cheap and contract-critical: a legal minimum or
maximum, one representative of each leg, or the error-injection case itself.
Such a cell set is small and adds no boot, no cold reset and no transfer of real
size per cell, and its coverage bins fill in one `all --cov` regression. The
seed draws every other cell of a larger cross (`SepSeededRng`; a weighted draw
where the entry says so). The coverage of a drawn cell closes on a merge of
regressions and never in one regression (<<sep-fcov-rand>>). For N equally
likely cells and d draws per leaf, the expected closure is about
N * ln(N) / (d * reseed) regressions.
* *ESRC interrupt status.* The specification does not state whether an ESRC
`INTR_STATUS` bit latches while its `INTR_ENABLE` bit is clear (see
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). A TRNG reset returns
`INTR_ENABLE` to 0. So an entry that grades an `INTR_STATUS` bit writes 1 to
the `INTR_ENABLE` bit of that source after every TRNG reset and before the
stimulus. `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test` writes an enable bit 0 under a
status event; it logs that status and does not grade it.
* *Test data only.* All drawn data is test data. Keys, key shares, IV seeds and
ABR seeds come from a fixed table of published test constants. The seed picks
the table row and never the bytes. No document quotes a per-seed value as
evidence.
* *Log shape.* The leaf logs its drawn plan first (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`). Each
check logs `CHK-<NAME> PASS|FAIL seed=<n> <values>`. The leaf ends with
`RESULT <test> seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>`. PASS needs positive evidence in
`results.xml`. A clean simulator exit is not evidence. A blocked check
logs `OBS-BLOCKED <check> reason=<item>` and is not counted in
`checks=<k>`. A control that shows no
cause logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. A line that starts with `OBS-` is
logged and never graded.
* *Observation points.* Register readback, AXI response code and read data come
from the CPU-LSU master (in `no_cpu` mode the master spliced onto the CPU bus).
Every entry lists its probes and injects under "Probes and injects" and marks
each one `approved`, observation tap or added hook. An approved item is in the
closed set of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` or in `tb_top.sv`. The approved items that the
entries use are: the interrupt vector probe `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`
(interrupt source N on bit N-1); `crypto_edn_req_o[i]`, `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]`
and `crypto_edn_fips_o[i]` (client 0 AES, 1 KMAC, 2 OTBN RND, 3 OTBN URND);
`km_entropy_*`, `axis1_*`, `axis2_*` and `pool_edn_*` (the streams after the
source mux and the pool client); `esrc_decor_bytes_o`, `esrc_decor_valid_o`,
`esrc_whiten_push_o`, `esrc_compress_vld_o` and `esrc_compress_data_o`;
`esrc_noise_ext_i` (the noise inject); `trng_axi_isolated_probe_o` and
`trng_reset_active_probe_o`; the gated-reset probes of HMAC, KMAC, AES, OTBN
and ABR and the isolate probes of HMAC, KMAC and ABR; and the inject
`hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`; and the ESRC decorrelator input tap
`esrc_decor_noise_o`, which only the coverage sampler reads. An observation tap is a read-only probe or tap
outside that closed set: the test adds it to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe
list, and a check on it is graded. An added hook is an error inject or a drive
of a `sep` port outside that closed set: the test adds an error inject to the
inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and a top-port drive to the port table
of its HDL Top section, and a check on it is graded. Testbench hooks lists the
observation taps and the added hooks. A check that needs a PROPOSED item
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>) is blocked and is marked `Blocked: needs <item>`.
It is not counted as a reached check. The leaf runs its other checks.
* *Control for "never" checks.* Each "never" check names a window in which a
healthy design drives the signal, or a same-seed cell in which the cause is
present. The leaf logs that control in the same line or the next line.
* *Time bounds.* A bound is a multiple of the time of a clean run of the same
operation in the same seed, and the leaf logs both. No check uses an absolute
clock count as a contract. A wait that has no clean run of the same operation
in the leaf (the run that measures a clean time, a Key Manager operation, a
wait of a leaf that measures no clean time) has a fixed outer limit. The limit
is a test parameter, not an expected value, and an expiry fails the check that
owns the wait. Stated case: in
`sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test` each Key Manager operation waits for each word of its response
frame for at most 400000 polls of the Key Manager mailbox `STATUS` register, 20
`clk_i` cycles apart.
* *One fault per injection.* The state is clean before each trial. The reset
kinds are the block reset bit of `SW_RESET_N` for AES, HMAC, KMAC, OTBN and
ABR, and the TRNG bit for ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Each reset injection changes one bit of
`SW_RESET_N` from the baseline value of the leaf: 0x7E with the engines outside
the draw held at 0 (see Run mode). The leaf logs the baseline. Bit 0 (Key Manager) stays 0 unless
the entry needs the Key Manager (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`).
* *Disable vector.* The vector stays at the harness value. SYS_DIS bit 0 is
never randomized, because it filters inbound traffic.
* *Data.* Message and plaintext words are unique per word and differ from the
register reset value, so a reorder is visible.
* *Run mode.* `no_cpu` leaves use target `lsu_stub_all_live` with
`+skip_fuse_sense`. Entropy leaves add `+esrc_noise_force`. Leaves that need
the Key Manager use the firmware image and the plusargs of the open Key Manager
sideload tests, with real fuse sense. Each leaf is in `crypto.toml` and in the
groups `cpu_stub` and `all`. An extension leg runs in the testlist entry of its
open test. Every leaf that runs an AES, KMAC or OTBN operation
also adds `+esrc_noise_force`, selects the internal source in
`SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` (the reset value selects the external source,
which the open elaboration ties to zero), brings up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN, and
holds in reset the engines that are not in the draw. Stated exceptions:
`sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test` and `sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test`
keep ABR released in every run, because the Key Manager firmware writes ABR
registers at boot and an access to a domain held in reset completes with SLVERR
(`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing);
`sep_trng_ext_sink_reset_rand_test` takes the words of its AES, KMAC and OTBN
operations from the external stream, so it adds no `+esrc_noise_force`, selects
the external source and leaves ESRC, CSRNG and EDN uninitialized; the extension
leg of `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` keeps HMAC out of reset for
its bystander; `sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test` keeps HMAC out of reset and
idle for the control of its no-interrupt check. Tool "Verilator and VCS" means
both.
Tool "VCS" means a `tools = ["vcs"]` pin, because the leaf reads state after
reset.
* *Noise injection.* The leaf drives `esrc_noise_ext_i` (one bit per generator)
per lane with the stuck modes `stuck0` and `stuck1` or with an exact pattern
(next rule). It changes a lane bit only in the `clk_i` cycle after a strobe of
`esrc_decor_valid_o` and holds it to the next strobe. With BYPASS set for a
lane the decorrelator byte is then 0x00 or 0xFF, the held bit in all eight
bits: the feedback is zero, the chain shifts the noise bit unmixed into the
tap stages 21 to 28, and the byte period (`SAMPLE_CLK_DIV` + 1, 64 at the reset
value) is longer than the 29-stage chain
(`hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc`, Decorrelation and BIW Mixing).
With BYPASS clear the feedback from the last stage mixes the noise, so a noise
bit does not give a stated byte. Every leg that drives a stuck mode or a pattern
therefore sets `DECORRELATOR_CTRL.BYPASS` on all 12 lanes, and keeps
`SAMPLE_CLK_DIV` and `ENTROPY_BYTE_MASK` at their reset values unless the entry
states another value. Control: in each window whose health-test outcome a check
uses, the leaf compares the 12 lane bytes of `esrc_decor_bytes_o` at every
strobe with the held bits (0xFF for 1, 0x00 for 0); a mismatch logs
`CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. Expected values come from this rule and
never from a copy of the RTL.
* *Exact patterns.* A pattern repeats every 64 words (strobes of
`esrc_decor_valid_o`). Every window size of the area is a multiple of 64, so a
window of W words holds W/64 whole periods whatever its start, and each count
below is exact for every window. `balanced`: lane j carries at word n the
parity of m_j AND p(n mod 64), where m_0 to m_11 are 12 different 6-bit masks
of odd weight and p is a permutation of 0 to 63 drawn from the seed. Each lane
has 32 ones per period, so W/2 ones per window. No mask is the XOR of two
others, so each bit of a GF(2^8) multiply-add a * b + c of three different lane
bytes (0x00 or 0xFF) also has 32 ones per period, whichever lanes feed the four
BIW multiply-adds (the specification does not state the lane map). `bias70`
and `bias30`: 45 and 19 ones per period at positions drawn from the seed, so
45W/64 and 19W/64 ones per window. `square` L (L = 8, 16 or 32): runs of exactly
L zeros and L ones, with a phase per lane drawn from the seed; a window holds
W/2 ones, its longest run is L, and it holds W/L - 1 or W/L changes from word
to word (the window edge can drop one) and at most W/L pair alternations
(convention "Health-test limit trips"). `alternate`: the lane bit changes at
every word, with a start value per lane drawn from the seed; a window holds W/2
ones, its longest run is 1, and every pair of consecutive words differs. The
leaf logs the masks, the permutation, the one positions, the phases and the
start values in its plan.
* *Clean start.* Every ESRC start of the area (after a TRNG reset, and at every
rise of `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`) is a clean start unless the entry states a
failing start. While `MODULE_ENABLE` = 0 the leaf writes
`DECORRELATOR_CTRL.BYPASS` = 0xFFF, the window W (2048 unless the entry states
another) and the clean limits of W, drives `balanced` on all 12 lanes, and then
writes `MODULE_ENABLE` = 1. The clean limits of a window W are
`APT_PROPORTION_1BIT` = 3W/4, `APT_PROPORTION_LO` = W/4,
`PROB_01_THRESHOLD` = W + 1, `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` = 0 and `REPETITION_LIMIT`
= 200. No window of a `balanced`, `bias70`, `bias30`, `square` or `alternate`
lane, and no 32-bit lane of a `balanced` stream, reaches a clean limit: their
one counts lie between 19W/64 and 45W/64, a window holds at most W
alternations, every count meets `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` = 0, and no
run is longer than 45 words, or 90 words across one change of pattern. So the
startup window passes by construction. A stuck lane fails the clean limits: 0
or W ones per window, and a run above 200 words. An entry writes other
limits only after `BOOT_PHASE_DONE` reads 1 and states them. A leg that grades
a reset limit writes it after the clean start. An entry can state a live start
instead, which has no `CTRL` write: right after the TRNG reset release, with
`MODULE_ENABLE` at its reset value 1, the leaf writes
`DECORRELATOR_CTRL.BYPASS` = 0xFFF and the clean limits of the reset window
2048 (the window itself is not written), and the leaf fails if more than 16
strobes come from the release to the end of the last write. Each of those words
changes a one count by at most one and a run by at most one word, inside the
margin of 3W/64 = 96 one counts and of 155 words to the clean limits, and under
the reset `REPETITION_LIMIT` of 25 before that write, so the first window passes.
* *Field bits.* Every graded compare, poll and X check of a register value uses
the field bits of the register text only (the overlay or generated register
text, or the RDL). A bit that the register text lists as `Reserved`, with access
"-" and reset "-", has no stated read value: the leaf masks it out, logs it in
the line of the check (`rsvd=0x<r>`) or as `OBS-RSVD seed=<n> reg=<r> rsvd=0x<r>`,
and no check grades it. A written value is compared with the field bits of the
readback. A register that is one 32-bit field (DIGEST, `STATE`, DATA_OUT, IV,
`INSN_CNT`, the HMAC `ERR_CODE`) is compared in full. The CSRNG and EDN
`ERR_CODE` registers hold `Reserved` bits, so only their field bits are compared.
* *Unstated values.* A leg that would rest on a value the specification does not
state is not part of an entry. It is listed under
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>.

===== Conventions where the text is silent

* Register codes that the generated register text lists by name only (AES
`MODE` ECB, CBC and CTR; AES `PRNG_RESEED_RATE` PER_64 and PER_8K; KMAC `mode`,
`kstrength` and `CMD`; HMAC `digest_size` and `key_length`; the KMAC
`ENTROPY_SEED` write count; the KMAC `STATE` share layout) come from the
vendored upstream register description
`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/<ip>/data/<ip>.hjson`, the ruling
source for them. The rate order PER_1 > PER_64 > PER_8K is the one in that
file. The `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` codes come from the `WATERMARK_TEST` enum of
`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl`.
* Window scaling: the reset health-test limits belong to the reset window size 2048.
A leaf that writes another window writes it in the clean start, with the clean
limits of that window (common rule "Clean start"), and logs them. A leaf that
sets its own limits states them in the entry.
* Alert receiver index: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` gives each block a range of
receiver indices (HMAC [0]; OTBN [2:1]; AES [4:3]; KMAC [6:5]; CSRNG [8:7]; EDN
[10:9]) and does not map an `ALERT_TEST` bit to one index of the range. The
leaves of this area grade the aggregate source 33 (vector bit 32) only. The check
that exactly one receiver of the range of the block pulses belongs to
`sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test` and reads the alert receiver probe, an
observation tap.
No leaf grades which index of the range pulses (see
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).
* Malformed alert: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (OpenTitan Alert Termination)
states that a malformed encoding on the alert pair raises the integrity-fail of
the receiver and that the Crypto alert source is the OR of every receiver pulse
and integrity-fail and of the two EDN adapters' ack FSM faults. It does not list the malformed encodings. The leaf uses one:
`alert_p` and `alert_n` at the same value, which is not a differential code.
* Health-test limit trips: `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.REPETITION_LIMIT` is the maximum
allowed repetition count, `MARKOV_TEST_PROB_THRESHOLDS.PROB_01_THRESHOLD` is the
maximum allowed alternation count and `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` is the minimum allowed
alternation count at the window boundary (`entropy_source.adoc`). The Markov
test counts bit-pair transitions (`architecture.adoc`, Health Testing) with the
internal structure of the OpenTitan `entropy_src` Markov test: it splits the
words of a window into non-overlapping pairs of consecutive words and counts,
per lane, the pairs whose two bits differ. That count is the alternation count
of this area. A count of the changes from one word to the next is the other
reading, and every limit trip below trips under both readings. The repetition
and Markov low trials drive `square` L on all 12 lanes: the longest run is L,
and a window holds at most W/L pair alternations (the phase of a lane sets
where its changes fall against the pairs) and W/L - 1 or W/L changes from word
to word. The Markov high trial drives `alternate` on all 12 lanes: every pair
of consecutive words differs, so a window holds exactly W/2 pair alternations
whatever the phase and the window start, and W - 1 or W changes from word to
word. The failing limit lies at least three counts beyond these statistics:
`REPETITION_LIMIT` = L - 3, `PROB_01_THRESHOLD` = W/2 - 3 or
`PROB_10_THRESHOLD` = W/L + 3. The margin covers the window edge and both
readings of "allowed" (a fail above the limit or at it). The other limits keep
their clean values. The clean `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` of 0 is met by every count,
a count of 0 included, because the register text names it the minimum allowed
count. The leaf writes the
failing limit only after two whole windows of the pattern, so every window that
the limit meets holds only that pattern. The leaf counts words at
`esrc_decor_valid_o` and uses no absolute byte period.
* Shadow update error: for the AES `CTRL_SHADOWED` and `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`
registers the upstream `aes.hjson` states that two writes are required, that two
writes of different values trigger a recoverable alert, and that a read restarts
the phase tracking, so the next write is a first write. The injection is a
second write with a different value and no read between the two writes. For
KMAC the register text names the update error and does not state the cause, so
the KMAC shadowed trials log their outcome and grade nothing on it (see
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).
* HMAC digest order: `hmac.hjson` gives `digest[511:0] = {DIGEST0, DIGEST1, ...}`
(`DIGEST`, `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson:465-469`)
for the SHA-2 hash value, which FIPS 180-4 (Secure Hash Standard, section 6, the
final hash value H0 || H1 || ...) defines, so at
`digest_swap` = 0 DIGEST word n is the big-endian read of digest bytes 4n to
4n+3. `digest_swap` = 1 "reverses the byte-order in the final HMAC or SHA hash"
(`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/doc/programmers_guide.md:13`), so
each word is the little-endian read, and the register order does not change.
`sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test`, `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test`,
`sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test`, `sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test`,
`sep_efuse_token_channel_rand_test` and the four-engine concurrency leg of
`sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` grade a readback in that order (the
shared ones through `env/sep_hmac_golden.py`, `SepDigestOrder` and `DSWAP_ORDER`).
`sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test`, `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test` and the
concurrency leg log the order the first readback matches as `OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-ORDER`;
`sep_efuse_token_channel_rand_test` puts it on its CHK-TOKEN-HMAC line. The open
owner `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test` compares DIGEST word n with the big-endian
read of digest bytes 4n to 4n+3.
* Multi-bit boolean: the RDL of CSRNG and EDN gives kMultiBitBool4True = 6 and
kMultiBitBool4False = 9, and the reset value 0x9.
* OTBN: the specification points to the OpenTitan OTBN documentation for the
core. Expected results come from independent Python operators, never from the
DUT.
* ESRC observe FIFO: `NOISE_OBS_OVERFLOW` and `BIW_OBS_OVERFLOW` say that a word
is discarded when the FIFO is full. The entries grade the plateau level, the
overflow bit and its write-one clear. Which word is discarded is blocked.
* Window offsets of HMAC, KMAC and OTBN come from the integration RDL, the
ruling source for them.

===== Testbench hooks

Every hook below is outside the closed set of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. A read-only
probe, tap or monitor is an observation tap: the test adds it to the
SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list, and a check on it is graded. A drive of a
`sep` port and a fault force are added hooks: the test adds a top-port drive to
the port table of the HDL Top section of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and an error inject
to its inject exceptions, and a check on it is graded. Each force is released
after the trial.

* External stream driver (added hook, top-port drive). A driver on `ext_trng_axis_req_i[2:0]` and
a driver on `ext_trng_irq_i` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`). The open
integration wrapper ties the request array and `ext_trng_irq_i` to zero, so the
driver is a wrapper variant or a testbench force at the `sep` boundary. The
driver has a source reset: when a leaf asserts the TRNG reset it can withdraw
the beat it holds. This is a reset of the source, not a dropped `tvalid` of a
live beat, and the leaf logs it. It serves
`sep_trng_ext_stream_handshake_rand_test`,
`sep_trng_ext_sink_reset_rand_test` and the external Key Manager leg of
`sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test`.
* External stream response monitor (observation tap). A read-only monitor on
`ext_trng_axis_rsp_o[2:0]`. It serves the same tests as the external stream
driver.
* Alert receiver probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the 11-bit
receiver pulse vector `crypto_alert_pulse` in `sep_crypto`. It serves
`sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test` of the mailbox, interrupt and PIC area,
which owns the receiver-range check. No leaf of this area uses it.
* Alert sender inject (added hook, error inject). A force of the `alert_p` and `alert_n` pair of
one crypto alert receiver to the same value for one trial. It serves the
malformed alert leg of `sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test`.
* Detune probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the per-lane
ring-oscillator detune select. It serves `sep_esrc_autotune_detune_test`.
* OTBN status probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the OTBN operational
state code (`status_q` of the OTBN core), sampled on every `clk_i`. It serves
`sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test`.
* Internal DRBG endpoint probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the
internal DRBG stream endpoint of stream i before the source mux
(`drbg_int_axis_req[i]`, `drbg_int_axis_rsp[i]` in `sep_crypto`). It serves
`sep_trng_ext_stream_handshake_rand_test` and the external Key Manager leg of
`sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test`.
* ESRC SHA-256 status tap (observation tap). A read-only tap on the next value
of the ESRC `SHA256_STATUS` register fields, sampled on every `clk_i`. It
serves `sep_esrc_conditioning_matrix_rand_test`.
* ESRC FIFO integrity inject (added hook, error inject). A one-bit force of one stored parity
bit of the main FIFO entry at the read pointer, and a one-bit force of one bit
of one pointer copy of the main FIFO. It serves the integrity leg of
`sep_esrc_fifo_flow_rand_test`.
* ESRC noise-source stuck-at inject (added hook, error inject). A force of the
output of the noise ring oscillator of each lane to a constant 0 or 1, a stuck
noise source. The constant reaches the sampling flop, the synchronizer and the
pre-decorrelator samples of the noise observe FIFO (`NOISE_OBS_RDATA`). It
serves the lane, flush and lock legs of `sep_esrc_observe_fifo_rand_test`.
* ESRC main state machine force (added hook, error inject). A cocotb force of the state flops of the ESRC main state machine to one value for two clocks, and a release. It serves the illegal-state leg of `sep_esrc_persistent_alert_rand_test`, on VCS.
* DRBG seed ingress tap (observation tap). A read-only tap on `esrc_compress_vld_o` and on the word-ready output of the DRBG seed adapter (`drbg_seed_word_ready_o`). It serves `sep_drbg_cmd_sequence_rand_test`.

The ABR entry takes its register map from `integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl`. The two entries `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` are extension legs of open tests: they add cells to the run of the open test and are not separate leaves. The external stream checks of the TRNG entries, the malformed alert check, the FIFO integrity checks and the noise observe word checks use the added driver and injects listed under Testbench hooks.

===== Entries
[[sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test_leg]]
===== 1.25 `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, extension leg: crypto interrupt lines

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (Interrupts)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 1 seed per regression, as the entry of the open test; Verilator and VCS (the testlist entry of the open test has no `tools` pin; VCS is 4-state and shows an X in the zero reset readback of step 2 that the 2-state Verilator hides). The leg runs in the testlist entry of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` (`system.toml`, 1 seed per regression) after the body of the open test and keeps its run mode: `INTR_TEST` sets a state bit, so the leg needs no entropy and no fuse set-up.

*Source Module*: `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback and write of `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE` and `INTR_TEST` of HMAC, KMAC, CSRNG and EDN, and of `SW_RESET_N` (approved); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`, source N on bit N-1 (approved); the gated-reset probes of HMAC and KMAC and `trng_reset_active_probe_o` (approved); register model from the generated register description. No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: per block the `INTR_ENABLE` mask E, the `INTR_TEST` mask T and the clear mask C; the block order; the source order inside a block; the order of the three cells after the first cell; the flip order. Legal range: the driven bits of a block, which are its defined source bits except the `fifo_empty` bits of HMAC and KMAC. E, T and C hold 0 at the `fifo_empty` positions. Every block has more than one driven bit. In the two drawn trials of a block, E and T each hold at least one 0 and one 1 over the driven bits. C is not 0, and C shares a set bit with T when T has one. The order of the bits that stay set after the clear (the flip order) is drawn per trial. Each regression draws a new seed, so the masks and orders vary between regressions. The plan shape (all 10 sources, all four cells of each, the corner masks) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: Each interrupt line of HMAC (sources 18 and 20), KMAC (21 and 23), CSRNG (24 to 27) and EDN (28 and 29) is the `INTR_STATE` bit AND the `INTR_ENABLE` bit, and vector bit N-1 carries source N. `INTR_TEST` forces a state bit. The 10 state bits clear on a write of one. The `fifo_empty` lines (sources 19 and 22) and the OTBN line (source 30) belong to other entries (see the scope limit). Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/<ip>/regs/gen/adoc/<ip>.adoc` for hmac, kmac, csrng and edn (`INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `INTR_TEST`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, which walks `INTR_TEST` one source at a time with the enable set for the CSRNG, EDN and HMAC `err` sources and grades the write-one clear and the one-hot isolation of the vector, and `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`, which raises `hmac_done`, `kmac_done`, `cs_cmd_req_done` and `edn_cmd_req_done` together with the enable set. This leg adds the source `kmac_err` to the table of the first test. For every source it adds the cell with the enable clear, the enable flip with the state set, the write-one clear with the enable clear and drawn mixed masks. This leg grades only the new cells. The open owners keep the rest. The cell (1, 1) and the write-one clear with the enable set of the sources that the open tests walk run as controls of the new cells, and so does the cell (0, 0) of the seven sources that `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` walks (the four CSRNG sources, the two EDN sources and `hmac_err`), whose baseline check grades the line low with the state and the enable of the source at 0. Cells: 10 sources in the four (state, enable) cells; an enable flip at state 1 and a write-one clear at enable 0 and 1 for each of the 10 bits; drawn mixed masks per block; the corner masks all ones, all zeros and a single common bit over the driven bits. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_w1c_clear`, `cp_enable_flip`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`, the run mode of the open test. Write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E, read it back and log it as the baseline (CTRL-INTR-BASELINE). The Key Manager bit 0 stays 0 (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`). Print `PLAN seed=<n>` with the drawn masks and orders.
. Define the block reset that every trial uses. Write the baseline with the bit of the block cleared (HMAC bit 3, KMAC bit 4; the TRNG bit 5 for CSRNG and EDN). Wait for the gated-reset probe of the block (`trng_reset_active_probe_o` for the TRNG) to show active. Write the baseline back. Wait for the probe to show the reset released and for the first OKAY readback of `INTR_STATE` of the block. Each wait is bounded and a timeout fails the leaf. Then read `INTR_STATE` and `INTR_ENABLE` of the block. Both read 0 over the driven bits and the enable fields (RDL reset value). On a 4-state simulator a read with an X or Z bit fails in the master. A non-zero read fails the check of the trial (`baseline_ok=0` in CHK-INTR-BITWALK; `CTRL-INTR-RESET-CLEAN` before a mask trial), because the state is not clean. The graded coverage window opens at the first write of a trial and closes before each block reset.
. From here to the end, a watcher (`cocotb/env/sep_bit_watch.py`) samples `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` on every `clk_i` in the read-only phase and records the clock of each rise and fall of the vector bits of the four blocks. The bounded waits and the hold checks of the steps below grade that record. The record itself is not printed; the graded lines carry the values they compare.
. Cell walk. For each of the 10 sources, in the drawn block order and the drawn source order inside the block, run the cell (1, 1) first and then the other three cells in the drawn order. Per cell: reset the block (step 2); write `INTR_ENABLE` with only the bit of the source set when the enable is 1; write `INTR_TEST` with only the bit of the source set when the state is 1, and write the enable before the test; read `INTR_STATE`; take the vector sample of the block (CHK-INTR-BITWALK for a graded cell, `CTRL-INTR-OPEN` for a cell that the open tests walk).
. In the cell (1, 1) of a source, log t_rise (`OBS-INTR-TRISE`): the clocks from the write response that completes the cell to the first clock with the vector bit high (0 when it is high at the response). The wait for the bit is bounded and a timeout fails CHK-INTR-BITWALK. After the last cell of a source, `CTRL-INTR-CELL11` requires a logged t_rise. Every sample of that source takes a hold of 4 times t_rise (at least 4 clocks) after the readback. An expected-low bit stays low from the first write of the trial to the end of the hold; an expected-high bit is high at the end of the hold and does not fall after the write response. The leaf logs t_rise and the hold.
. Enable flip, for each source in a cell with the state 1. Write `INTR_ENABLE` with the bit of the source toggled. Wait for the line to read state AND the new enable (a bounded wait of 8 times t_rise) and hold for 4 times t_rise with no edge (CHK-INTR-FLIP). Toggle the bit back and take the same sample. The two toggles give one rise and one fall; after the toggle to enable 1 the line shows a rise (`CTRL-INTR-FLIP-RISE`).
. Write-one clear, for each source in a cell with the state 1. Read the state (it reads 1, `CTRL-INTR-W1C-PRE`). Write 1 to the bit of the source in `INTR_STATE`. Read `INTR_STATE`, wait for the line to read 0 (a bounded wait of 8 times t_rise) and hold for 4 times t_rise (CHK-INTR-BITWALK, or `CTRL-INTR-OPEN` for the clear with the enable set of a source that the open tests walk). The enable value at the clear is the enable value of the cell.
. Mask trial. For each block, run 2 drawn trials and the five corner trials in the drawn order. The corner trials use (E, T) = (all ones, all ones), (all ones, 0), (0, all ones), (0, 0) and one pair that shares exactly one set bit. All ones means every driven bit of the block. Per trial: reset the block (`CTRL-INTR-RESET-CLEAN`); write E to `INTR_ENABLE`; write T to `INTR_TEST`; read `INTR_STATE`; take the vector sample of the block with a hold of 4 times the largest t_rise of the block (CHK-INTR-LINE). Each expected-high bit rises within a bounded wait of 8 times that t_rise, and a timeout fails CHK-INTR-LINE. `CTRL-INTR-LINE-CAUSE` requires a logged t_rise for every source of the block.
. In the same trial, check that the state read of step 8 shows the bits of C that T set (`CTRL-INTR-CLEAR-PRE`). Write C to `INTR_STATE`. Read `INTR_STATE`, wait for each line that must fall (a bounded wait of 8 times t_rise) and take the vector sample with a hold of 4 times t_rise (CHK-INTR-CLEAR). C holds driven bits only.
. In the same trial, for each state bit that remains after the clear, in the drawn flip order, run the enable flip of step 6 from the enable value E (CHK-INTR-FLIP).
. ESRC leg (source 16, vector bit 15), with only the TRNG domain released. Before the first case, with the window closed, stop the hardware interrupt causes: write `INTR_ENABLE` 0, clear `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` and `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE`, write one to every `HEALTH_TEST_STATUS` and `INTR_STATUS` bit, wait the rise bound and require `INTR_STATUS` and `HEALTH_TEST_STATUS` to read 0 (`CTRL-ESRC-QUIET`; a set-up error otherwise). The `INTR_STATUS` bits are level interrupts (`entropy_source.rdl:113`), so a bit whose hardware cause holds sets again after its clear. After the last case, write back `CTRL` and `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL` as read. Before each case, write `INTR_ENABLE` 0 and a write-one clear of every bit and require both to read 0 and the line 0 (`CTRL-ESRC-CLEAN`). For each of the 8 `INTR_STATUS` bits in a drawn order: write `INTR_ENABLE` and `INTR_TEST` with that bit only (CHK-ESRC-LINE), then clear it with a write of one (CHK-ESRC-CLEAR). Then one case with drawn non-zero disjoint E and T (CHK-ESRC-DISABLED, with a probe that adds one bit of T to E), and one case with two bits a and b set and enabled (CHK-ESRC-PAIR), cleared one at a time (CHK-ESRC-ONE-CLEARED, then CHK-ESRC-CLEAR).
. The whole leg (steps 4 to 10) runs twice with the same plan: first with the coverage window closed, then, when every check passed, once more with owner window 125 open per case. A FAIL closes the window first, and no window opens after it.
. Write the baseline to `SW_RESET_N`. Print `RESULT sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-INTR-LINE
|After the `INTR_TEST` write, `INTR_STATE` of the block reads the test mask T and every vector bit of the block equals its state bit AND its enable bit (`interrupts.adoc`, source N on bit N-1; `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE` and `INTR_TEST` of each block). A wrong DUT shows an ignored enable (a line high with the enable 0), an aliased bit (another bit of the block moves), a line that never rises, or a state bit that does not set. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Control for the low lines: the cell (1, 1) of the same bit raises the line in the same seed, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-INTR-LINE PASS seed=<n> block=<b> trial=<kind> enable=0x<e> test=0x<t> state=0x<s> vec=0x<v> expect=0x<s&e> bound=<b> hold=<h>`; `vec` is the lines of the block in the bit positions of the `INTR_*` registers.
|CHK-INTR-CLEAR
|A write-one clear of the drawn subset C leaves `INTR_STATE` = T AND NOT C, the vector follows (state AND enable), and no bit outside C changes (`INTR_STATE` is RW1C for the driven bits). A wrong DUT clears extra bits, leaves a bit of C set, or leaves a line high. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and the vector probe. Control: the state read of CHK-INTR-LINE in the same trial shows the bits of C that are set before the clear (`CTRL-INTR-CLEAR-PRE`). Evidence: `CHK-INTR-CLEAR PASS seed=<n> block=<b> trial=<kind> clear=0x<c> state=0x<s2> expect=0x<t&~c> vec=0x<v2> bound=<b> hold=<h>`.
|CHK-INTR-FLIP
|For each set bit, writing the toggled enable bit moves the line to state AND the new enable, and writing it back moves the line back, so the line follows the enable in both polarities. A wrong DUT shows a line that ignores a cleared enable or does not return. Anchor: the vector probe. Control: each flip holds one toggle to enable 1, and the line rises after it (`CTRL-INTR-FLIP-RISE`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-INTR-FLIP PASS seed=<n> block=<b> src=<s> bit=<i> enable=<0 or 1> vec=<0 or 1> t=<t> bound=<b> hold=<h>`; one line per toggle.
|CHK-INTR-BITWALK
|For each of the 10 sources and each (state, enable) cell, the vector bit equals state AND enable and no other bit of the block changes from its value after the block reset. For a bit in a cell with the state 1, the write-one clear drops the state and the line with the enable 0 and with the enable 1. A wrong DUT shows a line that ignores the enable, a bit that moves a neighbour, a clear that works with one enable value only, or a state that stays set. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and the vector probe. Control: the cell (1, 1) of the same bit raises the line with a logged t_rise (`CTRL-INTR-CELL11`), and the state reads 1 just before the clear (`CTRL-INTR-W1C-PRE`). For the sources that the open tests walk, the cell (1, 1) and the clear with the enable set log `CTRL-INTR-OPEN` and are not claimed as new coverage, and so does the cell (0, 0) of the seven sources that `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` walks (CSRNG, EDN and `hmac_err`). Evidence: `CHK-INTR-BITWALK PASS seed=<n> block=<b> src=<s> bit=<i> cell=<s><e> state=0x<st> vec=<v> others_unchanged=1 baseline_ok=1 baseline_x=<0 or n/a> undriven=0x<u> hold=<h> w1c=<n/a, 0 or 1>`; `w1c` is the enable value at the clear, and n/a for the cell line before the clear. `baseline_x` is 0 on a 4-state simulator, where a read with an X or Z bit fails, and n/a on the 2-state Verilator. Each cell also reads `INTR_ENABLE` back equal to the enable of the cell, and a cell with the line low passes only when the line rose in the cell (1, 1) of the same run.
|CHK-ESRC-LINE
|With `INTR_ENABLE` and `INTR_TEST` set to bit k alone, vector bit 15 rises once, does not fall and reads 1 after the hold; `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE` read bit k on their field bits (`entropy_source.adoc:157-172`, `:181-196`; `interrupts.adoc:109-111`). Catches a line that ignores source k, a status that does not latch, a dead `INTR_TEST` and an enable that is not stored.
|CHK-ESRC-CLEAR
|A write of one to the last set bit drops the line within 8 times t_rise with no rise after the write, and `INTR_STATUS` reads 0 on its field bits (`entropy_source.adoc:133-148`, write one to clear).
|CHK-ESRC-DISABLED
|With E and T non-zero and disjoint, the line stays 0 over the hold; `INTR_STATUS` reads T and `INTR_ENABLE` reads E. Probe: adding one bit of T to E raises the line within the bound, so the status bits are live. Catches a line equal to the OR of the status or of the enable.
|CHK-ESRC-PAIR
|With two bits set and enabled, the line is 1 with no fall, and both registers read both bits.
|CHK-ESRC-ONE-CLEARED
|A write of one to one of the two bits leaves the line 1 with no fall, and `INTR_STATUS` reads the other bit only.
|CTRL-ESRC-QUIET
|(Set-up control, not a counted check.) With `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` 0 (`entropy_source.rdl:45`, the main state machine held idle), `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE` 0 (`:314`, every 32-bit stream test disabled) and `HEALTH_TEST_STATUS` and `INTR_STATUS` cleared, both read 0 after the rise bound. A status bit that a hardware cause sets again fails the leaf as a set-up error before any graded case, so every case sees only the bits its `INTR_TEST` writes set.
|OBS-ESRC-CONTRIBUTOR
|Logged only, no pass or fail. Which entropy-source causes the specification names as contributors to source 16 is an open specification question.
|OBS-ESRC-RSVD
|Logged only. The reserved bits of `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`, which have no stated reset.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This leg grades only the cells that the open tests do not walk. The cell (1, 1) and the write-one clear with the enable set of the sources that the open tests walk, and the cell (0, 0) of the seven sources that `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` walks, are controls here, and the open owners keep their grade. The `hmac_fifo_empty` and `kmac_fifo_empty` lines belong to `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`, which makes the status read 1 from the FIFO and grades the line gate. The `otbn_done` line belongs to `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test`: the OTBN reset in a cell needs the entropy chain, which this run mode does not bring up, and the status arc of the initial wipe after a reset is graded there (CHK-OTBN-INITDONE). The real done and error events of HMAC and KMAC are graded in `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test` and `sep_kmac_stream_cfg_rand_test`. The ESRC interrupt bits are graded in `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test`; the ESRC leg of this entry grades the source 16 line as the OR of `INTR_STATUS` AND `INTR_ENABLE`.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test_leg]]
===== 1.26 `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`, extension leg: four-engine concurrency

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (Cross-engine)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, `reseed = 4`; Verilator and VCS (the testlist entry of the open test has no `tools` pin). The leg runs in the testlist entry of `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` (`crypto.toml`, `reseed = 4`) after the checks of the open test and keeps its run mode. The internal source is selected in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, and ESRC, CSRNG and EDN are up as in the open test. The engines outside a group are held in reset. Stated exception: HMAC stays out of reset and idle in a group without an HMAC member, for the bystander.

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master with a fixed non-zero AXI ID per engine and one for the bystander reads (approved); `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]`, client 0 AES, 1 KMAC, 2 OTBN RND, 3 OTBN URND (approved; the leg counts the EDN words of the whole run, all four clients, from the first word of the run); the gated-reset probes of HMAC, KMAC, AES and OTBN (approved); independent models for the results: the software SHA-2 and HMAC model `sep_hmac_golden` (an HMAC DIGEST readback, member or bystander, is compared with the big-endian read of digest bytes 4n to 4n+3, convention "HMAC digest order", `SepDigestOrder`; the order is logged as `OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-ORDER seed=<n> dswap0=<be or le> other_order_reads=<k>`), a Keccak model, an AES model and a Python operator for the OTBN program. No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the drawn pair of the third group; the start order (OTBN starts last in every group that has it); the start skew of each member after the first, measured from the start write response of the first member, 0 to half the shortest clean busy time of the group; the operation of each member; the message and block counts; the data. Legal ranges: HMAC SHA-256 or SHA-512 with 64 to 512 bytes; KMAC SHA3-256 or SHAKE-128 with 0 to 300 bytes in EDN entropy mode; AES ECB or CBC with a 128-bit or 256-bit key and 4 to 64 blocks; OTBN ADD, XOR or AND. Keys, IV seeds and operands come from the fixed table of test constants, and the seed picks the row. A count that would take the EDN word total of the run above the generate budget of the open test is halved against the running total (see the steps). The skews of a group are drawn after its clean runs, from the same generator, because their range depends on the clean busy times. The bystander message is drawn so that its SHA-256 digest has no zero word. `reseed = 4` samples different pairs, orders, skews and counts. The plan shape (three groups on every seed) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: HMAC, KMAC SHA-3, AES and OTBN are separate engines, each with its own host path and its own software reset, reached through one 64-bit slave port. An operation on one engine does not change the result of another. The specification implies this and states no direct rule. Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (Cryptographic Subsystem); `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` and `hw/sys/sep/doc/kmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (Isolation and Reset Sequencing).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`, which runs AES and KMAC at the same time and grades both results (`CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE`) and the overlap of their EDN beats (`CHK-OVERLAP`), and `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`, which holds one sibling result across a reset. This leg adds the HMAC and OTBN members, skewed starts, interleaved host reads and the HMAC bystander. It runs no group of the pair AES and KMAC alone. This leg grades only the new cells. The open owners keep the rest. The EDN word total of the whole run (the open test's own AES and KMAC operations, every clean run, every group run and the OTBN post-reset wipes) stays inside the generate budget of the open test. Cells: on every seed the group of all four engines, the group AES, KMAC and OTBN, and one drawn pair that is not AES and KMAC; each result against its independent golden; the overlap of every pair of a group; the finished HMAC digest read while the other engines run. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` (`cp_engine_pair`, `cp_busy_count`, `cp_bystander_read`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and `+esrc_noise_force` as the open test does. Select the internal source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, and bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Run the groups inside the run of the open test, after the operations of the open test, with the entropy stack that it brings up. The generate budget is half of the boot Generate that the open test leaves open. The open test does not write `EDN.BOOT_GEN_CMD`, so EDN issues the boot Generate with the reset word 0xFFF003 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/edn/regs/gen/adoc/edn.adoc`, `BOOT_GEN_CMD`). Its GLEN of 0xFFF blocks of 128 bits, under the CSRNG command header that the register text names, is 16380 EDN words of 32 bits, so the budget is 8190 words. The other half covers the words that the EDN endpoint FIFOs of the other clients buffer. The `glen` of 32 that the open test names is the length of its `GENERATE_CMD` and of its legal Generate segments, not the length of the open boot Generate. The leg writes no EDN or CSRNG command register, so the boot Generate stays open for the whole run and the Generate segmentation check of the open test sees no completed command. Log the budget. Keep a running total W of the EDN words acked on `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` for all four clients from the first word of the run. W includes the words of the operations of the open test, of every clean run, of every group run and of the OTBN wipe after each OTBN reset. Log W at the end of this step.
. Draw the plan: the group of all four engines, the group AES, KMAC and OTBN, and one pair from HMAC with AES, KMAC or OTBN, and AES or KMAC with OTBN. Draw the operation, counts and data of each member. Print `PLAN seed=<n> group=<g> members=<m> start_order=<o> bystander=<0 or 1> ops=[...]` per group; `start_order` is the effective order (the drawn order with OTBN moved last).
. For each group in the order of step 2, wait until the EDN request of each engine outside the group is low (a bounded wait), then write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E with the bits of the engines outside the group cleared (OTBN bit 1, AES bit 2, HMAC bit 3, KMAC bit 4). In a group without an HMAC member the HMAC bit stays 1. The Key Manager bit 0 stays 0. Wait for the gated-reset probe of each engine that is cleared to show active (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Then, for each of AES and OTBN that is a member of the group, wait until `AES.STATUS.IDLE` = 1 or `OTBN.STATUS.status` = IDLE (EDN is up; a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Steps 3 to 13 run once for each group.
. Reset each member of the group and, in a group without an HMAC member, the HMAC, one bit at a time. Clear the bit, wait for the gated-reset probe to show active, set the bit, then wait for the first OKAY readback of the `STATUS` of the engine. For AES, then wait until `AES.STATUS.IDLE` = 1. For OTBN, then wait until `OTBN.STATUS.status` = IDLE (EDN is up). Each wait is bounded and a timeout fails the leaf. No configuration write goes to AES or OTBN before this idle wait ends.
. Clean runs. Run each member alone, in the drawn order. Configure it, start it, poll its end bit (`HMAC.INTR_STATE.hmac_done` = 1, `AES.STATUS.IDLE`, `KMAC.STATUS.sha3_squeeze` = 1, `OTBN.STATUS.status` = IDLE) until it shows the end (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), then read its result. A KMAC member reads `STATE` in the squeeze stage and then writes `CMD.done` (`kmac.hjson`, `CMD.done`: the engine goes back to idle). An AES member writes blocks 0 and 1 to `DATA_IN` when `STATUS.INPUT_READY` = 1, then reads block k from `DATA_OUT` when `STATUS.OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 and writes block k + 2; its run ends when `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 after the last block is read. For KMAC, a `STATUS` read that shows busy is a read with `sha3_squeeze` = 0. For HMAC the end is `hmac_done` (`hmac.hjson`, `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`: HMAC/SHA-2 has completed), because the register text does not state when `STATUS.hmac_idle` reads 1, and a read that shows busy is an `INTR_STATE` read with `hmac_done` = 0. The member reset before each run clears `hmac_done`. Compare the result with its golden: a mismatch fails the leaf (CTRL-CONC-CLEAN). Log t_op, the clocks from the start write response to the last `STATUS` read that shows busy, and log the EDN words of the run from `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` (the change of W over the run). Reset the member after the run, as in step 4.
. The group run repeats each member operation once. If W plus the sum of the clean-run EDN words of the group is above the generate budget, halve the drawn count of the member with the most clean-run EDN words and repeat steps 4 and 5. The words of the discarded clean runs stay in W. If every count of the group is at its minimum and the sum is still above the budget, the leaf fails (CTRL-CONC-BUDGET).
. In a group without an HMAC member, run the bystander job: reset HMAC as in step 4, write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1, `hmac_en` = 0 and `digest_size` = SHA2_256, write `CMD.hash_start`, write a 64-byte message to `MSG_FIFO`, write `CMD.hash_process`, poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), read DIGEST, compare it with the software golden (`CTRL-CONC-BYSTANDER`), and write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Keep the digest as the bystander golden. HMAC stays out of reset and idle.
. Configure every member of the group: keys, IV or cipher mode, `CFG`, the OTBN program and the operands. An AES member gets its KEY words first, then a bounded wait until `AES.STATUS.IDLE` = 1 (a timeout fails the leaf), then its IV words. An HMAC member sets `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1, `hmac_en` = 1, the drawn `digest_size` and a `key_length` that is legal for it (not Key_None; Key_1024 only with SHA2_512), and writes the `KEY` words of the table row. A KMAC member has `entropy_mode` = EDN, `entropy_ready` = 1 and `sideload` = 0. The OTBN result word holds the complement of its golden. Read back the configuration of each member and log it (`INFO-CONC-CFG`).
. Start the members as independent tasks on the CPU-LSU master with non-zero IDs. The start order is the drawn order with OTBN moved last. Each member after the first starts when its drawn skew has elapsed from the start write response of the first member; OTBN also waits until every other member has started (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-CONC-RESULT of OTBN). Each task starts its engine: an HMAC or KMAC task writes its start command and its message, and an AES task writes its first two blocks to `DATA_IN` when `STATUS.INPUT_READY` = 1 (its start is the write response of the last word of block 0). Until every member of the group has started, an HMAC task holds `CMD.hash_process`, a KMAC task holds `CMD.process`, and an AES task reads no `DATA_OUT`, so the engine stays busy (`kmac.hjson`, `CMD.process`; `hmac.hjson`, `CMD.hash_process`; `aes.hjson`, `STATUS.IDLE` and `STATUS.STALL`: with the output unread the AES stalls, and IDLE reads 0 while it is stalled). Each hold is bounded by the longest poll bound of the group, and a timeout fails CHK-CONC-RESULT of the member. Then each task polls the end bit of its engine as in step 5 and reads its result after the end. A KMAC task then writes `CMD.done`. An AES task keeps one block ahead: it reads block k from `DATA_OUT` when `STATUS.OUTPUT_VALID` = 1, then writes block k + 2. The polls and result reads of the tasks interleave on the one master. Each poll is bounded by 8 times the t_op of the member plus its skew, counted from the end of the hold, and a timeout fails CHK-CONC-RESULT of the member.
. In a group without an HMAC member, run a further task from the first start to the end of the last member task: it reads `DIGEST_0` to `DIGEST_7` in turn, with a fixed read interval of 8 `clk_i` cycles that the leaf logs (`interval_clk`), on its own AXI ID. Every read completes OKAY.
. For each pair of the group, take the busy window of each member from its start write response to its last `STATUS` read that shows busy. A window ends only at a DUT status read, never at a write of the testbench. The response clock of the HMAC `hash_process` and the KMAC `process` write is logged as `INFO-CONC-PROCESS-WRITE seed=<n> group=<g> engine=<e> start_clk=<s> process_clk=<p> busy_end_clk=<b>` and does not end a window. Compute the overlap of the pair and log it (CTRL-CONC-OVERLAP). An overlap of 0 logs `CTRL-MISSING`.
. Read W. The words of the group are the change of W over steps 3 to 12. A W above the generate budget fails the leaf (CTRL-CONC-BUDGET).
. Compare each result with its golden, and require every host access of the member to complete OKAY, no member timeout and `ERR_BITS` = 0 for OTBN (CHK-CONC-RESULT). Compare each bystander read with the bystander golden (CHK-CONC-HOLD).
. After the last group, read W and log the total line of the run. A total above the generate budget fails the leaf (CTRL-CONC-BUDGET).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONC-RESULT
|Each engine result under overlap equals its independent golden: the software SHA-2 or HMAC digest, the Keccak model digest, the AES model ciphertext, and the Python operator result in OTBN DMEM. Every host access of the group completes OKAY. The engines have separate host paths and resets (`crypto.adoc`; `reset_controller.adoc`), so a wrong DUT shows cross-talk between engines, a lost host access or a wrong result only under overlap. Anchor: the `DIGEST`, `STATE`, `DATA_OUT` and DMEM readback through the CPU-LSU master. Control: the clean run of the same member in the same seed returns its golden (CTRL-CONC-CLEAN), and the busy windows of every pair of the group overlap (CTRL-CONC-OVERLAP, `overlap_clk` > 0), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-CONC-RESULT PASS seed=<n> group=<g> engine=<e> golden_ok=1 all_okay=1 accesses=<a> timeout=0 skew=<s> busy_clk=<b> err_bits=0x0 result0=0x<r> golden0=0x<g> not_okay=-`, one line per member; `CTRL-CONC-OVERLAP seed=<n> group=<g> pair=<e1,e2> overlap_clk=<k>`, one line per pair; `CTRL-CONC-CLEAN seed=<n> group=<g> engine=<e> golden_ok=1 t_op=<t> edn_words=<w> op=<o>`, one line per clean run; `CTRL-CONC-BUDGET seed=<n> group=<g> words=<w> run_total=<t> budget=<b>`, one line per group, with `words` the change of W over the group; and one total line `CTRL-CONC-BUDGET seed=<n> total=<t> budget=<b>` at the end of the run. A total above the budget fails the leaf.
|CHK-CONC-HOLD
|In a group without an HMAC member, every HMAC DIGEST word read while the other engines run equals the digest of the HMAC job of step 7, and no read returns another value. A wrong DUT shows a bystander digest that a peer operation changes. Anchor: the `DIGEST` readback. Control: the count of reads that completed while at least one peer is busy (from the busy windows of step 11) is at least 1 (`CTRL-CONC-HOLD-BUSY-READS`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`; the bystander DIGEST equals its software golden before the group runs (`CTRL-CONC-BYSTANDER`), and the golden has no zero word, because the leaf draws the bystander message so that it has none. Evidence: `CHK-CONC-HOLD PASS seed=<n> group=<g> bystander=hmac reads=<r> total_reads=<t> stable=1 interval_clk=8 first_wrong=-`; `r` counts the reads that completed while a peer is busy.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This leg claims only the HMAC and OTBN members, the skewed starts, the interleaved host reads and the HMAC bystander. It grades the AES and KMAC results only in groups that also have an HMAC or an OTBN member. The open owners keep AES and KMAC alone (`CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE`, `CHK-OVERLAP`) and the sibling result across a reset. The leg claims no EDN beat overlap, grant order or routing. A busy window includes the hold of step 9, so a pair overlaps while one member holds its process command or its unread output; the leg does not claim that the computations of two members overlap clock for clock. The specification implies separate engines with their own host paths and software resets and states no direct rule, so the contract is the implied one.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test]]
===== 1.27 `sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (HMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `reseed = 4`; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback and write of the HMAC registers (`INTR_STATE`, `CFG`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `DIGEST`, `ERR_CODE`, `KEY`, `MSG_FIFO`) (approved); the gated-reset probe of HMAC (approved); `sep_hmac_golden`, a software SHA-2 and HMAC model with the key word order of `hmac.hjson` (the `KEY` multireg). DIGEST is compared with the big-endian read of each word at `digest_swap` = 0 and the little-endian read at 1 (convention "HMAC digest order"). No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the eleven classes, the digest size of the plain SHA-2 class, the invalid `digest_size` value (two or three of the bits 2:0 set), the invalid `key_length` value (two to five of the bits 4:0 set), the `digest_size` of the SHA2_None class with `hmac_en` = 1 (all-zero or an invalid multi-bit value) and its `key_length` (Key_128, Key_256, Key_384 or Key_512), the message length of each hash that runs (0 to 64 bytes), the key row of the fixed table, the digest size and the key length of each recovery hash (legal for its digest size), the message bytes, and last the message of the worst-case hash (HMAC-SHA2_512, Key_1024, 64 bytes). The leaf redraws the length and bytes of a message until its golden digest is non-zero, and of a recovery message until its golden differs from D_prev. `reseed = 4` samples four orders and draws. The plan shape (all eleven classes on every seed) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: In HMAC mode (`hmac_en` = 1) a start with `key_length` Key_None (an invalid or all-zero value maps to Key_None), or with Key_1024 on SHA2_256, is blocked and signalled: `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` sets, no `hmac_done` is raised and DIGEST is unchanged. A start with `digest_size` SHA2_None (an invalid or all-zero value maps to SHA2_None) is blocked and signalled in the same way, with `hmac_en` = 0 or 1. A plain SHA-2 start (`hmac_en` = 0) ignores `key_length`. Key_1024 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512 is legal. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc` (`INTR_STATE`, `CFG`, `STATUS`, `ERR_CODE`); upstream `hmac.hjson` (`CFG.digest_size`, `CFG.key_length`) for the codes and the blocked starts.

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`, which drives the legal cells and reads a zero error status (`CHK-ERR`), and `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, which forces `hmac_err` with `INTR_TEST`. This test raises `hmac_err` from a real configuration error and grades the blocked cells and the recovery. The legal cells run only as controls (CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK), and the open owner keeps them. Cells: `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_None on SHA2_256, SHA2_384 and SHA2_512; `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_1024 on SHA2_256; `hmac_en` = 1 with an invalid multi-bit `key_length` on SHA2_256; `hmac_en` = 0 with `digest_size` all-zero; `hmac_en` = 0 with an invalid multi-bit `digest_size`; `hmac_en` = 1 with `digest_size` SHA2_None (all-zero or invalid) and a key length legal on every digest size; the write-one clear of `hmac_err` and a legal hash after each blocked start. These eight blocked cells, their `ERR_CODE` value and the recovery are graded. The three legal cells (`hmac_en` = 0 with Key_None on a drawn digest size; `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_1024 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512) run as controls. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg` (`x_hmac_start_class`, `cp_hmac_key_src`, `cp_hmac_err_code`) and `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` for `hmac_err`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`. Print `PLAN seed=<n>` with the class order and the draws. Write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E and log it (`INFO-HMAC-BASELINE`). Reset HMAC: write `SW_RESET_N` with bit 3 cleared from the baseline, wait for the gated-reset probe of HMAC to show active, write the baseline back, and wait for the first OKAY readback of `STATUS` (each wait bounded; a timeout fails the leaf). Read `ERR_CODE`: it reads its reset value 0x0 (`CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-RESET`, `hmac.adoc:171`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Every `CFG` write of this test sets `sha_en` = 1, `endian_swap` = 0, `digest_swap` = 0 and `key_swap` = 0. A message is written as 32-bit writes of its whole words and one-byte writes of its tail.
. Reference hashes, with the graded window closed. First the worst-case hash: write `CFG` (SHA2_512, Key_1024, `hmac_en` = 1), the 32 `KEY` words of the drawn row, `CMD.hash_start`, the 64-byte message and `CMD.hash_process`, and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Log t_worst, the clocks from the `hash_start` write response to the first read that shows `hmac_done`. Read DIGEST and compare it with the golden (`CTRL-HMAC-REF ref=sha512_key1024`). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Then the reference hash: the same sequence with `CFG` SHA2_256, `hmac_en` = 0 and a drawn message (`CTRL-HMAC-REF ref=sha256_nokey`); log its time. t_clean is the larger of the two times. t_worst is the start-to-done time of the longest hash that any class can start, so it exceeds the process-to-done time of any started class. The hold is 3 times t_clean and the poll bound of a later hash is 64 times t_clean.
. For each class in the drawn order, write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done` and `hmac_err`, read `STATUS` and log `hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE` (not graded), and read `INTR_STATE`: `hmac_err` and `hmac_done` read 0 (`CTRL-HMAC-PRESTATE`). Then run steps 4 to 8 for a blocked class or step 9 for a legal class.
.
