Blocked class (the three Key_None classes, Key_1024 on SHA2_256 and the invalid key length, all with `hmac_en` = 1; `digest_size` all-zero and the invalid `digest_size`, both with `hmac_en` = 0; `digest_size` SHA2_None with `hmac_en` = 1 and Key_128..Key_512). Reset HMAC as in step 1 and read `ERR_CODE`: it reads its reset value 0x0 (`CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-CLR`, `hmac.adoc:171`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. No text states a clear of `ERR_CODE` by a write, a legal hash or the write-one clear of `hmac_err`, so the reset is the only stated way back to 0x0. Run the last completed hash again (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Before the `CFG` write of the class, read DIGEST under the `CFG` of the last completed hash (the words of its digest size) and keep it as D_prev. D_prev equals the golden digest of that hash and is non-zero (`CTRL-HMAC-DPREV`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; the leaf draws the message of that hash so that its golden is non-zero. Read `ERR_CODE`: it is not 0x6 (`CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-BEFORE`), so a 0x6 after the start comes from that start, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Its exact value after the legal hash is not stated and is logged only.
. Open the graded window. Write `CFG` with the digest size, key length and `hmac_en` of the class. When `hmac_en` = 1 write the 32 `KEY` words of a table row (the engine is idle, so the write is legal). Hold for 3 times t_clean and read `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE`: `hmac_err` reads 0 and `ERR_CODE` is not 0x6 (`CTRL-HMAC-ERR-PRESTART`: the error is raised "when hashing starts", `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc:52-53`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails.
. Error injection. Point: `CMD.hash_start`. Kind: a start with the blocked `digest_size`, `key_length` and `hmac_en` of the class. Expected response: `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` = 1, no `hmac_done` and DIGEST unchanged. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Hold for 3 times t_clean. Read `INTR_STATE`, `STATUS` and `ERR_CODE`, before any message write, so that only the start can set them.
. Write the recovery message of the class to `MSG_FIFO` and write `CMD.hash_process`: the stimulus that completes a legal hash within t_clean. Log the count of message writes that do not answer OKAY as `OBS-HMAC-BLOCKED-MSG-RESP` (not graded: the register text does not state the response of a message write after a blocked start). Hold for 3 times t_clean again and read `INTR_STATE`. Then write `CFG` back to the `CFG` of the last completed hash (`sha_en` = 1, so the digest is not cleared) and read the DIGEST words of its digest size (CHK-HMAC-BLOCK). The register text gives the DIGEST layout per digest size and does not state the readback after a change of `digest_size`, so DIGEST is compared only under the `CFG` under which D_prev was read. Compare the `ERR_CODE` value of step 6 with 0x6 (CHK-HMAC-ERRCODE). Log `STATUS.hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE` (not graded).
. Recovery. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_err`, read `INTR_STATE` (`hmac_err` reads 0) and close the graded window. Write `CFG` for a legal HMAC hash (a drawn digest size and a key length that is legal for it), write the `KEY` words, write `CMD.hash_start`, write the recovery message (its golden digest differs from D_prev), write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait of 64 times t_clean). Read DIGEST and compare it with the golden and with D_prev (CHK-HMAC-RECOVER). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. A timeout resets HMAC. Go to step 3 for the next class.
. Legal class (`hmac_en` = 0 with Key_None on a drawn digest size; `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_1024 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512). Write `CFG`, write the `KEY` words when `hmac_en` = 1, write `CMD.hash_start`, write a drawn message, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait of 64 times t_clean). Read DIGEST, write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done` and read `INTR_STATE`: `hmac_err` reads 0 and DIGEST equals the golden (CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK); a miss logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Go to step 3 for the next class.
. Log the DIGEST byte order of the seed as `OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-ORDER seed=<n> dswap0=<be or le>`. Print `RESULT sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HMAC-BLOCK
|For a blocked class, `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` reads 1 at the end of the first hold (it reads 0 before the start, `CTRL-HMAC-PRESTATE`), `hmac_done` reads 0 at the end of both holds, although the message and `hash_process` of a legal hash are written between them, and DIGEST read under the restored `CFG` of the last completed hash equals D_prev (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: "starting is blocked and an error is signalled to SW"; `hmac.hjson`, `CFG.digest_size` and `CFG.key_length`). The hold is 3 times t_clean, and t_clean is at least the start-to-done time of the longest hash that a class can start, so a started engine sets `hmac_done` inside the hold. `STATUS.hmac_idle` is logged and not graded, because the register text does not state its value after a blocked start (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). A wrong DUT starts the hash (`hmac_done` rises or DIGEST changes) or blocks it with no error. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` and `DIGEST` readback; `hmac_err` reads 0 before the class (`CTRL-HMAC-PRESTATE`) and again after the blocked `CFG` and `KEY` writes and one hold, just before the start (`CTRL-HMAC-ERR-PRESTART`), so the error comes from the start and not from the configuration writes. Control for "never starts": D_prev equals the golden of the last completed hash and is non-zero by the draw (`CTRL-HMAC-DPREV`); the reference hashes return their goldens (`CTRL-HMAC-REF`); the legal classes of the same seed raise no error, set `hmac_done` and return their golden digest (CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK); and the recovery hash of the same seed changes DIGEST. Without these the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-BLOCK PASS seed=<n> class=<c> digest=0b<s> keylen=0b<k> hmac_en=<e> err=1 done=0 digest_unchanged=1 d_prev=0x<p> digest0=0x<d> msg_len=<m> window_clk=<w> hold=<h> t_clean=<c>`; `p` is DIGEST_0 of D_prev. `OBS-HMAC-BLOCKED-MSG-RESP seed=<n> class=<c> refused=<r>` and `OBS-HMAC-IDLE seed=<n> class=<c> phase=<before or blocked> idle=<i>` are logged and not graded. `CTRL-HMAC-REF seed=<n> ref=<sha512_key1024 or sha256_nokey> len=<L> t_clean=<t> bound=<b>`, `CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK seed=<n> class=<c> digest=<s> keylen=<k> hmac_en=<e> err=0 done=1 digest_eq_golden=1 bound=<b>`, one line per legal class.
|CHK-HMAC-RECOVER
|After the write-one clear of `hmac_err` the bit reads 0, and a legal HMAC hash returns its golden digest, which differs from D_prev (`hmac.adoc` register text of `INTR_STATE`: RW1C). A wrong DUT keeps the error bit set or leaves the engine unable to start. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and the DIGEST readback against the golden. Control: the digest differs from D_prev, so a started hash does change DIGEST. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-RECOVER PASS seed=<n> class=<c> err_cleared=1 sha=<s> klen=<k> digest_eq_golden=1 digest_changed=1 t_done=<t> bound=<b>`.
|CHK-HMAC-ERRCODE
|For each blocked class, all 32 bits of `ERR_CODE`, read after the first hold and before any message write, equal 0x6 (`SwInvalidConfig`: "The error is reported when HMAC has been configured incorrectly by SW, i.e. invalid digest size for SHA-2/HMAC modes or invalid key length for HMAC mode.", `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/doc/programmers_guide.md:128`; TT `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc:52-53`: "An unset or unsupported length raises a configuration error when hashing starts."). `err_code` is the whole field 31:0 (`hmac.adoc:171`), so no bit is masked. A wrong DUT reports another code, or none. Anchor: the `ERR_CODE` readback. Control: `ERR_CODE` reads its reset value 0x0 after the engine reset and before any start (`CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-RESET`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Before each blocked class the engine reset returns `ERR_CODE` to 0x0 (`CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-CLR`), and just before the start it is not 0x6 (`CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-BEFORE`, and again after the blocked `CFG` and `KEY` writes and one hold, `CTRL-HMAC-ERR-PRESTART`), so each class shows its own change to 0x6 at the start. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-ERRCODE PASS seed=<n> class=<c> err_code=0x6 expected=0x6`, `CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-RESET seen: seed=<n> err_code=0x0 expected=0x0`, `CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-CLR seen: seed=<n> class=<c> err_code=0x0 expected=0x0` `CTRL-HMAC-ERRCODE-BEFORE seen: seed=<n> class=<c> err_code=0x<v> not_expected=0x6` and `CTRL-HMAC-ERR-PRESTART seen: seed=<n> class=<c> err=0 err_code=0x<v> hold=<h>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test claims the blocked start cells, their `ERR_CODE` value and the recovery. The legal start cells run only as controls, and the open owner keeps them. It does not grade `ERR_CODE` after a legal hash or after the write-one clear of `hmac_err` (no text states it), nor the response of a message write after a blocked start. `STATUS.hmac_idle` is logged and not graded: the register text gives only its reset value 1 and the rule that context writes are legal while it is 1. It does not drive a blocked `hash_continue`. The open owner (`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`) keeps the legal cells and the zero error status. The `hmac_err` line to the interrupt aggregator, driven with `INTR_TEST`, belongs to `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test]]
===== 1.28 `sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (HMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `reseed = 4`; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback, write and write-response timing (`STATUS`, `INTR_STATE`, `CFG`, `CMD`, `KEY`, `DIGEST`, the `MSG_FIFO` window at offset 0x1000) (approved); the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`, which holds the read side of the message FIFO idle (approved); the gated-reset probe of HMAC (approved); `sep_hmac_golden`, a software SHA-2 and HMAC model; DIGEST word n is compared with the big-endian read of digest bytes 4n to 4n+3 (`digest_swap` = 0, convention "HMAC digest order"; `env/sep_hmac_golden.py`, `SepDigestOrder`). No PROPOSED item. CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE is blocked (an unstated value, see the steps); every other check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the cell order, the write mode of each cell, the gap of the gapped mode (0 to 200 clocks), the message words (unique per word and different from the register reset value), `hmac_en` of each cell, the key row of the fixed table, the key length (legal for the digest size), the number of writes between two `STATUS` reads (1 to 8; a stalled leg reads `STATUS` only as the steps say), the order of the three stalled legs, the digest size and plain or HMAC choice of each stalled leg and each `fifo_empty` leg, the 1000-byte message of the measurement run and of the graded run of each stalled leg, and the digest size, the key length, the message and the wipe value w of the wipe leg (w has four different bytes and differs from every digest word of the hash before the wipe). The narrow-write message is fixed. `reseed = 4` samples different orders, modes and gaps. The plan shape (25 cells, three stalled legs, two `fifo_empty` legs, one wipe leg and one narrow-write leg on every seed) is the same on every seed. The word `w2` that the busy-wipe leg writes to `WIPE_SECRET` is a 32-bit draw made after every other draw. The busy-wipe leg runs on every seed.

*Objective*: `STATUS.fifo_depth`, `fifo_full` and `fifo_empty` report the message FIFO occupancy. A write to a full FIFO back-pressures the bus and the data is not lost. `MSG_FIFO` takes 8-bit and 16-bit writes at any byte lane of the window, and each write adds its bytes to the message. `INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` is raised only if the FIFO has been full before, software can still write and HMAC is not in the second round. A write of w to `WIPE_SECRET` overwrites the digest and the secret key with w. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc` (`INTR_STATE`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (`WIPE_SECRET`; `MSG_FIFO`, `byte-write: "true"`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (Single-Beat Access Only).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`, which uses messages of 1 to 16 words. This test grades the FIFO status, long messages, back-pressure and the `fifo_empty` interrupt. The open owner keeps the legal digest cells. Cells: every (digest size, length class) cell, SHA-256 with 0, 1, 3, 4, 55, 56, 63, 64, 65, 200 and 1000 bytes and SHA-384 and SHA-512 with 111, 112, 127, 128, 129, 200 and 1000 bytes, plain SHA-2 or HMAC drawn per cell; three write modes (a burst of back-to-back single-beat posted writes, single writes with drawn gaps, single-byte writes), each with a full FIFO reached with the drain stall and drained after the release; the `fifo_empty` interrupt after a full FIFO and with a FIFO that never reaches full; the `WIPE_SECRET` overwrite of the digest and of the key after a hash; one fixed narrow-lane SHA-256 message per seed (8-bit writes at byte lanes 1, 2 and 3 and 16-bit writes at byte lane 2), graded by CHK-HMAC-NARROW-WRITE outside the coverage window. Functional coverage: `sep_hmac_cfg_op_cg` (`x_len_class`, `x_fifo_state_mode`, `cp_backpressure`, `cp_wipe_secret`) and `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`cp_hmac_fifo_empty_qual`, `cp_hw_raise` for `hmac_fifo_empty`). The `x_line_gate` cells of the `fifo_empty` sources belong to `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`, so this test fills no `x_line_gate` cell. The narrow-write leg fills no Phase 2 bin. Each cell is held at its DUT event and samples only after its owning check passes: the leaf writes the pass label `hmac_fifo_pass` after CHK-HMAC-MSG (cell legs), CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE-HOLD (stalled legs), CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY (the full leg; the not-full leg after a wait of 64 clocks) and CHK-HMAC-WIPE (wipe leg). A FAIL or a missing control closes the graded window for the rest of the leaf.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`. Print `INFO-PLAN seed=<n>` with the cell order and the draws, and log the `SW_RESET_N` baseline as `INFO-BASELINE sw_reset_n=0x7e`. Define "reset HMAC": write `SW_RESET_N` with bit 3 cleared from the baseline 0x7E, wait for the gated-reset probe of HMAC to show active, write the baseline back, and wait for the first OKAY readback of `STATUS` (each wait bounded; a timeout fails the leaf). Every `CFG` write sets `sha_en` = 1. In an HMAC hash, write the 32 `KEY` words of a table row after `CFG` and before `CMD.hash_start`.
. Reset HMAC. Read `STATUS`. `fifo_empty` and `hmac_idle` read 1 (RDL reset values). Count the read as a `STATUS` sample (CHK-HMAC-STATUS).
. Cell leg, for each of the 25 cells in the drawn order. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` for the cell, with plain SHA-2 or HMAC drawn. Write `CMD.hash_start`. The drain stall is not applied.
. Write the message of the cell to the `MSG_FIFO` window in the drawn write mode: a burst of back-to-back single-beat posted writes (`AxLEN` = 0, no gap; no AXI burst goes to the crypto region), single writes with the drawn gap, or single-byte writes. A posted write is issued before the write response of the write before it. Read `STATUS` after the drawn number of writes (CHK-HMAC-STATUS); in burst mode the 32-bit writes go out in posted groups of the drawn number of writes, at least 2, and `STATUS` is read after each group. Log `hmac_idle` of each read as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE` (not graded: the register text does not state when the bit reads 0).
. Write `CMD.hash_process`. Poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-MSG). Read `STATUS` (CHK-HMAC-STATUS) and log `hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE`. Read DIGEST and compare it with the software golden of the whole message (CHK-HMAC-MSG). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Stalled leg, for each of the three write modes in the drawn order. First the measurement run, which finds the write count N that fills the FIFO: Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` (and the `KEY` words) with a drawn digest size and plain SHA-2 or HMAC. Apply the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Write the writes of the measurement message in the width of the leg mode (32-bit for the burst and gap modes, one byte for the byte mode), one at a time, and read `STATUS` after each write, until a read shows `fifo_full` = 1 (CHK-HMAC-STATUS, phase stalled). Log the depth of that read as D and the count of writes as N. If the 1000-byte message ends before `fifo_full` reads 1, log `CTRL-HMAC-STALL-FULL` as missing (`CTRL-MISSING`). With the stall held, issue up to 8 more writes, each held for 16 times the clean write latency, and log how many complete (`OBS-HMAC-STALL-N seed=<n> mode=<m> n_full=<N> completed_with_stall=<c> answered_after_full=<a> t_wr=<t> held=<0 or 1>`, not graded). Release the stall, wait for a pending write (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf) and reset HMAC. The hash is not processed.
. Run the leg with the same `CFG` and the graded 1000-byte message. Open the graded window. Write `CFG`. Apply the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`. Write `CMD.hash_start`.
. Write the first write of the message in the leg mode. Log t_clean_wr, the clocks from the issue of the beat to its write response, and require an OKAY response within a bounded wait (`CTRL-HMAC-BP-CLEAN`). The FIFO is empty, so this is a clean write.
. Write the next writes of the message in the leg mode until exactly N writes are posted, with no `STATUS` read between them (the gap mode waits its drawn gap after each write; the burst mode posts them in groups of the drawn number of writes, at least 2). Read `STATUS` once (CHK-HMAC-STATUS, phase stalled): `fifo_full` reads 1 (`CTRL-HMAC-STALL-FULL`). If it reads 0, log `CTRL-MISSING`.
. With the stall held and `fifo_full` read 1, issue the next beats of the message one at a time, up to 16 beats (`BP_BEAT_BOUND`, logged as `bound=`). The bound is a termination bound of the leg, not a spec value: the text states no packer capacity, and which beat is held is the blocked question of CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE. After each beat, wait 16 times t_clean_wr. A beat with no write response at the end of that wait is the held beat, and the leaf issues no further beat. A beat that completes is an accepted beat; its response is recorded. Do not read `STATUS` during a hold: the host path carries one transaction at a time, so a read behind the held write waits for it. The register text states that a write to a full FIFO is back-pressured, but a byte packer sits in front of the FIFO and the text does not state which write is held, so the index of the held beat is logged only: `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE reason=<item>` and `OBS-HMAC-BP-HOLD-BEAT seed=<n> mode=<m> n_full=<N> hold_beat=<i or none> accepted_after_full=<a> no_resp_clk=<w> hold_clk=<h>`.
. Release the stall. Wait for the write response of the held beat (a bounded wait of 64 times t_clean_wr; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE-HOLD and ends the leaf, because the one host path is blocked).
. Write the rest of the message in the leg mode. Read `STATUS` after the drawn number of writes (phase drain, from the release on).
. Read `STATUS` until `fifo_depth` reads 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-STATUS). Each read is a sample.
. Write `CMD.hash_process`. Poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-MSG). Read `STATUS` (CHK-HMAC-STATUS) and log `hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE`. Read DIGEST and compare it with the software golden of the 1000 bytes (CHK-HMAC-MSG and CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE-HOLD). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Full `fifo_empty` leg. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` for a drawn digest size and plain SHA-2 or HMAC. Apply the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Write unique 32-bit words, one at a time, and read `STATUS` after each, until a read shows `fifo_full` = 1. Log the depth of that read as D.
. Release the stall. Read `STATUS` until `fifo_depth` reads 0. Log t_drain, the clocks from the release to the first read that shows `fifo_depth` = 0. Hold for 4 times t_drain. Read `INTR_STATE` (CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY, leg full). Do not write `CMD.hash_process` before this read.
. Not-full `fifo_empty` leg. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` for a drawn digest size and plain SHA-2 or HMAC. Do not apply the stall. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Write 2 unique words, one at a time, each after the write response of the one before, and read `STATUS` after each. The depth D that the full leg logs is above 2, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`.
. Read `STATUS` until `fifo_depth` reads 0 (a bounded wait of 4 times t_drain). Hold for 4 times t_drain. Read `INTR_STATE` (CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY, leg notfull). Do not write `CMD.hash_process` before this read. Then close the graded window, read `INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` as 0 (`CTRL-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST`) and write `INTR_TEST.fifo_empty`. Read `INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` one clock later as 1 (CHK-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST). Read it again after 64 clocks and after a write-one clear, and log the bit of the notfull-leg read and these two reads (`OBS-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST`).
. Wipe leg. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` with the drawn digest size and HMAC, write the `KEY` words of a table row with the drawn key length, write `CMD.hash_start`, write the drawn message, write `CMD.hash_process` and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait). Read DIGEST (CHK-HMAC-MSG). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Open the graded window and write w to `WIPE_SECRET`. Read the DIGEST words of the digest size (CHK-HMAC-WIPE, digest half).
. Write `CFG` with the same digest size, HMAC and key length, and write no `KEY` word (the one exception to the key rule of step 1). Write `CMD.hash_start`, write a second drawn message, write `CMD.hash_process` and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait). Read DIGEST (CHK-HMAC-WIPE, key half). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. The wipe leg runs once. After CHK-HMAC-WIPE passes, write the pass label, then close the graded window.
. Busy-wipe leg, after the wipe leg, with the graded window closed. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` with the digest size and key of the wipe leg, HMAC and the drawn key length, write the `KEY` words and `CMD.hash_start`, and write half of the second message of the wipe leg to `MSG_FIFO`. Read `STATUS`, write the drawn `w2` to `WIPE_SECRET`, write the rest of the message and `CMD.hash_process`. Poll `INTR_STATE` for `hmac_done` or `hmac_err` (a bounded wait), read DIGEST and log the result (`OBS-HMAC-WIPE-BUSY`). Reset HMAC.
. Narrow-write leg, last, with the graded window closed. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` (plain SHA-256, `endian_swap` = 0) and `CMD.hash_start`. Write the fixed 10-byte message as 8 writes at the `MSG_FIFO` window, in the fixed order: 8-bit writes at byte lanes 1, 2 and 3 and 16-bit writes at byte lane 2 (`AxSIZE` 0 or 1, the address of the byte lane). Record the response of each write. Write `CMD.hash_process`, poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check), read DIGEST (CHK-HMAC-NARROW-WRITE). Reset HMAC.
. Print `RESULT sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HMAC-STATUS
|On every `STATUS` sample `fifo_empty` is 1 if and only if `fifo_depth` is 0, `fifo_full` implies `fifo_empty` is 0, and no sample with `fifo_full` = 0 reports a `fifo_depth` above that of a sample with `fifo_full` = 1 in the same seed (`hmac.adoc` register text of `STATUS`). A wrong DUT shows a depth that disagrees with the empty or full bit. Anchor: the `STATUS` readback. Control: the stalled leg reads `fifo_full` = 1 (`CTRL-HMAC-STALL-FULL`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-STATUS PASS seed=<n> phase=<stalled, drain, run or idle> depth=<d> full=<f> empty=<e> idle=<i>`.
|CHK-HMAC-FIFO-CAP
|On every `STATUS` sample with `fifo_full` = 1, `fifo_depth` reads 32, so every sample with `fifo_depth` below 32 reads `fifo_full` = 0 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/README.md:24` "32 x 32-bit message FIFO buffer"; `hmac.hjson:396-402`, `fifo_full` "FIFO full", `fifo_depth` "FIFO entry count"). Every sample is graded, not only the first full one. A DUT whose FIFO goes full at another depth (16, 31 or 33) fails. Anchor: the `STATUS` readback. Control: `CTRL-HMAC-STALL-FULL`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-FIFO-CAP PASS seed=<n> phase=<p> depth=<d> full=<f> cap=32`.
|CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE-HOLD
|With the drain stall held and `fifo_full` read 1, one of the next 16 beats into the message window (16 is the termination bound of the leg, not a spec value; which beat is held is the blocked question of CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE) gets no write response over a hold of 16 times the clean write latency. After the stall is released, the held beat and every beat accepted before it complete with OKAY, and the digest of the whole message equals its golden, so no data is lost (`hmac.adoc` register text of `STATUS`: "Data written to the FIFO whilst it is full will cause back-pressure on the interconnect"). A wrong DUT never holds a beat, refuses a beat, drops data, or never answers the held beat. Anchor: the write response timing at the CPU-LSU master, the `STATUS` readback, the stall inject and the DIGEST readback. Control: a beat written with the FIFO empty completes OKAY (`CTRL-HMAC-BP-CLEAN`), and the `STATUS` read before the hold shows `fifo_full` = 1 (`CTRL-HMAC-STALL-FULL`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE-HOLD PASS seed=<n> mode=<burst, gap or byte> full=1 held=1 bound=16 hold_clk=<h> clean_clk=<c> accepted_resp=<OKAY,... or none> held_resp_after_release=OKAY digest==golden=1`.
|CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE (blocked, `OBS-BLOCKED`)
|Which write after the `STATUS` read of `fifo_full` = 1 is held. The register text does not state it, because a byte packer sits in front of the FIFO. The leaf logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE reason=<item>` and `OBS-HMAC-BP-HOLD-BEAT`. No credit.
|CHK-HMAC-MSG
|The digest equals the software golden of the whole message, so no word is lost or reordered. The golden takes the message bytes in write order, each write in lane order (`endian_swap` = 0). A wrong DUT loses a word under back-pressure or reorders words. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: the cells of the same seed cover every length class (`CTRL-HMAC-LENGTHS`), so a dropped final word changes the digest. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-MSG PASS seed=<n> len=<L> mode=<burst, gap or byte> sha=<s> hmac_en=<e> digest==golden`, one line per hash.
|CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY
|`INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` reads 1 in the full leg and 0 in the not-full leg, both before `hash_process` (`hmac.adoc` register text of `INTR_STATE`: the interrupt needs a FIFO that has been full). A wrong DUT raises it with no full FIFO before it, or never raises it. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback. Control for "0 when not full": the full leg of the same seed sets it, and the not-full leg writes fewer words than the logged depth at full (`CTRL-HMAC-FE-DEPTH`). Control that the not-full writes reached the message: `hash_process` after the check gives the golden digest of the written bytes (`CTRL-HMAC-FE-MSG`). Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY PASS seed=<n> leg=<full or notfull> full_before=<w> state=<s> t_drain=<d>`.
|CHK-HMAC-WIPE
|After a write of w to `WIPE_SECRET`, every DIGEST word of the digest size reads the big-endian read of the bytes of w (convention "HMAC digest order"), and a following HMAC hash with no `KEY` write returns the HMAC golden of the second message under a key whose words are all w (`hmac.hjson`, `WIPE_SECRET`: the written value overwrites the secret key and the digest; for SHA-384 and SHA-512 the 32-bit value is duplicated into each 64-bit word). The wipe writes w into every digest word, so a DIGEST word reads the big-endian read of the bytes of w (convention "HMAC digest order"). The second digest equals its golden in the big-endian read of each word (convention "HMAC digest order"), with the key words in the `key_swap` = 0 order, and the four different bytes of w make a wrong key byte order visible. A wrong DUT that ignores the write keeps the digest of the hash before it and hashes with the table key. Anchor: the DIGEST readback against w and against the software golden. Control: the DIGEST read before the wipe equals the golden of the first message in the big-endian read, and the expected wipe word differs from each of its words (`CTRL-HMAC-WIPE-PRE`; the draw keeps w out of both byte orders of that golden). Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-WIPE PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> klen=<k> w=0x<w> expect_word=0x<e> dswap0=<be or le> words=<m> digest_all_w=1 key_half_digest==golden_key_w=1`.
|CHK-HMAC-NARROW-WRITE
|A write of 8 or 16 bits at a non-zero byte lane of the `MSG_FIFO` window completes OKAY and adds its bytes to the message (`hmac.hjson`, `MSG_FIFO`, `byte-write: "true"`: any write to the window is appended to the FIFO; only address bits [1:0] matter to a write in the window). The golden takes the bytes in write order, each value in little-endian byte order (`endian_swap` = 0). A wrong DUT that refuses the write (SLVERR) or drops or moves its bytes returns a non-OKAY response or a digest that differs from the golden of the bytes in write order. Anchor: the B response of each write and the DIGEST readback against the `sep_hmac_golden` SHA-256 digest. Control: the cells of the same seed hash 32-bit and one-byte writes at byte lane 0 to their goldens (CHK-HMAC-MSG). Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-NARROW-WRITE PASS seed=<n> sha=256 len=10 lanes_widths=<l/w,...> writes=8 all_okay=1 refused_lane_width_resp=- digest_eq_golden=1 digest0=0x<d> golden0=0x<g>`.
|OBS-HMAC-WIPE-BUSY
|Not a graded check. No text states the effect of a `WIPE_SECRET` write while a keyed hash is in progress. The leaf logs the `STATUS` read before the write, whether `hmac_done` or `hmac_err` rises, whether the digest equals the golden of the key, whether every digest word equals `w2`, and DIGEST word 0. Evidence: `OBS-HMAC-WIPE-BUSY seed=<n> sha=<s> klen=<k> w2=0x<w> words_before=<a> words_total=<b> status_at_wipe=0x<s> idle_at_wipe=<0 or 1> done=<d> hmac_err=<e> digest==golden_key=<g> digest_all_w2=<a> digest0=0x<w>`.
|CHK-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST
|With the graded window closed, `INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` reads 0 right before a write of 1 to `INTR_TEST.fifo_empty`, and the first read one clock after the write reads 1 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc:74`: "Write 1 to force !!INTR_STATE.fifo_empty to 1."). A DUT that ignores the `INTR_TEST` write fails. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback. Control: the read before the write shows 0 (`CTRL-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST state_pre_write=0`), so the 1 comes from the write. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST PASS seed=<n> state_pre_write=0 state_1clk=1`.
|OBS-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST
|Not a graded check. No text states whether `INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` stays set after an `INTR_TEST` write while the FIFO is empty, or whether a write-one clear acts on it. The leaf logs the bit of the notfull-leg read, the bit after 64 clocks and the bit after the write-one clear. Evidence: `OBS-HMAC-FE-INTRTEST state_before=<b> state_settle=<s> settle_clk=64 state_after_w1c=<c>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: `STATUS.hmac_idle` is logged (`OBS-HMAC-IDLE seed=<n> phase=<p> idle=<i>`) and not graded during or after a hash: the register text gives only its reset value 1 and the rule that context writes are legal while it is 1. The second round of HMAC mode, and a `fifo_empty` with `hash_process` or `hash_stop` already written, have no stimulus here, so the unmet conditions of the interrupt rule are not claimed. The open owner (`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`) keeps the digest cells of 1 to 16 words. The HMAC key and message order are claimed only through the digest golden. The `WIPE_SECRET` overwrite of the intermediate hash and of the message schedule has no read path other than the digest registers and is not claimed apart from the digest. The index of the held beat after `fifo_full` reads 1 is logged and not graded (CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE is blocked). The narrow-write leg grades one fixed message; it does not walk every lane and width with every digest size.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test]]
===== 1.29 `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (HMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `reseed = 4`; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback and write of the HMAC registers (`CFG`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `DIGEST`, `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER`, `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`, `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `MSG_FIFO`), with write strobes of 4, 2 and 1 bytes (approved); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`, source 18 on bit 17 (approved); the gated-reset probe of HMAC (approved); `sep_hmac_golden`, a software SHA-2 model; DIGEST is compared with the big-endian read of each word at `digest_swap` = 0 and the little-endian read at 1 (convention "HMAC digest order"). No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the digest size order, the order of the two `digest_swap` values for each `endian_swap` value, the message length (4 to 200 bytes in the swap cells; in the context trips the whole blocks of each part and a final part of 0 to one block) and the message bytes (distinct per message), the write widths of each message (32, 16 and 8 bit; at least one multi-byte write), the part count and the block count of each part (at least one block in each part before the last), the interleave choice (at least one interleaved trip per digest size on every seed, so every seed needs the restore) and the unrelated message, and which `hmac_done` events are graded. Constraint filters on the software golden: the message of each digest size gives, for both `endian_swap` values, a digest with a non-zero word and a word that differs from its 32-bit byte swap; across the 12 cells every write width appears. The first completion event of the seed is always graded. `reseed = 4` samples different lengths, splits and widths. The plan shape (12 swap cells, 15 context trips, one `sha_en` clear per digest size on every seed) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: With `endian_swap` = 1 each multi-byte value written to `MSG_FIFO` joins the message in big-endian byte order, and a one-byte write is not affected. At `digest_swap` = 0 each DIGEST word is the big-endian read of its four digest bytes, with `digest_swap` = 1 the bytes of each word are reversed, and the register order does not change (convention "HMAC digest order"). `hash_stop` sets `hmac_done` after the current block. DIGEST and MSG_LENGTH are written while the engine is idle and `sha_en` = 0. `hash_continue` hashes on from the restored digest and length. The context switch follows the OpenTitan HMAC programmer's guide, which `doc/programmer/src/sep-programming.adoc` (HMAC) names. Clearing `sha_en` clears the digest. A completed hash sets the `hmac_done` state bit, and with `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set vector bit 17 (source 18) rises. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc` (`CFG`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `DIGEST`, `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER`, `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`, `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (`CMD.hash_stop`, the context to save).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`, which pins one byte-order convention and never uses `hash_stop`, `hash_continue` or a context restore. The done-line check grades a real completion event; the enable gate of the line belongs to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`. This test grades the swap, context and `sha_en` cells and the rise of the done line. The open owners keep the rest. Cells: the four (`endian_swap`, `digest_swap`) cells for each digest size, with a mix of 32-bit, 16-bit and 8-bit writes; the same message at `digest_swap` 0 and 1; plain SHA-2 context round trips of 2 to 4 parts for each digest size, split at block boundaries, with an optional unrelated hash between parts; the corners of a split after the last byte and a split at the first block boundary; the single-pass run of each trip as its control; the `sha_en` clear after the last swap cell of each digest size; the vector rise at the completion events with `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set. Functional coverage: `sep_hmac_cfg_op_cg` (`x_swap_cell`, `x_write_width`, `cp_context_round_trip`, `cp_ctx_write_legality`, `cp_sha_en_clear`) and `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` for `hmac_done`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`. Print `PLAN seed=<n> sha_order=<o> swap=[...] trips=[...] events=<e> graded_events=<g>` with the draws. Start a watch of vector bit 17 for the whole run. Write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E and log it. Reset HMAC: write `SW_RESET_N` with bit 3 cleared from the baseline, wait for the gated-reset probe of HMAC to show active, write the baseline back, and wait for the first OKAY readback of `STATUS` (each wait bounded; a timeout fails the leaf). Every `CFG` write sets `hmac_en` = 0, `sha_en` = 1 and `key_swap` = 0, except the writes with `sha_en` = 0 of the context switch (steps 10 to 12) and of the `sha_en` clear (step 17).
. Swap cells. For each digest size, draw a message with distinct bytes (4 to 200 bytes) and a write plan with a mix of 32-bit, 16-bit and 8-bit writes, with at least one multi-byte write. Across the 12 cells of the seed, every width appears with each `endian_swap` value.
. For each digest size and each `endian_swap` value, run the two `digest_swap` values in the drawn order. Per cell: reset HMAC, open the graded window, write `CFG`, write `CMD.hash_start`, write the message by the plan to `MSG_FIFO`, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-SWAP and ends the cell). Read the DIGEST words of the digest size (8, 12 or 16 words) and write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Build the golden of each cell. The message bytes follow the plan: a multi-byte write adds its bytes in little-endian order when `endian_swap` = 0 and in big-endian order when `endian_swap` = 1, and a one-byte write adds its byte as written. Hash the bytes with the software SHA-2 model. The readback equals the golden when every DIGEST word n is the big-endian read of digest bytes 4n to 4n+3 at `digest_swap` = 0 and the little-endian read at 1 (convention "HMAC digest order"; DIGEST_0 holds the first 4 bytes of the digest) (CHK-HMAC-SWAP).
. For each digest size and `endian_swap` value, compare every DIGEST word at `digest_swap` = 1 with the 32-bit byte swap of the word at `digest_swap` = 0 (CHK-HMAC-DSWAP-REL); the golden has a word that differs from its byte swap (`CTRL-HMAC-DSWAP-VISIBLE`). For each digest size, compare the four register sets of the four cells: they are four different sets (`CTRL-HMAC-SWAP-SETS`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`.
. Context trips, plain SHA-2 only, with `endian_swap` = 0 and `digest_swap` = 0. Run one trip for each digest size and each part count 2 to 4, and the corner trips: for each digest size one trip split after the last byte (an empty final part, with a drawn part count of 2 to 4), and one trip of 2 parts whose first part is one block. A block is 64 bytes for SHA-256 and 128 bytes for SHA-384 and SHA-512. Every part before the last is one to three whole blocks, and the last part is 1 byte to one block (0 bytes in the split after the last byte).
. Per trip, with the graded window closed, run the whole message in one pass first: reset HMAC, write `CFG`, `CMD.hash_start`, the message, `CMD.hash_process`, poll until `hmac_done` reads 1. Log t_single, the clocks from the `hash_start` write response to the first read that shows `hmac_done`. Read DIGEST, compare it with the software golden of the whole message in either byte order (`CTRL-HMAC-CTX-SINGLE`), and keep it as the single-pass digest. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Every trip has 2 or more parts. The single-pass digest of step 7 is the control of CHK-HMAC-CTX; a single-pass digest alone is graded by `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test` and not here. Each wait of the trip is bounded by 3 times t_single; a timeout fails the check that owns the wait and ends the trip with an HMAC reset.
. Open the graded window. Write `CFG` and `CMD.hash_start`. Write part 1 (whole blocks) to `MSG_FIFO`. Read `INTR_STATE`: `hmac_done` reads 0 (`CTRL-HMAC-STOP-PRE`). Write `CMD.hash_stop`. Poll until `hmac_done` reads 1 (CHK-HMAC-STOP), write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`, then poll `STATUS` until `hmac_idle` reads 1 (CHK-HMAC-STOP).
. Read DIGEST_0 to DIGEST_15 for every digest size, and `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER` and `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`. Keep them as the saved context. `hmac.hjson` (`CMD.hash_stop`) names DIGEST_0 to DIGEST_15 and the message length as the context to save, and the SHA-384 state has 16 words although its digest has 12. Write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 0 (the other fields unchanged), which clears the digest.
. If the trip interleaves, run an unrelated plain SHA-2 hash of a drawn message (1 to 128 bytes) with the same `CFG` and the graded window closed: `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1, `CMD.hash_start`, the message, `CMD.hash_process`, poll until `hmac_done` reads 1, write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Read DIGEST. It equals the software golden of the unrelated message in either byte order (`CTRL-HMAC-CTX-UNRELATED`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. The leaf logs whether it differs from the saved digest as `INFO-HMAC-CTX-CHANGED` (not graded). Write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 0 and open the graded window again.
. Poll `STATUS` until `hmac_idle` reads 1. Read `CFG`: `sha_en` reads 0 (`CTRL-HMAC-CTX-RESTORE-PRE`). Write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 0, the 16 saved DIGEST words, the saved `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER` and `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`, then `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1. The engine is idle, so the writes are legal.
. Write `CMD.hash_continue`. For a part that is not the last, write the part to `MSG_FIFO`, then `CMD.hash_stop`, wait for `hmac_done` and `hmac_idle` as in step 9, and repeat steps 10 to 12. For the last part, write the part to `MSG_FIFO`, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll until `hmac_done` reads 1. An empty last part writes no data.
. Read DIGEST. Compare it word for word with the single-pass digest (DUT against DUT), and with the software golden of the whole message in the big-endian read at `digest_swap` = 0 (CHK-HMAC-CTX). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Done-line events. A completion event is the end of each swap-cell hash, single pass, `hash_stop`, unrelated hash and final `hash_process`, in run order. The leaf grades the first event of the seed and each later event with a drawn yes or no. At a graded event, write 1 to `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done`, wait 2 clocks and sample vector bit 17 before the command that ends the hash (`hash_process` or `hash_stop`). After `hmac_done` reads 1, read `INTR_STATE` (`hmac_done` reads 1) and wait for a rise of vector bit 17 after the command (a bounded wait of 4 times the clocks from the command to the done read; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-DONE-LINE).
. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`; this clear replaces the clear of the step that ran the event. Read `INTR_STATE` and log the state and vector bit 17 as `OBS-HMAC-DONE-FALL`. Write 0 to `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done`.
. `sha_en` clear, once per digest size, after the last swap cell of the digest size. Read DIGEST: it equals the readback of that cell, which CHK-HMAC-SWAP graded, and has a non-zero word (`CTRL-HMAC-SHAOFF-BEFORE`). Write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 0 and the other fields unchanged. Read DIGEST until every word of the digest size reads 0 (a bounded wait of 4 times the hash time of that cell; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-SHAOFF). Write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1.
. Log the DIGEST byte order of the seed as `OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-ORDER seed=<n> dswap0=<be or le> dswap1=<be or le>`. Print `RESULT sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HMAC-SWAP
|For each swap cell and digest size the digest equals the software golden built with the message byte order that `endian_swap` selects, in the in-word byte order of its `digest_swap` value: the big-endian read at 0 and the little-endian read at 1 (convention "HMAC digest order"; `hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: `endian_swap`, `digest_swap`). A wrong DUT uses the wrong byte order for a multi-byte write, swaps a one-byte write, or uses the wrong in-word byte order for a `digest_swap` value. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: the four cells of a digest size give four different register sets (`CTRL-HMAC-SWAP-SETS`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-SWAP PASS seed=<n> endian=<e> dswap=<d> sha=<s> len=<L> w32=<a> w16=<b> w8=<c> digest_eq_golden=1 order=<be or le> order_of_seed=<be or le> got0=0x<g> be0=0x<b> bound=<b>`.
|CHK-HMAC-DSWAP-REL
|For the same message every DIGEST word at `digest_swap` = 1 equals the 32-bit byte swap of the word at `digest_swap` = 0, and the word order is the same (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: "does not affect the order of the digest output registers"). This is DUT against DUT and needs no byte order of `digest_swap` = 0. A wrong DUT reorders the words, swaps part of them, or ignores `digest_swap`. Anchor: the DIGEST readback of both runs. Control: the golden digest has a word that differs from its 32-bit byte swap (`CTRL-HMAC-DSWAP-VISIBLE`), because the leaf draws the message so that it has, so the relation is visible. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-DSWAP-REL PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> endian=<e> words=<n> rel=bswap32 mismatches=0 first_bad=-`.
|CHK-HMAC-CTX
|A message that is split, stopped, saved, restored and continued returns the digest of the single-pass run word for word, and that digest equals the software golden of the whole message in the big-endian read of each word (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CMD`: `hash_stop` and `hash_continue`; `hmac.hjson`, `CMD.hash_stop`: DIGEST_0 to DIGEST_15 and the message length are the context, and the trip saves and restores all 16 words). An unrelated hash between the parts does not change the result. A wrong DUT loses the saved digest or length, or continues from the wrong state. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: the single pass equals the golden (`CTRL-HMAC-CTX-SINGLE`); `sha_en` reads 0 before the restore (`CTRL-HMAC-CTX-RESTORE-PRE`); after the unrelated hash DIGEST equals the golden of the unrelated message (`CTRL-HMAC-CTX-UNRELATED`), so the restore, and not a leftover state, returns the right state; whether that digest differs from the saved digest is logged as `INFO-HMAC-CTX-CHANGED`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-CTX PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> kind=<parts, split_last or first_one_block> parts=<k> split=<b1,b2,...> interleave=<0 or 1> len=<L> digest_eq_single_pass=1 digest_eq_golden=1 order=be order_of_seed=be first_bad=- got0=0x<g> single0=0x<s> be0=0x<b>`.
|CHK-HMAC-STOP
|After `hash_stop` the engine sets `hmac_done` (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CMD`). A wrong DUT never sets `hmac_done`. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback. Control: `hmac_done` reads 0 just before the `hash_stop` write (`CTRL-HMAC-STOP-PRE`). `STATUS.hmac_idle` right after `hmac_done` is logged, not graded (`OBS-HMAC-STOP-IDLE`): the register text does not state it. The context save waits for `hmac_idle` within the bound (`CTRL-HMAC-STOP-IDLE-WAIT`). Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-STOP PASS seed=<n> tag=<t> part=<i> done_after_stop=1 bound=<b> t_single=<t>`.
|CHK-HMAC-SHAOFF
|Writing `sha_en` = 0 after a finished hash makes every DIGEST register of the digest size read 0 (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: "When the SHA-2 engine is disabled the digest is cleared"). A wrong DUT keeps the digest. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: the DIGEST read before the write equals the graded readback of the finished hash and has a non-zero word (`CTRL-HMAC-SHAOFF-BEFORE`). Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-SHAOFF PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> nonzero_before=1 digest_after=0 bound=<b> t_cell=<t>`.
|CHK-HMAC-DONE-LINE
|At a graded completion event with `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set, a finished hash sets the `hmac_done` state bit and vector bit 17 (source 18) rises after the command that ends the hash (`interrupts.adoc`, source 18 on bit 17). A wrong DUT sets the state bit and never raises the line, or raises it before the hash ends. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Control: vector bit 17 reads 0 before the command that ends the hash, so the rise comes from the event. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-DONE-LINE PASS seed=<n> event=<k> tag=<t> enable=1 state=1 vec17_before=0 vec17=1 bound=<b> t_done=<t>`; `OBS-HMAC-DONE-FALL seed=<n> event=<k> state_after_w1c=<s> vec17_after_w1c=<v>` is logged and not graded.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test does not write DIGEST or MSG_LENGTH while the engine is not idle, and does not stop an HMAC-mode hash, because the specification states no rule for either. The test grades the in-word byte order at each `digest_swap` value (convention "HMAC digest order") and the byte swap between the two values, and logs the order as `OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-ORDER`. It does not grade the MSG_LENGTH value, only its save and restore. The open owner (`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`) keeps the byte-order convention of its legal cells. The line check grades the rise at a completion event with the enable set. The enable clear, the enable flip and the write-one clear of the line belong to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, which grades them for every source of its table.

'''''
[[sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test]]
===== 1.30 `sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (Key delivery)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), `+esrc_noise_force`, the firmware image and plusargs of the open Key Manager sideload tests, an eFuse image with the PROD lifecycle state (the Key Manager reads OTP at boot), `reseed = 4`; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_km_mailbox_seq.py` (Key Manager key load, key transfer and engine shred); `seq_lib/sep_hmac_seq.py` and `env/sep_hmac_golden.py` (HMAC driver and golden); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. DIGEST word n is compared with the big-endian read of digest bytes 4n to 4n+3 (`digest_swap` = 0, convention "HMAC digest order"; `env/sep_hmac_golden.py`, `SepDigestOrder`). Probe: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved; vector bit 32 is the Crypto alert). Inject: none. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the table rows of the delivered HMAC key K_h and the delivered AES key K_a (K_h differs from K_a), the table row of the software decoy key K_d of HMAC (K_d differs from K_h), the table row of the software AES key K_s (K_s differs from K_a in every 32-bit word), the message (1 to 128 bytes; each whole word unique and non-zero), the AES key size and mode of the software-key leg, the order of the 11 mismatched HMAC cells, the digest size of the Key_None leg, and the AES `ALERT_TEST` field of the alert control (`recov_ctrl_update_err` or `fatal_fault`). The AES plaintext block is fixed. The seed picks table rows and never key bytes. `reseed = 4` samples other rows, messages, AES sizes and modes and cell orders. The 14 keyed HMAC cells, the Key_None leg, the AES legs and the two-engine leg run on every seed. The AES key size of the key-size leg (128 or 192 bits) is drawn after every other value.

*Objective*: HMAC uses the delivered key whenever `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` is set and has no select bit. The delivered 256-bit key sits at the most-significant end of the 1024-bit key field, and only `CFG.key_length` = 256 matches it. A supported `key_length` that disagrees with the delivered key gives a repeatable digest and no error; the engine takes key material from the top of the 1024-bit field, so `key_length` 128 takes the top 128 bits of the delivered key. An unset `key_length` (Key_None) with `hmac_en` = 1 raises a configuration error at start. AES ignores the delivered key while `CTRL_SHADOWED.sideload` = 0 and reports no error, and uses it while `sideload` = 1. Two engines that hold two different delivered keys each use their own key. After `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` of AES, a new message with `sideload` = 1 in automatic mode does not start: the shred clears `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, Crypto Engine Drivers: "Clear `key_valid`, then overwrite both shares"), and the unit "only starts encryption/decryption if the sideload key is marked as valid" (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/theory_of_operation.md`, System Key-Manager Interface). Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (Key Manager Key Delivery); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (`key_length` and `digest_size` codes).

*Test Coverage*: The 14 legal keyed HMAC cells (digest size x `key_length`; `key_length` 1024 is illegal on SHA2_256) run, and 13 are graded: 2 matched cells (`key_length` 256 on SHA2_384 and SHA2_512) and 11 mismatched cells. The matched SHA2_256 cell is a control. The Key_None start with `hmac_en` = 1 and a valid delivered key. AES with `sideload` = 0 and a software key while a delivered key is valid, with `sideload` = 1 as the control. Two delivered keys transferred back to back, then both engines run. An AES engine shred, then one `sideload` = 1 block in automatic mode with no valid delivered key. This entry extends `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test` (the matched length on SHA2_256 against a software decoy key) and `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test` (`sideload` = 1). Only the 13 keyed cells, the Key_None start, the `sideload` = 0 leg, the two-engine leg and the no-key leg are graded, and each open test keeps the cells that it owns: the SHA2_256 matched cell and the `sideload` = 1 run appear here as controls only. Functional coverage: `sep_hmac_cfg_op_cg`, `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg`, `sep_aes_cfg_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the TRNG, AES and ABR reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the OTBN and KMAC bits at 0, the HMAC bit at 0 until step 4 and the Key Manager bit at 0 until its boot in step 4. ABR stays released because the Key Manager boot writes ABR registers, and an access to a domain held in reset completes with SLVERR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing). Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed (K_h, K_a, K_d, K_s, message, AES size and mode, mismatched cell order, Key_None digest size, `ALERT_TEST` field) and log it (`INFO-PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Boot the Key Manager and load K_h and K_a into two key handles, with the firmware image and the command sequence of the open sideload tests. Then release the HMAC reset bit and wait for HMAC idle. Open the graded window.
. Transfer K_h to HMAC and then K_a to AES, back to back, with no engine access between the two transfers.
. Write K_d to the 32 HMAC `KEY` registers (non-zero, distinct words). Clear `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` and `hmac_done`. Write `CFG` with `hmac_en` = 1, `sha_en` = 1, SHA2_256, `key_length` = 256 and the byte-order bits of the open HMAC sideload test. Write `CMD.hash_start`, write the message to the message FIFO window, write `CMD.hash_process` and wait for `hmac_done` in a bounded wait (a timeout fails the check). Read DIGEST. This sequence is the HMAC run of the later steps. Keep this digest as H_two.
. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 of AES in a bounded wait. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 1, AES_256, ECB, automatic mode. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 again. Write the four DATA_IN words of one block and wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` in a bounded wait. Read the four DATA_OUT words. Keep them as A_two.
. For each of SHA2_384 and SHA2_512, run the HMAC run with `key_length` = 256 and read DIGEST. Then, with the graded window closed, run SHA2_256 with `key_length` = 256 (the control) and open the window again.
. For each of the 11 mismatched cells (SHA2_256 with `key_length` 128, 384 and 512; SHA2_384 and SHA2_512 each with `key_length` 128, 384, 512 and 1024), in the drawn order, do the HMAC run twice. Error injection: point `HMAC.CFG.key_length`; kind a legal length for another key size; expected response a repeatable digest and no `hmac_err`. After each run read `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` and DIGEST. For `key_length` 128 compare DIGEST with the HMAC golden of the top 128 bits of K_h. For the other lengths log the comparison of DIGEST with the matched digest of the same digest size from step 8 and with the decoy golden (`OBS-KEYSEL-MISMATCH`).
. Wait for `STATUS.hmac_idle` = 1 in a bounded wait. Write K_d, clear `INTR_STATE.hmac_err`, and write `CFG` with `hmac_en` = 1, `sha_en` = 1 (it stays 1, so the digest is not cleared), a drawn digest size, `key_length` = Key_None and the byte-order bits of the HMAC run. Then read DIGEST as d_prev, after the `CFG` write and before the start, so that d_prev and the DIGEST read after the start use the same `digest_size` (the register text does not state the readback after a change of `digest_size`). Write `CMD.hash_start`. Error injection: point `HMAC.CFG.key_length`; kind Key_None; expected response a configuration error and no digest. Poll `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` in a bounded wait, then read DIGEST. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_err`. Then run one HMAC run in which the shred lands in mid-operation, with the graded window closed: transfer K_h to HMAC again, write K_d to the `KEY` registers, write `CFG` with `hmac_en` = 1, SHA2_256 and `key_length` = 256, write `CMD.hash_start` and the first half of the message, send `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` for HMAC and read `STATUS`, write the rest of the message and `CMD.hash_process`, and read DIGEST. Log the return codes, the status, `hmac_err` and whether the digest equals the golden of K_h or of K_d (`OBS-KEYSEL-SHRED-MIDOP`).
. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 of AES. Sample vector bit 32 on every clock from this step to the end of step 12. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 0 and the drawn key size and mode (AES_ENC, automatic mode). The software key write of the next step lands only after this write, because a `sideload` = 1 value in the register drops software key writes.
. Write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of K_s and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. For CBC and CTR write the IV from the table and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of one block, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words. Read `STATUS` (bits `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` and `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT`).
. Control: write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 1, AES_256 and ECB, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write the same block and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Control: sample vector bit 32 on every clock from before the `ALERT_TEST` write is issued, write 1 to one drawn `ALERT_TEST` bit of AES, and keep sampling to the end of a bounded window after the write response. The specification does not order the pulse against the write response, so a high clock anywhere from the issue of the write to the end of the window counts (`ctrl_alert_cycles`). No high clock logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf.
. Key-size leg. With the delivered key K_a valid, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 1, the drawn AES key size (128 or 192 bits) and ECB, and run one block as in step 13. Compare the ciphertext with the ECB golden under the low `bits / 32` words of K_a (`CHK-KEYSEL-AES-KEYLEN`).
. No-key leg. Open the graded window. Send `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` for AES and keep its return code. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 1, AES_256, ECB, automatic mode (a new message, `theory_of_operation.md`, System Key-Manager Interface). Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Read `STATUS` as the base sample (`CTRL-KEYSEL-AES-NOKEY-BASE`: `IDLE` = 1, `INPUT_READY` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails). Write the four DATA_IN words of one block. Read `STATUS` back to back for a window of 20 x t_side clocks (a test parameter); t_side is the clocks from the last DATA_IN write to the `OUTPUT_VALID` sample of the `sideload` = 1 control of step 13. Close the graded window.
. Compare every value with its golden and log the checks, then log `RESULT sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=7`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MATCH
|With `key_length` = 256 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512 the DIGEST equals the HMAC golden of the delivered key K_h, although K_d fills the software `KEY` registers (`hmac.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: the delivered key replaces the software key whenever `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` is set, and `key_length` 256 matches it). A DUT that uses the software key gives the decoy digest and fails. Anchor: DIGEST readback against `env/sep_hmac_golden.py`. Controls: the SHA2_256 cell with `key_length` 256 equals the same golden; after `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` of HMAC a run with the same KEY writes equals the HMAC golden of K_d, so the decoy writes reach the software KEY (`CTRL-KEYSEL-DECOY`); otherwise the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MATCH PASS seed=<n> sha=<384\|512> keylen=256 digest==golden_kh=1 digest==golden_decoy=0` for each of the two sizes, and `CTRL-KEYSEL-256 seed=<n> sha=256 keylen=256 digest==golden_kh=1`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MISMATCH
|For each of the 11 mismatched cells the two runs give equal digests and `hmac_err` stays 0. For the three cells with `key_length` 128 (SHA2_256, SHA2_384 and SHA2_512) the digest equals the HMAC golden of the top 128 bits of K_h (`hmac.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: a supported length that disagrees gives a wrong but repeatable digest and no error, and the engine takes key material from the top of the 1024-bit field, where the delivered key is most-significant-bit justified; `crypto.adoc`, Key Manager Key Delivery table). The longer lengths mix the padding below the delivered key into the key, and the specification does not state the padding value, so their digest has no stated value: the leaf logs its comparison with the matched digest and with the decoy golden as `OBS-KEYSEL-MISMATCH` and grades no value. A DUT that uses the software key, or that ignores `CFG.key_length` and uses the full delivered key, fails the golden of a 128 cell; a DUT that raises an error sets `hmac_err`; a DUT that gives two different digests fails the repeat. Control: CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-NONE of the same seed observes `hmac_err` = 1 (`CTRL-KEYSEL-HMAC-MISMATCH none_leg_hmac_err=1`), so the bit can be seen to rise; every run reads `CFG` back equal to the written value with the cell's `key_length` (`CTRL-KEYSEL-CFG`); a leaf in which a control does not hold logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Anchor: DIGEST and `INTR_STATE` readback, and the golden of the top 128 bits of K_h; the `hmac_done` of each run is the positive evidence that the run completed. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MISMATCH PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> keylen=<k> err=0 run1==run2=1 digest==golden_kh128=<1 or na>` for each of the 11 cells, `na` for the longer lengths, and `OBS-KEYSEL-MISMATCH seed=<n> sha=<s> keylen=<k> eq_matched=<b> eq_decoy=<b>` for the longer lengths.
|CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-NONE
|With `hmac_en` = 1, Key_None and a valid delivered key, `CMD.hash_start` sets `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` and leaves DIGEST at d_prev (`hmac.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: an unset length raises a configuration error when hashing starts; `hmac.hjson` `key_length`: the start is blocked). A DUT that hashes with the delivered key shows no error and a new digest. The `CFG` write keeps `sha_en` = 1 and HMAC is idle before it, so the digest is not cleared and the start is blocked only by Key_None. `hmac_err` reads 0 after the Key_None `CFG` write and before the start, so only `CMD.hash_start` can set it. Anchor: `INTR_STATE` and DIGEST readback; d_prev is the DIGEST read after the `CFG` write and just before the start, under the same `CFG` as the read after the start. `ERR_CODE` reads `SwInvalidConfig` (0x6) after the error (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/doc/programmers_guide.md:128`). Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-NONE PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> err_before_start=0 err=1 err_code=0x6 digest_unchanged=1`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-AES-SW
|With `sideload` = 0 and a valid delivered key the ciphertext equals the golden of K_s. No pulse occurs on vector bit 32 during the leg, and `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` and `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` read 0 (`aes.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: the delivered key has no effect and no error is reported). K_s differs from K_a in every 32-bit word, so a DUT that takes any part of K_a gives a different ciphertext. For AES_256 the golden of K_s also differs from the golden of K_a (both come from fixed table rows, and the leaf checks the two goldens before the run); for a shorter key the part of K_a that the engine takes is not stated, so that comparison is logged as `na`. A DUT that uses the delivered key, or that signals an alert, fails. Anchor: DATA_OUT, `STATUS` and the vector probe. Controls: the `ALERT_TEST` write of the same seed pulses bit 32 (`ctrl_alert_cycles` above 0), and the `sideload` = 1 run equals the golden of K_a; either control missing logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-AES-SW PASS seed=<n> keylen=<k> mode=<m> ct==golden_ks=1 ct!=golden_ka=<1\|na> alert_cycles=0 status_err=0 ctrl_alert_cycles=<c> ctrl_sideload_ct==golden_ka=1`; the controls log `CTRL-KEYSEL-AES-SIDELOAD` and `CTRL-KEYSEL-ALERT-TEST seed=<n> alert_test_field=<f> ...`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-TWO
|After K_h is transferred to HMAC and K_a to AES back to back, both transfers return code 0, the HMAC digest equals the golden of K_h and the AES ciphertext (`sideload` = 1, AES_256) equals the golden of K_a (`crypto.adoc`, Key Manager Key Delivery). A DUT that sends one key to both engines, or that lets the second transfer overwrite the first, gives one wrong result. Anchor: DIGEST and DATA_OUT readback against the goldens (H_two and A_two). Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-TWO PASS seed=<n> rc_hmac=0 rc_aes=0 hmac==golden_kh=1 aes==golden_ka=1`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-AES-NOKEY
|After `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` of AES returns code 0, a new `CTRL_SHADOWED` pair with `sideload` = 1 in automatic mode and one written block, every `STATUS` read of a window of 20 x t_side clocks shows `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0 (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, Crypto Engine Drivers: "*Shred.* Clear `key_valid`, then overwrite both shares"; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`: the two shares and `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` drive the engine's `keymgr_key_i` sideload port; `crypto.adoc`, Key Manager Key Delivery: "while it is 0 the destination has no valid key or seed"; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/theory_of_operation.md`, System Key-Manager Interface: "In normal/automatic mode, the AES unit only starts encryption/decryption if the sideload key is marked as valid."). A DUT that starts on the shredded key, or that keeps the delivered key valid after the shred, sets `OUTPUT_VALID` inside the window. Anchor: `STATUS` readback and the shred return code. Controls: the `sideload` = 1 block of step 13 with a valid key equals the golden of K_a and gives t_side, so the same block with a valid key completes inside one twentieth of the window (`CTRL-KEYSEL-AES-NOKEY seed=<n> ctrl_t_side=<t>`); every sample after the `DATA_IN` writes reads `STATUS.INPUT_READY` = 0, so the writes landed and were not loaded (`aes.hjson`, `STATUS.INPUT_READY`; `CTRL-KEYSEL-AES-NOKEY-INPUT`); the base sample reads `IDLE` = 1, `INPUT_READY` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0 (`CTRL-KEYSEL-AES-NOKEY-BASE`), so the `INPUT_READY` = 0 of the samples follows the `DATA_IN` writes. Either control missing logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-AES-NOKEY PASS seed=<n> rc_shred=0 samples=<k> output_valid_samples=0 window_clk=<w> ctrl_t_side=<t>`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-AES-KEYLEN
|With `sideload` = 1 and a drawn AES key size of 128 or 192 bits, the ECB ciphertext of the fixed block equals the golden under the low 4 or 6 words of K_a. Delivered word N carries key bits [32N+31:32N] (`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:186-190`), and AES-128 and AES-192 use `KEY_SHARE0_0` to `_3` and `_0` to `_5` with the same words of `KEY_SHARE1` (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/programmers_guide.md:31-32`). A DUT that takes the high words or the whole 256-bit key gives another ciphertext and fails. The leaf requires the golden to differ from the AES-256 golden of K_a, so a check that cannot tell the two cannot pass. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against the golden of the low words. Control: the AES-256 `sideload` = 1 run of the preceding steps equals the golden of K_a (`CTRL-KEYSEL-AES-SIDELOAD`). Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-AES-KEYLEN PASS seed=<n> keylen=<128 or 192> ct==golden_low_words=1 ct==golden_256=0 timed_out=0`.
|OBS-KEYSEL-SHRED-MIDOP
|Not a graded check. No text states which key an HMAC run uses when `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` lands between `hash_start` and `hash_process`. The leaf logs both return codes, `STATUS` after the shred, `hmac_err`, the split of the message and whether the digest equals the golden of K_h or the golden of the software key K_d. Evidence: `OBS-KEYSEL-SHRED-MIDOP seed=<n> rc_transfer=<c> rc_shred=<c> status_busy_at_rc=<b> hmac_err=<e> split_bytes=<n> digest==golden_kh=<0 or 1> digest==golden_decoy=<0 or 1>`; a timeout adds `reason=timeout:<what> bound=<b>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the digest of a mismatched length above 128 bits: `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications) calls it "wrong but repeatable" but does not state the padding below the delivered key, so its compare with the matched digest is logged only (`OBS-KEYSEL-MISMATCH`). It grades the part of a delivered key that a 128-bit or 192-bit AES key takes with `sideload` = 1 only (`CHK-KEYSEL-AES-KEYLEN`). It does not grade the behaviour when the delivered-key valid qualifier drops during an operation (`OBS-KEYSEL-SHRED-MIDOP` logs one HMAC run), the AES outcome with no valid key in manual mode, or the delivered-key outcome of KMAC, OTBN and ABR. `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test` owns the matched length on SHA2_256 and `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test` owns `sideload` = 1; this entry reads those two cells as controls and the open tests keep them.

'''''

[[sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test]]
===== 1.31 `sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probes: none beyond CPU-LSU readback of `STATUS`, `DATA_OUT` and `CTRL_SHADOWED` (approved). Inject: none beyond the invalid register writes of the procedure. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the key table row and the plaintext P (drawn again until the AES-256 and AES-128 goldens of P differ and the encryption of C = E_K(P) differs from P), the multi-hot value of each field (KEY_LEN from 3'b011, 3'b101, 3'b110 and 3'b111; OPERATION 2'b11; MODE a value with two to five bits set, never 6'b11_1111 because that is the code of AES_NONE itself) and the order of the six cells. `reseed = 4` samples other keys, plaintexts, multi-hot values and cell orders. The six cells {KEY_LEN, OPERATION, MODE} x {all-zero, multi-hot} run on every seed.

*Objective*: An invalid one-hot value written to `CTRL_SHADOWED` maps to a safe value: KEY_LEN (several bits, or 3'b000) to AES_256, OPERATION (several bits, or 2'b00) to AES_ENC, MODE (several bits, or 6'b00_0000) to AES_NONE, and `CTRL_SHADOWED` reads the mapped OPERATION and MODE. A `TRIGGER.START` does not start the unit when MODE is AES_NONE. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `TRIGGER`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (the codes of `KEY_LEN`, `OPERATION` and `MODE`).

*Test Coverage*: Invalid values of KEY_LEN, OPERATION and MODE, each in an all-zero cell and a multi-hot cell, and each against a legal neighbour cell in the same trial. The suppressed start with MODE = AES_NONE in manual mode. This entry extends `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`, which writes legal encodings only: only the invalid-encoding cells are graded, and the open test keeps every legal cell. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg`, `sep_aes_op_status_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES reset bit of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the HMAC, KMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed (key K as two shares from the table, plaintext P, cell order, multi-hot values) and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Pulse the AES reset bit (0 then 1) and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Calibration: write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC and `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1. Write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of K and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of P and write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait. Log the clocks from the START write response to that sample as t_blk. Read the four DATA_OUT words. Compare the block with the golden; a mismatch logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. The golden gives C = E_K(P) for the OPERATION cells.
. For each of the six cells in the drawn order, start a trial: pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Steps 7 to 16 give the actions of a trial for its cell type (KEY_LEN, OPERATION or MODE). The invalid-value steps of a cell run with the graded coverage window open; the control of the cell runs with the window closed. Every key load in these steps follows the order: write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice, write the key words, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR`, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. `CTRL_SHADOWED` is read only after both writes of a pair: a read clears the phase tracking (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`).
. KEY_LEN cell. Error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.KEY_LEN`; kind 3'b000 or one drawn multi-hot value; expected response the AES-256 behaviour. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with that KEY_LEN, ECB, AES_ENC and automatic mode (both writes answer OKAY). Write the eight key words of K, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Read `CTRL_SHADOWED` once and log the field (`OBS-AES-ENC-RB`).
. KEY_LEN cell. Write the four DATA_IN words of P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. KEY_LEN control. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with KEY_LEN = AES_128 and the other fields as before. Write the first four key words of K (the 128-bit key of the AES-128 golden), wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. OPERATION cell, legal pair. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with OPERATION = AES_DEC, ECB, AES_256 and automatic mode, then read `CTRL_SHADOWED` once. The readback must show the written OPERATION, MODE, KEY_LEN and `MANUAL_OPERATION`, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING AES-INV-OP-PRE` and fails. AES_DEC differs from the reset value and from the mapped value AES_ENC. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. OPERATION cell. Error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.OPERATION`; kind 2'b00 or 2'b11; expected response the AES_ENC behaviour. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with that OPERATION, ECB, AES_256 and automatic mode. Write the eight key words of K, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Read `CTRL_SHADOWED` once. Write the four DATA_IN words of C, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. OPERATION control. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with OPERATION = AES_DEC, then write the eight key words of K (a decryption needs the key written after the control register, so that the decryption key is generated), wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write C, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. MODE cell, legal pair. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with MODE = ECB, AES_128, AES_ENC and `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0, then read `CTRL_SHADOWED` once. The readback must show the written OPERATION, MODE, KEY_LEN and `MANUAL_OPERATION`, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING AES-INV-MODE-PRE` and fails. ECB differs from the reset value and from the mapped value AES_NONE. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. MODE cell. Error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.MODE`; kind 6'b00_0000 or one drawn multi-hot value; expected response AES_NONE, which does not start. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with that MODE, AES_256, AES_ENC and `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1. Write the eight key words of K, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Read `CTRL_SHADOWED` once. Read the four DATA_OUT words as the base words.
. MODE cell. Write the four DATA_IN words of P and write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back for a window of 20 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter). Then read the four DATA_OUT words.
. MODE control. Do a key load in the order of step 6 with MODE = ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1 and the eight key words of K (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`: a Control Register write starts a new message, and software gives a new key afterwards). Then write the four DATA_IN words of P and write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Log the clocks as `ctrl_valid_clk`, then read the four DATA_OUT words and log their compare with the AES-256 golden as `OBS-AES-INV-MODE-CTRL-CT` (not graded).
