. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks and log `RESULT sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=5`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-INV-KEYLEN
|After an invalid KEY_LEN (3'b000 or several bits) the ciphertext of P equals the AES-256 golden of K and differs from the AES-128 golden (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.KEY_LEN`: invalid values map to AES_256). The two goldens differ: the leaf computes both before the run and draws the plaintext again if they are equal, so the inequality follows from the equality with the AES-256 golden. A DUT that maps the value to AES_128 or AES_192 gives the wrong ciphertext. Anchor: DATA_OUT against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Control: KEY_LEN = AES_128 in the same trial gives the AES-128 ciphertext, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-INV-KEYLEN PASS seed=<n> cell=keylen.<zero or multi> written=0b<v> ct==golden256=1 ct!=golden128=1 ctrl_ct==golden128=1`.
|CHK-AES-INV-OP
|After an invalid OPERATION (2'b00 or 2'b11) the result for the input C is the encryption of C, not its decryption, which is P (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.OPERATION`: invalid values map to AES_ENC). The encryption of C differs from P: the leaf computes both goldens before the run and draws the plaintext again if they are equal. A DUT that maps the value to AES_DEC returns P. Anchor: DATA_OUT against the encrypt and decrypt goldens. Control: OPERATION = AES_DEC in the same trial returns P, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-INV-OP PASS seed=<n> cell=op.<zero or multi> written=0b<v> result==enc_golden=1 result!=dec_golden=1 ctrl_result==dec_golden=1`.
|CHK-AES-INV-OP-RB
|After the invalid OPERATION pair, `CTRL_SHADOWED` reads OPERATION = AES_ENC and MODE, KEY_LEN and `MANUAL_OPERATION` as written (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED.OPERATION`: invalid values, 2'b00 and several bits set, map to AES_ENC). The legal AES_DEC pair before it reads back as written (`CTRL-AES-INV-OP-PRE`), so a DUT that drops the invalid pair reads AES_DEC and fails, and a DUT that stores the raw value reads it and fails. The check also requires the OPERATION of the word written to the bus to be an invalid code (not one-hot), so a trial that writes a legal code fails. Anchor: `CTRL_SHADOWED` readback over the CPU-LSU path. Evidence: `CTRL-AES-INV-OP-PRE seen: seed=<n> cell=op.<zero or multi> key_len=0x4 op=0x2 mode=0x1 manual=0x0` and `CHK-AES-INV-OP-RB PASS: seed=<n> cell=op.<zero or multi> written=0b<v> written_invalid=1 key_len=0x4 op=0x1 mode=0x1 manual=0x0`.
|CHK-AES-INV-MODE
|With an invalid MODE in manual mode a `TRIGGER.START` leaves `OUTPUT_VALID` at 0 at every sample of a window of 20 clean block times, and DATA_OUT keeps its base words (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.MODE`: invalid values map to AES_NONE; `TRIGGER.START` is cleared if MODE = AES_NONE). A DUT that maps the value to a real mode starts, sets `OUTPUT_VALID` and changes DATA_OUT. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: MODE = ECB in the same trial, with a new key load, the same input and a new START, sets `OUTPUT_VALID` in a bounded wait, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; this shows that the input and the START path work. CHK-AES-INV-MODE-RB grades the readback of the invalid MODE. Evidence: `CHK-AES-INV-MODE PASS seed=<n> cell=mode.<zero or multi> written=0b<v> output_valid=0 data_out_unchanged=1 window_clk=<w> samples=<s> ctrl_valid_clk=<t>`.
|CHK-AES-INV-MODE-RB
|After the invalid MODE pair, `CTRL_SHADOWED` reads MODE = AES_NONE (6'b11_1111), KEY_LEN = AES_256, `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1 and OPERATION = AES_ENC (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED.MODE`: invalid values, 6'b00_0000 and several bits set, map to AES_NONE; KEY_LEN and `MANUAL_OPERATION` are the written legal values). The legal pair before it (ECB, AES_128, `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0) reads back as written (`CTRL-AES-INV-MODE-PRE`), so a DUT that drops the invalid pair reads ECB, AES_128 and 0 and fails, and a DUT that stores the raw value reads it and fails. The check also requires the MODE of the word written to the bus to be an invalid code (not one-hot and not AES_NONE). The register stores AES_NONE for a written AES_NONE and for a mapped invalid value, so this part grades the stimulus: a trial that writes the legal AES_NONE code fails here. Anchor: `CTRL_SHADOWED` readback over the CPU-LSU path. Evidence: `CTRL-AES-INV-MODE-PRE seen: seed=<n> cell=mode.<zero or multi> key_len=0x1 op=0x1 mode=0x1 manual=0x0` and `CHK-AES-INV-MODE-RB PASS: seed=<n> cell=mode.<zero or multi> written=0b<v> written_invalid=1 key_len=0x4 op=0x1 mode=0x3f manual=0x1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the readback of an invalid KEY_LEN: the KEY_LEN cell grades the mapping by its ciphertext and logs the readback as an open question (`OBS-AES-ENC-RB seed=<n> field=keylen written=0x<w> read=0x<r>`). It does not grade the invalid PRNG_RESEED_RATE values (owned by `sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test`), the AES_192 and GCM encodings, or any legal cell. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` owns the legal cells and keeps them.

'''''

[[sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test]]
===== 1.32 `sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, HMAC out of reset and idle, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and streaming golden); `seq_lib/sep_hmac_seq.py` (the HMAC control operation); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved; all 43 vector bits). Inject: none. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the three mode streams (ECB, CBC and CTR, all three on every seed), and for each stream the key size, the operation (encrypt or decrypt for ECB and CBC; encrypt for CTR), the block count N (34 to 100), the input gap and the output gap of every block (0 to 40 clocks each), the indexes of the stall pairs and of the control pairs (1 to 2 of each per stream; each index i is drawn from 1 to N - 2, and the pairs {i, i + 1} do not overlap, so no block belongs to two pairs and block 0 belongs to none), the stall delay G of each pair (2 to 20 times t_blk), and the wait of the manual leg (2 to 8 times t_blk), and, after every other draw, the three blocks of the `INPUT_READY` leg. The key, IV and CTR start counter come from the table; the CTR start counter keeps its low 32 bits at or below 2^32 minus N, so the counter does not wrap. `reseed = 4` samples other mode orders, sizes, counts, gaps, indexes and delays. The busy-write leg draws last: the share-0 key word and the non-zero mask of the changed key, the new IV, the four blocks of each case and the missing IV word (0 to 3) of the partial-IV case. The leaf draws the mask again when a graded block gives the same ciphertext under both keys, and the new IV again when it equals a chain value, so the old and new goldens differ by construction. The legs (the three mode streams, stall pairs with control pairs, automatic START, manual overwrite with its control, IDLE samples, busy-write cases, no-interrupt check) run on every seed.

*Objective*: In automatic mode (`MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0) the unit starts when new input arrives and stalls in the last cycle while the previous output is unread (`STATUS.STALL` = 1, `IDLE` = 0). A `TRIGGER.START` write in automatic mode does not start the unit. In manual mode the unit starts only on `TRIGGER.START` and overwrites unread output, which sets `STATUS.OUTPUT_LOST` until the Control Register is rewritten. `IDLE` is 0 while a block runs. `STATUS.INPUT_READY` is 0 while the present DATA_IN values are not loaded into the module and becomes 1 when the unit loads them. AES has no interrupt. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `TRIGGER`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/theory_of_operation.md` (stall: "It only continues once the previous output data has been read"); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/programmers_guide.md` (Block Operation); `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (OCAH Modifications: AES exports `idle_o` and no interrupt); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (PIC source map).

*Test Coverage*: An ECB, a CBC and a CTR stream on every seed, each of 34 to 100 blocks with drawn input and output gaps. Stall pairs and control pairs inside each stream. A START write in automatic mode with no input. A block written to DATA_IN in manual mode and held there before `TRIGGER.START`. A manual-mode overwrite of unread output, and the same sequence with the output read between the blocks. `IDLE` samples over block runs. A block written while the unit is stalled behind unread output, for `INPUT_READY`. A whole changed key (ECB) and a whole new IV (CBC) written while the unit is stalled behind unread output, each with a twin written at idle, and three of the four IV words plus the four DATA_IN words written at idle (CBC). The interrupt vector over the whole leaf, with an HMAC operation as the control. This entry extends `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`, which runs 2 blocks per cell in automatic mode and reads `STATUS` for alert bits only: only the cells above are graded, and the open test keeps its cells. Functional coverage: `sep_aes_op_status_cg`, `sep_crypto_intr_cg`. `cp_busy_write` fills in the busy-write cases only: the leaf writes the pass label `aes_busy_write_pass` after the check of a case passes (`CHK-AES-BUSY-KEY`, `CHK-AES-BUSY-IV`, `CHK-AES-PARTIAL-IV`), holds it two clocks and writes 0, and a FAIL closes the graded window, so no later case samples. The other owned bins of `sep_aes_op_status_cg` (`x_mode_status`, `cp_lost_clear`, `cp_nonidle_cause`, the automatic and manual-legal bins of `cp_start_suppress`, `cp_input_ready_hold`) are held by the sampler at their DUT event, also across a window close and an AES reset, and sample only when the leaf writes the `aes_stream_pass` bit of the check that grades them: after `CHK-AES-STALL` of each stream, `CHK-AES-START-AUTO`, `CHK-AES-LOST` with `CHK-AES-START-MANUAL`, `CHK-AES-INPUT-READY` and `CHK-AES-IDLE` pass, and only while no check or control has failed. The leaf opens the window for the label with AES idle and makes no access in it.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES and HMAC reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the KMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed and log it (`INFO-PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Read the HMAC `STATUS` and require `hmac_idle` = 1 (`CTRL-AES-NOIRQ-HMAC-IDLE`; another value logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Write HMAC `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` = 1.
. Write 0 to `INTR_ENABLE` of ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Read all 43 vector bits of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` and log them as S0. Sample these bits on every clock from this step until the sampling stops in step 30. Vector bits 36 and 37 (source 37, entropy pool low, and source 38, entropy pool fill stall) are level sources with no enable that follow the pool occupancy and the EDN fill, which still change after the entropy bring-up, so the leaf logs their changes (`OBS-POOL-VEC`) and excludes them from the compare.
. Steps 6 to 13 run once for each of the three mode streams, in the drawn order, with the draws of that stream. Pulse the AES reset bit (0 then 1) and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0 and the mode of the stream, key size and operation. Write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of the drawn key and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. For CBC and CTR write the IV and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Block 0: write the four DATA_IN words with no input gap. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, and keep every sample. Read the four DATA_OUT words at once. Log the clocks from the last DATA_IN write response to the sample with `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 as t_blk. In the first stream, the first read of the four DATA_OUT words of a block is made word by word, with a `STATUS` read after each word (`OBS-AES-OV-PARTIAL`).
. For every other block that is not part of a stall pair or a control pair, in order: wait the drawn input gap, write the four DATA_IN words, poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait (keep every sample), wait the drawn output gap and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Stall pair at each drawn index j. After block j sets `OUTPUT_VALID`, do not read it. Write the four DATA_IN words of block j + 1, wait G clocks and read `STATUS`. Keep the `STALL` and `IDLE` bits of that sample.
. Stall pair. Read the four DATA_OUT words of block j. Poll `STATUS` until `STALL` = 0 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read the four DATA_OUT words of block j + 1.
. Control pair at each drawn index c. After block c sets `OUTPUT_VALID`, read its four DATA_OUT words at once. Write the four DATA_IN words of block c + 1, wait the drawn delay G of the control pair and read `STATUS`. Keep the `STALL` bit. Poll until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words of block c + 1.
. Compare the N outputs of the stream with the streaming golden (CBC chains and CTR advances by one per block over all N blocks).
. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0 and ECB. Write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Read `STATUS`. Require `IDLE` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0 as the base sample (`CTRL-AES-START-AUTO-BASE`; another value logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf).
. Write `TRIGGER.START` = 1 with no DATA_IN word written. Poll `STATUS` back to back for a window of 20 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter) and keep every sample.
. Control: write the four DATA_IN words of one block. Poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 0 in a bounded wait, then until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Read the four DATA_OUT words. Keep the clocks from the last DATA_IN write response to the sample with `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 as the automatic start time (the control of CHK-AES-START-MANUAL).
. Control: poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf).
. Manual leg. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1 and ECB. Write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Manual leg. Write the four DATA_IN words of block 1. Read `STATUS` back to back for a window of 20 x t_blk clocks and keep every sample (CHK-AES-START-MANUAL). Write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, and keep every sample.
. Manual leg. Without reading DATA_OUT, write the four DATA_IN words of block 2 and write `TRIGGER.START`. Wait the drawn multiple of t_blk, then poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Manual leg. Read `STATUS` and keep `OUTPUT_LOST`. Read the four DATA_OUT words and keep them. Read `STATUS` again and keep `OUTPUT_LOST` (the value after the full DATA_OUT read).
. Manual leg. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with the same value as before. Read `STATUS` and keep `OUTPUT_LOST`.
. Control trial: repeat steps 18 and 19 without the 20 x t_blk window. Read all four DATA_OUT words of block 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of block 2 and write `TRIGGER.START`, wait the same time and poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read `STATUS` and keep `OUTPUT_LOST`. Read the four DATA_OUT words and keep them.
. `INPUT_READY` leg. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0, ECB and AES_256. Write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Open the graded window. Write the four DATA_IN words of block 0 and poll `STATUS` until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. `INPUT_READY` leg. Without reading DATA_OUT, write the four DATA_IN words of block 1 and poll `STATUS` until `STALL` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read `STATUS` (the control sample). Write the four DATA_IN words of block 2. Read `STATUS` back to back for a window of 8 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter) and keep every sample.
. `INPUT_READY` leg. Read the four DATA_OUT words of block 0. Poll `STATUS` until `INPUT_READY` = 1 in a bounded wait of the same 8 x t_blk clocks, and log the clocks from the last DATA_OUT read to that sample as t_load. Read the four DATA_OUT words of block 1. Poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words of block 2. Close the graded window.
. Busy key leg. Pulse the AES reset bit, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0, ECB and AES_256, and write the key (the set-up runs with the graded window closed). Write block 0 and wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1, write block 1 and wait for `STALL` = 1, all in bounded waits. Write the pass label 0, open the graded window and wait one clock. Read `STATUS`, write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of the changed key, read `STATUS`. Read output 0, wait until `STALL` = 0, read output 1 and run block 2. Then, at `IDLE` = 1, write the same changed key again, wait for `IDLE` = 1 and run block 3 (the twin). Grade `CHK-AES-BUSY-KEY` and write the pass label.
. Busy IV leg. The same sequence in CBC with all four IV words written while the unit is stalled and again at idle (`CHK-AES-BUSY-IV`).
. Partial IV leg. Pulse the AES reset bit, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` for CBC and AES_256 and the key, and wait for `IDLE` = 1. Open the graded window. Write three of the four IV words, the missing word being the drawn one, and the four DATA_IN words. Read `STATUS` back to back for a window of 20 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter) and keep every sample. Write the missing IV word, poll `STATUS` until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words (`CHK-AES-PARTIAL-IV`).
. Stop the vector sampling. Control: write HMAC `CFG` with `hmac_en` = 0, `sha_en` = 1 and SHA2_256, write `CMD.hash_start`, write the message for one hash to the message FIFO window and write `CMD.hash_process`. Wait for vector bit 17 to rise in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks and log `RESULT sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=11`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-STREAM
|Every block of the stream equals the streaming golden: CBC chains and CTR advances across all N blocks, with the drawn gaps (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.MANUAL_OPERATION`: in automatic mode the unit starts on new input). A DUT that drops, repeats or reorders a block, or that loses the chain across a stall, gives a mismatch. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against the streaming golden of `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Evidence: `CHK-AES-STREAM PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> keylen=<k> op=<o> blocks=<N> mismatches=0 max_in_gap=<a> max_out_gap=<b>`.
|CHK-AES-STALL
|At each stall pair, G clocks after the next input is written while the previous output is unread, `STATUS.STALL` is 1 and `IDLE` is 0. After the previous output is read, `STALL` falls and the next block equals its golden (`aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS.STALL` and `STATUS.IDLE`: the unit is not idle while it is stalled). A DUT that overwrites the unread output without a stall, or that reports `IDLE` while stalled, fails. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: at each control pair the previous output is read first, and the sample at the same G reads `STALL` = 0; a stall that shows in both pairs would be a stuck bit. Evidence: `CHK-AES-STALL PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> block=<j> gap_clk=<G> stall=1 idle=0 then_stall=0 ct==golden=1 ctrl_stall=0`.
|CHK-AES-START-AUTO
|In automatic mode a `TRIGGER.START` write with no input leaves `IDLE` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0 at every sample of a window of 20 clean block times (`aes.adoc` register text, `TRIGGER.START`: the trigger is cleared if `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0). A DUT that starts on the trigger shows `IDLE` = 0 or `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1. The base sample before the write shows `IDLE` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0, so the window starts from a unit that is able to show a change. Anchor: `STATUS` readback. Control: four DATA_IN words in the same seed start a block (`IDLE` falls) and the block completes with the golden ciphertext, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-START-AUTO PASS seed=<n> idle_samples=<k> idle=1 output_valid=0 window_clk=<w> ctrl_data_in_idle=0`.
|CHK-AES-START-MANUAL
|In manual mode, with block 1 written to DATA_IN and no `TRIGGER.START` write, every `STATUS` sample of a window of 20 clean block times shows `IDLE` = 1, `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0 and `INPUT_READY` = 0, and after the `TRIGGER.START` write `OUTPUT_VALID` reads 1 in a bounded wait (`aes.adoc:106`, `MANUAL_OPERATION`: "In manual mode (1), the AES unit i) only starts to encrypt/decrypt after receiving a start trigger"; `aes.hjson`, `STATUS.INPUT_READY`: "If the present values in the DATA_IN registers have not yet been loaded into the module this flag is `0`", and `DATA_IN`: loaded upon starting the next block, so `INPUT_READY` = 0 shows that block 1 reached DATA_IN and was not loaded). A DUT that starts on the DATA_IN writes shows `IDLE` = 0 or `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in the window; a DUT that ignores the trigger never shows `OUTPUT_VALID`. Anchor: `STATUS` readback. Control: in automatic mode the same kind of DATA_IN write gives `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 sooner than the window (`CTRL-AES-START-MANUAL`, the automatic start time of step 16), so a unit that starts on DATA_IN shows it inside the window; otherwise the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-START-MANUAL PASS seed=<n> pre_samples=<k> pre_idle=1 pre_output_valid=0 pre_input_ready=0 run_clk=<r> window_clk=<w> auto_start_clk=<a>`; `CTRL-AES-START-MANUAL seen: seed=<n> auto_start_clk=<a> window_clk=<w>`.
|CHK-AES-LOST
|In manual mode a second block started without a read of the first output sets `OUTPUT_LOST`, DATA_OUT holds the result of block 2, `OUTPUT_LOST` still reads 1 after all four DATA_OUT words are read, and a rewrite of `CTRL_SHADOWED` clears it (`aes.adoc` register text, `MANUAL_OPERATION` and `STATUS.OUTPUT_LOST`: once set, the bit stays set until the operation is restarted by a rewrite of the Control Register). A DUT that keeps the first output, that never sets the bit, that clears it on the DATA_OUT read, or that never clears it, fails. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: with all four output words read between the blocks, `OUTPUT_LOST` stays 0 and DATA_OUT holds the result of block 2. Evidence: `CHK-AES-LOST PASS seed=<n> output_lost=1 data_out==block2=1 kept_after_read=1 rewrite_clears=1 ctrl_reads_between=4 ctrl_output_lost=0 wait_factor=<f>`.
|CHK-AES-IDLE
|After the last DATA_IN write of a stream block, and after the `TRIGGER.START` of manual block 1, `IDLE` reads 0 at some sample (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check) and at every later sample until the sample that shows `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 (`aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS.IDLE`: the flag is 0 while a block runs). A DUT that reports idle during a run fails; a DUT that never drops `IDLE` times out. Samples before the first `IDLE` = 0 and the sample that shows `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 are not graded. The reset value of `IDLE` is not graded. Anchor: `STATUS` readback. Control: after the last output is read and the unit is quiet, `IDLE` reads 1 in a bounded wait, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-IDLE PASS seed=<n> idle_busy_samples=<k> idle_busy=0 bad=none ctrl_idle_after_quiet=1`.
|CHK-AES-NOIRQ
|The 41 vector bits of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` other than bits 36 and 37 keep the value S0 on every clock from before the first AES write to the end of the busy-write cases, and no sampled bit is X or Z (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: AES exports no interrupt; `interrupts.adoc`: the vector has no AES source, and an AES alert or bridge fault would reach the CPU on source 33, bit 32, or source 43, bit 42). The leg enables the interrupt of no block except HMAC and starts no HMAC operation before the sampling stops, so no compared bit has a stated reason to change. Bits 36 and 37 are the entropy pool low and fill-stall sources (`interrupts.adoc`, sources 37 and 38; `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc`): they have no enable and follow the pool occupancy and the EDN fill, so a healthy design can change them, and the leaf logs them and does not grade them. A DUT that raises an interrupt or an alert on a busy, stall or lost-output event changes a bit. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` sampled on every clock. Control: with HMAC `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set, one HMAC operation at the end raises vector bit 17 (source 18), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; HMAC is out of reset and idle for the whole leaf. Evidence: `CHK-AES-NOIRQ PASS seed=<n> vector_bits=41 vector_changes=0 x_samples=0 ctrl_hmac_done_seen=1` and `OBS-POOL-VEC seed=<n> bit36_changes=<a> bit37_changes=<b>`.
|CHK-AES-INPUT-READY
|With output 0 unread and block 1 stalled, block 2 is written: every `STATUS` read of a window of 8 x t_blk clocks shows `STALL` = 1 and `INPUT_READY` = 0 (`aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS.INPUT_READY`: "If the present values in the DATA_IN registers have not yet been loaded into the module this flag is `0` (not ready)."; `theory_of_operation.md`, the stalled unit "hangs and does not drop data. It only continues once the previous output data has been read", and it starts the next block only "once the previous encryption/decryption is finished and new input data is available"). `STALL` = 1 in every sample shows that the hold lasts for the whole window, so block 2 cannot have been loaded. After the four DATA_OUT words of block 0 are read, `INPUT_READY` reads 1 within 8 x t_blk clocks (`programmers_guide.md`, Block Operation: the bit becomes 1 when the unit loads the input block "and start operation"; the input is loaded in the cycle `OUTPUT_VALID` becomes 1), and blocks 0, 1 and 2 equal their goldens, so block 2 was held and not dropped. A DUT that loads or drops the waiting block shows `INPUT_READY` = 1 in the window or a wrong block 2; a DUT that never loads it times out. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: the `STATUS` read with `STALL` = 1 before block 2 is written shows `INPUT_READY` = 1 (block 1 is loaded and no new input waits), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-INPUT-READY PASS seed=<n> held_samples=<k> held_input_ready_bad=0 held_stall0=0 t_load_clk=<t> window_clk=<w> ct==golden=[1,1,1] ctrl_input_ready=1` and `CTRL-AES-INPUT-READY seed=<n> ctrl_stall=1 ctrl_input_ready=1`.
|CHK-AES-BUSY-KEY
|In ECB with output 0 unread and block 1 stalled, a whole changed key (all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1, one share-0 word changed) is written while the unit is busy: the `STATUS` reads before and after the writes each show `IDLE` = 0, `STALL` = 1, `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1, `INPUT_READY` = 1 and no alert bit, so the unit is busy around the whole write, and outputs 0 to 2 equal the old-key golden (`aes.hjson:489`, "If the AES unit is non-idle, writes to these registers are ignored"; `aes.hjson:486`, every key register is written when the key changes). A DUT that takes a key written while stalled gives block 2, or block 1, on the new key. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: the same key written at `IDLE` gives the new-key golden for block 3 (`CTRL-AES-BUSY-KEY`, `twin_new_key=1`; otherwise the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails). `OUTPUT_LOST` is not graded in automatic mode (`aes.rdl`: not meaningful if `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0) and is logged. Evidence: `CHK-AES-BUSY-KEY PASS seed=<n> status_at_write=<s> status_after_write=<s> stall_at_write=1 idle_at_write=0 output_lost_obs=<b> key_word=<w> key_mask=<m> ct==old_key_golden=[1,1,1] twin_new_key=1`.
|CHK-AES-BUSY-IV
|The same in CBC with all four IV words written while the unit is stalled: the `STATUS` reads before and after show the same busy state, and outputs 0 to 2 equal the CBC golden of the unaltered chain (`aes.hjson:546`). A DUT that takes an IV written while stalled as the chain of block 2 fails. Control: the same IV written at `IDLE` gives the golden of block 3 chained on the new IV (`CTRL-AES-BUSY-IV`, `twin_new_iv=1`). Evidence: `CHK-AES-BUSY-IV PASS seed=<n> status_at_write=<s> status_after_write=<s> stall_at_write=1 idle_at_write=0 output_lost_obs=<b> iv_new=[<w0>,<w1>,<w2>,<w3>] ct==chain_golden=[1,1,1] twin_new_iv=1`.
|CHK-AES-PARTIAL-IV
|In CBC at `IDLE`, with three of the four IV words and the four DATA_IN words written, every `STATUS` read of a window of 20 x t_blk clocks shows `IDLE` = 1, `STALL` = 0, `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0, `INPUT_READY` = 0 and no alert bit, and after the fourth IV word `OUTPUT_VALID` reads 1 in a bounded wait and the output equals the CBC golden (`aes.hjson:543`, the unit does not start with a partially updated IV). A DUT that starts on three IV words shows `IDLE` = 0 or `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in the window. The output after the fourth word shows that the held DATA_IN block was used. Evidence: `CHK-AES-PARTIAL-IV PASS seed=<n> held_samples=<k> held_bad=0 first_bad=none ov_in_window=0 window_clk=<w> iv_missing_word=<j> t_out_clk=<t> ct==golden=1`.
|OBS-AES-OV-PARTIAL
|Not a graded check. No text states `OUTPUT_VALID` after a read of only some of the four DATA_OUT words. The leaf reads the four DATA_OUT words of the first block that it reads after a run one by one, reads `STATUS` after each word and logs `OUTPUT_VALID` after each. The four words still go to the stream compare of `CHK-AES-STREAM`. Evidence: `OBS-AES-OV-PARTIAL seed=<n> mode=<m> block=<i> ov_after=[<w0>, <w1>, <w2>, <w3>]`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the reset value of `STATUS.IDLE`, the fall of `OUTPUT_VALID` on a partial read of DATA_OUT (no fall condition is stated; `OBS-AES-OV-PARTIAL` logs it), a DATA_OUT read before `OUTPUT_VALID`, a CTR decrypt cell, or `STATUS.OUTPUT_LOST` in the busy-write cases. The CFB, OFB and GCM modes are graded by `sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_rand_test`. `STATUS.INPUT_READY` is graded only for a block that waits behind a stall and its load after the stall; its value at idle with no input written and during a run with no input waiting is not graded (no sentence states it). `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` owns the 2-block cells of the open test and keeps them.

'''''

[[sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test]]
===== 1.33 `sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` (approved; the AES client of EDN). Inject: none beyond the invalid register writes of the procedure. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the multi-hot invalid value (one of 3'b011, 3'b101, 3'b110 and 3'b111), the order of the five rate runs (PER_1, PER_64, PER_8K, the all-zero invalid value 3'b000 and the drawn multi-hot value), the block count N (129 to 300, one value per seed), the order of the two clear trials, the rate of the trigger leg (one of the three legal rates), the key table row, the plaintexts, and last the reload key row of the key clear (a table row that differs from the key row). All five rate runs use the same key and the same plaintexts, so only the rate differs between them. `reseed = 4` samples other multi-hot values, counts, orders, rates, rows and data. The three legal rate runs, the all-zero run, one multi-hot run, the trigger leg and both clear trials run on every seed. The multi-hot value is a seed draw and has no bin of its own: the coverage class is multi-hot.

*Objective*: `CTRL_SHADOWED.PRNG_RESEED_RATE` (one-hot; PER_1 at reset, PER_64, PER_8K) sets how often the masking PRNG reseeds from EDN, and an invalid value maps to PER_1. A reseed request comes from the rate, from `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` = 1, or from a new key while `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` is at its reset value 1. A `TRIGGER` write with `PRNG_RESEED` = 0 keeps the current states of the PRNGs. `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` clear the named registers with pseudo-random data; the specification states no value after a clear. `STATUS.IDLE` is 0 while register clearing or PRNG reseeding is running (`aes.hjson`, `STATUS.IDLE`). A register clear returns to idle before the STATUS read that follows the trigger write, so that clause is not a sample. The PRNG reseed runs until the EDN acknowledge, and the STATUS poll of that wait samples `IDLE` = 0. After `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` the engine takes a new key, IV and plaintext and encrypts them. Each block is the AES result of its key and plaintext at every rate (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`: an AES cipher with the stated key sizes and modes). Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, `TRIGGER`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (the rate codes and their order; `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED`).

*Test Coverage*: The rates PER_1, PER_64 and PER_8K, the all-zero invalid value and one drawn multi-hot invalid value, each over N blocks (N of 129 or more, so PER_64 reseeds at least twice). The reseed trigger on an idle AES: a `TRIGGER` write with `PRNG_RESEED` = 0 and a write-and-acknowledge control. `STATUS.IDLE` = 0 on a sample of the `PRNG_RESEED` = 1 wait, after a read of 1. Both clear triggers while idle, each followed by a reload and an encryption. This entry extends `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`, which writes `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` and grades the request (`CHK-EDN-PEND-AES`) and the acknowledge (`CHK-EDN-SIBLING`), and `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`, which writes the same trigger and grades the acknowledge (`CHK-NO-STARVE`). Here the write-and-acknowledge is a control. Only the `PRNG_RESEED` = 0 window, the rate order, the invalid-value mapping and the encryption after the key clear and a reload are graded; the values after both clear triggers are logged, and the open tests keep the request and the acknowledge cells. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` keeps the grade of the AES beats against the stream. Functional coverage: `sep_aes_cfg_cg` (`cp_idle_reseed`), `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES reset bit of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the HMAC, KMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`). `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` stays at its reset value for the whole leaf.
. For each of the five rate runs in the drawn order, pulse the AES reset bit (0 then 1) and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Rate run. For an invalid value the write is an error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.PRNG_RESEED_RATE`; kind 3'b000 or several bits set; expected response the PER_1 behaviour. For an invalid value, first write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with PER_8K (same other fields), so the rate before the invalid write is not the PER_1 reset value. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with the run's rate value, ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC and automatic mode.
. Rate run. Start the counter of rises on `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`, then write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of the key. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 again. These actions are the same in all five runs.
. Rate run. For each of the N blocks write the four DATA_IN words, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, read the four DATA_OUT words and compare them with the golden.
. Rate run. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait, stop the counter and log its value. In the first run also log the clocks from the last DATA_IN write response of block 0 to `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 as t_blk.
. Compare the five counts: count(PER_1) > count(PER_64) > count(PER_8K), and each of the two invalid values gives a count equal to count(PER_1) and above count(PER_64).
. Trigger leg. Pulse the AES reset bit, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice (ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic, the drawn legal rate), write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 again.
. Trigger leg. Read `STATUS` and expect `IDLE` = 1 (`CTRL-AES-IDLE-RESEED`). Write `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` = 1. Poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails `CHK-AES-IDLE-RESEED`) and expect `IDLE` = 0 on a sample before that read. Log the clocks from the write response to the first rise of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` in that wait as t_r (a timeout of this rise fails `CHK-AES-RESEED-TRIG`).
. Trigger leg. Write `TRIGGER` with every field 0, so that `PRNG_RESEED` = 0, and count the rises of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` over a window of 4 x t_r clocks (a test parameter).
. Trigger leg, control. Write `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` = 1 again and count the rises of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` over a window of 4 x t_r clocks.
. Clear trial for `DATA_OUT_CLEAR` (in the drawn order). Pulse the AES reset bit, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice (ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic, PER_1), write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Clear trial for `DATA_OUT_CLEAR`. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 (the same seed clear as in the rate runs). Write one block P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, read the four DATA_OUT words twice and keep them as O0. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Clear trial for `DATA_OUT_CLEAR`. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` = 1, wait 4 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter), wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Clear trial for `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` (in the drawn order). Pulse the AES reset bit, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice (ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic, PER_1), write the eight key words, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write the four IV words from the table and read the four IV words.
. Clear trial for `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR`. Write `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` = 1, wait 4 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter), wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four IV words.
. Clear trial for `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR`. Write the eight key words of the drawn reload key row (it differs from the key loaded before the clear) and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four IV words again, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks, log `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT reason=no exact form: aes.hjson:909-913 states pseudo-random data only` and log `RESULT sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=6`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-RESEED-RATE
|Over N blocks (one N per seed) the number of rises of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` satisfies count(PER_1) > count(PER_64) > count(PER_8K) (`aes.hjson`, `PRNG_RESEED_RATE`: PER_1 reseeds once per block, PER_64 about once per 64 blocks, PER_8K about once per 8192 blocks). Every run has the same prefix, which includes the reseed that the first key write causes, so only the rate differs. A DUT that ignores the rate, or that swaps two rates, breaks the order. The number of acknowledges per reseed is not stated and is not graded; only the order is. Anchor: the EDN acknowledge probe `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RESEED-RATE PASS seed=<n> n=<N> c_per1=<a> c_per64=<b> c_per8k=<c> order=a>b>c`.
|CHK-AES-RESEED-INVALID
|For each of the two invalid rate values (3'b000 and the drawn multi-hot value) the count equals count(PER_1) of the same seed (`aes.adoc` register text, `PRNG_RESEED_RATE`: invalid values map to PER_1). The five runs use the same key and plaintexts and the same prefix, so a run at PER_1 gives the same count. A DUT that maps an invalid value to anything but PER_1, or ignores the invalid write and keeps the PER_8K written before it, gives a count that differs from count(PER_1). Anchor: `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`. Control: each invalid count is above count(PER_64) (`CTRL-AES-RESEED-INVALID`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RESEED-INVALID PASS seed=<n> value=0b<v> c=<c> c_per1=<a> c_per64=<b>` for each of the two values.
|CHK-AES-RESEED-TRIG
|On an idle AES with EDN enabled, after a `TRIGGER` write with `PRNG_RESEED` = 0, no rise of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` occurs in a window of 4 x t_r clocks (`aes.hjson`, `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED`: 0 keeps the current states). A DUT that reseeds on any `TRIGGER` write shows a rise. The window starts after `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, so a reseed that is still running is not counted. Anchor: `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`. Control: a `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` write in the same seed is acknowledged within a window of the same length, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; this shows that the window is long enough to see an acknowledge and that the probe sees a rise. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RESEED-TRIG PASS seed=<n> window_clk=<w> acks_after_zero_write=0 ctrl_acks_after_write=<k> t_r_clk=<t>`.
|CHK-AES-IDLE-RESEED
|`STATUS.IDLE` is 0 while PRNG reseeding is running (`aes.hjson`, `STATUS.IDLE`). After `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` = 1, a `STATUS` sample before `IDLE` returns to 1 reads `IDLE` = 0. A DUT that leaves `IDLE` at 1 through the reseed, or that never returns to idle, fails. Anchor: `STATUS` readback. Control: `IDLE` reads 1 before the write (`CTRL-AES-IDLE-RESEED`). A register clear is not this check: it returns to idle before the `STATUS` read that follows the trigger write. Evidence: `CHK-AES-IDLE-RESEED PASS seed=<n> busy_samples=<n> status_reads=<n> status_idle_before=1`.
|CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT
|Blocked, no exact form: `aes.hjson` (`TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR`) states only "clear those registers with pseudo-random data", with no value of DATA_OUT after the clear and no read effect (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs,Blocked legs of tested features>>). `STATUS.IDLE` during the clear is not a sample: the clear returns to idle before the STATUS read. `CHK-AES-IDLE-RESEED` grades that sentence for the PRNG reseed. A check that DATA_OUT differs from the output before the clear is a random inequality and passes a partial clear, so it is not graded. The leaf logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT reason=no exact form: aes.hjson:909-913 states pseudo-random data only` and `OBS-AES-CLEAR-OUT seed=<n> before=0x<b> after=0x<a> ctrl_reads_equal=<e>`, does not log `CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT PASS`, and does not count the check. Each rate run of the same seed writes `DATA_OUT_CLEAR` before its first block, so CHK-AES-RATE-CT grades that the engine encrypts after the clear.
|CHK-AES-CLEAR-KEY
|After `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` and a reload of a key row that differs from the key loaded before the clear, the IV and the plaintext, the encryption equals the golden of the reloaded key (`aes.adoc` register text, `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR`; `aes.hjson`, `KEY_SHARE0`, `KEY_SHARE1` and `IV`: the registers take new values while the engine is idle). A DUT that stays busy or broken after the clear, or that ignores the reloaded key, fails the golden; the goldens of the two keys differ (the leaf checks this before the run). The IV words after the clear are logged as `OBS-AES-CLEAR-IV` and not graded: the specification states pseudo-random data and no value (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs,Blocked legs of tested features>>). Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Control: the IV read before the clear equals the written IV, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-CLEAR-KEY PASS seed=<n> reload_ct==golden=1 ctrl_iv_read_equal=1 key_row=<r> reload_row=<r2>`, and `OBS-AES-CLEAR-IV seed=<n> iv_before=0x<b> iv_after=0x<a>`.
|CHK-AES-RATE-CT
|Every block of every rate run equals the golden ciphertext. The contract is the AES cipher definition (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`: an AES cipher with the stated key sizes and modes), which the golden `env/sep_aes_golden.py` implements; the cipher result is a function of the key and the plaintext only, so the reseed rate has no term in it. A DUT whose reseed corrupts the data path shows a mismatch. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RATE-CT PASS seed=<n> blocks=<5N> mismatches=0 bad_runs=none`, the total over the five runs, and per run `OBS-AES-RATE-RUN seed=<n> run=<r> value=0b<v> acks=<c> mismatches=<m>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes, except the blocked CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT, which logs `OBS-` lines, is not counted, and does not fail the leaf. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the number of acknowledges per reseed (not stated), the values of DATA_OUT and IV after a clear trigger (no exact form: `aes.hjson` states pseudo-random data only), `FORCE_MASKS`, or a write of `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` (so `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` stays at its reset value). `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` and `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test` own the request and the acknowledge of a reseed write and keep them. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` owns the grade of the AES beats against the stream.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test]]
===== 1.34 `sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES, KMAC and alerts)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, `tools = ["vcs"]` (a read after reset must show no X), 4 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `seq_lib/sep_kmac_seq.py` and `env/sep_kmac_golden.py` (KMAC driver and SHA3 golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved; vector bit 32 is the Crypto alert). Added hook: the alert sender inject, an error inject that forces the `alert_p` and `alert_n` pair of one crypto alert receiver to the same value for one trial (CHK-ALERT-MALFORMED); the test adds it to the inject exceptions of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the legal values A and B of each shadowed control register (A differs from B), the legal values of the AES AUX pair (they differ in `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` only, never in `FORCE_MASKS`) and of the KMAC threshold pair (two different 10-bit values), the gap between the two writes of a pair (0 to 64 clocks), the message of the KMAC digest, the order of the six blocks of the malformed alert leg, the receiver of each block (one of the two of its range, or the one of HMAC), the wire value of the malformed encoding (0 or 1) and the `ALERT_TEST` bit of each block; then the GCM values A_GCM and B_GCM (a GCM_INIT next phase and a `NUM_VALID_BYTES` of 1 to 16, different in one drawn field), the gap of each GCM pair and the 96-bit GCM IV. `reseed = 4` samples other values, gaps and orders. The four shadowed targets, the AUX lock, the AES reset leg and the six malformed alert trials run on every seed.

*Objective*: An update error in the shadowed Control Register of AES (`CTRL_SHADOWED`) sets `STATUS.ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` and a recoverable alert, and the unit restarts when the register is rewritten (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`). The KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` register has a status bit of the same name, and the specification does not state what causes its update error, so the KMAC trials log their outcome. For `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, two writes of different values trigger a recoverable alert, and two equal writes change its content (`aes.hjson:825-826`); the AES recoverable alert is ORed into the Crypto alert source (`crypto.adoc:123-128`, `:142`, `:152-153`). `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` follows the same rule: two writes of different values trigger a recoverable alert, and two equal writes change its content (`aes.hjson:1023-1024`); it takes a value only while the unit is idle (`aes.hjson:1021-1022`). The update error is the recoverable alert and is not a fatal fault cause, so every `STATUS` read that shows `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` shows `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` 0 (`aes.hjson:233-235`, `:1001-1011`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc:37-38`). `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` (RW0C, reset 1), once cleared, locks `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, and a write of 1 does not set it again. An AES reset clears the error (the AUX reset value is graded in `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test`). A malformed encoding on the alert pair of any crypto block raises the Crypto alert source (PIC source 33, vector bit 32), the source that an `ALERT_TEST` write of the block raises. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`STATUS`, `CFG_SHADOWED`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (OCAH Modifications: the AES core uses `rst_ni` for both reset domains); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (OpenTitan Alert Termination); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (source 33); the `ALERT_TEST` registers of HMAC, OTBN, AES, KMAC, CSRNG and EDN in the register descriptions.

*Test Coverage*: The four targets AES `CTRL_SHADOWED`, AES `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` and KMAC `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED`, each with a mismatched pair and an equal pair. The AES results are graded, except the update-error status bit after an AUX pair, which is logged (`aes.hjson:995` names the shadowed Control Register only). The KMAC results (the update error, the alert, the readback and the digest after the rewrite) are logged. Recovery of the AES control register by a rewrite and one operation. A mismatched and an equal pair of `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` after GCM_INIT with a key and an IV. Every AES `STATUS` read inside a graded window that shows the update error also shows no fatal fault. The AUX lock. An AES reset with the AUX register at a value other than its reset value and the update error set. The malformed alert leg on six blocks (HMAC, OTBN, AES, KMAC, CSRNG, EDN) with an `ALERT_TEST` write as a timing reference that the leaf does not grade. This entry extends `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`, which clears `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN`, pulses the AES reset bit and requires `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` = 1 after it: only the status bit, the X-free readback after the reset, the new AUX pair after the reset and the lock of `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` are graded here, and the open test keeps its cell. `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` grades the AUX register value after an AES reset. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` (`check_status_clean`) grades a clean AES status after equal-pair writes of `CTRL_SHADOWED`, and the equal-pair cell here grades the pulse on vector bit 32, the readback and the update-error status bit. The Crypto alert for each `ALERT_TEST` bit and the receiver range of each alert belong to `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`. This entry sets the update error and drives the malformed alert encoding. Functional coverage: `sep_aes_fault_cg` (`x_shadow_pair_aes`, `x_shadow_pair_aux`, `x_shadow_pair_gcm`, `cp_update_err_recovery`, `cp_reset_restore`, `cp_fatal_quiet`), `sep_crypto_reg_lock_cg`, `sep_crypto_alert_cg`. Each cell except `cp_fatal_quiet` is held at its DUT event inside the owner window and samples only after its check passes: the leaf writes the bit of the pass label `aes_shadow_pass` after the check and its controls passed, and then closes the window. `cp_fatal_quiet` samples at the graded `STATUS` read. A FAIL closes the window first, and the window does not open again in the run.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES and KMAC reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the HMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed and log it (`INFO-PLAN seed=<n> ...`). Read the bench port `aes_sec_params_o` and log the AES build parameters (`OBS-AES-SEC-PARAMS`).
. Steps 4 to 11 run once for AES and then once for KMAC. For KMAC every value of steps 5 to 11 is logged as `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC` and no check grades it, and a timeout of a KMAC wait logs `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC` and does not fail the leaf. Start the injection trial of the control register: pulse the reset bit of the block (0 then 1) and wait for the block to be idle (`STATUS.IDLE` = 1 for AES, `STATUS.sha3_idle` = 1 for KMAC) in a bounded wait.
. Error injection. Write A to the control register (`CTRL_SHADOWED` of AES, `CFG_SHADOWED` of KMAC), wait the drawn gap and write B, with no read of the register between the two writes. Point: the shadowed register. Kind: two different legal values. Expected response: update-error status and a recoverable alert. Both writes answer OKAY. Every AES word is legal, and A and B differ in one drawn legal field (KEY_LEN, MODE or OPERATION). Every KMAC word keeps SHA3, L256, `kmac_en` = 0, `sideload` = 0, `entropy_mode` = EDN and `entropy_ready` = 1, and A and B differ in one drawn endianness bit.
. Vector bit 32 is sampled on every clock from before the issue of the first write of the pair. Poll the `STATUS` of the block until `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout fails CHK-SHADOW-ERR). Log as c_err the clocks from the issue of the second write to the first high clock of vector bit 32, counted from 1, so c_err is 1 or more whether the pulse comes before or after the write response (the specification does not order them; a bounded wait, a timeout fails the check). Count the clocks with the bit high as `alert_cycles`. For KMAC take the window base c_err from the AES trial of the same seed.
. Read `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` of the block.
. Recovery. Write the legal word R twice (AES: ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic; KMAC: the word with both endianness bits 0). Run one operation: for AES write the eight key words, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR`, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write one block, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words; for KMAC run one SHA3-256 hash of the drawn message with the command sequence of `seq_lib/sep_kmac_seq.py`, wait for `kmac_done` in a bounded wait and read the digest from STATE.
. Equal-pair trial of the control register: pulse the reset bit of the block, wait for the block to be idle, write A, wait the drawn gap and write A again. Count the high clocks of vector bit 32 from the issue of the first write to 4 x c_err clocks after the issue of the second write (a test parameter), read the update-error status (CHK-SHADOW-CTRL), and read the register back.
. AUX or threshold target of the same block, the equal pair. For AES: pulse the reset bit, wait for idle and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` with the graded window closed: it reads the reset value 0x1 (`aes.adoc:131`, `CTRL-AUX-SHADOW-PRE`). Open the graded window and write the value A_aux (`KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` = 0, which differs from the reset value) twice. Count the high clocks of vector bit 32 from the issue of the first write to 4 x c_err clocks after the issue of the second write (0, `CTRL-AUX-SHADOW-EQ`), read the update-error bit and read the register back (CHK-AUX-SHADOW-WRITE), then close the window. For KMAC the threshold pair runs mismatched first and then equal, and every value is logged as `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC`. For KMAC, one more trial follows the equal pair: pulse the KMAC reset bit, wait for idle, write the legal `CFG_SHADOWED` word A once, read `CFG_SHADOWED`, write A again, wait 4 x c_err clocks, then read `STATUS` and `CFG_SHADOWED`, and log them with the first rise of vector bit 32 (`OBS-SHADOW-KMAC-RDGAP`).
. AES only: the AUX mismatched pair. Pulse the reset bit, wait for idle, open the graded window, write the first value of the pair, wait the drawn gap and write the second value. Record the rises of vector bit 32 from the issue of the first write to 4 x c_err clocks after the issue of the second write (CHK-AUX-SHADOW-ERR), read the update-error bit of `STATUS` and log it as `OBS-SHADOW-AUX`, then close the window. Then run the GCM legs. Each leg pulses the AES reset bit, writes `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with GCM mode, writes `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` twice with GCM_INIT, writes the key, waits for idle, writes the IV with the fourth word 0, waits for idle and reads `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED`, with the graded window closed; the read clears the phase tracking, so the next write is the first write of a pair. Mismatched leg: open the graded window, write A_GCM, wait the drawn gap, write B_GCM, count the high clocks of vector bit 32 from the first write, read `STATUS` (CHK-GCM-SHADOW-ERR) and close the window. Equal leg: take the read at the end of the set-up as the value before the pair (`CTRL-GCM-SHADOW-PRE`), open the graded window, write A_GCM twice, count the high clocks over 4 x c_err clocks after the second write, read `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` and `STATUS` (CHK-GCM-SHADOW-WRITE) and close the window. Log the reserved bits as `OBS-SHADOW-GCM`; the status bit of the mismatched leg is logged as `OBS-SHADOW-GCM` too.
. AUX lock leg. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the AUX value A_aux (`KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` = 0, which differs from the reset value) twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`.
. AUX lock leg. Write 0 to `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` and read it. Write the AUX value B_aux (`KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` = 1) twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`. Write 1 to `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` and read it.
. AES reset leg. With `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` = 0 and the AUX register at A_aux, write A and B to `CTRL_SHADOWED` of AES (a mismatched pair) and wait for `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`.
. AES reset leg. Start the read-data tap of the leaf on the `s_axi` port: it samples the read data of every read of `STATUS` and `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` and counts the beats whose accessed byte lanes carry X or Z. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait, with the graded window open across the reset. Read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` and `STATUS`.
. AES reset leg. Write the AUX pair A_aux twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`. Then write the AUX pair B_aux twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` again. A_aux differs from the reset value and B_aux differs from A_aux, so each pair, the first one after the reset included, changes the register. Stop the tap. The tap must have seen at least five graded read beats (`CTRL-AUX-RESET-TAP`: the idle poll read, the two reads after the reset and the two AUX readbacks), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails.
. Malformed alert leg. Release the HMAC and OTBN reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`.
. Malformed alert leg. Steps 18 to 20 run once for each of the six blocks in the drawn order. Sample vector bit 32 on every clock from before the `ALERT_TEST` write is issued. Write 1 to the drawn `ALERT_TEST` bit of the block and log c, the clocks from the issue of the write to the first high clock of bit 32, counted from 1 (the specification does not order the pulse against the write response), as the timing reference of the trial, as `INFO-ALERT-REF seed=<n> block=<b> clk=<c>` (a bounded wait; a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). The rise for each `ALERT_TEST` bit is graded by `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`. Wait for vector bit 32 to fall.
. Malformed alert leg. Quiet window before the injection: with no stimulus, count the high clocks of vector bit 32 over 4 x c clocks, then sample the bit (it reads 0). Error injection (the alert sender inject). Force `alert_p` and `alert_n` of the drawn receiver of the block to the same drawn value. Point: the alert pair of one receiver. Kind: both wires at one value, which is not a differential code. Expected response: a rise of vector bit 32. Take the watcher mark on the last clock sampled before the injection; drive the injection and write `alert_rise_bound` = 4 x c to the FCOV sampler on the next clock (clock 1), and write 0 when the wait ends. The alert sender inject forces the pair at the falling edge after clock 1, so clock 2 is the first clock that can show the rise. Hold the force until the bit rises or 4 x c clocks pass. Log the clocks from the mark to the rise as `raise_clk`.
. Malformed alert leg. Release the force, wait 4 x c clocks and sample vector bit 32. Quiet window after the release: count the high clocks of vector bit 32 over a further 4 x c clocks. Log the sample and the count as `OBS-ALERT-MALFORMED-POST` (no text states the level after the release). The graded window is open from the injection to the end of this window. Grade CHK-ALERT-MALFORMED for the block before the window closes, and write the `aes_shadow_pass` bit of the block after the check passes.
. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks and log `RESULT sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=11`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SHADOW-ERR
|For AES `CTRL_SHADOWED`, a second write that differs from the first sets `STATUS.ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR`, pulses vector bit 32 and leaves `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` at 0 (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`; `aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS`; `crypto.adoc`, OpenTitan Alert Termination; `interrupts.adoc`, source 33). A DUT that takes B or ignores the mismatch shows no status and no pulse, and a DUT that raises the fatal path shows `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` = 1. Anchor: `STATUS` readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[32]`. Control: CHK-SHADOW-CTRL of the same target, where the equal pair gives no pulse. The AES AUX pairs are graded by CHK-AUX-SHADOW-ERR and CHK-AUX-SHADOW-WRITE; the AES status bit after an AUX pair, the threshold target and the KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` are logged and not graded. Evidence: `CHK-SHADOW-ERR PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_ctrl a=0x<a> b=0x<b> status_err=1 alert_cycles=<c> fatal=0 c_err=<e>` `OBS-SHADOW-AUX seed=<n> reg=aes_aux pair=mismatched status_err=<s>` and `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC seed=<n> reg=<kmac_cfg\|kmac_thr> pair=<mismatched\|equal> status_err=<s> fatal=<f> alert_cycles=<c> c_err=<e> readback=<r>`.
|CHK-SHADOW-CTRL
|For AES `CTRL_SHADOWED`, an equal pair (A, A) raises no pulse on vector bit 32 over a window of 4 x c_err clocks and reads back A (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`: two writes of the same value commit; a recoverable alert follows two different values only). The update-error status read after the pair is 0: the pair follows an AES reset, the bit resets to 0 (`aes.hjson:992-995`, `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` resval 0) and only two different values raise the update error. A DUT that flags every write, sets the status bit on an equal pair, or fails to commit an equal pair, fails. The window runs from the issue of the first write to 4 x c_err clocks after the issue of the second write, and c_err is the delay from the issue of the second write of the mismatched pair to its first high clock, so the window covers a pulse before or after the write response. Anchor: the vector probe and register readback. Control: the mismatched pair of the same target pulses in the same seed (`c_err` above 0), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-SHADOW-CTRL PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_ctrl a=0x<a> alert_cycles=0 status_err=0 readback=0x<a> rsvd=0x<r> window_clk=<w> ctrl_c_err=<e>`.
|CHK-SHADOW-RESTART
|After the legal word R is written twice, the next AES block equals its golden (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`: a write of the Control Register starts a new message; `aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS`). A DUT that stays in the error state or corrupts the result fails. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`; the golden compare is the positive evidence. The KMAC digest after the rewrite is compared with `env/sep_kmac_golden.py` and logged as `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC`. Evidence: `CHK-SHADOW-RESTART PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_ctrl op==golden`.
|CHK-GCM-SHADOW-ERR
|For AES `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` after GCM_INIT, a mismatched pair (A_GCM and B_GCM, which differ in the next phase or in `NUM_VALID_BYTES`, one drawn field) raises no pulse on vector bit 32 up to the issue of the second write, then pulses it within 4 x c_err clocks after that write, and leaves `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` at 0 (`aes.hjson:1024`: two different values trigger a recoverable alert; `:1032`: `recov_ctrl_update_err`; `crypto.adoc:142`, `:152-153`: the AES recoverable alert reaches the Crypto alert source). A wrong DUT that does not compare the GCM writes, compares one field only, raises the alert on the first write, does not route it to bit 32, or raises the fatal path shows it. Each pair follows a GCM_INIT run with a key and an IV, because the next phases are legal only after the two initial encryptions (`aes.hjson:1049-1053`). c_err is the alert delay of the `CTRL_SHADOWED` mismatched pair of the same seed. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[32]` sampled on every clock, and the `STATUS` readback. Control: CHK-GCM-SHADOW-WRITE of the same seed shows that an equal pair commits with no pulse, so the pair path is live. The status bit is logged (`OBS-SHADOW-GCM`; `aes.hjson:995` names the Control Register only). Evidence: `CHK-GCM-SHADOW-ERR PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_gcm a=0x<a> b=0x<b> draw=<d> early_rises=0 rise_clk=<r> alert_cycles=<c> window_clk=<w> fatal=0 c_err=<e>`.
|CHK-GCM-SHADOW-WRITE
|For AES `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` after GCM_INIT, an equal pair of A_GCM raises no pulse on vector bit 32 over 4 x c_err clocks after the second write, the read after the pair returns A_GCM on the `PHASE` and `NUM_VALID_BYTES` fields (`aes.hjson:1023`: two subsequent writes change the content; `:1024`: only different values raise the alert), and `STATUS.ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` reads its reset value 0 (`aes.hjson:992-995`; the pair follows an AES reset and no mismatch). A wrong DUT whose equal pair raises the alert, does not commit or needs more writes shows a pulse or the earlier value. Anchor: the vector probe and the register readback. Control (`CTRL-GCM-SHADOW-PRE`): the read at the end of the GCM_INIT set-up, before the pair, equals the RDL reset value of the fields (0x401: `PHASE` GCM_INIT, `NUM_VALID_BYTES` 16) and differs from A_GCM, so a pair that does not land is visible; a failing control fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-GCM-SHADOW-WRITE PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_gcm a=0x<a> alert_cycles=0 readback=0x<a> status_err=0 window_clk=<w> ctrl_pre=0x<r> ctrl_c_err=<e>`.
|CHK-SHADOW-FATAL-QUIET
|Every AES `STATUS` read in a graded window that shows `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` = 1 shows `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` = 0 (`aes.hjson:233-235`: the update error is the recoverable alert; `:1001-1011`: the fatal causes, none of them an update error; `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc:37-38`: life-cycle escalation is tied inactive). A wrong DUT that raises the fatal path with an update error fails. The leaf grades at the bus: a second read tap on `s_axi` sees every accepted AES `STATUS` beat, including the polls and the helper reads, and a read with the fatal bit closes the window at that beat. A read with X or Z on an accessed lane is not graded. Anchor: the read data of the `STATUS` beats. Control (`CTRL-SHADOW-FATAL-QUIET`): the mismatched `CTRL_SHADOWED` pair of the AES pass and the one of the AES reset leg each give at least one such read (`ctrl_leg_err_reads`, `reset_leg_err_reads`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-SHADOW-FATAL-QUIET PASS seed=<n> err_reads=<k> fatal_reads=0 first_fatal=none per_leg=<leg:n,...> ctrl_leg_err_reads=<a> reset_leg_err_reads=<b>`.
|CHK-AUX-SHADOW-ERR
|For AES `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, a mismatched pair (the two drawn AUX values, which differ in `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED`) raises no pulse on vector bit 32 up to the issue of the second write, then pulses it within 4 x c_err clocks after that write (`early_rises` = 0, `rise_clk` 1 to 4 x c_err). Spec: "This register is shadowed, meaning two subsequent write operations are required to change its content. If the two write operations try to set a different value, a recoverable alert is triggered (See Status Register)." (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson:825-826`); the AES recoverable alert is ORed into the Crypto alert source (`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:123-128`, `:142`, `:152-153`; `interrupts.adoc`, source 33). c_err is the measured alert delay of the `CTRL_SHADOWED` mismatched pair of the same seed. A DUT that does not compare the AUX writes shows no pulse, and one that raises the alert on the first write shows an early rise. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[32]`, sampled on every clock. Control: the equal AES AUX pair of the same seed gives no pulse over the same window (`CTRL-AUX-SHADOW-EQ`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. The update-error status bit after the mismatched pair is logged (`OBS-SHADOW-AUX`; `aes.hjson:995` names the Control Register only). Evidence: `CHK-AUX-SHADOW-ERR PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_aux first=0x<a> second=0x<b> early_rises=0 rise_clk=<r> alert_cycles=<c> window_clk=<w> ctrl_eq_alert_cycles=0`.
|CHK-AUX-SHADOW-WRITE
|For AES `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, an equal pair of A_aux (0x0) after an AES reset makes the register read A_aux (`aes.hjson:825`: two subsequent writes change its content), and `STATUS.ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` reads its reset value 0 (`aes.hjson:992-995`; no mismatch since the reset). A DUT that needs more writes, or ignores the pair, keeps the reset value 0x1; one that flags the equal pair reads 1. Anchor: the `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` readback. Control: the read before the pair gives the reset value 0x1 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc:131`), which differs from A_aux (`CTRL-AUX-SHADOW-PRE`), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. The read is before the first write, so it does not split the pair (`aes.hjson:827`). Evidence: `CHK-AUX-SHADOW-WRITE PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_aux value=0x0 readback=0x0 status_err=0 ctrl_aux_pre=0x1 ctrl_eq_alert_cycles=0`.
|CHK-AUX-REGWEN
|After 0 is written to `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` it reads 0, a new AUX pair leaves `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` at the value that it held before the pair, and a write of 1 leaves `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` at 0 (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN`: RW0C; a cleared bit blocks writes). A DUT that lets the AUX write land, or that sets the bit again, fails. Anchor: register readback. Control: the same AUX write before the lock lands, so the refused write is one that would change the register, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AUX-REGWEN PASS seed=<n> regwen_after0=0 aux_before=0x<x> aux_after=0x<x> regwen_after1=0 ctrl_pre_lock_lands=1`.
|CHK-AUX-RESET
|After an AES reset `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` reads 0, the read data of the `STATUS` and `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` reads on `s_axi` carry no X or Z on the accessed byte lanes, and two new AUX pairs of different values (A_aux, which differs from the reset value, then B_aux) each land, so each readback equals the value of its pair (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: the AES core uses `rst_ni` for both reset domains; `aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` and `STATUS`). `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` reads its RDL reset value after the reset (`FORCE_MASKS` 0x0 at `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc:126`, `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` 0x1 at `:131`); `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` also grades it. A DUT that keeps the error across the reset, that returns an unknown value, that leaves the AUX register at another value than its reset value, or that does not unlock the AUX register fails. Anchor: register readback and the read-data tap of the leaf on the `s_axi` port, on VCS. Controls: before the reset the AUX register differs from its reset value and the status bit reads 1, and the tap saw at least five graded read beats (`CTRL-AUX-RESET-TAP`); a missing control logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. Evidence: `CHK-AUX-RESET PASS seed=<n> ctrl_aux_pre_reset=0x<v> aux=0x<a> aux_reset=0x<r> ctrl_err_pre_reset=1 status_err=0 x_free=1 graded_read_beats=<k> aux_after_a=0x<a> aux_after_b=0x<b> write_pairs_land=1` and `OBS-AUX-RESET seed=<n> status_rsvd=0x<r>`. A refused pair leaves one readback at the other value and fails the check.
|CHK-ALERT-MALFORMED
|For each of the six blocks, vector bit 32 has no high clock over a quiet window of 4 x c clocks with no stimulus before the injection and reads 0 at its end, a malformed encoding on the alert pair of one receiver (both wires at one value) raises the bit on a clock 2 to 4 x c - 1 (inclusive) after the mark (`raise_clk`) (`crypto.adoc`, OpenTitan Alert Termination: a malformed encoding raises the integrity-fail of the receiver, and the Crypto alert source is the OR of every receiver pulse and integrity-fail). A DUT whose integrity-fail does not reach the Crypto alert source shows no rise. A DUT that raises the bit with no stimulus shows a high clock in the quiet window before the injection, which shows that the rise comes from the injection. No text states that the bit drops once the pair is legal again, so the level 4 x c clocks after the release and the high clocks of a further quiet window of 4 x c clocks are logged and not graded (`OBS-ALERT-MALFORMED-POST`). Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[32]`, sampled on every clock. The rise during the injection is the positive evidence. The bound of the hold and the post-release window are multiples of the `ALERT_TEST` delay of the same block (`INFO-ALERT-REF`). The FCOV sampler counts the same clocks from the same mark (2 on the first clock that sees the malformed pair) and fills `x_alert_source` only for a rise in the same inclusive window (`alert_rise_bound`). Evidence: `CHK-ALERT-MALFORMED PASS seed=<n> block=<hmac\|otbn\|aes\|kmac\|csrng\|edn> index=<i> wire=<0 or 1> pre=0 quiet_pre_high_clk=0 raised=1 quiet_clk=<4c> raise_clk=<r> expect_raise_clk_min=2 expect_raise_clk_max=<4c-1> ref_clk=<c>` and `OBS-ALERT-MALFORMED-POST seed=<n> block=<b> post=<p> quiet_post_high_clk=<q> quiet_clk=<4c>`.
|OBS-SHADOW-KMAC-RDGAP
|Not a graded check. `aes.hjson` states that a read of an AES shadowed register restarts the write sequence, and `kmac.hjson` does not say it for KMAC. The leaf writes one equal `CFG_SHADOWED` pair with a read between the two writes and logs the value written, the value read between the writes, the stored value, the `STATUS` error and fatal bits and the clocks to the first rise of vector bit 32. Evidence: `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC-RDGAP seed=<n> written=0x<a> read_between=0x<r> stored=0x<s> err=<e> fatal=<f> c_err=<c>`; a timeout logs `reason=timeout:<what> bound=<b>`.
|OBS-AES-SEC-PARAMS
|Not a graded check. `aes.hjson` gives only the OpenTitan defaults of the AES core parameters (`:76` `SecMasking` 1, `:105` `SecAllowForcingMasks` 0, both overridable), and `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` and `crypto.adoc` do not state the SEP build values. The leaf reads the monitor-only port `aes_sec_params_o` (`{SecAllowForcingMasks, SecMasking}` of the AES core, `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`) once at leaf start and logs both bits. The VCS build drives the port; the Verilator build ties it to 0, and this entry runs on VCS only. Evidence: `OBS-AES-SEC-PARAMS seed=<n> sec_masking=<0 or 1> sec_allow_forcing_masks=<0 or 1>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the AES status bit after a mismatched AUX or GCM pair (`aes.hjson:995` names the Control Register only), the level of vector bit 32 after a malformed pair is released, a status bit, an alert or a readback for the KMAC threshold target (not stated; logged only), the update error of the KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` (its cause is not stated; logged only), which receiver or which index of a receiver range pulses on an `ALERT_TEST` write or an update error (owned by `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`), the alert ping (it is disabled), a block status bit after a malformed encoding, or the fatal-fault path. `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` owns the `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` reset value after an AES reset and keeps it. `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` also grades the AUX register reset value.

'''''

[[sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test]]
===== 1.35 `sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (KMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 3 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip with the CPU-LSU master readback of the KMAC `INTR_STATE`, `STATUS`, `ERR_CODE` and `STATE` registers (approved); `sep_kmac_golden` for the SHA3-256 digest (FIPS 202). No observation tap. No inject beyond the unsupported configuration writes of the scenario.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the 8 supported cells, the order of the 6 unsupported cells with `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 0, the order of the 6 unsupported cells with it set, one message of 0 to 100 bytes for the recovery hash of each blocked cell, and last the message of the SHA3-128 cell recovery, the strength of the mode 2'b01 cell and its recovery message of 0 to 100 bytes. `reseed = 3` samples different orders, messages and strengths. The 21 cells, the SHA3-256 control and the mode 2'b01 cell are the same on every seed; only the strength of the mode 2'b01 cell is drawn.

*Objective*: SHAKE and cSHAKE accept only the strengths 128 and 256, and another strength gives an error when hashing starts. SHA3 with strength 128 is not one of the listed combinations and reports `UnexpectedModeStrength` (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/doc/theory_of_operation.md:784-785`). With `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 0 KMAC does not propagate `CMD.start` to the SHA3 engine. With the bit set the engine moves forward, and the error is still reported (`theory_of_operation.md:785` names no condition on the bit). `CMD.err_processed` returns the state machine to its reset state. `ERR_CODE` resets to 0; bits 31:24 hold the error code (`kmac.adoc:344`), and `UnexpectedModeStrength` is 0x06 (`theory_of_operation.md:726`). Bits 23:0 have no stated value.
