The ignored-command leg writes one unrecognised `CMD` value at IDLE with `ERR_BITS` still non-zero from the software-error leg, then writes the drawn value to `ERR_BITS`. The RND-clean leg runs the `RND` program after an OTBN reset with the entropy chain up and the TRNG running. The software-error-kind leg runs, after a control run of a legal variant, one program of the drawn kind with `software_errs_fatal` = 0. The bus-access leg, the last leg, holds OTBN at BUSY_EXECUTE with the TRNG reset, reads one drawn IMEM or DMEM word from the host, and resets OTBN. Error injection: a `CTRL` write while busy, a `CMD` write while busy, an unrecognised `CMD` value at IDLE, an illegal instruction in each `software_errs_fatal` setting, a `CMD` write at LOCKED, a software error of the drawn kind and a host memory read while OTBN executes. Extends `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`, which runs EXECUTE of an ADD, XOR or AND program and grades `ERR_BITS` and a DMEM result. This entry grades only the wipe commands, the status sequence, `INSN_CNT`, the busy-write rule of `CTRL`, the ignored `CMD` writes, the status arc of the initial wipe, the software-error reactions, LOCKED, the `ERR_BITS` clear, the `RND` run, the kind of a recoverable software error and the host memory read while OTBN executes. The owner of the open test keeps the ADD, XOR and AND results. The done-line check extends `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`, which raises OTBN done with `INTR_TEST` and brackets it with a write-one clear. This entry owns the OTBN line cells of the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`: the enable clear, the rise, the enable flip and the write-one clear with the enable clear, for the real completion event and for `INTR_TEST`. The enable-set `INTR_TEST` cell is a control, and the owner of the open test keeps its `INTR_TEST` walk. Functional coverage: `sep_otbn_op_cg` (`cp_cmd`, `x_cmd_first_busy`, `cp_cmd_ignored`, `cp_insn_cnt`, `cp_done_event`, `cp_post_reset_arc`; `cp_post_reset_done` is `ignore_bins`), Phase 1 `sep_otbn_execute_cg`, `sep_otbn_err_lock_cg` (`cp_ctrl_write_legality`, `cp_sw_error_end`, `cp_locked_hold_exit`, `cp_err_bits_clear`), `sep_otbn_op_cg` `cp_post_reset_done`, `cp_cmd_ignored_err`, `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_w1c_clear`, `cp_enable_flip`, `cp_hw_raise` otbn_done), `sep_otbn_err_lock_cg` (`cp_sw_err_kind`, `cp_fatal_cause`, `cp_rnd_clean`). `cp_sw_err_kind` is seed-dependent (one kind per seed; seeds 1 to 4 give the four kinds) and samples only the kind to which the label `otbn_sw_err_pass` rises after `CHK-OTBN-SWERR-KIND` passes; the label is held two clocks and then written 0. `cp_rnd_clean` has no label: the leaf evaluates every graded value except the sampled `ERR_BITS` read first and closes the window before that read when one fails, so a sample follows a pass. Every other owned bin of `sep_otbn_op_cg` and `sep_otbn_err_lock_cg`, `cp_intg_violation_end` and `cp_fatal_cause` among them, and the otbn_done bin of `cp_hw_raise`, is held by the sampler at its DUT event and samples only when the leaf writes the `otbn_cmd_pass` bit of the check that grades it, after that check passes and only while no check or control has failed (one bit per check: CHK-OTBN-CMD-BUSY, -CMD-END, -CMD-IGNORE, -CMDIGN-ERR, -INSN, -BUSYWR, -SWERR-FATAL, -LOCKED-HOLD, -SWERR-RECOV, -ERRCLR, -IMEM-INTG, -DMEM-INTG, -ILLEGAL-BUS, -DONE-LINE). The `x_line_gate`, `cp_w1c_clear` and `cp_enable_flip` cells of otbn_done are held at their DUT event and sample on the rise of the CHK-OTBN-DONE-LINE bit, which the leaf writes after each passing done-line run and after the passing `INTR_TEST` sub-leg; an OTBN reset drops the cells still held, so the line events of the other legs (for example the write-one clears of the command leg) never sample. The illegal `fall_without_write` bin of `cp_w1c_clear` samples at once. `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test_shard3` proves 21 bins: the execute bins of `cp_cmd`, `x_cmd_first_busy` and `cp_done_event`, `cp_post_reset_arc`, both bins of `cp_insn_cnt` and of `cp_cmd_ignored`, and `cp_cmd_ignored_err` (`sep_otbn_op_cg`); `cp_ctrl_write_legality`, `cp_err_bits_clear` and the recoverable bin of `cp_sw_error_end` (`sep_otbn_err_lock_cg`); and the otbn_done cells of `x_line_gate`, `cp_w1c_clear`, `cp_enable_flip` and `cp_hw_raise` (`sep_crypto_intr_cg`). `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test_shard2` proves `cp_sw_err_kind` and `cp_rnd_clean`. Twelve bins are proven only in the full entry: the sec_wipe_dmem and sec_wipe_imem bins of `cp_cmd`, `x_cmd_first_busy` and `cp_done_event`, which need a SEC_WIPE command at IDLE, and the fatal bin of `cp_sw_error_end`, both bins of `cp_locked_hold_exit`, both bins of `cp_intg_violation_end` and `cp_fatal_cause`, which need the LOCKED state. The full entry also fills the bins of both shard entries.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`. Bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Set `SW_RESET_N` to the baseline of the common rules with the bits of KMAC, AES, HMAC and ABR clear. Log the baseline.
. Draw the plan with `SepSeededRng`. Log `INFO-PLAN seed=<n>` with the sequence, the gaps, the operation, the operands, a, b, the order of the enable runs, k, the unrecognised `CMD` value, j and the `ERR_BITS` clear value, and the EXECUTE-only sequence and gaps of the shard 3 entry.
. Reset OTBN (bit 1 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high). Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE (a bounded wait). Log every `STATUS` value read.
. Poll `STATUS` until it reads 0x00 (the power-on wipe has ended), then expect the status probe to hold 0x00 from that read up to the clock before the gated OTBN reset falls (`CTRL-OTBN-INITDONE`; `theory_of_operation.md`:98 "After reset (init), OTBN performs a secure wipe of the internal state and then becomes idle.", :104 "If OTBN is idle, the STATUS register is set to IDLE.", 0x00 per `otbn.hjson`:536). After the reset expect 0x04 and then 0x00 on the status probe (CHK-OTBN-INITDONE). Read `INTR_STATE.done`, write 1 to it and read it again; log both values (`OBS-OTBN-INITDONE-DONE`): no sentence states `done` after the post-reset wipe.
. Command leg. Steps 6 to 9 run once for each command of the drawn sequence (in the shard 3 entry, of the EXECUTE-only sequence). Wait the drawn gap with no bus access.
. For EXECUTE, load the compute program into IMEM, stage the operands in DMEM and pre-seed the result word with the complement of the golden. Do this before every EXECUTE, because a wipe clears the memories. Before each staging wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and write 0 to `LOAD_CHECKSUM`. After the program words and the three DMEM words are written, read `LOAD_CHECKSUM` (`CHK-OTBN-LOAD-CKSUM`).
. Read `INTR_STATE.done` and expect 0. Read `STATUS` and expect IDLE. Write `CMD` with the command.
. Poll `STATUS` until it reads IDLE (a bounded wait of 200000 `clk_i` cycles, the outer limit of the leaf; a timeout fails the check). Record every value read. Log the clock count from the `CMD` write to IDLE.
. Read `INTR_STATE.done`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` and read it as 0. After the last command take t_cmd as the longest logged count. Every later wait for `STATUS` = IDLE is bounded by 8 x t_cmd. After a SEC_WIPE command, read two words of the wiped memory (DMEM offsets 0x0 and 0x8, or IMEM words 0 and 1) and log them (`OBS-OTBN-WIPE-MEM`).
. `INSN_CNT` leg. Steps 10 to 12 run for the program P(a), then for P(b). P(k) is the compute program with k extra independent ADD instructions. Reset OTBN, wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and clear `INTR_STATE.done` as in steps 3 and 4. Read `INSN_CNT` and expect 0 (the reset value in `otbn.adoc`).
. Load P(k) into IMEM. Stage the operands in DMEM and pre-seed the result word with the complement of the golden. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE (a bounded wait).
. Read `INSN_CNT` and the DMEM result word.
. Done-line leg. Steps 13 to 16 run for two EXECUTE runs in the drawn order, one with `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1 and one with it = 0. Reset OTBN, wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` as in steps 3 and 4, so that the `done` of the earlier run does not remain. Read `INTR_STATE.done` and vector bit 29 as 0. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` with the value of the run and read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0.
. Load the compute program and the operands. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Sample vector bit 29 of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` at every clock from the `CMD` write. In the enable-set run, poll `INTR_STATE.done` and take a clock mark before each read; the lower bound of the rise is the mark of the last read that shows `done` = 0. Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and `INTR_STATE.done` = 1 (a bounded wait).
. With the enable clear, keep bit 29 under watch for a window of 2 x t_cmd (logged). Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1. Wait for bit 29 = 1 (a bounded wait of 64 clocks). With the enable set, wait for the first rise of bit 29 after the `CMD` write (a bounded wait of 64 clocks after the reads that show `done` = 1 and IDLE). In both runs bit 29 then has no fall until the write-one clear of step 16.
. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`. Read it as 0 and read bit 29 as 0.
. `INTR_TEST` leg. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 0, then write `INTR_TEST.done` = 1. Read `INTR_STATE.done` as 1 and keep bit 29 under watch for the window. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1 and wait for bit 29 = 1 (bit 29 then has no fall until the next enable write). Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 0 and wait for bit 29 = 0 (a bounded wait of 64 clocks). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` with the enable clear, then read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0 and bit 29 as 0. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-DONE-LINE`, the enable-set cell): write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1, write `INTR_TEST.done` = 1, wait for bit 29 = 1 with no fall before the write-one clear, then write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` and read bit 29 as 0.
. Busy-write leg. Run the bring-up if the chain is not up. Reset OTBN and wait for `STATUS` = IDLE. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`. Read `CTRL` and expect 0.
. Load a program that reads the RND source into IMEM. The leaf builds it from the base instruction set of OTBN.
. Assert the TRNG reset (bit 5 of `SW_RESET_N` low) and hold it, so that the RND request gets no acknowledge. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Wait for `STATUS` = 0x01 (a bounded wait).
. This is the error injection. Point: `CTRL`. Kind: `software_errs_fatal` = 1 at `STATUS` 0x01. Expected response: the write is ignored. Write `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` = 1, k times. After each write read `CTRL` and expect 0, and read `STATUS` and expect 0x01. This is a second error injection. Point: `CMD`. Kind: `CMD` = SEC_WIPE_DMEM (0xc3) at `STATUS` 0x01. Expected response: the write is ignored. Write `CMD` = 0xc3, j times. After each write read `STATUS` and expect 0x01 (CHK-OTBN-CMD-IGNORE).
. Release the TRNG reset. Run the bring-up. Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE (a bounded wait). Read `ERR_BITS` and expect 0 in every bit except `rnd_rep_chk_fail` (CHK-OTBN-BUSYWR-ERR); record that bit as `INFO-OTBN-BUSYWR-RNDREP`. Read `STATUS` and expect it is not 0xFF.
. Write `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` = 1 while OTBN is idle. Read `CTRL` and expect 1. Reset OTBN.
. RND-clean leg. With the entropy chain up and the TRNG running, reset OTBN (the reset arc is 0x04 then 0x00), write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`, read `CTRL` and expect `software_errs_fatal` = 0, load the `RND` program, read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0 and start a watch on `crypto_edn_ack_o[2]`. Open the graded window and write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Poll `STATUS` back to back until IDLE in a bounded wait, read `INTR_STATE.done` and read `ERR_BITS` (`CHK-OTBN-RND-CLEAN`). Every value except `ERR_BITS` is read first, and the window closes before the `ERR_BITS` read when one of them fails. Log bit 6 as `INFO-OTBN-RND-CLEAN-RNDREP`.
. Integrity leg, run once for IMEM and once for DMEM. Start a watch on the OTBN alert receivers `[2:1]` (`crypto_alert_pulse_probe_o`). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`. Load the compute program and its operands. Set the flip memory and bit (the drawn IMEM bit; for DMEM, the drawn bit of the drawn word) and keep `otbn_intg_flip_en_i` at 0. Read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0 and require no pulse on `[2:1]` since the watch began. This is the error injection. Point: the OTBN memory read word, ahead of the integrity check. Kind: one inverted bit. Expected response: a fatal error. Start the arming of the inject: the leaf drives `otbn_intg_flip_en_i` to 1 at the first clock that the status probe shows BUSY_EXECUTE. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Poll `STATUS` until it reads IDLE or LOCKED (a bounded wait of 8 x t_cmd). Read the status probe, `INTR_STATE.done`, `ERR_BITS`, `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` and `otbn_intg_flip_count_o`, and the pulses on `[2:1]` since the `CMD` write. Drop `otbn_intg_flip_en_i`, then count the rises of each index of `[2:1]` in a 256-clock window while OTBN stays LOCKED, with no new stimulus. Reset OTBN, read `STATUS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`, and watch `[2:1]` for 256 clocks (CHK-OTBN-IMEM-INTG, CHK-OTBN-DMEM-INTG). Log the high clocks of each index of `[2:1]` after the `CMD` write and the rises of each index in the LOCKED window (`OBS-OTBN-INTG-ALERT-IDX`) and the full `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` (`OBS-OTBN-INTG-CAUSE`).
. Software-error leg, fatal (not in the shard 3 entry). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`. Write `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` = 1 at IDLE and read it as 1. Load the `unimp` program (`CSRRW x0, 0xC00, x0`, then `ecall`). Read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0. This is the error injection. Point: the OTBN program. Kind: an illegal instruction with `software_errs_fatal` = 1. Expected response: a fatal error. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Poll `STATUS` until it reads IDLE or LOCKED (a bounded wait of 8 x t_cmd). Read `INTR_STATE.done`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` (CHK-OTBN-SWERR-FATAL).
. LOCKED hold (not in the shard 3 entry). This is the error injection. Point: `CMD`. Kind: EXECUTE at LOCKED. Expected response: the write is ignored. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Poll `STATUS` back to back for a window of 4 x t_cmd. Read `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`. Reset OTBN, wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and read `STATUS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` (CHK-OTBN-LOCKED-HOLD).
. Software-error leg, recoverable. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`. Write `CTRL` = 0 and read it as 0. Load the `unimp` program and read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Poll `STATUS` until it reads IDLE or LOCKED (a bounded wait). Read `INTR_STATE.done`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` (CHK-OTBN-SWERR-RECOV). Log `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` as `OBS-OTBN-SWERR-CAUSE`. Do not clear `ERR_BITS`.
. Ignored-command leg. Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE (a bounded wait) and write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`. Read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0. Read `ERR_BITS` (non-zero from the recoverable run). This is the error injection. Point: `CMD`. Kind: the drawn unrecognised value at `STATUS` IDLE. Expected response: the write is ignored. Write `CMD` with the drawn value. Poll `STATUS` back to back for a window of 4 times t_cmd (logged). Read `INTR_STATE.done`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`, and expect `ERR_BITS` unchanged (CHK-OTBN-CMDIGN-ERR).
. `ERR_BITS` clear. Read `STATUS` as IDLE. Write the drawn value to `ERR_BITS`. Read `ERR_BITS` and expect 0 (CHK-OTBN-ERRCLR).
. Software-error-kind leg. With the window closed, run the control first: reset OTBN, clear `done`, read `CTRL` as 0, load the program of the drawn kind with a legal instruction in place of the faulting one, read `done` as 0, write `CMD` = EXECUTE, poll `STATUS` to IDLE, and require `done` = 1 and `ERR_BITS` = 0 (`CTRL-OTBN-SWERR-KIND`). Then run the faulting program the same way with the window opened before the `CMD` write: `lw x10, off(x0)` at the drawn offset 1 to 3 (BAD_DATA_ADDR), `jalr x0, x0, 2` (BAD_INSN_ADDR), `jalr x0, x1, 0` with an empty call stack (CALL_STACK) or `loopi 0, 1` with a `nop` body (LOOP), each followed by `ecall`. Read `done`, then `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` (`CHK-OTBN-SWERR-KIND`). After a PASS write the label `otbn_sw_err_pass` to the kind plus 1, hold it two clocks and write 0. Log the full `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` as `OBS-OTBN-SWERR-KIND-CAUSE`.
. Bus-access leg, last because it locks OTBN. With the window closed, reset OTBN, clear `done`, load the `RND` program and, for a DMEM target, write the drawn non-zero word. Read `STATUS` as IDLE and read the target word as the staged non-zero value (`CTRL-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS`). Grade and end the entropy scoreboard, hold the TRNG reset and wait until `trng_reset_active_probe_o` is 1. Open the window, write `CMD` = EXECUTE and poll `STATUS` until BUSY_EXECUTE. Issue one host `AxSIZE` 2 read of the target word, poll `STATUS` until LOCKED, then read `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` (`CHK-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS`, phase lock). Log the response code of the read and the full `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` as `OBS-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS-CAUSE`. Close the window. Release the TRNG reset, bring up the chain with the URND sink in observe mode, reset OTBN, and require the reset arc 0x04 then 0x00, `STATUS` = IDLE and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` = 0 (phase exit).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OTBN-CMD-END
|After each of EXECUTE (0xd8), SEC_WIPE_DMEM (0xc3) and SEC_WIPE_IMEM (0x1e), `STATUS` reaches IDLE (0x00), no value read on the way is 0xFF, `INTR_STATE.done` reads 1, and `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` read 0 (`otbn.adoc`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE`, `ERR_BITS`, `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`). A wrong DUT hangs (the bounded wait times out), locks (0xFF), raises no `done`, or sets an error bit. Anchor: `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` readback. Control: `INTR_STATE.done` reads 0 before the command, so the 1 comes from the command. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-CMD-END PASS seed=<n> idx=<i> cmd=0x<c> pre=0x00 final=0x00 locked_seen=0 done_state=1 err_bits=0x0 fatal_cause=0x0 ctrl_done_before=0`.
|CHK-OTBN-CMD-BUSY
|After each command the first non-IDLE `STATUS` code after the `CMD` write is the busy code of that command: 0x01 for EXECUTE, 0x02 for SEC_WIPE_DMEM and 0x03 for SEC_WIPE_IMEM. The code 0x04 may follow it before IDLE (`otbn.adoc`, `STATUS`: each busy value represents an operation started by a write to `CMD`). A wrong DUT shows another busy code, or goes to IDLE with no busy code. Anchor: the OTBN status probe (an observation tap), sampled on every clock, because a polled read can miss a short code. Control: `INTR_STATE.done` reads 0 before the command. The leaf logs the polled values as `OBS-OTBN-STATUS-SEQ seed=<n> idx=<i> cmd=0x<c> polled=<codes>`. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-CMD-BUSY PASS seed=<n> idx=<i> cmd=0x<c> first_busy=0x<b> expect=0x<b>`.
|CHK-OTBN-INSN
|For the programs of a and b extra instructions `INSN_CNT` equals the instruction count of each straight-line program (the 7-instruction compute program, `ecall` included, plus the extras) and differs by b minus a, and the DMEM result of each run equals the Python golden (`otbn.adoc`, `INSN_CNT`: the number of executed instructions). After each OTBN reset of the leg the OTBN status probe shows 0x04 at the first clock after the gated reset releases, and then 0x00 within the bounded wait for `STATUS` = IDLE (`otbn.adoc`, `STATUS`: reset value 0x4; 0x00 is IDLE). A wrong DUT with a stuck counter shows a difference of 0, and one that does not reset to 0 shows a non-zero read after the OTBN reset. A DUT that leaves the reset in another state, or never reaches IDLE, fails the reset arc. The pre-seeded complement of the golden makes a program that never stores its result fail. Anchor: `INSN_CNT` and DMEM readback, and the OTBN status probe (an observation tap) for the reset arc. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-INSN PASS seed=<n> a=<na> b=<nb> cnt_reset=0,0 cnt_a=<ca> cnt_b=<cb> expect_cnt=<7+na>,<7+nb> diff=<d> expect=<nb-na> result_eq_golden=1,1 reset_arc=a:0x04,0x00 b:0x04,0x00`.
|CHK-OTBN-DONE-LINE
|Vector bit 29 (source 30) equals `INTR_STATE.done` AND `INTR_ENABLE.done` at every sample (`interrupts.adoc`, `otbn.adoc`, `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `INTR_TEST`). With the enable clear a finished command sets the state bit and bit 29 stays low for the window. After the enable write bit 29 rises, and after a write of 0 to the enable it falls. After the write-one clear of the state bit it falls, with the enable set and with the enable clear. With the enable set, the first rise of bit 29 after the `CMD` write is at or after the mark of the last `INTR_STATE` read that showed `done` = 0, and at the latest 64 clocks after the reads that show `done` = 1 and `STATUS` IDLE. While the state bit and the enable are both 1, bit 29 has no fall up to the write-one clear (or the write of 0 to the enable). An `INTR_TEST.done` write sets the state bit and the line follows the enable in the same way; the enable-set `INTR_TEST` cell is a control that the open owner grades. A wrong DUT that raises the line without the enable, ignores the enable, raises it before the command ends, drops it while state and enable are 1, or keeps the line after the clear shows it at one of these samples. Anchor: `INTR_STATE` and `STATUS` readback and the vector probe sampled on every clock. Control: the rise after the enable write in the same run, the enable-set run of the same seed, and the enable-set `INTR_TEST` cell (`CTRL-OTBN-DONE-LINE`). Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-DONE-LINE PASS seed=<n> via=cmd enable=0 vec29_falls_before_w1c=0 state=1 vec29_high_clk=0 window_clk=<w> then_enable=1 vec29_rose=1 vec29=1 then_w1c_state=0 w1c_vec29=0`, `CHK-OTBN-DONE-LINE PASS seed=<n> via=cmd enable=1 state=1 vec29_rose=1 rise_clk=<r> last_done0_clk=<l> cmd_clk=<c> rise_after_done0=1 vec29=1 ...` and `CHK-OTBN-DONE-LINE PASS seed=<n> via=test enable=0 state=1 vec29_high_clk=0 window_clk=<w> then_enable=1 vec29_rose=1 vec29_falls_while_enabled=0 then_enable0_vec29_fell=1 ...`; the control logs `CTRL-OTBN-DONE-LINE seen seed=<n> via=test enable=1 vec29_rose=1 vec29_falls_before_w1c=0 w1c_vec29=0`.
|CHK-OTBN-CMD-IGNORE
|A `CMD` write with an unrecognised value at `STATUS` IDLE leaves `STATUS` at IDLE at every poll of a window of 4 times t_cmd and `INTR_STATE.done` at 0, and a `CMD` write of SEC_WIPE_DMEM while `STATUS` is 0x01 leaves `STATUS` at 0x01 after each of the j writes (`otbn.hjson`, `CMD`: writes are ignored if OTBN is not idle; unrecognized commands are ignored). A wrong DUT that starts a command from an unrecognised code shows a busy status or a `done`; a DUT that accepts a command while busy shows a `STATUS` other than 0x01. CHK-OTBN-CMDIGN-ERR grades `ERR_BITS` after the unrecognised value. Anchor: `STATUS` and `INTR_STATE` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-CMD-IGNORE`, logged at the end of the leaf): the recognised commands of the command leg of the same seed end with `INTR_STATE.done` = 1 from a `done` read as 0 before the command (CHK-OTBN-CMD-END), so the `done` read of this check can show a started command, and `STATUS` reads 0x01 before the busy writes (CHK-OTBN-BUSYWR); a missing control logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. Each ignored `CMD` write is logged with its B response. The write of an unrecognised value at IDLE is a legal write of a `wo` register that no rule refuses, so it must answer OKAY and the leaf fails on any other code. A `CMD` write while busy is refused ("Writes are ignored if OTBN is not idle") and no text states its code, so its B response is logged and not graded. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-CMD-IGNORE PASS seed=<n> case=<idle_unrecognised or busy_wipe> cmd=0x<c> status=<0x00 or 0x01,...> done=<0 or n/a> window_clk=<w or n/a> writes=<1 or j> bresp=<OKAY for idle_unrecognised; the logged code of each write for busy_wipe>`.
|CHK-OTBN-BUSYWR
|A write of `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` = 1 while `STATUS` is 0x01 is ignored: `CTRL` reads 0 after each of the k writes, `STATUS` still reads 0x01, and after the chain returns `STATUS` is not 0xFF (`otbn.adoc`, `CTRL`: writes are ignored if OTBN is not idle). The busy hold relies on the stall of an `RND` read (`csr.yml`, `rnd`) and on the missing acknowledgement during the TRNG adapter clear (`trng.adoc`, Reset and Recovery Boundaries); if `STATUS` does not reach 0x01 the bounded wait fails the check. CHK-OTBN-BUSYWR-ERR grades `ERR_BITS` after the run. A wrong DUT that stores the write shows `CTRL` = 1. Anchor: `STATUS` and `CTRL` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-BUSYWR`): the same write while idle reads back 1, so the register can hold the bit. The B response of each write while busy is logged as `ctrl_bresp` and is not graded, because no text states the code of a write that OTBN ignores. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-BUSYWR PASS seed=<n> ctrl_pre=0 busy_status=0x01 busy_writes=<k> ctrl_after_busy_write=0,... ctrl_bresp=<r>,... status_after_busy_write=0x01,... idle_after_release=1 status_after_release=0x<s> ctrl_after_idle_write=1`.
|CHK-OTBN-INITDONE
|After the first OTBN reset of the leaf the OTBN status probe shows 0x04 at the first clock after the gated reset releases, and its next value is 0x00 (`theory_of_operation.md`:103 "After reset, OTBN is busy with the internal secure wipe and the STATUS register is set to BUSY_SEC_WIPE_INT."; :98 "After reset (init), OTBN performs a secure wipe of the internal state and then becomes idle."; :104 "If OTBN is idle, the STATUS register is set to IDLE."; codes 0x04 and 0x00 from `otbn.hjson` `STATUS`). A wrong DUT that skips the wipe, or does not reach IDLE after it, shows another arc. Anchor: the status probe and the gated reset probe. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-INITDONE`): the status probe before the reset pulse is not 0x04, so the 0x04 after the release comes from the reset. `INTR_STATE.done` after the wipe is logged only (`OBS-OTBN-INITDONE-DONE`): no sentence states its value (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>> of the verification plan). Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-INITDONE PASS: seed=<n> reset_arc=arc=0x04,0x00`; `CTRL-OTBN-INITDONE seen: seed=<n> status_before_reset=0x00`; `OBS-OTBN-INITDONE-DONE seed=<n> done=<d> done_after_w1c=<d> reason=done_after_reset_wipe_unstated`.
|CHK-OTBN-SWERR-FATAL
|With `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` = 1 (read back 1 at IDLE) the `unimp` program ends with `STATUS` = LOCKED (0xFF), `INTR_STATE.done` = 1 (read 0 before), `ERR_BITS` non-zero with `fatal_software` (bit 23) set and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE.fatal_software` (bit 7) set. Each value comes from one sentence: `unimp` is an ILLEGAL_INSN error (`base-insns.yml`, `csrrw` and `unimp`), a software error (`theory_of_operation.md`, List of Errors); with `software_errs_fatal` set a software error becomes a fatal error, which is treated as in Reaction to Fatal Errors (Errors); that section sets `ERR_BITS` to a non-zero value, sets `done` and sets `STATUS` to LOCKED; FATAL_SOFTWARE is the error "a software error was seen and `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` was set"; `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` names the error and `ERR_BITS` reflects it when OTBN was running (Alerts). A wrong DUT that keeps the error recoverable ends at 0x00; one that does not record the cause leaves the bit at 0. Anchor: `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-SWERR-FATAL`): the same program with `software_errs_fatal` = 0 ends at 0x00 with no 0xFF read. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-SWERR-FATAL PASS: seed=<n> ctrl=1 status=<codes> final=0xff expect=0xff done=1 pre_done=0 err_bits=0x<e> fatal_cause=0x<f> clk=<c> bound=<b>`.
|CHK-OTBN-LOCKED-HOLD
|At LOCKED a `CMD` = EXECUTE write leaves `STATUS` at 0xFF at every poll of a window of 4 x t_cmd and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` unchanged; after an OTBN reset the status probe shows 0x04 then 0x00, `STATUS` reads IDLE and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` reads 0 (`theory_of_operation.md`, Operational States: the locked state is terminal and needs an OTBN reset; `otbn.hjson`, `CMD`: writes are ignored if OTBN is not idle; `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`: the value does not change until OTBN is reset, reset value 0). A wrong DUT that leaves LOCKED on a command, or keeps the cause after the reset, shows it. Anchor: `STATUS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` readback and the status probe. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-LOCKED-HOLD`, logged at the end of the leaf): a recognised command at IDLE of the command leg starts an operation and sets `done` (CHK-OTBN-CMD-END), so a window of 4 x t_cmd can show a started command. The `CMD` write is logged with its B response and the response is not graded, because no text states the code of a write that OTBN ignores. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-LOCKED-HOLD PASS: seed=<n> cmd=0xd8 bresp=<r> hold_status=0xff hold_polls=<p> window_clk=<w> fatal_before=0x<f> fatal_after_cmd=0x<f> reset_arc=arc=0x04,0x00 status_after_reset=0x00 fatal_after_reset=0x0`.
|CHK-OTBN-SWERR-RECOV
|With `software_errs_fatal` = 0 (read back 0) the `unimp` program ends with `STATUS` = IDLE and no 0xFF read, `INTR_STATE.done` = 1 (read 0 before) and `ERR_BITS.illegal_insn` (bit 3) = 1 (`theory_of_operation.md`, Errors: software errors are recoverable unless `software_errs_fatal` is set; Reaction to Recoverable Errors: `ERR_BITS` non-zero, `done` set, `STATUS` set to IDLE; `base-insns.yml`, `csrrw`: setting bit `illegal_insn`). The other `ERR_BITS` bits and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` are logged only (`OBS-OTBN-SWERR-CAUSE`): no sentence states them here. A wrong DUT that ignores the illegal instruction runs the `ecall` and leaves `illegal_insn` at 0; one that locks shows 0xFF. Anchor: `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_BITS` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-SWERR-RECOV`): the compute runs of the command leg end with `ERR_BITS` = 0 (CHK-OTBN-CMD-END), so the bit is not stuck at 1. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-SWERR-RECOV PASS: seed=<n> ctrl=0 status=<codes> final=0x00 expect=0x00 done=1 pre_done=0 illegal_insn=1 clk=<c> bound=<b>` and `OBS-OTBN-SWERR-CAUSE seed=<n> err_bits=0x<e> fatal_cause=0x<f>`.
|CHK-OTBN-ERRCLR
|At IDLE a write of the drawn 32-bit value to `ERR_BITS` makes it read 0 (`theory_of_operation.md`, Alerts: writing any value to `ERR_BITS` clears it to zero when OTBN is not running; `otbn.hjson`, `ERR_BITS`). A wrong DUT that keeps the bits or stores the written value shows a non-zero read. Anchor: `STATUS` and `ERR_BITS` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-ERRCLR`): `ERR_BITS` reads non-zero before the write. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-ERRCLR PASS: seed=<n> status=0x00 written=0x<v> err_bits_before=0x<e> err_bits_after=0x0`.
|CHK-OTBN-BUSYWR-ERR
|After the RND program of the busy-write leg ends at IDLE, `ERR_BITS` reads 0 in every bit except `rnd_rep_chk_fail` (`theory_of_operation.md`, Software Execution: `ERR_BITS` is 0 for a successful execution; the program ends with `ecall`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc`: the FIPS tag is tied high, so `rnd_fips_chk_fail` does not set). `rnd_rep_chk_fail` sets when two EDN words are equal (List of Errors), a chance outcome, so it is recorded as `INFO-OTBN-BUSYWR-RNDREP` and not graded. A wrong DUT that records an error for the ignored busy writes shows a non-zero bit. Anchor: `ERR_BITS` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-BUSYWR-ERR`): the recoverable software-error run of the same seed reads `ERR_BITS` non-zero, so the register records errors. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-BUSYWR-ERR PASS: seed=<n> idle_after_release=1 err_bits=0x<e> err_bits_without_rnd_rep=0x0 expect=0x0` and `INFO-OTBN-BUSYWR-RNDREP seed=<n> rnd_rep_chk_fail=<0 or 1>`.
|CHK-OTBN-IMEM-INTG
|With one bit of every IMEM word that the core fetches inverted ahead of the integrity check, the EXECUTE run ends with `STATUS` = LOCKED (0xFF) on the bus and on the OTBN status probe, `INTR_STATE.done` = 1 (read 0 before), `ERR_BITS.imem_intg_violation` (bit 16) set and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE.imem_intg_violation` (bit 0) set. No pulse occurs on crypto alert `[2:1]` from the program load to the `CMD` write, at least one pulse occurs from the `CMD` write to the LOCKED read, and at least one rise occurs on `[2:1]` in a 256-clock LOCKED window after the LOCKED reads and the flip drop and before the OTBN reset, so the alert repeats after the error with no new stimulus. After the OTBN reset the status probe shows 0x04 then 0x00, `STATUS` reads IDLE, `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` reads 0, and `[2:1]` is high on no clock of a 256-clock window after the reset, the length of the LOCKED window ("A fatal alert can only be cleared by resetting OTBN through the `rst_ni` line", `theory_of_operation.md:314`; each bit of `sep_crypto_gated_rst_no` and `otbn_sw_rst_n` reset OTBN, `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:60-66`, `:97`). The rise count in the LOCKED window is the control that a window of that length sees the repeat, so a fatal alert that keeps pulsing after the reset fails. Sources: `theory_of_operation.md` (IMEM_INTG_VIOLATION is a fatal error: "Data read from the instruction memory failed the integrity checks."; "A fatal error is raised whenever a data integrity violation is detected"; Reaction to Fatal Errors: `ERR_BITS` non-zero, `done` set, `STATUS` set to LOCKED, fatal alert; "A fatal alert is a continuously triggered alert" that "can only be cleared by resetting OTBN", `theory_of_operation.md:311-314`); `crypto.adoc:125-126` (the receiver pulses `alert_o` when the handshake starts, so each repeat is a rise); `registers.md` (`ERR_BITS` bit 16, `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` bit 0, LOCKED 0xFF must be reset to unlock); `crypto.adoc` (OTBN receivers `[2:1]`). The check grades the named `ERR_BITS` bit, the named cause bit a pulse on the range and a rise on the range in the LOCKED window; which index of `[2:1]` pulses, the repeat period, and the other bits of `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`, are logged only (`OBS-OTBN-INTG-ALERT-IDX`, `OBS-OTBN-INTG-CAUSE`), because no sentence names them. A wrong DUT that treats the error as recoverable ends at IDLE, one that sets no cause bit leaves it at 0, one that does not raise the alert shows no pulse, one that raises it once and does not repeat it shows no rise in the LOCKED window, and one that stays locked after the reset fails the exit. Anchor: `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` readback, the status probe and the alert receiver probe. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-IMEM-INTG`): the inject was armed during BUSY_EXECUTE and `otbn_intg_flip_count_o` is non-zero at LOCKED, so a corrupted read response reached the integrity check; with no flip the compute runs of the command leg end with `ERR_BITS` = 0 and no `[2:1]` pulse (CHK-OTBN-CMD-END). Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-IMEM-INTG PASS seed=<n> mem=imem flip_bit=<b> status=<codes> final=0xff probe=0xff done=1 pre_done=0 err_bit=1 fatal_bit=1 alert_high_before_cmd=0 alert_high_after_cmd=<n> alert_rises_locked=<r> locked_window_clk=256 reset_arc=arc=0x04,0x00 status_after_reset=0x00 fatal_after_reset=0x0 alert_high_after_reset=0 after_reset_window_clk=256`.
|CHK-OTBN-DMEM-INTG
|The same effects for a DMEM word that a load of the program reads, inverted ahead of the integrity check of the DMEM: `STATUS` = LOCKED, `done` = 1, `ERR_BITS.dmem_intg_violation` (bit 17) and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE.dmem_intg_violation` (bit 1) set, no pulse on `[2:1]` before the `CMD` write, a pulse after it, a rise in the LOCKED window, the exit after the OTBN reset, and no high clock on `[2:1]` in the 256-clock window after the reset. Sources: `theory_of_operation.md` (DMEM_INTG_VIOLATION is a fatal error: "Data read from the data memory failed the integrity checks."; the same fatal-error sections as CHK-OTBN-IMEM-INTG); `registers.md` (`ERR_BITS` bit 17, `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` bit 1); `crypto.adoc` (OTBN receivers `[2:1]`). The same logged-only values and the same control (`CTRL-OTBN-DMEM-INTG`). Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-DMEM-INTG PASS` with the fields of CHK-OTBN-IMEM-INTG and `mem=dmem`.
|CHK-OTBN-CMDIGN-ERR
|The unrecognised `CMD` value at IDLE leaves `ERR_BITS` at its value before the write (`otbn.hjson`, `CMD`: unrecognized commands are ignored; `theory_of_operation.md`, Software Execution: `ERR_BITS` is set at the end of an operation). A wrong DUT that starts an operation or clears `ERR_BITS` on the write reads another value. Anchor: `ERR_BITS` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-CMDIGN-ERR`): `ERR_BITS` is non-zero before the write (the recoverable run), so a clear is visible. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-CMDIGN-ERR PASS: seed=<n> cmd=0x<c> err_bits_before=0x<e> err_bits=0x<e>`.
|CHK-OTBN-RND-CLEAN
|The run of the `RND` program, after an OTBN reset whose arc is 0x04 then 0x00, reads `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` = 0 and `INTR_STATE.done` = 0 before the `CMD` write, ends at IDLE in a bounded wait and never reads 0xFF on the way, reads `INTR_STATE.done` = 1, shows at least one rise of `crypto_edn_ack_o[2]` (the OTBN RND client, `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:92-93`) between the `CMD` write and IDLE, and reads `ERR_BITS` 0 in every bit except `rnd_rep_chk_fail` (`theory_of_operation.md:137`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc:68-69`: the FIPS tag is tied high, so no FIPS-tag failure sets). Bit 6 is a chance outcome and is logged as `INFO-OTBN-RND-CLEAN-RNDREP`. A DUT that records an RND error on a tagged word, never ends, or is not served by the EDN fails. Anchor: `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_BITS` readback and the EDN acknowledge. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-RND-CLEAN PASS seed=<n> reset_arc=<a> ctrl=0 pre_done=0 status=<codes> idle=1 done=1 rnd_ack_rises=<k> err_bits=0x<e> err_bits_without_rnd_rep=0x0 expect=0x0 clk=<c> bound=<b>`.
|CHK-OTBN-SWERR-KIND
|With `CTRL.software_errs_fatal` = 0 (read back 0) and `INTR_STATE.done` = 0 before the `CMD` write, the program of the drawn software error ends at IDLE in a bounded wait, never reads 0xFF, reads `done` = 1, and has the `ERR_BITS` field of that error set. The seed draws one of four errors: `BAD_DATA_ADDR` (`lw` at an offset of 1 to 3 from address 0; "if the computed address is not a valid 4-byte aligned DMEM address", `base-insns.yml:284`), `BAD_INSN_ADDR` (`jalr` to PC 2; "if the computed address is not a valid PC", `:380`), `CALL_STACK` (`jalr x0, x1, 0` with an empty call stack, `:38`) and `LOOP` (`loopi` with zero iterations, `:636`). All four are software errors (`theory_of_operation.md:211-236`) and recoverable with `software_errs_fatal` = 0 (`:153`, `:168-170`). A DUT that does not detect the error, sets another field, locks, or never ends fails. The other `ERR_BITS` bits and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` are logged only (`OBS-OTBN-SWERR-KIND-CAUSE`): no sentence states them. Anchor: `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_BITS` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-SWERR-KIND`, window closed, before the graded run): the same program with a legal instruction in place of the faulting one ends at IDLE with `ERR_BITS` = 0, which also shows that the `loopi` encoding of the leaf is valid. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-SWERR-KIND PASS seed=<n> kind=<k> off=<o> reset_arc=<a> ctrl=0 pre_done=0 status=<codes> final=0x00 expect=0x00 done=1 err_bits=0x<e> err_mask=0x<m> kind_bit=1 clk=<c> bound=<b>`.
|CHK-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS
|Phase lock. With the TRNG reset held and the `RND` program executing, one host `AxSIZE` 2 read of a staged non-zero IMEM or DMEM word starts while `STATUS` is BUSY_EXECUTE: the read returns 0, the OTBN status probe shows BUSY_EXECUTE at the start of the read and never IDLE or X after it, `STATUS` polls to LOCKED (0xFF), and `ERR_BITS.illegal_bus_access` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE.illegal_bus_access` are set (`theory_of_operation.md:30-31`: reads return zero and the access is a fatal error with the code `ILLEGAL_BUS_ACCESS`; class fatal, `:286-288`; `:312-313`). A DUT that serves the stored word, does not lock, or records another cause fails. Phase exit, window closed: after the TRNG release, the chain bring-up and an OTBN reset, the status probe shows 0x04 then 0x00, `STATUS` reads IDLE and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` reads 0 (`theory_of_operation.md:114`; `otbn.hjson:672-673`). The response code of the host read and the full `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` are logged only (`OBS-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS-CAUSE`): no sentence states the code. Anchor: the status probe, the host read data, and `STATUS`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` readback. Control (`CTRL-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS`, window closed): the same host read at IDLE returns the staged non-zero word, so a zero read at BUSY_EXECUTE comes from the busy state. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS PASS seed=<n> phase=lock mem=<imem or dmem> idx=<i> trng_held=1 busy_polled=<codes> status_at_ar=0x01 probe_after_rd=<codes> staged=0x<w> rdata=0x00000000 expect_rdata=0x00000000 status=<codes> expect=0xff err_bit=1 err_mask=0x<m> fatal_bit=1 fatal_mask=0x<m> clk=<c> bound=<b>` and `CHK-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS PASS seed=<n> phase=exit reset_arc=<a> status_after_reset=0x00 fatal_after_reset=0x0`.
|CHK-OTBN-LOAD-CKSUM
|At each compute staging, `LOAD_CHECKSUM` reads the CRC-32-IEEE of the stream of 48-bit records `{imem, idx, wdata}`, one per write since the write of 0 to `LOAD_CHECKSUM`. "The CRC used is the 32-bit CRC-32-IEEE checksum" and is compatible with the `binascii` crc32 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/doc/theory_of_operation.md:447-448`); "Each write is treated as a 48b value, `{imem, idx, wdata}`" over the writes since the last write to `LOAD_CHECKSUM` (`:449-453`). `imem` is 1 for an IMEM write, `idx` is the word index in 15 bits and `wdata` is the 32-bit word. The text does not state the byte order of a record, so the check accepts the little-endian and the big-endian 6-byte record. The golden stream is the program words to IMEM, then the three operand words to DMEM. A DUT that drops a write, counts one twice or takes another record layout fails. Anchor: `LOAD_CHECKSUM` readback after the staging. Evidence: `CHK-OTBN-LOAD-CKSUM PASS seed=<n> writes=<k> got=0x<g> want_le=0x<a> want_be=0x<b>`.
|OBS-OTBN-CKSUM-ORDER
|Not a graded check. The byte order of a `LOAD_CHECKSUM` record is not stated. The leaf logs which of the two orders matched, or `none` after a failed `CHK-OTBN-LOAD-CKSUM`. Evidence: `OBS-OTBN-CKSUM-ORDER seed=<n> order=<little, big or none>`.
|OBS-OTBN-WIPE-MEM
|Not a graded check. The wipe replaces the memory scrambling key and makes the stored data unusable (OpenTitan `otbn/doc/theory_of_operation.md`, Data Memory and Instruction Memory Secure Wipe; `otbn/doc/registers.md`, `CMD`: securely removes all contents), but no sentence states the value that a bus read returns after it, and `hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc` ties the OTP scramble-key handshake to fixed values. After each SEC_WIPE command the leaf reads two words of the wiped memory over the bus and logs them. Evidence: `OBS-OTBN-WIPE-MEM seed=<n> idx=<i> cmd=0x<c> mem=<dmem or imem> words=['0x<w0>', '0x<w1>']`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test grades the command and status sequence, the done line, `INSN_CNT`, the busy-write rule of `CTRL`, the status arc of the initial wipe, the reactions to an illegal instruction in both `software_errs_fatal` settings, the LOCKED hold and exit, the `ERR_BITS` value after an ignored `CMD` write and after a clean run, the `ERR_BITS` clear, the `ERR_BITS` value after the `RND` run, the `ERR_BITS` field of the drawn software-error kind, and the host memory read while OTBN executes. The following are not claimed. `INTR_STATE.done` after the post-reset wipe is logged only (the value is not stated). `rnd_rep_chk_fail` after the RND program is logged only (a chance outcome). The `ERR_BITS` bits other than `illegal_insn` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` after the recoverable error are logged only. The leaf grades the fatal reaction to an integrity error of one inverted bit in IMEM and in DMEM, not an error that the memory itself detects without a read. The software errors other than the kind drawn for the seed and `ILLEGAL_INSN` (`KEY_INVALID` is never driven), the fatal errors other than `FATAL_SOFTWARE`, `IMEM_INTG_VIOLATION`, `DMEM_INTG_VIOLATION` and `ILLEGAL_BUS_ACCESS`, and a write to `ERR_BITS` while OTBN is running are not driven. The response code of the host memory read during BUSY_EXECUTE, and the `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` bits other than the named ones in the software-error-kind and bus-access legs, are logged only. The index of the fatal alert within `[2:1]` and the `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE` bits other than the named ones of the integrity runs are logged only. The ADD, XOR and AND results belong to `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`, the `INTR_TEST` walk of the done line with the enable set belongs to `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`, and the line gate of the other sources belongs to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`.

*Known limitation*: On VCS the full entry fails on each SEC_WIPE_DMEM and SEC_WIPE_IMEM command at IDLE and on each entry to LOCKED, with no failing checker; every checker of the leaf, including `CHK-OTBN-RND-CLEAN`, `CHK-OTBN-SWERR-KIND` and `CHK-OTBN-ILLEGAL-BUS`, passes, and the only failure is the assertion below. The full entry is therefore an expected-RED entry on VCS. A SEC_WIPE_DMEM write at `STATUS` 0x01, which OTBN ignores, does not fire the assertion. `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test_shard2` and `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test_shard3` issue no SEC_WIPE command at IDLE and never enter LOCKED, and pass on VCS. `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test_shard3` proves 21 bins: the execute bins of `cp_cmd`, `x_cmd_first_busy` and `cp_done_event`, `cp_post_reset_arc`, both bins of `cp_insn_cnt` and of `cp_cmd_ignored`, and `cp_cmd_ignored_err` (`sep_otbn_op_cg`); `cp_ctrl_write_legality`, `cp_err_bits_clear` and the recoverable bin of `cp_sw_error_end` (`sep_otbn_err_lock_cg`); and the otbn_done cells of `x_line_gate`, `cp_w1c_clear`, `cp_enable_flip` and `cp_hw_raise` (`sep_crypto_intr_cg`). `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test_shard2` proves `cp_sw_err_kind` and `cp_rnd_clean`. Twelve bins are proven only in the full entry: the sec_wipe_dmem and sec_wipe_imem bins of `cp_cmd`, `x_cmd_first_busy` and `cp_done_event`, which need a SEC_WIPE command at IDLE, and the fatal bin of `cp_sw_error_end`, both bins of `cp_locked_hold_exit`, both bins of `cp_intg_violation_end` and `cp_fatal_cause`, which need the LOCKED state. The full entry also fills the bins of both shard entries. The SEP mock OTP scramble-key responder (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_otbn_wrapper.sv`) holds `ack` one clock after `req` falls, and the vendored request synchronizer of OTBN asserts that an acknowledge needs a request (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/prim/rtl/prim_sync_reqack.sv`, `SyncReqAckAckNeedsReq` and `SyncReqAckHoldReq`). Verilator compiles these assertions out, so the leaf passes there.

*Issue*: #3156

'''''

[[sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test]]
===== 1.39 `sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (ABR)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip with the CPU-LSU master readback and write of the ABR window (approved); published NIST ACVP known-answer vectors as the expected values of every graded result (ML-DSA key generation, signature generation and verification, ML-KEM key generation, encapsulation and decapsulation). Vector source: `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/common/gen_abr_nist_vectors.py` extracts the vectors from the NIST ACVP-Server internalProjection files of the ML-DSA-87 keyGen, sigGen and sigVer groups (FIPS 204; the sigGen and sigVer groups with external mu) and of the ML-KEM-1024 keyGen and encapDecap groups (FIPS 203; the decapsulation group), and `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_abr_nist.py` and `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_abr_kem_nist.py` parse them. The vector table holds every external-mu sigVer reject case (`testPassed` false) of the published group with its ACVP `reason` (`NIST_SV_REJECTS` of `sep_abr_nist.py`), and every `modified ciphertext` decapsulation case of the published group with its expected shared key K (`NIST_KEM_DEC_MODIFIED` of `sep_abr_kem_nist.py`), from one pinned ACVP-Server snapshot that also holds the cases the open KAT tests use. The expected keys K of the decapsulation cases in the table and the key of the valid case are pairwise different, and the table loader checks this. No other reference model. The ABR reset bit of `SW_RESET_N`. Around the reject verify the leaf sets the coverage label `abr_reject_class` of the sampler (a testbench variable that drives no DUT net), and after the owning check of an outcome passes it sets the pass label `abr_cmd_pass`. No observation tap. No inject beyond the register writes of the scenario.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the single-step cell of the seed (one of the six), the order of the 3 trials (the two combined cells and the single-step cell; a first trial has the predecessor "none"), the re-arm of each trial (ZEROIZE or an ABR reset), the vector row of the single-step cell (a table with one row gives that row), the reason class of the ML-DSA reject trial (one of the `reason` values of the reject cases in the published external-mu sigVer group: `modified message`, `modified signature - commitment`, `modified signature - hint`, `modified signature - z`) and then one reject row of that class, and two different `modified ciphertext` decapsulation rows, and last the words of the ABR entropy input that each command writes (a masking input, not a key). The draws exclude the reject row that `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test` grades and the modified-ciphertext row that `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test` grades. `reseed = 4` samples different cells, orders, re-arms, reason classes and rows. The two combined cells, one single-step cell, one ML-DSA reject trial and two ML-KEM reject trials run on every seed. Each trial is an ML-DSA-87 or ML-KEM-1024 operation with real-size window transfers, so the leaf draws the single-step cell, the re-arms and the reject rows and does not walk them. The single-step cell, the re-arm of each combined cell and the reason class close on a merge of regressions. The predecessor of a cell, the row inside a reason class and the ML-KEM rows have no bin.

*Objective*: ML-DSA-87 runs key generation, signature generation and verification, and ML-KEM-1024 runs key generation, encapsulation and decapsulation. After an operation ABR parks with `STATUS.VALID` set, and `STATUS.READY` returns only after a zeroize or a reset. The command field also has the codes KEYGEN+SIGN and KEYGEN+DECAPS. ML-DSA-87 verification (FIPS 204) writes to `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` the c~' that the algorithm computes from the public key, mu and the signature, and a signature verifies only when c~' equals c~. ML-KEM-1024 decapsulation (FIPS 203) returns the key that the algorithm computes from the decapsulation key and the ciphertext; for a ciphertext that fails the re-encryption check this is the implicit-rejection key. Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc` (Adams Bridge (ABR), OCAH Modifications); `integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl` (`MLDSA_CTRL`, `MLKEM_CTRL`, `MLDSA_STATUS`, `MLKEM_STATUS`, `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES`, `MLDSA_SIGNATURE`, the ruling source for the command encodings and the window sizes).

*Test Coverage*: On every seed the two combined cells (ML-DSA KEYGEN+SIGN, ML-KEM KEYGEN+DECAPS) and one drawn single-step cell (ML-DSA KEYGEN, SIGNING or VERIFYING; ML-KEM KEYGEN, ENCAPS or DECAPS), in drawn order, each with a drawn re-arm (ZEROIZE or an ABR reset). Over a merge of regressions every single-step cell and both re-arms of each combined cell run. Inputs come from published vectors. One ML-DSA-87 reject trial of the VERIFYING operation per seed: a drawn ACVP external-mu sigVer case whose expected result is fail, of a drawn reason class; and two ML-KEM-1024 reject trials of the DECAPS operation: two different drawn ACVP `modified ciphertext` cases. Error injection: the modified input of the published case (the mu input, or the commitment, hint or z part of the signature; the ciphertext). Extends `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`, `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test` and `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`, which drive the six single-step cells and grade the valid status, the zeroize clear, the self-reset and READY after zeroize and after an ABR reset (`CHK-STATUS-VALID`, `CHK-ZEROIZE`, `CHK-RESET-STATUS`, `CHK-KEM-ZEROIZE`, `CHK-ABR-SELF-RESET`). The first of them grades one ACVP sigVer reject case and the second one ACVP modified-ciphertext case. This entry grades only the new cells: the two combined cells (park, a command written while parked, and re-arm), READY low after the single-step operation, the order independence, and the drawn ACVP reject cases that the open tests do not drive. The owners of the open tests keep the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_abr_completion_cg` (`x_op_protocol`, `cp_combined_cmd`, `cp_verify_outcome`, `cp_parked_cmd`). `cp_combined_cmd` and the single-step, ready-after-zeroize and ready-after-reset cells of `x_op_protocol` are held at their DUT event and sample only after the owning check passes: the leaf writes the pass label `abr_cmd_pass` after CHK-ABR-COMBO, CHK-ABR-PARK (single-step cell, with the result windows equal to the vector) and CHK-ABR-REARM pass. The first FAIL or missing control closes the graded window for the rest of the leaf.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Set `SW_RESET_N` to the baseline of the common rules with the bits of KMAC, AES, HMAC and OTBN clear and the ABR bit (bit 6) set. Log the baseline.
. Draw the plan with `SepSeededRng`. Log `PLAN seed=<n>` with the single-step cell, the trial order, the re-arm of each trial, the vector row, the reason class and ACVP `tcId` of the ML-DSA reject row, and the ACVP `tcId` of the two ML-KEM reject rows.
. Trials. For each of the 3 trials in the drawn order, run steps 4 to 7 and then the re-arm of the trial: step 8 (ZEROIZE) or step 9 (ABR reset).
. Wait for `READY` = 1 of the block of the cell (a bounded wait). Read `STATUS` and require `VALID` = 0 at that read (`CTRL-ABR-VALID-PRE`; a 1 logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf), so the `VALID` wait of step 5 sees the `VALID` of this command. Every command of the leaf runs this read. In a combined trial, first run the command Y alone on the same block with the graded window closed: for ML-DSA, run KEYGEN+SIGN alone with the inputs of the cell, read its signature S_pre and write `ZEROIZE`, then run Y = VERIFYING of the public key, mu and S_pre; for ML-KEM, run Y = ENCAPS of the encapsulation key and the message m of the published vectors. Log T_Y, the clocks from the `CTRL` write to the first `VALID` read, read the output windows of Y as G, write `ZEROIZE` and reopen the window. The inputs of Y are written before the inputs of the combined cell. Write the drawn ABR entropy input and the inputs of the cell from the vector row, as the open helper of that cell does: ML-DSA KEYGEN, the seed; SIGNING, the private key and mu with `EXTERNAL_MU` set; VERIFYING, the public key, mu and the signature with `EXTERNAL_MU` set; KEYGEN+SIGN, the seed and mu with `EXTERNAL_MU` set; ML-KEM KEYGEN, the seeds d and z; ENCAPS, the encapsulation key and the message m; DECAPS, the decapsulation key and the ciphertext; KEYGEN+DECAPS, the seeds d and z and the ciphertext of the DECAPS row.
. Write the command code to `MLDSA_CTRL` or `MLKEM_CTRL`: 1 KEYGEN, 2 SIGNING or ENCAPS, 3 VERIFYING or DECAPS, 4 KEYGEN+SIGN or KEYGEN+DECAPS. Wait for `STATUS.VALID` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the owning check with `reason=timeout`, resets ABR and moves on). Read `STATUS`.
. For a single-step cell, read the result windows that the open helper of that cell compares (ML-DSA: the public key for KEYGEN, the signature for SIGNING, `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` for VERIFYING; ML-KEM: the encapsulation key and the decapsulation key for KEYGEN, the ciphertext and the shared key for ENCAPS, the shared key for DECAPS) and compare each with the vector. In the KEYGEN+SIGN trial, read the signature window and keep it for step 11. No check grades a result of a combined cell.
. Read `STATUS` again, after the result reads and before the re-arm. In a combined trial, after CHK-ABR-PARK passes, run the parked leg: read the output windows of Y as P and the readable inputs of Y, write Y to the `CTRL` of the parked block with no `ZEROIZE` bit, wait 2 x T_Y + 200 clocks with no `STATUS` read, read the output windows (CHK-ABR-PARKED-CMD) and read `STATUS` once. In a ZEROIZE trial of a combined cell, then read the secret output windows of step 8 and log for each window whether it holds a non-zero word (`INFO-ABR-SECRET-PRE seed=<n> cell=<c> window=<w> nonzero=<0 or 1>`).
. ZEROIZE trial. Write `ZEROIZE` of the block of the cell. Wait for `READY` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read `VALID` and log it. For a combined cell read the secret output windows (`MLDSA_PRIVKEY_OUT`, `MLKEM_DECAPS_KEY` and `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY`) and expect every word to read 0. A window that held no non-zero word in step 7 records `INFO-ABR-SECRET-EMPTY` and is not graded. The KEYGEN+DECAPS cell must have at least one graded window. The KEYGEN+SIGN cell fills no secret output window: when every window is empty it logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-ABR-REARM reason=secret_wipe_dsa_keygen_sign_fills_no_secret_window` and grades READY only.
. ABR reset trial. After step 7 reset ABR (bit 6 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high). Wait for `READY` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read `VALID` and log it. For a combined cell read the secret output windows and log them (`OBS-ABR-RESET-WINDOW`).
. Combined signature, logged only. Wait for `READY` = 1. Run ML-DSA KEYGEN with the seed of the KEYGEN+SIGN cell and read the public key. Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.
. Write the public key, mu and the signature kept in step 6 and write VERIFYING with `EXTERNAL_MU` set. Wait for `VALID` = 1. Compare `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` with c~ of the signature as written and log `accept` or `reject`. Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.
. Tamper control. Write the public key, mu and the signature of the ACVP accept case (`testPassed` true) of the vector table, and write VERIFYING with `EXTERNAL_MU` set. Wait for `VALID` = 1 and expect `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` to equal c~ of the signature as written. Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.
. This is the error injection. Point: the input that the drawn ACVP case modifies (the mu input, or the commitment, hint or z part of the signature). Kind: a published sigVer reject case. Expected response: reject (ACVP `testPassed` false). Wait for `READY` = 1, write the public key, mu and the signature of the drawn reject row, write VERIFYING with `EXTERNAL_MU` set, and wait for `VALID` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` and `ERROR`, log `ERROR` (`OBS-ABR-TAMPER-ERROR`), compare the result with c~ of the signature as written (the first 16 words of the signature window), then write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.
. KEM control. Write the decapsulation key and the ciphertext of the ACVP `valid decapsulation` case of the vector table, write DECAPS, wait for `VALID` = 1, read the shared key and compare it with the expected key K of the case. Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.
. This is the error injection of the KEM leg. Point: the ciphertext of the drawn ACVP case. Kind: a published `modified ciphertext` decapsulation case. Expected response: the expected key K of the case (the implicit-rejection key). Write the decapsulation key and the ciphertext of the first drawn row, write DECAPS, wait for `VALID` = 1 (a bounded wait), read the shared key and compare it with K of the row. Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1. Repeat with the second drawn row.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-COMBO
|The combined commands KEYGEN+SIGN (`MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` = 4) and KEYGEN+DECAPS (`MLKEM_CTRL.CTRL` = 4) are accepted and park: when `VALID` first reads 1, `READY` reads 0 (`abr_reg.rdl`, the command encodings; `adams_bridge.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: the sequencer parks with `STATUS.VALID` set). `ERROR` is logged in the `obs_error` field and not graded: the RDL describes the bit ("the process ended with an error") and states no rule for its value at the end of a combined command, and `adams_bridge.adoc` names only `VALID` and `READY`. A wrong DUT that ignores the combined code never shows `VALID` (the bounded wait times out), or shows `READY` with `VALID`. `CTRL-ABR-VALID-PRE` shows `VALID` = 0 before the command write, so the `VALID` comes from this command. Anchor: `MLDSA_STATUS` and `MLKEM_STATUS` readback with the field masks of the RDL. Control: the single-step cell of the same seed matches its vector (CHK-ABR-ORDER), so the trial flow of inputs, command and wait produces a result. The leaf also logs the outcome of verifying the combined signature, which no check grades. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-COMBO PASS seed=<n> cell=<dsa_keygen_sign or kem_keygen_decaps> via=<zeroize or reset> valid=1 ready=0 obs_error=<e>` and `OBS-ABR-COMBO-VERIFY seed=<n> verify=<accept or reject or n/a>`.
|CHK-ABR-PARK
|At the end of every cell, after the result reads and before the re-arm, a combined cell shows `VALID` = 1 and `READY` = 0, and a single-step cell shows `READY` = 0 (`adams_bridge.adoc`: `STATUS.READY` re-asserts only after a zeroize or a reset). `ERROR` is logged in the `obs_error` field and not graded, for the reason of CHK-ABR-COMBO. For a single-step cell `VALID` is logged, because the open tests grade it. A wrong DUT that returns to READY by itself shows `READY` = 1. Anchor: `STATUS` readback. Control: `READY` reads 1 after the re-arm of the same cell (CHK-ABR-REARM for a combined cell; `CTRL-ABR-PARK` for the single-step cell), so the bit can be read as 1. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-PARK PASS seed=<n> cell=<c> valid=<v> ready=0 obs_error=<e>`.
|CHK-ABR-REARM
|After a combined operation a ZEROIZE or an ABR reset makes `READY` 1 (the re-arm of each combined cell is a seed draw, so both re-arms of each cell close on a merge of regressions), and after a ZEROIZE each secret output window that held a non-zero word before the ZEROIZE reads 0. The ZEROIZE leg of KEYGEN+DECAPS needs at least one such window (it fills `MLKEM_DECAPS_KEY` and `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY`), else the check fails; KEYGEN+SIGN fills no secret output window, so its ZEROIZE leg grades READY and logs the wipe part as `OBS-BLOCKED` when every window is empty (`adams_bridge.adoc`: READY returns only after a zeroize or a reset; `abr_reg.rdl`, `ZEROIZE`: zeroize all internal registers and memories). A wrong DUT that keeps `READY` at 0, or leaves a secret window readable, shows it. `VALID` after the re-arm and the windows after a reset are logged and not graded. Anchor: `STATUS` and window readback. The re-arm of the single-step cells is a control of the open checks. A window is graded only when its read before the ZEROIZE (`INFO-ABR-SECRET-PRE`) shows a non-zero word, so a window that reads 0 whatever the ZEROIZE does is not graded; such a window records `INFO-ABR-SECRET-EMPTY seed=<n> cell=<c> window=<w>`. The control for the zero windows is part of CHK-ABR-REARM: it fails when `graded_windows` is below `min_graded`. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-REARM PASS seed=<n> cell=<combined> via=zeroize ready=1 secret_window=<window:0 or window:empty,...> graded_windows=<g> min_graded=<0 or 1> live=none`, `CHK-ABR-REARM PASS seed=<n> cell=<combined> via=reset ready=1 secret_window=n/a` and `OBS-ABR-VALID seed=<n> cell=<c> via=<zeroize or reset> valid=<v>`.
|CHK-ABR-ORDER
|The result of the single-step cell equals the published vector whatever operation ran before it (the cell and its predecessor are seed draws) (`abr_reg.rdl`; the vectors of the open helpers). A wrong DUT whose result depends on leftover state of the earlier operation shows a mismatch in the cell with a different predecessor. Anchor: result window readback against the vector. The log lists the operations in the order they ran. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-ORDER PASS seed=<n> cell=<c> ops=<list> all_match=1 mismatch=none`.
|CHK-ABR-TAMPER
|For the drawn ACVP external-mu sigVer case whose expected result is fail (`testPassed` false), `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` differs from c~ of the signature as written, which is the first 16 words of the signature window (`abr_reg.rdl`, `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES`: the signature is verified when the register equals c~; FIPS 204, ML-DSA.Verify_internal: the signature verifies only when the computed c~' equals c~, z is inside its bound and the hint is well formed). The ACVP verdict is the expected result, and the register is the verdict of the device, so for every published reject case the register differs from c~, whatever the reason class. A wrong DUT that does not read the modified part, or that copies c~ into the result, shows c~. `ERROR` is logged (`OBS-ABR-TAMPER-ERROR`), because the text does not state it for a rejected signature. Anchor: `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` readback against the signature words as written. Control (`CTRL-ABR-TAMPER`): the ACVP accept case of the same seed gives a result equal to c~, so the flow can produce an accept. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-TAMPER PASS seed=<n> tcid=<id> reason=<class> verify_res_eq_c=0 first_diff_word=<w> res=<hex> c=<hex> control_accept=1`.
|CHK-ABR-PARKED-CMD
|A command Y written to the `CTRL` of a block that is parked (`VALID` 1, `READY` 0) is not run: after a wait of 2 x T_Y + 200 clocks with no `STATUS` read, every output window of Y equals its value at the park, and `STATUS` still reads `VALID` 1 and `READY` 0 (`abr_reg.rdl:77`, `:81-83`, `:328`: `swwe = abr_ready` on the command fields; `:91-92`, `:340-341`: `READY` indicates that the core is ready to take a control command; `adams_bridge.adoc:186-190`: the sequencer parks until a zeroize or a reset). Y is ML-DSA VERIFYING (output `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES`) after KEYGEN+SIGN, and ML-KEM ENCAPS (outputs `MLKEM_CIPHERTEXT`, `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY`) after KEYGEN+DECAPS. A wrong DUT that runs Y while parked changes an output window, and one that leaves the park shows `READY` 1 or `VALID` 0. T_Y is the time of Y alone on the same block from `READY`, measured from the `CTRL` write to the first `VALID` read with the graded window closed. The `CTRL` write answers OKAY (the register driver raises on any other response, and the line carries `ctrl_b=okay`). `STATUS.ERROR` is logged and not graded: the graded `STATUS` read also logs `READY`, `VALID`, `ERROR`, ML-DSA `MSG_STREAM_READY` and the whole word with the response of the `CTRL` write (`OBS-ABR-START-PARKED seed=<n> block=<dsa or kem> cmd=0x<y> ctrl_b=okay ready=<r> valid=<v> error=<e> msg_stream_ready=<0, 1 or na> status=0x<s>`); `abr_reg.rdl:96-106` gives `ERROR` only for the end of a process, and no text states `ERROR`, `MSG_STREAM_READY` or the bus response of a command written while parked. No `STATUS` read is added: the wait keeps its "no `STATUS` read" rule, so the logged values are those of the graded read after the wait. Anchor: the output window and `STATUS` readback with the field masks of the RDL. Controls (a failing control logs `CTRL-MISSING` and closes the graded window): `CTRL-ABR-PARKED-Y` (Y alone from `READY` completes: ML-DSA `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` equals c~ of the signature it verifies, `abr_reg.rdl:171-173`; ML-KEM `VALID` 1 and both outputs hold a non-zero word; T_Y above 0), `CTRL-ABR-PARKED-INPUTS` (at the park the readable inputs of Y equal the pre-run inputs: ML-DSA `MLDSA_PUBKEY` and `MLDSA_SIGNATURE`, ML-KEM `MLKEM_ENCAPS_KEY`), `CTRL-ABR-PARKED-DIFF` (the outputs at the park differ from the outputs G of Y alone, so a run of Y changes a graded window) and `CTRL-ABR-PARKED-BOUND` (the wait exceeds T_Y). `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` is write-only (`abr_reg.rdl:190`), so the leaf writes the same mu in both runs. The `MLKEM_MSG` readback at the park is logged only (`OBS-ABR-PARKED-MSG match=<n> words=8 nonzero=<0 or 1>`). Evidence: `CHK-ABR-PARKED-CMD PASS seed=<n> block=<dsa or kem> cmd=0x<y> ctrl_b=okay t_y=<T_Y> w=<W> windows=<NAME:words,...> first_diff=none valid=1 ready=0 obs_error=<e>`; a FAIL carries `reason=window_changed`, `not_parked` or `no_pre_run`.
|CHK-ABR-KEM-FLIP
|For each of the two drawn ACVP `modified ciphertext` decapsulation cases the shared key equals the expected key K of the case, all 8 words (FIPS 203, ML-KEM.Decaps_internal: a ciphertext that fails the re-encryption check returns the implicit-rejection key J(z, c); ACVP publishes K for each case). Each case carries its own decapsulation key. The expected keys of the vector table and the key of the valid case are pairwise different, so a wrong DUT that skips the implicit rejection, returns a key cached from an earlier operation, or returns a constant, fails the exact compare. Anchor: shared key readback against the vector. Control (`CTRL-ABR-KEM-FLIP`): the ACVP `valid decapsulation` case gives its expected key. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-KEM-FLIP PASS seed=<n> tcid=<id> ss==K=1 ss=<hex> k=<hex>`, one line for each case.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. The `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-ABR-REARM` line of the KEYGEN+SIGN wipe part is not counted and does not fail the leaf. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test grades the combined cells, READY after the single-step operation, the order independence and the drawn ACVP reject cases that the open tests do not drive. The following are not claimed. The `MLKEM_MSG` readback at the park is logged only. The output registers and the signing randomness of the combined commands have no independent expected value. `STATUS.ERROR` is logged and not graded. The secret-window wipe after a ZEROIZE of KEYGEN+SIGN is not graded, because that cell fills no secret output window. The verify outcome of a combined signature is logged only (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). Verify and decapsulation inputs other than the published ACVP cases are not driven, because the leaf has no expected value for them. Whether a reset clears the secret output windows is blocked. A zero window after a ZEROIZE proves that the window is not readable, because the read port depends on `VALID`, and does not prove that the memory is wiped. The single-step results, the valid status, the zeroize clear and the self-reset belong to the open ABR tests. The ABR interrupt sources and the key-vault seed path are not driven.

'''''
[[sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test]]
===== 1.40 `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS (the X check of the reset readback runs on VCS only)

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master); register lists from the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta.register_fields`), the RDL text (`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl`: access, side effects, hardware access and the lock phrase) and the RDL reset column. Approved probes: CPU-LSU master readback; the VCS X check on the readback; `esrc_compress_vld_o` (the stream tap that measures t_ref); `trng_reset_active_probe_o` and `trng_axi_isolated_probe_o` (end of the TRNG reset); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (logged, not graded). Approved inject: the noise model that drives `esrc_noise_ext_i` in the mode `balanced` on all 12 lanes (common rules "Noise injection" and "Exact patterns"; the `+esrc_noise_force` plusarg routes this port into the decorrelators and generates nothing). No other inject. No PROPOSED item.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the register order of the read-only leg and of the restore leg; the restore value v1 of each restore field (it differs from the reset value in at least one bit; `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` takes a supported encoding from 1 to 4 and `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL` takes a lane from 1 to 11); the order of the two `MODULE_ENABLE` trials; the masks and the permutation of the `balanced` pattern; the `INTR_ENABLE` mask E and the `INTR_TEST` mask T over the eight defined bits (each with at least one 0 and one 1; T holds bits 8 and 12 and at least one other set bit), the bit of the one-bit control and the clear subset C (it holds bits 8 and 12 and leaves at least one other set bit of T out, so it is a proper subset of T). `reseed = 4` samples different orders, values and masks; the plan shape (the registers, the cells and the legs) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: Read-only ESRC registers ignore writes, including `COMPONENT_ID`, and the write answers OKAY or SLVERR. After a TRNG reset every stored read-write field outside the `FIPS_LOCK` list and outside `CTRL` reads its reset value. Once `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` is set, a write of 0 to `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` is ignored and the module keeps running; without the lock the same write stops it. With all eight defined bits of `INTR_ENABLE` set, the eight `INTR_STATUS` bits follow `INTR_TEST`, are independent of each other and clear on write-one; a write to `INTR_TEST` leaves the stored `INTR_ENABLE` unchanged. The specification does not state whether the status latches with an enable bit clear; the test grades `INTR_ENABLE` as a stored value only and logs `INTR_STATUS` for a drawn mask. Spec: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`COMPONENT_ID`, `CTRL`, `FIPS_LOCK`, `HEALTH_TEST_WINDOW_SIZE`, `MAIN_SM_STATUS`, `FIFO_STATUS`, `SHA256_STATUS`, `INTR_STATUS`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `INTR_TEST`); `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` (Reset, SHA-256 Whitening); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (Single-Beat Access Only: DECERR is the answer of the internal error slave, which no accelerator sees).

*Test Coverage*: Read-only registers and `COMPONENT_ID` after a write of the complement (error case), with the response code graded; the restore of every stored unlocked read-write field by a TRNG reset; the `MODULE_ENABLE` write of 0 after the lock and without it; `INTR_TEST` and the write-one clear over the eight defined `INTR_STATUS` bits with `INTR_ENABLE` all ones, with a drawn test mask, the corner masks all ones and all zeros, and the clear of a proper subset that holds bits 8 and 12 (`FIFO_OVERFLOW` and `FIFO_UNDERFLOW`); the one-bit corner runs as a control only, because `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test` grades each source alone; `INTR_ENABLE` as a stored value for a drawn mask and for its complement (the status for a drawn enable mask is logged and not graded). This test extends `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`, `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` and `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`. It grades only the cells that those tests do not grade; the open owners keep the rest. Functional coverage: groups `sep_esrc_startup_health_cg` (`cp_component_id`), `sep_crypto_reg_lock_cg` (`cp_module_enable_locked`, the locked bin), `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_esrc_status_enable`) and `sep_crypto_reset_cg` (`cp_reset_evidence` bin `esrc_regs`). The single-source `INTR_TEST` raise and the unlocked `MODULE_ENABLE` trial are controls and fill no bin.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build the register lists from the generated IP-XACT and the RDL text at run time. The read-only list holds every register whose fields are all read-only, except the data ports. A restore field has software access rw, no RDL side effect (`onwrite`, `onread`, `singlepulse`, `woclr`, `woset`, `rclr` or `rset`), no hardware-only write (`hw = w`), and an RDL text without "FIPS_LOCK.LOCK prevents software changes"; the walk gives 12 restore fields (the eight `INTR_ENABLE` fields, `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE`, `HEALTH_TEST_WINDOW_SIZE.SIZE`, `HT_WATERMARK_NUM.WATERMARK_NUM` and `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL`). A volatile field is a read-write field with a side effect or a hardware write (the status and alert latches: `INTR_STATUS`, the `*_HEALTH_STATUS` registers, `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ALERT` and `ERR`). Log the size of the locked list as `INFO-LOCK-COUNT` and `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE`, whose text lacks the lock phrase, as the record `INFO-LOCK-LIST`.
. Exclude from the walk `FIFO_RDATA`, `BIW_OBS_RDATA`, `NOISE_OBS_RDATA`, `INTR_TEST`, `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.FLUSH`, `FIPS_LOCK`, `CTRL`, the locked list and every register that hardware updates while the module runs (status, count and total-failure registers). The volatile fields are read only, as `OBS-ESRC-VOLATILE`, and are not graded.
. Define the reset sequence: clear and set the TRNG bit of `SEP_RESET_CTRL.SW_RESET_N` (`TRNG_SW_RST_N`) with every other bit at the baseline of the leaf (0x20), and wait until `trng_reset_active_probe_o` and `trng_axi_isolated_probe_o` read 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Log the baseline.
. Run the reset sequence.
. Write `RING_OSC_ENABLE.ENABLE` = 0, then `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` = 0, then poll `MAIN_SM_STATUS.IDLE` until it reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). This is the quiet state.
. Read-only leg. In seeded order, read each read-only register twice. Log a register whose two reads differ in its RDL field bits as `INFO-RO-DYNAMIC` and do not write it.
. Error injection. Write the 32-bit complement of the value read to each read-only register whose two reads are equal, with all byte strobes set. The master does not treat the response code as an error. Expected response: the write answers OKAY or SLVERR and the register reads its first-read value. Log the response code as `OBS-RO-RESP`.
. Read each written register again and compare its RDL field bits with those of its first read. Log its Reserved bits (for example `COMPONENT_ID` bits 23:16) as `OBS-RO-RSVD`; the specification states no read value for them. `COMPONENT_ID` is one of the read-only registers of this leg and is always written.
. Interrupt leg, clean-up. Write 1 to the eight defined `INTR_STATUS` bits and read `INTR_STATUS`. The eight defined bits read 0 (`CTRL-INTR-CLEAN`); a bit that stays set logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf.
. Corner case all zeros. Write 0 to the eight defined bits of `INTR_ENABLE`, write 0 to `INTR_TEST`, then read `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`.
. Corner case all ones. Write all ones to the eight defined bits of `INTR_ENABLE`, write all ones to the defined bits of `INTR_TEST`, then read `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`. Write 1 to the eight defined `INTR_STATUS` bits, and read `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`.
. One-bit control. Write all ones to the eight defined bits of `INTR_ENABLE`, write the single bit i to `INTR_TEST`, read `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`, then write 1 to bit i of `INTR_STATUS` and read `INTR_STATUS`. The bit i is drawn from the set bits of the drawn test mask T that the two next steps write, so that the same bit is the control of those steps.
. Drawn enable mask. This step runs twice: with the drawn `INTR_ENABLE` mask E and test mask T, then with the complement of E over the eight defined bits and all ones on the eight defined bits of `INTR_TEST`, so that every source has its enable bit clear under a test write in every leaf. Write the enable mask of the run, write the test mask of the run, and read `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`. `INTR_ENABLE` is graded as the stored value of the run. Log `INTR_STATUS` as `OBS-STATUS-E` and vector bit 15 of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` as `OBS-PIC16`. Write 1 to the eight defined bits of `INTR_STATUS`, read `INTR_STATUS` (the eight defined bits read 0) and read `INTR_ENABLE` again.
. Drawn test mask. Write all ones to the eight defined bits of `INTR_ENABLE`, write T to `INTR_TEST`, and read `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`.
. Write 1 to the drawn clear subset C (it holds bits 8 and 12 and is a proper subset of the set bits of T) in `INTR_STATUS`, then read `INTR_STATUS` and `INTR_ENABLE`.
. Write 1 to the eight defined bits of `INTR_STATUS` and read `INTR_STATUS`.
. Restore leg. For every restore field, in seeded order, write its value v1 (a read-modify-write of its register). Read each field back and compare it with v1 (`CTRL-ESRC-RESTORE`). Read the volatile fields.
. Run the reset sequence, then read every restore field again and compare it with its RDL reset value. Read the volatile fields again and log the non-zero ones before and after the reset as `OBS-ESRC-VOLATILE`.
. VCS only: check each restore readback after the reset for X on its field bits.
