Log the B response of the lock write of 0 and of the select write of b. Reset the TRNG domain (step 3) and log the clocks to the rise and the fall of the probe (`CTRL-SEL-LOCK`). Read the lock: expect 1. Read the select: expect a.
. Global reset leg. Pulse `rst_ni` (assert, release, and wait until the CPU-LSU master reads again as the open tests do). Read the lock: expect 0. Read the select: expect 0x7. Control: the lock read 1 and the select read a, with a not 0x7, before the pulse.
. Lock-first leg. Write 1 to the lock before any select write and read it: expect 1. Write the select 0x0 and read the select: expect 0x7. Pulse `rst_ni`. Read the lock: expect 0. Write the select 0x0 and read it: expect 0x0 (control: the write lands when the lock is clear).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SEL-RW
|A drawn select value (d and s) reads back as written (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL.sel`, RW). A DUT that ignores the write, or stores another value, fails. Anchor: register readback. Evidence: `CHK-SEL-RW PASS seed=<n> wrote=0x<w> read=0x<w>` for each write; `CHK-SEL-RESET PASS seed=<n> sel=0x7 lock=0 want_sel=0x7 want_lock=0` for the reset read.
|CHK-EXT-XFER
|Every beat that completes at the external port of the stream appears at the post-mux probe in the same order and with the same data, and the `edn_fips` bit of each word that the consuming adapter accepts is 1 (`trng.adoc`: the external mux supplies `tuser` = 1; the stream contract table). A DUT that loses, reorders or alters a word, or tags it `edn_fips` = 0, fails. At least one beat completes and at least one accepted word is graded. On stream 2 the drawn even count of 8 to 64 beats fits the empty 32-entry pool, so every beat completes and the feed does not stall (`xfers` = `beats`, `ended_by_stall` = 0); a pool path that stalls early fails. Anchor: external stream response monitor (an observation tap), `axis1_*`, `axis2_*`, `crypto_edn_fips_o[i]` with `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]`, `pool_edn_fips_o` with `pool_edn_ack_o`. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-XFER PASS seed=<n> stream=<i> beats=<n> xfers=<n> post_mux=<n> order_ok=1 fips=1 fips_words=<k> gap=<p> ended_by_stall=<0 or 1>` (0 and `xfers` = `beats` on stream 2); for stream 1 `OBS-EXT-AES-REQ seed=<n> crypto_edn_req0=<r>`.
|CHK-EXT-STRB
|A beat with a partial strobe (0x0 to 0xE) stays pending at the mux output (`tvalid` 1, `tready` 0) for the hold H, and a pair with a partial second beat leaves `fifo_level` unchanged (`trng.adoc`: the crypto and pool adapters accept only 0xF; partial strobes stall them). A DUT that accepts the partial beat shows `tready` 1 or a level change. After the TRNG reset and the source reset a 0xF beat completes. Control: a 0xF pair sent first raises the settled `fifo_level` by 1 (stream 1: a 0xF beat completes, and a TRNG reset then empties the adapter before the partial beat), so the stall comes from the strobe and not from a full adapter (`CTRL-EXT-STRB`). Anchor: post-mux probe and the settled pool `STATUS`. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-STRB PASS seed=<n> stream=<i> tstrb=0x<s> pending=1 hold_clk=<H> pool_delta=<0\|na> settle_reads=<[r1,r2]\|na> ctrl_f_delta=<d> source_reset=1 post_reset_f_beat=1`.
|CHK-EXT-DROP
|With the select bit of the stream cleared every beat sees `tready` 1, the post-mux probe shows none of them, and `fifo_level` does not change (`trng.adoc`: an unselected external input receives ready high and its beats are discarded). The level reads are settled reads, so a pair still in flight from an earlier leg does not count as a change. A DUT that holds `tready` low, or passes a beat on, fails. Control: the transfer leg of the same stream and seed, with the bit set, delivers beats (`CTRL-EXT-DROP`). Anchor: port monitor (an observation tap), post-mux probe and the settled `STATUS`. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-DROP PASS seed=<n> stream=<i> sel=0x<s> sel_bit=0 tready_low=0 post_mux_beats=0 pool_delta=<0 or na> settle_reads=<[r0,r1] or na> beats=<n> ctrl_xfers=<c>`.
|CHK-EXT-BP
|With the pool full (`fifo_level` 32) `tready` falls with a beat pending; after the pops every pair appears once and in order (`trng.adoc`: the selected source receives the ready of the sink; the pool holds 32 entries of 64 bits). A DUT that keeps `tready` high at a full pool loses or duplicates a pair; a DUT that stalls at a lower level fails the level read. Every pop answers OKAY. Anchor: port monitor (an observation tap), the settled `STATUS` and `DATA` readback. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-BP PASS seed=<n> stream=2 tready_low_cycles=<c> level_at_stall=32 settle_reads=<r> ncal=<N_cal> pairs=<p> lost=0 dup=0 order_ok=1 bad_resp=0`.
|CHK-EXT-IRQ
|`ext_trng_irq_i` = 1 gives vector bit 16 = 1, and 0 gives 0 (`interrupts.adoc`: source 17 is the external TRNG interrupt; source N is on vector bit N - 1). A DUT with the line unconnected or inverted fails one of the two reads. Control: bit 16 reads 0 before the drive. Anchor: vector probe. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-IRQ PASS seed=<n> irq_i=<1\|0> vec16=<1\|0> clk=<t>` for each drive.
|CHK-SEL-READY
|For streams 1 and 2 with the select bit 1 and the sink not draining, `tready` falls with a beat pending; for stream 2 it stays high for at least 64 accepted beats (32 pool entries of two beats); for stream 1 an AES PRNG reseed after the fall completes the pending beat (`tready` rises) within a bounded wait (`trng.adoc`: the selected source receives the ready of the sink; `entropy_pool.adoc`; `aes.adoc`, `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED`). A DUT that ties `tready` to 1 for a selected stream, or that keeps it low when the sink drains, fails. The unselected input (ready high, beats discarded) is graded by CHK-EXT-DROP; the rise for stream 2 is graded by CHK-EXT-BP. The fall is the start of the last stall run of at least H clocks, so a short drop of `tready` while the adapter staging FIFO drains does not count as the fall. Control: at least one beat of the stream is accepted before the fall, so the fall is not a port that is held low (`CTRL-SEL-READY`). Anchor: port monitor (an observation tap). Evidence: `CHK-SEL-READY PASS seed=<n> stream=<i> sel_bit=1 tready_low_cycles=<c> accepted=<N_i> ncal=<N_i> final_stall_clk=<f> rise_after_drain=<1\|na>`; `OBS-SEL-READY0 seed=<n> sel=0x<s> sel_bit=1 tvalid_clk=<v> tready_low_clk=<l>`.
|CHK-SEL-READY0
|In the ready leg, when the select read back has bit 0 clear, the external stream 0 input sees `tready` 1 on every clock it holds `tvalid` 1 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:105-106`: an unselected external input receives ready high and its beats are discarded). A DUT that back-pressures an unselected stream 0 fails. Graded on each pass whose s has bit 0 clear, so it is not required on every seed; CHK-EXT-DROP grades the same rule for streams 1 and 2. Anchor: port monitor (an observation tap). Control: stream 0 held `tvalid` 1 on at least one clock of the leg (`CTRL-SEL-READY0`). Evidence: `CHK-SEL-READY0 PASS: seed=<n> sel=0x<s> sel_bit=0 tvalid_clk=<v> tready_low_clk=0`.
|CHK-SEL-HOLD
|With the external source selected and the internal endpoint filled, the endpoint holds `tvalid` 1, `tready` 0 and stable `tdata` for the hold window; after the reselect the held word is delivered first (`trng.adoc`: an unselected internal endpoint is back-pressured and retains queued data; selection has no automatic flush). A DUT that drains or flushes the endpoint, or changes the head word, fails. The held endpoint word and the first post-mux word after the reselect must be known: an X or Z on either fails the check (`reason=unknown:endpoint_tdata` or `reason=unknown:post_mux_tdata`). The select reads 0x7 before the hold and the reselect value after it, and for stream 2 the pool reads empty before the hold. Control: with the internal source selected and a consumer active the endpoint drains (`CTRL-SEL-HOLD`). Anchor: internal DRBG endpoint probe (an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list) and the post-mux probe. Evidence: `CHK-SEL-HOLD PASS seed=<n> stream=<i> tvalid=1 tready=0 tdata_changes=0 first_after_reselect==held=1 held=0x<w> ctrl_drain_beats=<b> hold_win=<h> sel_all=0x7 reselect=0x<r> pool_level_before_hold=<0 or na>`.
|CHK-EDN-FIFO-DEPTH
|With the external source selected for the stream and the endpoint output held (`tvalid` 1, `tready` 0) for 64 clocks with no push, the endpoint FIFO holds exactly 8 words, counted as the pushes minus the pops since the output last read empty (`hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:30-31`: EDN "fills one 8-entry AXI-Stream FIFO per endpoint"). Both streams 1 and 2 run on every seed. A DUT whose FIFO has another depth fails on the count. A pop while the output is held (`reason=hold_broken`) or no hold within the bound (`reason=timeout:quiet`) fails. Positive probes in the same line, all graded: at least 8 pushes seen on the push tap (`drbg_adapter_push_o`), no pop since the select write (`pops_since_select` = 0), and the select reads 0x7. The expected depth is the named constant 8 (`ENDPOINT_FIFO_DEPTH` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/tests/crypto/sep_trng_ext_stream_handshake_rand_test.py`). Anchor: the endpoint push, pop and valid taps. Control: `CTRL-EDN-FIFO-DRAIN`. Graded in `_depth_check` of `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/tests/crypto/sep_trng_ext_stream_handshake_rand_test.py`. Evidence: `CHK-EDN-FIFO-DEPTH PASS stream=<i> occ=8 expected=8 pushes=<p> pops_since_select=0 quiet_clk=<q> sel=0x7`.
|CTRL-EDN-FIFO-DRAIN
|After the reselect to the internal source, at least 8 words pass the post-mux probe of the stream and the first is the held word. Positive control of the depth count: the endpoint holds words that a consumer can take, so the depth compare is not an empty endpoint. A missing control fails the leaf. Logged as `drain_beats`, `need=8`, `first_after_reselect_held` and `aes_reseeds`.
|INFO-EDN-PUSH-GAP
|The largest gap between two pushes to the endpoint FIFO since the reselect, and the quiet length used. Logged and not graded: it records the timing of the EDN refill. No golden.
|CHK-SEL-LOCK
|After 1 is written to `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL_LOCK`, a write of 0 leaves it at 1, a write of another select value leaves the old value, and a TRNG reset leaves the lock at 1 (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`: `lock` is RW1S and holds until reset; `trng.adoc`: a TRNG reset alone does not unlock). A DUT that clears the lock, accepts the later select write, or clears the lock at the TRNG reset fails. Control: the pre-lock select write landed and changed the select, because a differs from the value held before it (`CTRL-SEL-LOCK seed=<n> held_sel=0x<h> pre_lock_sel=0x<a>`), and the select reads unchanged after the TRNG reset (control of the open check). Anchor: register readback. The write of 0 to the lock is a legal write of an RW1S field and must answer OKAY (`write0_bresp`). The select write under the lock must get a B response of any code (`sel_write_b_seen=1`), so a write that did not reach the register fails; no text states the code of a write the lock refuses (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`: the select "cannot be changed"), so the code is logged only (`OBS-SEL-LOCK-RESP`). The TRNG reset is a control: the probe rises and falls within the bound (`CTRL-SEL-LOCK seed=<n> trng_reset_rise_clk=<r> trng_reset_fall_clk=<f> cap=4000`). Evidence: `CHK-SEL-LOCK PASS seed=<n> lock=1 write0_lock=1 write0_bresp=OKAY sel_kept=0x<a> tried=0x<b> sel_write_b_seen=1 after_trng_reset_lock=1 ctrl_sel=0x<a> pre_lock_sel=0x<a>`.
|CHK-SEL-LOCKFIRST
|With the lock set before any select write, a select write leaves the select at 0x7 (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`: the lock prevents selection changes). A DUT that takes the write fails. Control: after a `rst_ni` pulse the lock is 0 and a select write lands (`CTRL-SEL-LOCKFIRST`). Anchor: register readback. The select write must get a B response of any code (`sel_write_b_seen=1`); its code is logged only (`OBS-SEL-LOCK-RESP`). Evidence: `CHK-SEL-LOCKFIRST PASS seed=<n> lock=1 write=0x0 sel_write_b_seen=1 sel=0x7 ctrl_after_reset_write_lands=1`.
|OBS-SEL-LOCK-RESP
|The B response code of the select write that the lock refuses, in the lock leg and the lock-first leg. No golden: `sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` states that the select cannot be changed under the lock and states no response code. Evidence: `OBS-SEL-LOCK-RESP seed=<n> leg=<lock or lockfirst> sel_write_bresp=<code>`.
|CHK-SEL-GLOBAL
|After a `rst_ni` pulse the lock is 0 and the select is 0x7 (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`: reset values). Before the pulse the lock reads 1 and the select reads a value other than 0x7, so a reset that leaves either one fails. Control: the lock read 1 and the select read a value other than 0x7 before the pulse (`CTRL-SEL-GLOBAL`). Anchor: register readback. Evidence: `CHK-SEL-GLOBAL PASS seed=<n> lock=0 sel=0x7 pre_lock=1 pre_sel=0x<a>`.
|CHK-POOL-DATA
|Each pop of a non-empty pool answers OKAY with the next driven pair in order, and the settled `fifo_level` equals the model level, the smaller of 32 and the pairs completed at the external port minus the pops, after every operation (`entropy_pool.adoc`, Access Policy; `sep_entropy_pool.adoc`, `STATUS`, `DATA`; `reset_controller.adoc`: the post-mux adapters hold buffered state, so a pair above 32 is staged and not in the pool). A DUT that pops twice, returns another word, loses a staged pair, or counts wrongly fails. After the pop that follows a feed left pending at level 32, the pending pair must complete at the external port: a beat that stays pending (`tvalid` 1, `tready` 0) for H clocks counted from the pop response fails CHK-POOL-DATA with `reason=timeout:pending_feed`, so a DUT that never takes the pair after room exists fails. The corner cells are a feed that brings the level to 32 followed at once by a pop, and a feed offered at level 32 (the level stays 32 and the pair appears in order after later pops). Control: the model level rises only on feeds that the leaf made, because the TRNG reset before the leg leaves only test data in the pool, and the select readback shows the pool stream external (`CTRL-POOL-DATA`). Anchor: `DATA` and the settled `STATUS` readback. Evidence: `CHK-POOL-DATA PASS seed=<n> op=fill level_after=<L> expect_level=<L> settle_reads=<r> pairs=<L>`, then one line per operation, `CHK-POOL-DATA PASS seed=<n> op=<feed\|feed_full\|pop\|status> level_before=<a> [pop=<k> resp=0 data=0x<w><w> expect=0x<w><w>] level_after=<b> expect_level=<b> settle_reads=<r> pending=<0 or 1>`.
|CHK-SEL-RESET
|The first reads of `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL.sel` and `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL_LOCK.lock`, before any write of the leaf, return the RDL reset values 0x7 and 0x0 (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc:367` and `:379`; the leaf takes the values from the generated register accessors). A DUT with another reset value fails. Anchor: register readback. Evidence: `CHK-SEL-RESET PASS seed=<n> sel=0x7 lock=0 want_sel=0x7 want_lock=0`.
|CHK-EXT-NOSRC-POP
|The `DATA` pop after the hold of the no-source step answers `SLVERR` when the second `STATUS` level is 0, and OKAY when it is at least 1, with no timeout (`entropy_pool.adoc`, Access Policy: a read of `DATA` pops one word, and a read of an empty pool returns `SLVERR`). A DUT that answers an empty pool with OKAY, or hangs the read, fails. Anchor: the AXI read response and the `STATUS` readback. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-NOSRC-POP PASS seed=<n> level=<l> resp=<SLVERR or OKAY> expect=<SLVERR or OKAY> timed_out=0`.
|OBS-EXT-NOSRC
|Not a graded check. No text states the data a sink receives when a stream is selected as external and no source drives it. The leaf logs the pool `fifo_level` before and after a hold of 4000 clocks and the data of the `DATA` pop that `CHK-EXT-NOSRC-POP` grades. Evidence: `OBS-EXT-NOSRC seed=<n> sel=0x<s> pending=0 wait_clk=4000 level_before=<n> level_after=<n> rdata=0x<d>`.
|OBS-EXT-WITHDRAW
|Not a graded check. No text states what a sink does when a source drops `tvalid` of a beat it holds. When a leg drops its source while a beat is on the port with `tvalid` 1, the leaf logs the held word, `tready` at the drop and the next word that completes at the port within a bound, and whether it equals the held word. Evidence: `OBS-EXT-WITHDRAW seed=<n> stream=<i> held=0x<w> tready_at_drop=<0, 1 or x> next=0x<w or none> next_eq_held=<0 or 1>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: owner window 151. The leaf opens the window only while no check or control has failed, and the first FAIL or missing control closes it for the rest of the run. The bins of `x_sel_stream`, `cp_lock_order`, `cp_reset_evidence[cpu_lock_survives]`, `cp_unselected_drop`, `cp_hold_internal` and `cp_ext_irq` and `cp_ext_tuser` sample the value that their check compares (`CHK-SEL-RW`, `CHK-SEL-LOCK`, `CHK-SEL-LOCKFIRST`, `CHK-SEL-GLOBAL`, `CHK-EXT-DROP`, `CHK-SEL-HOLD`, `CHK-EXT-IRQ`, `CHK-EXT-XFER`), and need no label. The other cells are held while the window is open and sampled on the rise of the pass label `xhs_pass` (`set_xhs_pass`), a 12-bit mask: bits 0 and 1 release `x_ext_beat` full for crypto and pool after `CHK-EXT-XFER` passed; bits 2 and 3 release `x_ext_beat` partial for crypto and pool after `CHK-EXT-STRB`; bits 4 and 5 release `cp_ext_stall` for crypto (`CHK-SEL-READY`, stream 1) and pool (`CHK-EXT-BP`, and `CHK-POOL-DATA` for the feed held at level 32 that completes after a pop); bits 6 to 8 release `cp_pool_pop` (pop at a level of 2 to 31, pop at 32, feed at 31) after `CHK-EXT-BP` and `CHK-POOL-DATA`; bits 9 to 11 release `cp_adapter_fifo_depth` for streams 0 to 2 after `CHK-EDN-FIFO-DEPTH` (stream 0 has no check, so its cell is never released). After a passing check the leaf writes its bits, holds them for 2 clocks, writes 0, waits 1 clock, and closes the window; a held cell that no passing check released is dropped when the window closes. `cp_adapter_fifo_depth` samples when the held output of a stream with the external select shows 8 words after at least 64 held clocks; the leaf waits the same 64 clocks, so the cell is held before the label rises and the output is still held at the release. `x_ext_beat` of the sink-reset owner is not changed by the label. Both streams run on every seed, so no bin depends on the seed.

*Scope limit*: The leaf does not claim a source that drops `tvalid` of a live beat (`OBS-EXT-WITHDRAW` logs the beat that a source drops at the end of a leg), an idle selected stream with a waiting consumer (`OBS-EXT-NOSRC` logs one case), the packing order inside a pair (pairs are {a, a}), or the `tready` fall of a selected stream 0 (logged as `OBS-SEL-READY0`). It does not claim the pool access-shape cells (half-word, unmapped and write accesses, empty pop) or `IRQ_CAUSE`: `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test` owns them; `CHK-EXT-NOSRC-POP` grades only the response of its one pop, outside the graded window. The reset values of the select and the lock are graded here at the first access (`CHK-SEL-RESET`) and belong to `sep_address_map_test` and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`; the survival of a non-reset select across a TRNG reset belongs to `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`. The tuser of stream 1 is graded only for words that a client accepts. The source reset of the driver that goes with a TRNG reset is the source's own reset after a stall, set-up and not a claim.

'''''

[[sep_trng_ext_sink_reset_rand_test]]
===== 1.52 `sep_trng_ext_sink_reset_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (TRNG and entropy pool)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, no `+esrc_noise_force`, `reseed = 4`; external stream driver (added hook, top-port drive); Verilator and VCS; `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`. The leaf takes the words of its AES, KMAC and OTBN operations from the external stream, selects the external source and leaves ESRC, CSRNG and EDN uninitialized.

*Source Module*: `sep_trng_ext_sink_reset_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master); independent Python references for the AES and KMAC goldens. Probes and injects. Approved: CPU-LSU master readback (pool `STATUS` and `DATA`, OTBN DMEM, AES `DATA_OUT`, KMAC `STATE`); `trng_reset_active_probe_o`; `crypto_edn_req_o[i]`, `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` and `crypto_edn_bus_o` (client 0 AES, 1 KMAC, 2 OTBN RND, 3 OTBN URND); `axis1_*`, `axis2_*` and `km_entropy_*`; the TRNG reset bit of `SW_RESET_N`. Added hook: the external stream driver, a top-port drive of `ext_trng_axis_req_i[2:0]` with a source reset, which the test adds to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. The external stream response monitor on `ext_trng_axis_rsp_o[2:0]` is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The client-side EDN synchronizer taps `crypto_edn_sync_req_o`, `crypto_edn_sync_ack_o` and `crypto_edn_sync_data_o` (the `src_req_i`, `src_ack_o` and `data_o[31:0]` of the `prim_sync_reqack_data` instance in each native client) are observation taps the test adds to the same list. Every check of this entry uses the driven external words.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. Dimensions and ranges: the reset moment (a fraction of 0 to 1000 per mille of the pool fill time T_fill, scaled to clocks after the beat stream restarts); the extra reset hold after the pool read returns (0 to 200 clocks); the fill count N (4 to 24 pairs); the count M of new pairs (2 to 8); the client order of the pre-reset leg and of the consumer trials; the delay between the request of a client and the TRNG reset in each trial (0 to 32 clocks); the beat words (unique and non-zero); the AES key row, plaintext and block count (1 to 4 blocks); the KMAC message (1 to 16 words); and the OTBN RND and URND read counts (1 to 8 each). `reseed = 4` samples four sets of these draws. The leaf logs its plan first (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`). The adapter-clear leg is fixed: every seed runs the same sweep of write delays (0 to 9 clocks per client); only the beat words come from the seed.

*Objective*: While the TRNG reset is active, the post-mux crypto and pool adapters and the pool clear, also when external inputs are selected. External traffic pauses during the clear and resumes when it ends, independently of the internal generators. The pool aperture stays responsive. An AES, KMAC or OTBN request that is pending during the clear gets no acknowledge and is served with fresh data afterwards. A word that the crypto adapter acknowledges to a client just before the clear is the word that the client takes, and OTBN RND completes with no error across the clear. Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (Resetting the TRNG); `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc` (Reset and Recovery Boundaries, lines 188-189: native requests receive no acknowledgement during adapter clear and can resume after fresh data arrives); `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc`; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/edn/README.md` line 45 (the EDN transmits random values on the bus and asserts the acknowledge to signify the arrival of fresh values); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/doc/theory_of_operation.md` line 58 (RND provides bits taken directly from the EDN) and lines 254-257 (`RND_FIPS_CHK_FAIL`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc` lines 120-121 and 139-142 (the external source mux attaches FIPS provenance 1 to every word and the native client receives it as `edn_fips`).

*Test Coverage*: The external source on the crypto and pool streams, with the Key Manager stream idle and the Key Manager in reset. A pool fill of 4 to 24 pairs, with beats offered on both the crypto and the pool stream across the reset. AES, KMAC, OTBN RND and OTBN URND served from the external words. A TRNG reset at a drawn moment with the pool non-empty and beats arriving. A pool `STATUS` read issued and answered inside the clear window. M new pairs after the release. Four consumer trials on every seed (AES, KMAC, OTBN RND, OTBN URND), each with a request pending across a TRNG reset. This entry extends `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`, which drives the reset with the internal source. This entry grades only the cells that the open test does not grade: the adapter clear with an external source, and the external source selected for a sink. The open owner keeps the rest. Out of scope: the word order inside an OTBN RND value (sets are compared); the isolation phase before the reset (it is not the clear window). The adapter-clear leg: KMAC, OTBN RND and OTBN URND each with ten TRNG resets, written 0 to 9 clocks after the first adapter acknowledge of the client, one acknowledge period of a client that keeps requesting. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_reset_cg` (`cp_clear_ext_sel`, `cp_xfer_phase`, `cp_pool_in_clear`, `cp_pool_scrub` non-zero cell, `cp_client_in_clear`, `cp_clear_after_ack`), `sep_entropy_delivery_cg` (`x_ext_beat`). The leaf writes `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` once at bring-up, before its first graded window, so it fills no `x_sel_stream` cell. The graded window of the pool leg opens before the half-pair beat, so that beat fills the pool `before_clear` cell of `cp_xfer_phase`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Set the baseline. Write `SEP_RESET_CTRL.SW_RESET_N` = 0x36 (TRNG, KMAC, AES and OTBN released; Key Manager, HMAC and ABR held in reset). Every TRNG reset below changes only bit 5 from this value. Write `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` = 0x6 (streams 1 and 2 external; stream 0 internal and idle). Log both as `INFO-BASELINE sw_reset_n=0x36 ext_trng_src_sel=0x6`. Do not initialize ESRC, CSRNG or EDN. Draw the plan and log it (`INFO-PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Pre-reset client leg, for each client in the drawn order. The driver supplies unique words on stream 1 for the whole operation; the sent set S is every word that the driver has offered. For AES: select ECB with the drawn 256-bit table key, `PRNG_RESEED_RATE` PER_1, and encrypt the drawn plaintext blocks. For KMAC: compute a KMAC-256 of the drawn message with the fixed table key and a 32-byte output, with the entropy source set to EDN. For OTBN RND: run an OTBN program that reads RND a drawn number of times and writes the values to DMEM. For OTBN URND: run an OTBN program that reads URND a drawn number of times. Expect at least one acknowledge, each word that the client accepts (`crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` with the word probe `crypto_edn_bus_o`), and every 32-bit word of each OTBN RND value, to be a member of S, and the AES ciphertext and the KMAC digest to equal the independent golden. Log the client and the counts. A client operation that raises an error fails `CHK-XRST-CLIENT` with the reason.
. Pool fill. Reset the TRNG domain (write 0 then 1 to bit 5 and wait for the fall of `trng_reset_active_probe_o`), so that the adapters and the pool are empty. The driver sends N pairs {a, a} with unique a on stream 2, one beat at a time, and it offers one unique word on stream 1, so both streams are active across the reset on every seed. No client runs in this leg, so the crypto adapter holds that one word when the clear starts and has room for a further beat. The first fill beat after the reset is the resume of a clear: if it does not complete (a bounded wait of 20000 clocks), the leaf logs `CHK-XRST-RESUME FAIL ... phase=fill` and ends the pool leg; a later fill beat that does not complete fails the leaf. Log t_xfer, the clocks of one clean handshake of the first beat on an empty adapter, and T_fill, the clocks until the 2N-th beat completes. Read the pool `STATUS` after the pool path is quiet for 64 clocks (no transfer at external port 2, no `axis2_*` handshake and no `pool_edn_ack_o`; a bound of 20000 clocks) until two consecutive reads agree, and expect `fifo_level` = N (`CTRL-XRST-POOL`). Log t_xfer, T_fill and the level as `INFO-XRST-FILL`.
. Restart the beat stream. The driver offers the first beat of one new pair {a, a} on stream 2 only, so the pool holds a half-packed word and does not fill. Read the pool `STATUS` and log `fifo_level` as `pre_reset_level` (at least 1). After the drawn offset, wait until that beat is taken and the pool path is quiet, and read `entropy_pool_packer_depth_o` as `packer_depth_at_reset` (non-zero: the pool holds a half-packed word). Then write 0 to bit 5 of `SW_RESET_N`.
. Wait for the rise of `trng_reset_active_probe_o` (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). At the rise the driver applies its source's own reset together with the sink reset: it forgets every beat it held and any partial pair, and a running source does not drop `tvalid`. Log it. From then on every beat that the driver offers belongs to the M new pairs. The driver offers the first beat of the first new pair on each active stream and holds it.
. Pool read in the clear. While the probe is high, issue one pool `STATUS` read. Wait for its response (a bounded wait of 20000 clocks; a read that does not answer fails `CHK-XRST-POOL` with `reason=timeout:read`). Sample the probe at the request and at the response. Sample the external port at every clock of the window. Hold the reset for the drawn extra hold after the response. Log `read_clk` and the window length.
. Write 1 to bit 5 of `SW_RESET_N` and wait for the fall of the probe (a bounded wait). Do not set up ESRC, CSRNG or EDN. Expect the first transfer on each active stream to complete within 16 times t_xfer plus 64 clocks of the fall (a timeout fails `CHK-XRST-RESUME`).
. After the release the driver sends all M new pairs (and unique words on stream 1). Wait until the monitor shows 2M beats complete on stream 2 (a bounded wait of 20000 clocks). Read `STATUS` after the pool path is quiet, as in step 3, until two consecutive reads agree, and expect `fifo_level` = M. Pop `DATA` M times: expect each pop to answer OKAY and the pairs to be the M new pairs in order, and none of the N pre-reset pairs. After the pool checks: send one pair {a, a} on stream 2 and wait until the pool holds it, and read the settled `STATUS` level. Start one pool `DATA` pop together with the write of 0 to bit 5 of `SW_RESET_N`, record the clocks from the read address handshake to the rise of `trng_reset_active_probe_o`, wait for the response of the pop (a bounded wait), release the reset, grade the response (`CHK-XRST-POOL-INFLIGHT`) and log which outcome occurred, the data and the two clock counts (`OBS-XRST-POOL-DATA-INFLIGHT`).
. Consumer trials, for each client in the drawn order. Reset the TRNG domain (write 0 then 1 to bit 5 and wait for the fall of the probe), so that the adapters are empty. Offer four stale words on stream 1 while the clients are idle; they join S, and the words that reach the adapter and no client takes stay buffered (`stale_buffered`, at least 1). Reset the TRNG domain again (the set-up clear). Start the client operation as in step 2. For KMAC the operation starts with a write of `CMD.entropy_req` = 1 while KMAC is idle, so that KMAC requests a new seed in the trial (`kmac.hjson`, `CMD.entropy_req`: manual reseed in EDN mode; KMAC took its first seed in step 2). For AES, KMAC and OTBN URND the driver stays silent. For OTBN RND the driver offers unique words, one beat at a time: it offers a beat only on a clock with `crypto_edn_req_o[3]` (OTBN URND) high, `crypto_edn_ack_o[3]` low and `crypto_edn_req_o[2]` low, and the next beat only after `crypto_edn_ack_o[3]` of the previous one, so no spare word stays in the post-mux staging of the crypto stream (`hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc`, Scan Protection Boundary). It stays silent otherwise. These words belong to S. For OTBN RND, require `crypto_edn_req_o[3]` = 0 on the first traced clock after the `CMD` write is queued (`CTRL-XRST-URNDFIRST`), and require the first rise of `crypto_edn_req_o[3]` (OTBN URND) strictly before the first rise of `crypto_edn_req_o[2]` (OTBN RND) (CHK-XRST-URNDFIRST). Run the client operation in its own task; an error of the operation is returned to the check, not raised. Wait until `crypto_edn_req_o[i]` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails `CHK-XRST-OTBN`). After the drawn delay write 0 to bit 5 of `SW_RESET_N`. The trial counts only if `crypto_edn_req_o[i]` = 1 and `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` has not risen since its request at the rise of the probe; otherwise log `INFO-XRST-SERVED` and repeat the trial (at most 4 repeats, a test parameter; reaching the bound fails `CHK-XRST-OTBN`).
. At the rise of the probe the driver applies its source reset on stream 1, and from then on it offers unique post-clear words Q on stream 1, one beat at a time (`tvalid` = 1 across the window, `tvalid_in_clear` at least 1 clock). Sample `crypto_edn_req_o[i]` and `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` at every clock of the window: expect the request to stay 1 and no acknowledge. Expect no client to accept a word offered before the set-up clear from the rise of the set-up clear to the end of the trial (`stale_served` = 0). An X or Z on `crypto_edn_req_o` or `crypto_edn_ack_o` on a clock of the window counts as a failure, not as no acknowledge (visible on VCS). Hold the reset for the drawn extra hold, write 1 and wait for the fall of the probe. Wait until the client completes (an outer cap of 400000 clocks, the bound of the longest client operation; a timeout fails `CHK-XRST-OTBN`). Expect the acknowledge to rise after the release, every word that the client accepts after the release to be a member of Q and not of S, every OTBN RND word to be a member of Q, and the AES ciphertext and the KMAC digest to equal their goldens. Control: step 2 shows an acknowledge for the same client.
. Adapter-clear leg. Reset the TRNG domain (write 0 then 1 to bit 5 and wait for the fall of the probe). Run one KMAC operation as in step 2 with the driver offering unique words on stream 1, and log `INFO-ADAPTER-CLEAR-WARMUP`. From here a monitor records, on every clock, the adapter acknowledge `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` with its word `crypto_edn_bus_o`, and the client synchronizer handshake `crypto_edn_sync_req_o[i]` and `crypto_edn_sync_ack_o[i]` with the word the client takes, `crypto_edn_sync_data_o`. One adapter acknowledge gives one client handshake, in order, so each handshake is paired with the oldest acknowledged word of that client.
. For each client in the fixed order KMAC, OTBN RND, OTBN URND and each write delay d of 0 to 9 clocks (one trial each, no draw): the driver offers unique words on stream 1 for the whole trial. Start the entropy burst of the client with bus accesses only before it: KMAC writes `CMD.entropy_req` = 1 while idle; OTBN RND fills its first 128 DMEM words with a sentinel that is no plan word, loads a program of 16 reads of the 256-bit RND WSR, each stored whole to DMEM (`bn.wsrr`, `bn.sid`; `otbn/data/bignum-insns.yml`, `otbn/data/wsr.yml`), and writes `CMD` EXECUTE without polling; OTBN URND loads a program that reads URND once and writes `CMD` EXECUTE without polling (the URND reseed at the start of the execution). Wait for the first adapter acknowledge of the client (a bounded wait of 200000 clocks; a timeout fails the leaf). Exactly d clocks after it, write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x16 (bit 5 cleared from the baseline: the TRNG in reset). No other bus access runs from that acknowledge until the rise of the probe, so the clocks from the write to the rise are the same in every trial. Hold the reset 16 clocks after the rise of the probe, write 0x36 and wait for the fall of the probe. Complete the operation: KMAC runs the KMAC operation of step 2; OTBN waits for idle and reads `ERR_BITS`; OTBN RND reads the 128 DMEM words back (an outer cap of 400000 clocks). Log the phase (clocks from the last adapter acknowledge of the client to the rise of the probe), the write-to-clear latency, the clocks from the acknowledge to the write and the acknowledge period as `INFO-ADAPTER-CLEAR-TRIAL`. With a fixed latency and an acknowledge about every ten clocks, the ten delays place the rise at every phase of one period, so at least one trial per client has the rise 1, 2 or 3 clocks after an acknowledge, while the client synchronizer still hands that word to the client.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-XRST-POOL
|A pool `STATUS` read that is issued and answered while `trng_reset_active_probe_o` is high returns OKAY and `fifo_level` 0 (`reset_controller.adoc`: the pool clears its FIFO contents while the TRNG reset is active and its AXI responder stays accessible). A DUT that keeps the pool contents, or hangs the read, fails. The leaf samples the probe at the request and at the response. Control: the settled `fifo_level` = N before the reset (`CTRL-XRST-POOL`). Anchor: `STATUS` readback and `trng_reset_active_probe_o`. Evidence: `CHK-XRST-POOL PASS seed=<n> level_before=<l> level_in_clear=0 resp=OKAY req_in_window=1 resp_in_window=1 read_clk=<r> window_clk=<w>`.
|CHK-XRST-PAUSE
|No transfer completes at any active external stream while `trng_reset_active_probe_o` is high: on every clock of the window with `tvalid` = 1, `tready` is a known 0 (`trng.adoc`, Reset and Recovery Boundaries: the post-mux crypto and pool adapters clear their pending and buffered state while the TRNG reset is active, and external crypto and pool traffic pauses during the adapter clear; the stream contract: a transfer occurs with `tready` high). The driver holds `tvalid` = 1 on the stream during the window, so the check does not pass on an idle port. On stream 1 the crypto adapter holds the one word of step 3 when the window opens, so it is not full: a DUT that keeps `tready` high in the window, or that drives `tready` X or Z there, fails also when it keeps the buffered word until the window ends. Control: transfers complete before and after the window in the same seed, and on stream 1 exactly one transfer completes between the set-up clear of the pool leg and the window (`CTRL-XRST-PAUSE`). Anchor: port monitor (an observation tap) and the probe. Evidence: `CHK-XRST-PAUSE PASS seed=<n> stream=<i> xfers_in_clear=0 tvalid_in_clear=1 tvalid_clk=<v> tready_low_clk=<v> clear_clk=<c> xfers_before=<b> xfers_after=<a>`, with `xfers_before=1` on stream 1.
|CHK-XRST-RESUME
|Transfers resume after the window with no ESRC, CSRNG or EDN set-up, within a bounded wait counted from the fall of the probe (`trng.adoc`: external traffic can resume when the clear ends, independently of internal generator initialization). The bound is 16 times t_xfer plus 64 clocks, where t_xfer is the clean first handshake on an empty adapter in the same seed. The first fill beat after the reset of step 3 is graded the same way (`phase=fill`, failure only). A DUT that waits for the internal chain, or never resumes (the wait times out), fails. Anchor: port monitor (an observation tap). Evidence: `CHK-XRST-RESUME PASS seed=<n> stream=<i> resumed_clk=<r> t_xfer=<t> internal_init=0 bound=<b>`.
|CHK-XRST-STALE
|After the release `fifo_level` equals the M new pairs, and every popped pair is one of them, in order (`reset_controller.adoc`: the pool clears its half-packed word, FIFO contents and fill-stall state). A DUT that keeps an old pair or a half-packed word shows a higher level or an old word. Every pop answers OKAY, and all 2M beats complete at the port before the level read. The level read is a settled read (step 8). Controls: the N pre-reset pairs sit in the pool before the reset (`pre_reset_level` at least 1), and the pool packer holds a half-packed word when the reset asserts (`packer_depth_at_reset` non-zero), both in `CTRL-XRST-STALE`. The leaf does not fill the pool to capacity, so it does not grade the clear of the fill-stall state. Anchor: `STATUS` and `DATA` readback. Evidence: `CHK-XRST-STALE PASS seed=<n> source_reset=1 pops=<M> level=<M> expect_level=<M> stale_pairs=0 order_ok=1 bad_resp=0 pre_reset_level=<n>`.
|CHK-XRST-CLIENT
|Before the reset each client is served with words from the sent set: the OTBN RND words, and the words that the adapter delivers to AES, KMAC and OTBN URND, are members of the sent set, and the AES ciphertext and the KMAC digest equal their goldens (`trng.adoc`: the crypto adapter distributes the selected stream to the native clients). Each client gets at least one acknowledge. A DUT that fabricates a word, or delivers one that the source did not send, fails. Anchor: OTBN DMEM, AES `DATA_OUT`, KMAC `STATE`, and `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` with the word probe `crypto_edn_bus_o`. Evidence: `CHK-XRST-CLIENT PASS seed=<n> client=<aes\|kmac\|otbn_rnd\|otbn_urnd> words_in_sent_set=<k>/<k> acks=<a> result==golden=<1 for AES and KMAC, na for OTBN RND and URND>`.
|CHK-XRST-OTBN
|A request of each client (AES, KMAC, OTBN RND, OTBN URND) that is pending during the clear stays 1 with no acknowledge in the window, with no X or Z on the request or acknowledge vector in the window, and is served after the release with words sent after the clear; the AES ciphertext and the KMAC digest equal their goldens (`reset_controller.adoc`: requests receive no acknowledgement while the post-mux adapter clears, stay asserted, and are served with fresh entropy). A DUT that acknowledges in the window, drops the request, drives an unknown value in the window, or serves it with a word sent before the clear fails. Control: the same client gets an acknowledge before the reset (step 2, `CTRL-XRST-OTBN`). Anchor: `crypto_edn_req_o[i]`, `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` and the word probe `crypto_edn_bus_o`. Evidence: `CHK-XRST-OTBN PASS seed=<n> client=<aes\|kmac\|otbn_rnd\|otbn_urnd> req_in_clear=1 ack_in_clear=0 tvalid_in_clear=<t> xz_in_clear=0 stale_buffered=<b> stale_served=0 ack_after_release=1 words_from_post_reset=1 post_words=<k> rnd_words=<r> result==golden=<1 for AES and KMAC, na for OTBN RND and URND> clear_clk=<c> ctrl_ack_before=1 attempt=<a>`; `INFO-XRST-SERVED seed=<n> client=<c> attempt=<a>` for a repeated trial.
|CHK-XRST-URNDFIRST
|Per OTBN RND trial: after the `CMD` = EXECUTE write, the first rise of `crypto_edn_req_o[3]` (OTBN URND) comes on a clock strictly before the first rise of `crypto_edn_req_o[2]` (OTBN RND). OTBN reseeds the URND PRNG from the EDN when it starts execution, before it fetches the instruction at address zero (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/doc/theory_of_operation.md:80`, `:132-134`; `otbn.hjson` `SecSkipUrndReseedAtStart` default 0), and RND asks the EDN only on a read or a prefetch (`theory_of_operation.md:66`, `:70-71`); the program first reads RND at its second instruction. Client numbers: `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:93`. A DUT whose RND request comes first, on the same clock, or with no URND request fails. Anchor: `crypto_edn_req_o`. Control: bit 3 reads 0 on the first traced clock (`CTRL-XRST-URNDFIRST seed=<n> urnd_at_begin=0`), so the rise is the EXECUTE reseed and not a request still high from an earlier reseed. Evidence: `CHK-XRST-URNDFIRST PASS seed=<n> urnd_before_rnd=1 urnd_rise_clk=<c3> rnd_rise_clk=<c2 or none> attempt=<a>`.
|CHK-ADAPTER-CLEAR-HOLD
|Per adapter-clear trial: on every clock with `src_req_i` and `src_ack_o` high at the synchronizer of a client, the word that the client takes equals the word that the adapter acknowledged to that client, and that word is a sent word; a handshake with no acknowledged word fails, and so does an X or Z on the request or acknowledge of the trial's client. The operation of the client completes with no error after the release (`trng.adoc` lines 188-189: native requests receive no acknowledgement during adapter clear and can resume after fresh data arrives; OpenTitan `edn/README.md` line 45: the acknowledge signals fresh values on the bus). A DUT that withdraws or zeroes an acknowledged word before the client takes it fails: the client takes a zero or another word. Controls per client (`CTRL-ADAPTER-CLEAR-HOLD`): at least one trial has the rise of the probe 1 to 3 clocks after an acknowledge of the client (`window_d`), and at least one trial with the rise 5 to 9 clocks after one passes (`clean_away_d`), so the stimulus reaches the window and the check can pass. Anchor: `crypto_edn_ack_o`, `crypto_edn_bus_o`, `crypto_edn_sync_req_o`, `crypto_edn_sync_ack_o`, `crypto_edn_sync_data_o` and `trng_reset_active_probe_o`. Evidence: `CHK-ADAPTER-CLEAR-HOLD PASS seed=<n> client=<kmac\|otbn_rnd\|otbn_urnd> d=<d> phase=<k> clear_seen=1 handshakes=<h> armed_handshakes=<a> mismatches=0 zero_taken=0 unmatched=0 xz=0 first_bad=none`.
|CHK-ADAPTER-CLEAR-RND
|Per OTBN RND trial of the adapter-clear leg: the program ends with `ERR_BITS` 0, and all 128 DMEM words, the eight 32-bit words of each of the 16 RND values, are sent words, so no sentinel is left (OpenTitan `otbn/doc/theory_of_operation.md` line 58: RND provides bits taken directly from the EDN; lines 254-257: `RND_FIPS_CHK_FAIL`; `trng.adoc` lines 120-121 and 139-142: the external source mux attaches FIPS provenance 1 to every word, the adapter forwards it into `edn_fips`, and OTBN RND checks that tag; `trng.adoc` lines 188-189). A DUT that hands OTBN a zero word with `edn_fips` 0 across the clear fails: OTBN sets `rnd_fips_chk_fail` and stores no value, or stores a word that the driver did not send. Controls (`CTRL-ADAPTER-CLEAR-RND`): at least one OTBN RND trial has the rise 1 to 3 clocks after an acknowledge, and at least one trial with the rise 5 to 9 clocks after one passes. Anchor: OTBN `ERR_BITS` and DMEM readback. Evidence: `CHK-ADAPTER-CLEAR-RND PASS seed=<n> d=<d> phase=<k> err_bits=0x00000000 rnd_fips_chk_fail=0 words_in_sent_set=128/128 sentinel_left=0 zero_words=0`; `INFO-ADAPTER-CLEAR-TRIAL seed=<n> client=<c> d=<d> phase=<k> latency_write_to_clear=<l> eff_d=<e> ack_period=<p> acks_before_clear=<a> golden_ok=<0 or 1>` per trial.
|CHK-XRST-POOL-INFLIGHT
|The pool `DATA` read that is in flight when the TRNG reset asserts completes, with `SLVERR` or with OKAY and the sent pair {a, a} (`reset_controller.adoc`: the fabric entropy-pool AXI responder is not reset, so accesses remain responsive; `entropy_pool.adoc`, Access Policy: a read of `DATA` pops one word, and a read of an empty pool returns `SLVERR`). A DUT that hangs the read (the bounded wait times out), answers with another response code, or returns OKAY with a word that was not sent fails. Control (`CTRL-XRST-POOL-INFLIGHT`): the pool holds one word before the read, the read address handshake is at or before the rise of the probe and the response is not before it. Anchor: the AXI read response and `trng_reset_active_probe_o`. Evidence: `CHK-XRST-POOL-INFLIGHT PASS seed=<n> timed_out=0 resp=<0 or 2> legal=SLVERR\|OKAY+sent_pair`; `CTRL-XRST-POOL-INFLIGHT PASS seed=<n> level_before=1 ar_to_rise_clk=<c>`.
|OBS-XRST-POOL-DATA-INFLIGHT
|Not a graded check. No text states which of the two legal outcomes of `CHK-XRST-POOL-INFLIGHT` a read in flight at the reset rise gets. The leaf logs the pool level before, the response and data of the pop, and the clocks from the read address handshake to the rise of the reset probe and from the rise to the response (`na` when none). Evidence: `OBS-XRST-POOL-DATA-INFLIGHT seed=<n> level_before=<n> resp=<r> rdata=0x<d> timed_out=<0 or 1> ar_to_rise_clk=<c or na> rise_to_resp_clk=<c or na>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Known limitation*: CHK-ADAPTER-CLEAR-HOLD (KMAC, OTBN RND and OTBN URND) and CHK-ADAPTER-CLEAR-RND fail on this DUT in the trials with the clear 1 to 3 clocks after an acknowledge. The crypto post-mux adapter forces `edn_bus` and `edn_fips` to 0 from the first clock of the clear, also for a word it acknowledged, so the client takes 0; OTBN RND sets `rnd_fips_chk_fail` (`trng.adoc`, Reset and Recovery Boundaries; OpenTitan `edn/README.md` line 45). The checks stay graded, and every other check of the leaf passes.

*Issue*: #3252

*Scope limit*: The leaf does not claim the word order inside an OTBN RND value (sets are compared), the Key Manager stream (idle, with the Key Manager in reset), or the isolation phase before the reset: the clear window is the window of `trng_reset_active_probe_o`. `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` keeps the reset with the internal source, the isolate and clear synchronizer timing, and the survival of a non-reset select across a TRNG reset. The source reset of the driver at the rise of the probe is the source's own reset together with the sink reset; the leaf does not claim a source that drops `tvalid` of a live beat. The adapter-clear leg grades the word that the client takes on its handshake clock; it does not grade a change of `data_o` on the clock after the handshake, the `edn_fips` bit of the OTBN synchronizers (OTBN RND grades it through `ERR_BITS`), the AES client (not driven in that leg), or the clear against an engine software reset (`endpoint_cancel_i`).

'''''

[[sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test]]
===== 1.53 `sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (TRNG and DRBG sinks)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense with the `LC_PROD` fuse image (the Key Manager reads OTP at boot), `+esrc_noise_force`, with the firmware image and plusargs of the open multi-sink test `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test` (`firmware = { name = "rom_main", mode = "km_rom_main" }`, `+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`), `reseed = 3`; the external Key Manager leg also uses the external stream driver (added hook, top-port drive); Verilator and VCS; `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`. Each seed runs two runs of the same firmware with a `rst_ni` pulse between them: the internal run, which holds one sink-set round, and the external Key Manager run. The leaf holds in reset the crypto engines that are not in the sink set (of OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC) and keeps the Key Manager and ABR released in both runs: the Key Manager firmware writes ABR registers at boot, and an access to a domain held in reset completes SLVERR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing), which the firmware reports as an unrecoverable fault. Each Key Manager operation (a key generate) waits for each word of its response frame for at most 400000 polls of the Key Manager mailbox `STATUS` register, 20 `clk_i` cycles apart (a test parameter, not an expected value); an expiry fails the check that waits on the operation.

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master); entropy golden (bring-up only); the AES and KMAC golden models (`cocotb/env/sep_aes_golden.py`, `cocotb/env/sep_kmac_golden.py`). Probes and injects. Approved: CPU-LSU master readback (`DATA_OUT`, `STATE`, `STATUS`, `DATA`, OTBN `ERR_BITS`); `crypto_edn_*`, `km_entropy_*`, `axis1_*`, `axis2_*` and `pool_edn_*`. Added hook: the external stream driver on stream 0, a top-port drive of `ext_trng_axis_req_i[0]` that the test adds to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, for `CHK-MIX-EXT-KM` only. The external stream response monitor on stream 0 and the internal DRBG endpoint probe of streams 0 to 2 (`drbg_int_axis_req[i]`, `drbg_int_axis_rsp[i]`, before the source mux) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. `CHK-MIX-SINK` uses approved items only. A per-clock accumulator samples the probes after each falling edge of `clk_i`. The request of a sink is `km_entropy_tready_o` (the sampler pulls), `crypto_edn_req_o[i]` or `pool_edn_req_o`; its completion is the `km_entropy` handshake, `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` or `pool_edn_ack_o`.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. Dimensions and ranges: the sink set; the counts (AES blocks 4 to 64, KMAC hashes 1 to 8, OTBN RND reads 1 to 8, OTBN URND reads 1 to 8, pool pops 1 to 32, Key Manager sampler words 1 to 16); the first-request delay of each unit (a fraction of 0 to 1000 per mille of half of the shortest clean busy time of the set, scaled in step 4); the AES table key row, plaintext and tail block, and the KMAC messages (4 unique words each). The internal run has one round with a drawn sink set: the full set of six sinks with weight 1/2, else the Key Manager sampler plus a drawn subset of 2 to 4 of the other five sinks (3 to 5 sinks). The external Key Manager run draws the gap pattern (random gaps of 0 to 32 clocks from a table of 256, or one gap of 64 clocks after a drawn beat 1 to 8; all gaps below the reset value of the sampler `CFG.TIMEOUT`, 256 active clocks, `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`) and the unique beat words; the number of beats that complete follows from the words that the Key Manager operation reads. `reseed = 3` samples different sink sets and the draws above. The Key Manager sampler is in the set on every seed. Each of the other five sinks is in the set of a leaf with a probability of 0.8, so the sink bins close on a merge of regressions; no check depends on that rate. The leaf logs its plan (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`) after the bring-up and before the first graded stimulus.

*Objective*: With the Key Manager sampler, the crypto sinks and the pool active in one run, every requesting sink completes its count. With `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` bit 0 set the Key Manager sampler receives the external stream: each word accepted at external port 0 appears unchanged and in order on the Key Manager stream after the mux, the external source sees the ready of the Key Manager, and no word reaches the crypto or pool stream. Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc` (Distribution and Source Selection: stream 0 goes directly to the Key Manager sampler; "The selected source receives the sink's ready signal"; an unselected external input receives ready high and its beats are discarded).

*Test Coverage*: On every seed one drawn sink set: the full set of six sinks (the Key Manager sampler, AES, KMAC, OTBN RND, OTBN URND and pool) with weight 1/2, else the Key Manager sampler plus 2 to 4 of the other five, with drawn start delays and counts, and the overlap of every pair held by a barrier. An external Key Manager run: unique external beats with full strobe and drawn bounded gaps on stream 0, offered until the Key Manager operation ends, with the sampler as the only sink; the beats that complete are graded and at least one completes. This entry extends `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`, which drives the four crypto sinks and the pool in one run and grades the routing, no starvation and the grant; `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`, which grades that every stream 1 and Key Manager word is a member of the generated bit multiset; and `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`, which arbitrates several sinks. This entry grades only the cells that those tests do not grade: the Key Manager sampler together with the crypto sinks and the pool in one run with drawn start delays, counts and an overlap of every pair, and an external stream into the Key Manager sampler. It does not repeat the membership check or the routing check. The open owners keep the rest. Out of scope: the grant order and the endpoint depth; the value of a delivered word (the specification gives no BIW coefficients); a partial strobe on stream 0 (the specification names the strobe rule for the crypto and pool adapters only); a TRNG reset during a sample; the `tuser` of the Key Manager stream (no probe). Functional coverage: `sep_entropy_delivery_cg` (`cp_active_sinks`, `x_sel_stream`, `cp_km_ext_stream`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the plan. The internal run has one round. Draw its sink set: the full set of six sinks with weight 1/2, else the Key Manager sampler plus a drawn subset of 2 to 4 of AES, KMAC, OTBN RND, OTBN URND and pool. Draw the count of each sink of the set. When both OTBN sinks are in the set, one OTBN program alternates RND and URND reads, so that their busy windows overlap, and they share one start delay. A unit is one operation of the set: the Key Manager, AES, KMAC, OTBN (RND, URND or both) or the pool.
. Internal run. Write `SW_RESET_N` with the Key Manager, ABR and the engines of the sink set released and the other crypto engines held in reset; log it. Write `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` = 0x0 (internal source on all streams). Bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN as the open entropy tests do, wait for CSRNG genbits, start the FIFO drain of the open tests, and boot the Key Manager with the firmware image of the open multi-sink test (wait for `RESP_KM_READY`). Log the plan.
. Run each unit of the set alone, one after the other. For the Key Manager run ceil(count / 8) key generates of 8 words to the AES destination. For AES use ECB with the drawn 256-bit table key and `PRNG_RESEED_RATE` PER_1, and encrypt the drawn plaintext blocks. For KMAC write `CMD.entropy_req` = 1 while KMAC is idle, so that KMAC requests EDN words in every run and not only for its first seed (`kmac.hjson`, `CMD.entropy_req`: manual reseed in EDN mode), then compute a KMAC-256 of each drawn message with the fixed table key; that write is the start of the KMAC sink, here and in step 5. For OTBN run the program of the drawn reads, then read `ERR_BITS`. For the pool wait until it fills to 32 entries, then pop the drawn number of words, each after a `STATUS` read that shows at least one entry. Log the clean busy time t_s of each sink: the clocks from its first request to its last completion. Log the request latency r_u of each unit: the clocks from its start to the first request of its sinks (`INFO-MIX-SOLO`). A sink with no request or no completion in its solo run fails the leaf.
. Scale each drawn delay fraction to a first-request delay d_u from 0 to half of the shortest t_s of the set. The delays set the skew of the first requests; the overlap comes from the barrier of step 5, not from the delays. Log the schedule (`INFO-MIX-SCHEDULE`).
. Run all units of the set. For the pool, wait first until it fills to 32 entries. Let R be the largest r_u of the set. Start each unit at R - r_u + d_u after a common start marker, so that its first request comes about R + d_u after the marker whatever its request latency. The barrier opens on the first clock T on which the request probe of every sink of the set has shown a request (a bounded wait of 16 times the largest start offset plus request latency of the set; a timeout fails `CHK-MIX-SINK` with `reason=timeout:barrier`). After its drawn operation and after T, each unit waits until the request probe of each of its sinks has been low for 16 clocks and marks that clock M. The pool refills to 32 entries before its tail pop. Each unit then runs one tail operation that makes its sinks request again: one Key Manager generate, one AES block (the drawn tail plaintext), one KMAC `CMD.entropy_req` write while KMAC is idle, one OTBN program with one RND and one URND read of the set, one pool pop. Then it waits until each of its sinks shows a request that starts after M and completes at least one word before the request probe drops (each wait is bounded by 16 times its request latency plus its longest clean busy time; a timeout fails `CHK-MIX-SINK` with `reason=timeout:tail_quiet` or `reason=timeout:tail_ack`). Every busy window then holds T, so every pair overlaps. Record every completion at the sinks (`crypto_edn_*`, `km_entropy_*`, `pool_edn_*`). The busy window of a sink is the span from its first request to its last completion. Log T and the first request of each sink (`INFO-MIX-BARRIER`).
. Expect each sink of the set to reach its count and complete, its tail operation to complete, and the AES ciphertext (the drawn blocks and the tail block) and the KMAC digests to equal their goldens. Compute the overlap of every pair of busy windows and log it (`CTRL-MIX-OVERLAP`). A pair with no overlap logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. Count the clocks of the overlapped run with two or more request probes high, and per pair the clocks with both request probes high, and log them (`INFO-MIX-REQ-CONCURRENT`, not graded). Stop the FIFO drain.
. External Key Manager run (a separate run of the same firmware). Pulse `rst_ni`. Hold OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC in reset and release the Key Manager and ABR. Write `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` = 0x0, bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN and boot the Key Manager as in step 2. The sink set of this run is the Key Manager sampler alone.
. Control. With bit 0 clear, start one Key Manager generate, which makes the sampler request words. The driver offers unique beats on stream 0, one beat at a time with the random gaps, until the operation ends, while the sampler takes its words from the internal source. Expect every beat to see `tready` = 1 and to complete, and on every clock with `km_entropy_tvalid_o` high expect `km_entropy_tdata_o` to equal the `tdata` of the internal DRBG endpoint of stream 0, whose `tvalid` is high on the same clock. Log the count of discarded beats.
. Write `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` = 0x1 (bit 0 set, bits 1 and 2 clear) while external port 0 shows `tvalid` 1 on every clock: before the write the driver queues 4 unique beats on port 0, and a feed keeps the queue at 4 until the write response. While bit 0 is clear each beat transfers in its clock and is discarded; after the write the held beat waits for the sampler. The selection is combinational with no switch handshake (`trng.adoc`, Distribution and Source Selection), and a sampler that sees `tvalid` fall before a handshake flags a stream error (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, DRBG Sampler), so the bridge keeps the stream legal across the switch. Expect at least one beat queued on every clock of the write and one beat held after it (`CTRL-MIX-EXT-KM bridge_*`). The beats still queued go to the sampler first and are graded like the others.
. Start one Key Manager generate. The driver offers unique beats with full strobe and the drawn gaps, one beat at a time, holding `tvalid` and `tdata` until `tready`, until the operation ends. A firmware error answer ends the operation and fails `CHK-MIX-EXT-KM` with `reason=km_op_error:<answer>`. A beat that is still held when the operation ends is not graded. Expect at least one beat to complete.
. Expect every beat that completes at external port 0 to appear at `km_entropy_*` on a clock with `km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o` high, with the same data and in order; the external `tready` to equal `km_entropy_tready_o` at every sampled clock, with at least one sampled clock on which `km_entropy_tready_o` is 0; no X or Z on a sampled probe; and on every clock with `tvalid` high at `axis1_*` or `axis2_*`, the `tdata` to equal the `tdata` of the internal DRBG endpoint of the same stream, whose `tvalid` is high on the same clock (bits 1 and 2 clear select the internal source, and the selection is combinational). Count the clocks with `tvalid` high at `axis1_*` and at `axis2_*` over the run as the control of that last read. Stop the FIFO drain.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-MIX-SINK
|Every sink of the set reaches its count and completes: the drawn number of AES blocks, KMAC hashes, OTBN reads (with `ERR_BITS` = 0), pool pops (each answering OKAY) or Key Manager sampler words finishes; its tail operation completes and is served by a request that starts after the barrier clock and after the clock M, with at least one word; the sink received words at its probe (at least the drawn count for the Key Manager sampler, at least one for the other sinks); and the AES ciphertext of the drawn blocks and of the tail block and the KMAC digests equal their goldens (`trng.adoc`: stream 0 to the Key Manager sampler, stream 1 to the crypto clients with round-robin arbitration, stream 2 to the pool). A DUT that starves a sink, or delivers a word that breaks an AES or KMAC result, fails. Control: the overlap of every pair of busy windows, each from the first request to the last completion of its sink (`CTRL-MIX-OVERLAP`; `CTRL-MISSING` and a failed leaf on 0). The control restates the construction: the barrier makes every busy window start at or before the barrier clock and end after it, so the control holds whenever the tail checks of CHK-MIX-SINK pass, and fails with them when the DUT does not serve a tail request. It shows that the busy windows overlap, not that two request probes are high on one clock; the leaf logs those clocks (`INFO-MIX-REQ-CONCURRENT`) and does not grade them. Anchor: the sink request and completion probes and the sink results. Evidence: one line per sink, `CHK-MIX-SINK PASS seed=<n> sink=<km\|aes\|kmac\|otbn_rnd\|otbn_urnd\|pool> words=<n> ops=<c> count=<c> done=1 tail_done=1 barrier_clk=<T> tail_mark_clk=<M> tail_req_clk=<r> tail_words=<w> orphan_acks=<o>`, with `result==golden=1` for AES and KMAC and `tail_result==golden=1` for AES; one `CTRL-MIX-OVERLAP seed=<n> pair=<a,b> overlap_clk=<k>` line for every pair; `INFO-MIX-REQ-CONCURRENT seed=<n> clocks_2plus=<k> both_req_<a>_<b>=<k> ...`; `INFO-MIX-BARRIER seed=<n> barrier_clk=<T> bound=<b> req_<sink>=<clk> ...`.
|CHK-MIX-EXT-KM
|Every beat that completes at external port 0 appears at `km_entropy_*` on a clock with `km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o` high, with the same data and in order; the external `tready` equals `km_entropy_tready_o` on every sampled clock; on every clock with `tvalid` high at `axis1_*` or `axis2_*`, the word equals the word of the internal DRBG endpoint of the same stream, whose `tvalid` is high on that clock; no sampled probe reads X or Z (`trng.adoc`: stream 0 goes directly to the Key Manager sampler; the selected source receives the ready of the sink; bits 1 and 2 clear select the internal source; selection is combinational). A DUT that drops or reorders a word, ties the external `tready` high, or routes a driven word to the crypto or pool stream fails. At least one beat completes, and the Key Manager operation ends with no firmware error. Controls (each `CTRL-MIX-EXT-KM`; a missing one logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf): with bit 0 clear the driven beats complete with `tready` 1, and on every clock with `km_entropy_tvalid_o` high the word at `km_entropy_*` equals the word of the internal DRBG endpoint of stream 0; the bridge keeps at least one beat queued across the select write and one beat held after it; `axis1_*` and `axis2_*` show `tvalid` high in the run, so both compares are live; `km_entropy_tready_o` reads 0 on at least one sampled clock of the run, so an external `tready` tied high shows as a difference. Anchor: external stream response monitor and internal DRBG endpoint probe (observation taps), `km_entropy_*`, `axis1_*`, `axis2_*`. Evidence: `CHK-MIX-EXT-KM PASS seed=<n> beats=<n> xfers=<n> km_xfers=<n> order_ok=1 data_ok=1 tready_eq=1 km_tready_low_clk=<k> axis1_eq=1 axis2_eq=1 ctrl_internal_discard=<n> ctrl_km_eq=1 ctrl_axis1_valid_clk=<a> ctrl_axis2_valid_clk=<b> sel=0x1 xz=0`; `CTRL-MIX-EXT-KM seed=<n> ctrl_internal_discard=<n> ctrl_not_ready=0 ctrl_km_eq=1 ctrl_km_valid_clk=<v>`; `CTRL-MIX-EXT-KM seed=<n> bridge_beats=<b> bridge_min_pending=<p> bridge_held_at_select=1`; `CTRL-MIX-EXT-KM seed=<n> ctrl_km_tready_low_clk=<k>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The leaf does not claim the grant order, the endpoint depth, or the value of a delivered word. It does not claim a partial strobe on stream 0, a TRNG reset during a sample, or the `tuser` of the Key Manager stream. `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test` keeps the routing, no starvation and the grant of the crypto sinks and the pool; `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test` keeps the membership of every stream 1 and Key Manager word in the generated bit multiset; `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test` also keeps the routing of a beat to one sink; `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` keeps the arbitration of several sinks.

'''''

[[sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_rand_test]]
===== 1.54 `sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` (AES driver); `env/sep_aes_mode_golden.py` (NIST SP 800-38A CFB-128 and OFB, NIST SP 800-38D GCM, on the FIPS-197 cipher of `env/sep_aes_golden.py`; `env/run_golden_selftests.py` checks it against the SP 800-38A vectors F.3.13 to F.3.18 and F.4.1 to F.4.6 and the GCM test cases of the SP 800-38D submission); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: none. Inject: none. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, drawn before the first stimulus: the mode (CFB, OFB or GCM), the key size (128, 192 or 256), the direction (encrypt or decrypt), the key, the IV and the input bytes. Every whole input word is unique and non-zero. The DATA_IN bytes past `NUM_VALID_BYTES` of a partial GCM block are 16 drawn non-zero bytes. CFB and OFB: 2 to 4 whole blocks. GCM: a 96-bit IV, an AAD length of 0 to 40 bytes and a text length of 0 to 48 bytes, so the last AAD or text block can be partial and either phase can be empty. The leaf redraws the input bytes until the construction guards of the controls hold; the plan stays a pure function of the seed. One cell runs per seed and the leaf does not walk the cross. The 18 cells of mode x key size x direction close over a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand-phase2,SEP_FCOV>>, `sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_cg`). The invalid `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED.PHASE` value of the phase-map step (0, or a value with more than one bit set) is drawn after every other value; the step runs on GCM seeds.

*Objective*: AES does both operations in CFB with a 128-bit segment, in OFB and in GCM at the three key lengths. Each CFB and OFB DATA_OUT block equals the SP 800-38A result for the written key, IV and input. Each GCM TEXT-phase DATA_OUT block equals the SP 800-38D ciphertext (encrypt) or plaintext (decrypt), and the DATA_OUT block after GCM_TAG equals the SP 800-38D tag over the AAD and the ciphertext. The GCM phases run INIT, then AAD when there is AAD, then TEXT when there is text, then TAG, and the last AAD or text block carries `NUM_VALID_BYTES` equal to its byte count. Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (Features); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (AES.MODE.CFB-128, AES.MODE.OFB, AES.MODE.GCM, `CTRL_SHADOWED.MODE`, `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED.PHASE` and `NUM_VALID_BYTES`); NIST SP 800-38A and SP 800-38D.

*Test Coverage*: One cell of {CFB, OFB, GCM} x {128, 192, 256} x {encrypt, decrypt} per seed, with a drawn key, IV and input, and for GCM drawn AAD and text lengths that include partial last blocks and empty phases. This entry extends `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` (1.3), which keeps the ECB, CBC and CTR cells: only CFB, OFB and GCM are graded here. Functional coverage: `sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_cg`.

*Register programming*: The GCM sequence follows `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/programmers_guide.md` (GCM Encryption, GCM Decryption): PHASE = GCM_INIT with `NUM_VALID_BYTES` = 16; the 96-bit IV in IV_0 to IV_2 (data byte order) and 0 in IV_3, so the hardware forms J0 = IV || 0^31 || 1 (SP 800-38D) (`programmers_guide.md`:153-155); for a partial last AAD or TEXT block, a wait for `STATUS.IDLE` and then `NUM_VALID_BYTES` = its byte count (`:167`, `:184`); PHASE = GCM_TAG and one block holding the AAD and ciphertext lengths (`:188-190`). Not stated in-tree: the byte order of that length block; the leaf writes [len(A)]_64 || [len(C)]_64 (bit lengths, big-endian) in the data byte order. Bytes past `NUM_VALID_BYTES` in a partial input block are the drawn non-zero fill bytes, not 0, so the device must mask them by `NUM_VALID_BYTES`. A DUT result that disagrees fails the check.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the plan from the seed and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> mode=<m> keylen=<k> dir=<d> ...`).
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with the HMAC, KMAC, OTBN and ABR bits of `SW_RESET_N` held at 0. Bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN as the open entropy tests do and wait for CSRNG genbits.
. Log the controls `CTRL-AES-OUT-NE-IN` and `CTRL-AES-MODE-DISTINCT` (a false control logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Write `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` and `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR`, each followed by a bounded wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0, the MODE code of the cell, the KEY_LEN code and the OPERATION code. The MODE and PHASE codes come from the enum lists of `aes.hjson`; the leaf checks at import that the ECB, CBC and CTR codes equal the codes of `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py`, that GCM_INIT equals the code that `aes.rdl` states, and that every code is one-hot in its RDL field width.
. CFB and OFB: write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write IV_0 to IV_3 and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. For each block: wait for `INPUT_READY` = 1, write the four DATA_IN words, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 and read the four DATA_OUT words. Every wait of the leaf has the fixed outer limit of the AES driver (`seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py`: 4000 `STATUS` polls, 20 `clk_i` cycles apart), a test parameter; an expiry fails the check of the cell with `reason=` logged.
. GCM: write `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` twice with PHASE = GCM_INIT and `NUM_VALID_BYTES` = 16. Write the key shares and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, then write the IV to IV_0 to IV_2 and 0 to IV_3 and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Every write of `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` is a pair with the unit idle before and after it.
. GCM, when there is AAD: write PHASE = GCM_AAD with `NUM_VALID_BYTES` = 16 (or the byte count when the AAD is one partial block). Write each block after `INPUT_READY` = 1; before a partial last block of two or more blocks, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 and rewrite PHASE = GCM_AAD with its byte count. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. GCM, when there is text: the same with PHASE = GCM_TEXT. After each block wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 and read the four DATA_OUT words; keep the valid bytes of the block. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. GCM: write PHASE = GCM_TAG with `NUM_VALID_BYTES` = 16, write the length block, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 and read the four DATA_OUT words as the tag. Log `STATUS` (`OBS-AES-GCM-TAG-STATUS`).
. GCM, after the tag with the unit in GCM_TAG: read the PHASE field of `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` (`CTRL-AES-GCM-PHASE-MAP`), write `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` twice with the drawn invalid PHASE value, and read the PHASE field (`CHK-AES-GCM-PHASE-MAP`).
. Compare the outputs with the golden and log the checks. Require `STATUS.ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` = 0 and `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` = 0. After a passing check write the FCOV record. Stop the FIFO drain, check the entropy alerts and the strict entropy scoreboard, and log `RESULT sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=1`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-CFB
|Every DATA_OUT block equals the SP 800-38A CFB-128 result (encrypt: C_j = P_j XOR E(C_(j-1)); decrypt: P_j = C_j XOR E(C_(j-1)); C_0 = IV) for the written key, IV and input. A DUT that runs another feedback mode, the inverse cipher, the wrong key length, or that returns DATA_IN, gives a mismatch. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_mode_golden.py`. Control: `CTRL-AES-OUT-NE-IN` (every golden block differs from its input block) and `CTRL-AES-MODE-DISTINCT` (the golden differs from the OFB, CTR and other-direction CFB results for the same key, IV and input). Evidence: `CHK-AES-CFB PASS seed=<n> mode=cfb keylen=<k> dir=<d> blocks=<b> first_bad_word=none`.
|CHK-AES-OFB
|Every DATA_OUT block equals the SP 800-38A OFB result (O_j = E(O_(j-1)), O_0 = IV, output = input XOR O_j) for the written key, IV and input. OFB decrypt has the same result as OFB encrypt, so the decrypt cells prove that a run programmed with AES_DEC uses the forward cipher. A DUT that runs CFB or CTR, the inverse cipher, the wrong key length, or that returns DATA_IN, gives a mismatch. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_mode_golden.py`. Control: `CTRL-AES-OUT-NE-IN` and `CTRL-AES-MODE-DISTINCT` (the golden differs from both CFB results and the CTR result). Evidence: `CHK-AES-OFB PASS seed=<n> mode=ofb keylen=<k> dir=<d> blocks=<b> first_bad_word=none`.
|CHK-AES-GCM
|Two lines. `part=text` (when there is text): every TEXT-phase DATA_OUT block equals the SP 800-38D GCTR result from inc32(J0), compared on the valid bytes of a partial last block. `part=tag`: the DATA_OUT block after GCM_TAG equals the 128-bit SP 800-38D tag GCTR(J0, GHASH(AAD, ciphertext, length block)); on decrypt the ciphertext is the input. A DUT that drops or mis-pads AAD, uses the wrong counter start, ignores `NUM_VALID_BYTES`, hashes the wrong text on decrypt, or uses the wrong key length gives a mismatch. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_mode_golden.py`. Control: `CTRL-AES-OUT-NE-IN`, and `CTRL-AES-MODE-DISTINCT` (the text golden differs from CTR mode started at J0, and the tag golden differs from the length block). Evidence: `CHK-AES-GCM PASS seed=<n> part=text mode=gcm keylen=<k> dir=<d> aad_bytes=<a> text_bytes=<t> first_bad_word=none` and the same with `part=tag`.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard of the bring-up (the masking reseed needs the crypto EDN leg). HMAC, KMAC, OTBN and ABR are held in reset, so AES is the only crypto EDN client.
|CHK-AES-GCM-PHASE-MAP
|On a GCM seed, after the tag, a write of an invalid `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED.PHASE` value (0, or more than one bit set) reads back GCM_INIT: "Invalid input values, i.e., values with multiple bits set and value 6'b00_0000, are mapped to GCM_INIT" (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/registers.md:525`). The unit is in GCM_TAG, from which GCM_INIT is a legal next phase, so only the mapping decides the readback. A DUT that stores the written value, or maps it to another phase, fails. Anchor: `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` readback. Control (`CTRL-AES-GCM-PHASE-MAP`): the PHASE field reads GCM_TAG before the write, so a GCM_INIT readback comes from the write. Evidence: `CTRL-AES-GCM-PHASE-MAP seen: seed=<n> before=0x20 want=0x20`; `CHK-AES-GCM-PHASE-MAP PASS seed=<n> written=0x<w> before=0x20 phase=0x01 want=0x01`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the GCM_RESTORE and GCM_SAVE phases, the readback of `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED` (except the PHASE readback of one invalid write, `CHK-AES-GCM-PHASE-MAP`), an illegal phase successor, an IV length other than 96 bits, a tag-compare status (the register text states none; the leaf compares the tag in software), or the DATA_OUT bytes past `NUM_VALID_BYTES` of a partial block (no value is stated). The GCM IV and length-block layout is DV stimulus, not a stated register contract.

'''''

[[sep_abr_combined_cmd_kat_test]]
===== 1.55 `sep_abr_combined_cmd_kat_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (ABR)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 1 seed; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_combined_cmd_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip with the CPU-LSU master readback and write of the ABR window (approved). Published NIST ACVP vectors as inputs: the ML-KEM-1024 keyGen vector (seeds d and z, encapsulation key ek, decapsulation key dk), the message m of the ML-KEM-1024 encaps vector, and the ML-DSA-87 keyGen seed and public key pk with the external-mu value mu of the sigGen vector. Vector source: `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/common/gen_abr_nist_vectors.py`; `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_abr_kem_nist.py` and `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_abr_nist.py` parse them. No published vector gives the shared key of ENCAPS for this (ek, m) pair, so the self-consistency compare has no reference model: both sides are DUT outputs. The implicit-rejection check uses the FIPS 203 function J = SHAKE256 (Python `hashlib`) as its reference model. The ABR reset bit of `SW_RESET_N`. No observation tap. No inject beyond the register writes of the scenario. No coverage label.
