*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed draws the 16 words of the ABR entropy input of the KEYGEN+SIGN command (a masking input, not a key; `SepAbrKeygenCfg`). No expected value and no logged match count depends on them. Every other input is a fixed published vector, so one seed runs the whole contract.

*Objective*: ML-KEM-1024 has the combined command KEYGEN+DECAPS (`MLKEM_CTRL.CTRL` = 4). It takes the keyGen seeds in `MLKEM_SEED_D` and `MLKEM_SEED_Z` and the ciphertext in `MLKEM_CIPHERTEXT`, and writes the shared key to `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY`. FIPS 203 correctness: for a key pair (ek, dk) from ML-KEM.KeyGen(d, z) and (K, c) from ML-KEM.Encaps(ek), ML-KEM.Decaps(dk, c) returns K. Spec: `integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl` (`MLKEM_CTRL`, `MLKEM_SEED_D`, `MLKEM_SEED_Z`, `MLKEM_MSG`, `MLKEM_CIPHERTEXT`, `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY`, `MLKEM_STATUS`, `MLDSA_CTRL`, `MLDSA_PUBKEY`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc` (OCAH Modifications: READY re-asserts after a zeroize or a reset).

*Test Coverage*: One ML-KEM-1024 KEYGEN+DECAPS whose result is compared with the K of a single-step ENCAPS against the same key pair, and one ML-DSA-87 KEYGEN+SIGN whose public-key window is logged. Extends `sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test`, which grades the park and the re-arm of both combined commands and logs the verify of the combined signature, and grades no result of a combined command; and `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`, which grades the single-step KEYGEN, ENCAPS and DECAPS against ACVP. This entry adds only the shared key of KEYGEN+DECAPS: the self-consistency compare with ENCAPS, and the implicit-rejection key when the seed d does not match the key pair of the ciphertext. Functional coverage: none.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Set `SW_RESET_N` to the baseline of the common rules with the bits of KMAC, AES, HMAC and OTBN clear and the ABR bit (bit 6) set. Log the baseline and `PLAN seed=<n>`.
. Wait for `MLKEM_STATUS.READY` = 1 (a bounded wait) and require `VALID` = 0 at that read (`CTRL-ABR-VALID-PRE`; a 1 logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Every ML-KEM command of the leaf runs this read.
. Write the ek of the ACVP keyGen vector to `MLKEM_ENCAPS_KEY` and the m of the ACVP encaps vector to `MLKEM_MSG`. Write ENCAPS (2). Wait for `VALID` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read the ciphertext c and the shared key K. Log them (`OBS-ABR-ENCAPS-STIMULUS`). No check grades this ENCAPS. Require K to hold a non-zero word (`CTRL-ABR-ENCAPS-K-NONZERO`).
. Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1. Reset ABR (bit 6 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high).
. Run step 2. Write d to `MLKEM_SEED_D`, z to `MLKEM_SEED_Z` and c to `MLKEM_CIPHERTEXT`, in that order and before the command write (the order of the KEYGEN+DECAPS cell of `sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test`; the register description does not state an order). Read `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` and require it to differ from K (`CTRL-ABR-SHARED-KEY-PRE`). Write KEYGEN+DECAPS (4). Wait for `VALID` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` and compare all 8 words with K.
. Read `MLKEM_ENCAPS_KEY` and `MLKEM_DECAPS_KEY` and log the number of words equal to the ACVP ek and dk (`OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-DECAPS-KEY`). Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.
. Reset ABR. Run step 2. Write d with bit 0 of its first word flipped to `MLKEM_SEED_D`, z to `MLKEM_SEED_Z` and c to `MLKEM_CIPHERTEXT`. Write KEYGEN+DECAPS (4). Wait for `VALID` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` and compare all 8 words with J(z‖c) = SHAKE256(z‖c, 256), z and c in the ABR byte order (byte b in word b/4, bits (b%4)·8). Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.
. Wait for `MLDSA_STATUS.READY` = 1. Write the ACVP keyGen seed to `MLDSA_SEED`, mu to `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` and the drawn entropy to `ABR_ENTROPY`. Write KEYGEN+SIGN (4) with `EXTERNAL_MU` set. Wait for `VALID` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read `MLDSA_PUBKEY` and log the number of words equal to the ACVP pk (`OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-SIGN-KEY`). The signature is not read. Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for `READY` = 1.

A wait that times out in steps 2-7 logs each ML-KEM check not yet graded as FAIL with `reason=timeout`, resets ABR and continues with step 8; the leaf fails at the end. A timeout in step 8 is logged in `OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-SIGN-KEY` with `pk_match=n/a`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-KEYGEN-DECAPS-SELFCONSIST
|After KEYGEN+DECAPS (`MLKEM_CTRL.CTRL` = 4) on the seeds d, z of an ACVP keyGen vector and the ciphertext c that a single-step ENCAPS produced against the ek of the same vector, all 8 words of `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` equal the K of that ENCAPS (FIPS 203 correctness; `abr_reg.rdl` `MLKEM_CTRL`, `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY`). This is a self-consistency compare, not a golden: K is a DUT output and no published vector holds it. A wrong DUT that ignores the keyGen half (decapsulates with a zero or stale dk), ignores the written ciphertext, or skips the decapsulation shows a different key: a wrong dk or c gives the implicit-rejection key or another value. `STATUS.ERROR` is logged in `obs_error` and not graded. Anchor: `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` readback. Controls: `CTRL-ABR-ENCAPS-K-NONZERO` (K has a non-zero word, so a window that reads 0 cannot match), `CTRL-ABR-SHARED-KEY-PRE` (the window differs from K immediately before the command write, after a ZEROIZE and an ABR reset, so a K left in the window does not match), `CTRL-ABR-VALID-PRE` (`VALID` = 0 before each command write, so the `VALID` wait sees this command). Evidence: `CHK-ABR-KEYGEN-DECAPS-SELFCONSIST PASS: seed=<n> compare=self_consistency_not_golden words=8 first_diff_word=none ss=<hex> k_encaps=<hex> valid=1 obs_error=<e>`, `CTRL-ABR-ENCAPS-K-NONZERO seen:`, `CTRL-ABR-SHARED-KEY-PRE seen: ... pre_zero=<z> pre=<hex>`, `CTRL-ABR-VALID-PRE seen: ... cmd=<encaps or keygen_decaps> valid=0 ready=1`, `OBS-ABR-ENCAPS-STIMULUS seed=<n> ... k=<hex>`, `OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-DECAPS-KEY seed=<n> ek_match=<m>/392 dk_match=<m>/792 ...` and `OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-SIGN-KEY seed=<n> pk_match=<m>/648 ...`.
|CHK-ABR-KEYGEN-DECAPS-REJECT
|After KEYGEN+DECAPS on the seed d with bit 0 of its first word flipped, the seed z of the same vector and the same ciphertext c, all 8 words of `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` equal the implicit-rejection key J(z‖c) = SHAKE256(z‖c, 256) (FIPS 203, ML-KEM.Decaps_internal: the key pair of the flipped d does not match the ek that produced c, so the re-encryption check fails and the result is K̄ = J(z‖c); J is defined in FIPS 203 section 4.1). This compare has a golden: a standard function of the written z and c. It proves the implicit-rejection path of the combined command and that the result reads z and c. A wrong DUT that keeps the key of the previous command, or returns the decrypted key without the re-encryption check, shows a different key. J(z‖c) does not depend on d, so this check alone does not show that the written d is used: a DUT that ignores d fails CHK-ABR-KEYGEN-DECAPS-SELFCONSIST in the same leaf. Anchor: `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` readback. Control: `CTRL-ABR-VALID-PRE`. Evidence: `CHK-ABR-KEYGEN-DECAPS-REJECT PASS: seed=<n> d_flip=word0_bit0 first_diff_word=none ss=<hex> exp=<hex> ne_k_encaps=1 obs_error=<e>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes and no control is missing. The `OBS-*` lines are not counted and do not fail the leaf. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test grades only the shared key of KEYGEN+DECAPS, and only against the DUT's own ENCAPS. The following are not claimed. The shared key has no independent expected value, because no published vector chains a keyGen output into an encapsulation for the same key pair. Which key windows KEYGEN+DECAPS and KEYGEN+SIGN fill is not stated, so `MLKEM_ENCAPS_KEY`, `MLKEM_DECAPS_KEY` and `MLDSA_PUBKEY` are logged and not graded (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>, the ABR combined-command row). The KEYGEN+SIGN signature and its verify are not read here; `sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test` logs the verify. The park and re-arm of the combined commands belong to `sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test`. The single-step ENCAPS is stimulus; `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test` grades ENCAPS against ACVP. A ciphertext for which decapsulation rejects is not driven through KEYGEN+DECAPS. `STATUS.ERROR` is logged and not graded. The key-vault seed path and the ABR interrupt sources are not driven.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa87_verify_test]]
===== 1.56 `sep_abr_mldsa87_verify_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa87_verify_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW (`abr_mldsa87_verify_test`, which links the boot ROM's `abr_driver.c`)

*Objective*: Proves the ML-DSA-87 verify sequence the boot ROM will use, run by
the SEP core against the RTL: pure ML-DSA-87 with an empty context, the message
streamed through `MLDSA_MSG[0]` with `MLDSA_MSG_STROBE`, and a verdict that holds
only when the accelerator preloaded `~c~`, asked for the whole message, reported
exactly one completion, read back the loaded key and c~, and produced c~. The
vectors are a 5903-byte blob and its signatures, made with the test-only key and
committed in `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/abr_mldsa87_verify_test/mldsa87_vectors.h`,
so the final message word ends with strobe `0x7`. The same image runs on the
virtual platform; each case prints one line, so the two runs' lines can be
compared.

*Test Coverage*: One genuine verify of each final-strobe form (5903 bytes,
strobe `0x7`; 5900 bytes, strobe `0x0` and a dummy word), a repeat of the
5903-byte verify as the last case, eight rejections on the accelerator, one
rejection in the driver alone, and an abort mid-stream. The identity status is a
diagnostic.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `cpu` and load the image. With `+abr_case_mask=<hex>`, rewrite the
  case mask in the firmware's `abr_case_params` block in the staged DCCM image so
  only the selected cases run. A mask that selects no case other than case 0, or a
  case above 13, fails the leaf before boot; the firmware refuses a mask that selects
  no case, or a case above 13, and reports FAIL.
. Case 0: read the ML-DSA name and version through the driver's identity check. No SEP or Adams Bridge document states the values, so the result follows `obs:` and is not graded.
. Cases 1 and 13: verify the genuine signature over the blob; case 13 repeats it
  after every other case. Case 2: verify the genuine signature over the first
  5900 bytes of the blob.
. Cases 3 to 8: verify with a flipped blob byte, a flipped z byte, a flipped c~
  byte, a wrong public key, a signature made with context `OCA`, and a signature
  over SHA-512 of the blob.
. Cases 9 and 10: a genuine c~ over a z coefficient at gamma1 - beta, and over a
  hint whose indices do not increase.
. Case 11: a c~ of zero, which the driver rejects at its argument check before
  it accesses the accelerator.
. Case 12: start a verify, stream half the blob, zeroize, read all 16 words of
  `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES`, then verify the genuine signature.
. The firmware prints the case mask it runs, one line per case (two for case 12)
  and a final line, and returns 0 only when every selected case met its
  expectation.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-FW-CONSOLE
|The console carries `abr_mldsa87_verify_test: case_mask=0x<mask>` with the mask the test selected, and `abr_mldsa87_verify_test: PASS (0 failed, <n> run)`, where n is the number of selected cases; the firmware completes with PASS. Every selected case has at least one line that starts `case <n> `, and every such line of a case other than case 0 carries `: PASS `, so a firmware that ran fewer cases than selected fails. The firmware grades each case. Case 0 is a diagnostic. Cases 1, 2 and 13: status 0 at stage DONE, 5903 or 5900 bytes streamed, VALID not before `MSG_STREAM_READY`, a non-zero result that is not `~c~`, and one command-done event. Cases 3 to 10: status `ABR_DRV_ERR_SIG_INVALID` and a reject verdict; the stage, `early`, `inv`, `written`, `nz` and `done` values follow `obs:` on the case line and are diagnostics, not graded. Case 11: status `ABR_DRV_ERR_SIG_INVALID` at stage ARGS with nothing streamed and no done event; these are values of the driver alone, because the driver does not access the accelerator in this case. Case 12: the `case 12 zeroize_midstream_abort` line needs status 0 from the load, the start, the partial stream and the zeroize, and every word of `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` at 0 after the zeroize (`res_or=0x00000000`); the `case 12 zeroize_midstream` line needs the result of a genuine case.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The verdict is the driver's, so a pass shows the RTL behaves as the
driver requires on this flow, not every behaviour of the aperture. The message is
streamed; the non-streamed and external-mu paths belong to
<<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test>>. The vectors are a committed file, so
every build and every seed runs the same bytes.
`gen_mldsa87_vectors.py --check`
(`hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/abr_mldsa87_verify_test/gen_mldsa87_vectors.py`) accepts
the header only when the genuine signatures verify with `cryptography`, each exact
mutated input of cases 3 to 8 does not verify, and the signatures of cases 9 to 11
decode as malformed. It prints the header's SHA-256, which is pinned as
`ABR_MLDSA87_VECTORS_SHA256` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/fw.mk`; the firmware build fails
unless the committed header has that SHA-256.

*Anti-vacuity*: the generator check proves each input of cases 3 to 8 fails a
reference verify, so a reject on the RTL is never caused by a bad vector, and a
genuine case that the RTL rejects fails. A reject case passes only on status
`ABR_DRV_ERR_SIG_INVALID`, so an accelerator that accepts the input fails. Case
12 reads `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` after the zeroize and then runs a genuine verify, so
a zeroize that leaves a result in the register, or leaves the accelerator
unusable, fails. The case-mask line and the per-case PASS rule fail a run whose
firmware default mask differs from the test's mask.

*Not claimed*: the command-drop fault, which <<sep_abr_cmd_drop_test>> injects;
ML-KEM; the key-vault seed path. Case 11 grades the driver only, not the
accelerator. For cases 3 to 10, the stage at which the accelerator rejects for
each cause, and the value `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` holds after a reject: no spec states
either, so the leaf logs them and does not grade them (open spec question).

'''''

[[sep_abr_cmd_drop_test]]
===== 1.57 `sep_abr_cmd_drop_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_abr_cmd_drop`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_cmd_drop_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW (`abr_mldsa87_verify_test`, genuine case only)

*Objective*: Proves that the boot ROM's ML-DSA verify driver does not trust a
verify the accelerator never ran. `+sep_abr_cmd_drop` holds the accelerator's
decoded ML-DSA command at NONE, so the command write is dropped while ZEROIZE and
the key, signature and message writes land: the effect of an instruction-skip
glitch on the command store. While the force holds, the hardware clear of the
`MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` field is also held at 0. Accepting VERIFY is what preloads
`MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` with `~c~`, so the driver's preload check is where it must
stop.

*Test Coverage*: One genuine verify with the command dropped.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Require `+sep_abr_cmd_drop`; without it the testcase refuses to run.
. Patch the firmware's case mask to the genuine case alone and boot in `cpu`.
. Expect the firmware to report the genuine case as refused at the preload
  check, before it streams a word, and to complete with FAIL.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-FW-CONSOLE
|The console carries `case 1 genuine: FAIL status=-7 stage=6 written=0 early=0 inv=0 nz=0`: the driver returned `ABR_DRV_ERR_STATE` (-7) at `ABR_DRV_STAGE_PRELOAD` (6) and streamed nothing. No `case 1 genuine: PASS` line appears. The line continues with `done=<n>`; the driver stops before it reads the command-done counter, so that field is the driver's initial value, and the test logs it and does not grade it.
|CHK-FW-DONE-FAIL
|The firmware signals completion, and the completion is FAIL. A firmware that stops after the case line fails, and so does a firmware that signals PASS.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: One fault, the dropped command. The driver's other interlocks against a
stale result -- the leading and trailing zeroize, the single command-done event,
the key and c~ read-back -- are exercised by its host unit tests, which can script
each fault on its own.

*Anti-vacuity*: <<sep_abr_mldsa87_verify_test>> runs the same image without the
fault and its genuine case passes, so the refusal here is caused by the dropped
command and not by the image or the flow.

*Not claimed*: whether the accelerator raises a command-done event for the
dropped command. No check reads that event in this leaf.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_hint_reject_kat_test]]
===== 1.58 `sep_abr_mldsa_hint_reject_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_hint_reject_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that ML-DSA-87 VERIFY rejects a signature whose hint
encoding FIPS 204 HintBitUnpack (Algorithm 21) rejects, on both interfaces the
SEP drives: pure ML-DSA with a context, the message streamed through
`MLDSA_MSG[0]` with `MLDSA_MSG_STROBE`, and external mu. A malformed hint the
engine accepted would give one signature a second valid encoding. The vectors
are every ML-DSA-87 sigVer case the NIST ACVP-Server (commit `15c0f3de`,
RELEASE/v1.1.0.42) marks "modified signature - hint": tcIds 61, 64 and 71
(pure, tgId 5) and 153, 159 and 163 (external mu, tgId 11). `gen_abr_nist_vectors.py --hint` checks
each one independently of the ACVP verdict: every z coefficient is in range
and the hint encoding is malformed, so the malformed hint is the cause of the
reject. The graded reject is the register contract: a signature verifies if and
only if `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` equals c~
(`vendor/chipsalliance/adams-bridge/upstream/src/abr_top/rtl/abr_reg.rdl`,
`MLDSA_VERIFY_RES`).

*Test Coverage*: Six malformed-hint rejections, one pure-with-context
verify that must pass (tcId 63), and one external-mu verify that must pass
(ACVP tgId 11 tcId 154, the `NIST_SV_ACCEPT` vector), run before the first
external-mu reject case.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`.
. For each case, tcId 63 first and tcId 154 just before tcId 153: write
  `ZEROIZE` and wait for VALID to drop and READY to rise. Write the public key and the signature.
. For an external-mu case, write mu to `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` and issue VERIFYING
  with `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` set. For an external-mu reject case, set the
  `sep_abr_completion_cg` reject-class label to 3 (hint) before VERIFYING and
  clear it after the `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` read.
. For a pure case, write the context length to `MLDSA_CTX_CONFIG` and the context
  to `MLDSA_CTX`, issue VERIFYING with `MLDSA_CTRL.STREAM_MSG` set, and read
  `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` and require ~c~ (`CHK-HINT-PRELOAD`). Wait for
  `MSG_STREAM_READY` or VALID. If VALID rises first, the engine rejected before
  it asked for the message, and nothing is streamed. Otherwise write each message
  word to `MLDSA_MSG[0]` after a `MLDSA_MSG_STROBE` of `0xF`, and end with the
  strobe of the trailing bytes, or `0x0` and a dummy word for a whole-word
  message.
. Wait for VALID with ERROR=0 and read `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` (CHK-HINT-CONTROL,
  CHK-HINT-REJECT). Then read back the hint words of `MLDSA_SIGNATURE` (words
  1136 to 1156: FIPS 204 sigEncode puts the 83-byte hint after the 64-byte c~
  and the 4480-byte z) and require the written values (`CTRL-HINT-SIG-LOADED`).
  The readback follows VALID, where the driver reads c~ back from `MLDSA_SIGNATURE`
  (`hw/sys/sep/bootrom/prod/doc/abr-mldsa-driver.adoc:80-83`); no text states
  what the signature reads before an operation completes. Then read back all
  648 words of `MLDSA_PUBKEY` (`sw = rw`) and require the written key
  (`CTRL-HINT-PK-LOADED`), as the driver does after VALID
  (`abr-mldsa-driver.adoc:82-83`).
. After the last case, zeroize and require that the leaf ran every case of the
  table (`INFO-HINT-CASES`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HINT-VALID
|For each case, `MLDSA_STATUS.VALID` rises after the VERIFYING command, from a
VALID that the zeroize before it cleared.
|CHK-HINT-CONTROL
|tcId 63 completes VALID with ERROR=0 and `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` equals c~: the
streamed message and the context reach the engine intact. tcId 154 (external
mu) completes VALID with ERROR=0 and `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` equals c~: the
`MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` path, which software cannot read back (`sw = w`,
`abr_reg.rdl`), delivers mu intact. Evidence: `CHK-HINT-CONTROL PASS: tcId 63
(pure, context) verifies: ...` and `CHK-HINT-CONTROL PASS: tcId 154 (external
mu) verifies: MLDSA_VERIFY_RES equals c~`.
|CHK-HINT-REJECT
|Each malformed-hint case completes VALID with ERROR=0, and `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES`
differs from c~ in at least one word: the engine did not accept the signature.
Control: `CTRL-HINT-SIG-LOADED` for the same case, so the malformed hint is in
`MLDSA_SIGNATURE` (`sw = rw`, `abr_reg.rdl`) when VERIFYING completes; the
leaf writes the signature only before VERIFYING, so a hint write that did not
land fails the control, not passes as a reject. Control: `CTRL-HINT-PK-LOADED`
for the same case, so the written public key is in `MLDSA_PUBKEY`; a lost key
write fails the control. On an external-mu case the mu write cannot be read
back; the tcId 154 control of CHK-HINT-CONTROL shows that the same write
sequence delivers mu.
|CHK-HINT-PRELOAD
|On each pure (streamed) case, `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` reads ~c~, the bitwise inverse of the c~ of the signature, right after VERIFYING is accepted and before the message stream: the driver is to "require `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` to read `~c~`: the accelerator preloads it when it accepts VERIFY" (`hw/sys/sep/bootrom/prod/doc/abr-mldsa-driver.adoc:70-72`). tcId 63 is a pure case, so every run grades it. A DUT that leaves the register at 0, at c~ or at another value fails. Anchor: `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` readback. Evidence: `CHK-HINT-PRELOAD PASS: <case>: MLDSA_VERIFY_RES after VERIFY accept is ~c~`.
|===

The leaf also logs two lines per reject case that no checker grades.
`OBS-HINT-REJECT-TIMING` (pure cases) logs `timing=early` when VALID rose
before `MSG_STREAM_READY` and `timing=late` when the whole message was streamed.
`OBS-HINT-REJECT-RES` logs the 16 words of `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` and whether they
are the inverted c~ (`res=preload`), all zero (`res=zero`) or another value
(`res=other`).

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The verdict is the engine's own result register; the command-done and
error events are graded by <<sep_abr_mldsa87_verify_test>>. The pure cases carry
contexts of 12 to 237 bytes and messages of 329 to 7927 bytes, so the context
path and a stream that ends on a whole word and on each partial length are
exercised as well.
Pre-hash ML-DSA (tgId 6) and the internal interface without external mu
(tgId 12) are not driven: the SEP uses neither.

*Anti-vacuity*: tcId 63 runs first through the same stream and context path and
must verify, so a broken stream or context fails on a known answer rather than
passing as a reject. The external-mu path has its accept control in
<<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test>>, which verifies an ACVP external-mu
signature through `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` with `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` set. CHK-HINT-VALID runs before each reject comparison, so a
VERIFY command that did not run fails there rather than passing as a reject.

*Not claimed*: the stage at which the engine rejects a malformed hint, before or
after the message stream, for each cause; no spec states it, so
`OBS-HINT-REJECT-TIMING` logs it and nothing grades it (open spec question). The
value `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` holds after a reject; no spec states it, so
`OBS-HINT-REJECT-RES` logs it and nothing grades it (open spec question).

'''''

[[sep_abr_zeroize_latency_test]]
===== 1.59 `sep_abr_zeroize_latency_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_zeroize_latency_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, `abr_zeroize_ready_probe_o`

*Objective*: Proves that an ML-DSA `ZEROIZE` returns `MLDSA_STATUS` to READY alone
from each state an ML-DSA command leaves the engine in: idle, waiting
for a streamed message, and parked at VALID after a completed VERIFY. The
sequencer parks at an operation's end with `STATUS.VALID` set, and `STATUS.READY`
re-asserts only after a zeroize or a reset (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`);
`ZEROIZE` clears every internal register and memory
(`integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl`, `MLDSA_CTRL.ZEROIZE`); and a zeroize leaves
`STATUS` reading READY alone (`hw/sys/sep/bootrom/prod/doc/abr-mldsa-driver.adoc`,
the driver's zeroize step). The leaf also measures how long the zeroize takes,
which no document states.

*Test Coverage*: Three zeroizes, one from each state.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` and wait for READY.
. Zeroize from idle.
. Write the public key, signature, context length and context of the hint
  table's pure-with-context accept case (tcId 63), issue VERIFY with
  `MLDSA_CTRL.STREAM_MSG` set, wait for `MSG_STREAM_READY`, and zeroize.
. Write the public key, signature and mu of the ACVP external-mu sigVer accept
  case, issue VERIFY with `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` set, wait for VALID, check
  READY clear at the park (CHK-ABR-PARKED), and zeroize.
. For each zeroize: from the `ZEROIZE` write, poll `MLDSA_STATUS` back to back, and
  watch `abr_zeroize_ready_probe_o` (the engine's zeroize request and its ready
  state) on every `clk_i` edge until the sequencer is back at RESET. Then read
  `MLDSA_STATUS` once (CHK-ABR-ZEROIZE-READY).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-PARKED
|After the external-mu VERIFY, the `MLDSA_STATUS` read that shows VALID set shows
READY clear (`adams_bridge.adoc`: READY re-asserts only after a zeroize or a
reset).
|CHK-ABR-ZEROIZE-READY
|After each zeroize, once the engine is back at RESET, `MLDSA_STATUS` reads READY
alone: READY set, and VALID, ERROR and MSG_STREAM_READY clear (`adams_bridge.adoc`;
`abr-mldsa-driver.adoc`, READY alone after a zeroize).
|===

The leaf also logs one line per zeroize that no checker grades.
`OBS-ABR-ZEROIZE-CYCLES` logs `engine`, the `clk_i` edges from the zeroize
request to the sequencer's return to RESET; `bus`, the cycles from the `ZEROIZE`
write's completion to the first STATUS read that returns READY after one that
did not; and `first`, whether the first STATUS read after the write still
returned READY.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: ML-DSA only; ML-KEM's zeroize is not driven. The streaming and VALID
states are entered with NIST vectors whose outcome other tests grade
(<<sep_abr_mldsa_hint_reject_kat_test>>, <<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test>>).

*Anti-vacuity*: The graded STATUS read is taken only after the probe has seen the
zeroize request, the ready state fall and the ready state return, so a zeroize
that never reached the engine, or a READY read before the sweep began, fails the
wait rather than passing the check. The streaming and VALID states are each
confirmed through STATUS before their zeroize. CHK-ABR-PARKED makes the VALID
zeroize move both bits: READY from clear to set and VALID from set to clear, so a
READY stuck at 1 or a VALID that never clears fails there. The streaming zeroize
moves MSG_STREAM_READY from set to clear. From idle, only READY is graded live.

*Not claimed*: the zeroize latency; no document states it, so
`OBS-ABR-ZEROIZE-CYCLES` logs it and nothing grades it. Whether the sweep clears
every memory word; the readable windows read 0 until VALID whatever they hold.

'''''
